# Gates · DECISIONS.md — spoken, and waiting to be spoken

Two lists. **Spoken** is append-only: date, the call, the operator's own
words, a pointer. **Open** is the knob registry — every **(knob)** in the
docs, deduped, with its shipping default. A loop may rely on a default;
only the operator moves a knob from open to spoken. No number gets
invented into code that isn't on this page.

## Spoken

| date | decision | pointer |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-18 | **The swing is `Sword_Attack`, retimed to fit the cadence — the clip was made to fit rather than the fit made to accept the clip.** (*"the problem with Punch_Cross is our model has a big head and its like leaning forward in that clip and the hands clip all into the head. can we make Sword_Attack fast and time perfectly with what we need?"*) This closes the previous row's open question in the direction it listed second. **The operator's report was checked and is exactly right, in numbers**: sampling both clips against the shipped mesh, `Punch_Cross` brings a hand to **0.147 m** of the head's centre while the vertices weighted to `Head` reach **0.295 m** — 15 cm of hand inside the log — and `Sword_Attack` holds **0.490 m**, the widest berth of any swing in the library. A big head is not a detail the clip's author knew about, which is the general lesson: **a retargeted clip inherits the source's proportions as assumptions**, and this body breaks them. **`SWING_CLIP_S` is now DERIVED rather than typed** — `SWING_INTERVAL_TICKS / TICK_HZ − ANIM_BLEND_S − one resample frame` = 1.05333 s — and `ci/retarget_anim.py --retime` cuts the asset to it, compressing the clock over the complete motion rather than dropping frames out of the middle. `tests/rig_asset.rs::the_swing_clip_fits_the_swing_cadence` closes the loop and was proven red against the 1.5 s asset. **The spare frame is the merge's doing and is load-bearing**: the parallel lane's `tests/anim.rs` asks for a STRICT `clip + blend < cadence`, and a clip derived to land exactly on the cadence quantizes to whole frames and can round just past it — so the derivation leaves one. That lane's gate also turned out to be checking `mannequin.gltf`, which `anim.rs` stopped loading a day earlier; it passed throughout by coincidence, since every name this client asks for came *from* the mannequin by retarget, so it could never have caught the one thing it existed to catch. Repointed at `stumpy.glb` and proven red under a renamed clip. In short: change the sim's cadence and the constant moves, the shipped clip no longer matches, and the gate says so instead of every arc being silently cut off by the next. | `crates/client/src/render/anim.rs` (`Clip::Swing`, `SWING_CLIP_S`) · `ci/retarget_anim.py` `--retime` · `crates/client/tests/rig_asset.rs` · `assets/models/MANIFEST.md` |

| 2026-08-17 | **An agent may `git push` when the operator has plainly asked for it.** (*"adjust claude.md so that when its clear that i wanna push you can push please and then push it"*.) `CLAUDE.md` had made publishing operator-only without exception — *"Publishing is an operator act: read the diff, then `git push --no-verify`"* — which is now narrowed rather than deleted. **The bar is an explicit instruction in the conversation** about a state the operator can see, and the distinction that matters is against the near miss: approval of the WORK is not approval to publish it, so *"looks good, merge it"* stays a merge and a green gate is never a licence. **Three things deliberately do not move.** The **loop** still never pushes — it has no conversation to be instructed in, so every autonomous pass still ends unpushed, which is what keeps this from being a general loosening. The **pre-push hook stays** and `--no-verify` remains the only way past it: the friction is the point, because a push is publication and hard to take back. And the other operator-only acts are **untouched** — the tag, the public-shard deploy, the depot publish, anything on-chain — because pushing a branch is none of them. One obligation is added rather than removed: **say what the push contains, including commits that are not the agent's**. A fast-forward publishes everything between the remote and the tip, and the operator is agreeing to the sentence put in front of them. Exercised immediately: this push carried `44acd31` plus three commits from another session on the same branch, and that was stated before it went. | `CLAUDE.md` §the loop that builds this repo · §loop discipline (unchanged) |
| 2026-08-17 | **First-person arms are built, and the reason they were called impossible is worth more than the feature.** (*"Ok lets keep building"*, against the previous row's costing.) That costing said "hide everything but the arms is not reachable on this asset" — one mesh, one material, so `Visibility` has no limb to reach, and the only lever that does (a joint at zero scale) inherits down the hierarchy. **The reasoning was sound and the conclusion was wrong**: what was missing was not a trick but *something to hide*. `ci/split_arms.py` makes one, splitting the mesh by skin weight into `char1_arms` (8,184 tris, 26%) and `char1_body` — two nodes sharing one skeleton, one material and **one set of vertex buffers**, differing only in their index array, so the split costs 0.4 MB rather than a second copy. Triangles are assigned by MAJORITY, not per vertex, because a per-vertex rule tears the shoulder and leaves both halves holed. **The placement is derived rather than dialled in**: the rig is *measured* to face +Z (`headfront` leads `Head` by 0.18 m; the toes lead the hips), a Bevy camera looks down −Z, so the arms are yawed 180°, and `VIEWMODEL_ARMS` is whatever puts the hold clip's right hand exactly on `VIEWMODEL_HOLD`. The clip itself was chosen by computing where all 53 put that hand in view space: nine land in frame and `Pistol_Idle_Loop` is the only two-handed hold that loops. Measured in the running client, the hand arrives at **(0.321, −0.305, −0.522)** against (0.32, −0.30, −0.52). ⚠ **And it still drew nothing.** Frustum culling had to be disabled, because a skinned mesh's `Aabb` is its bind box tested against the mesh NODE's transform, and that node hangs under an armature carrying `scale 0.01` — so the culler was rejecting a 2 cm blob 1.5 m below the eye while the GPU skinned the vertices in front of the camera. **That is the same fact — a skinned mesh is not where its node says it is — for the third time in one day, in a third disguise**: it made the bench report a 1.8 m character as 18 mm, it made `--hide` silently do nothing on a mesh node, and now it made a correct viewmodel invisible. The diagnostic that measured the hand is what separated "wrong numbers" from "right numbers, nothing drawn", and it stayed in for that reason. **Not built, on purpose**: no viewmodel render layer, because a second camera has to duplicate exposure, tonemap and atmosphere and `CLAUDE.md` says that coupled set has one owner — the held item has carried the same flaw since it existed; and the item is not parented to the hand, because a grip is judged by looking rather than derived. | `ci/split_arms.py` · `crates/client/src/render/viewmodel.rs` (`ViewArms`, `VIEWMODEL_ARMS`, `dress_arms`, `arms_report`) · `render/anim.rs` (`ARMS_HOLD_CLIP`, `ARMS_NODE`, `BODY_NODE`) · `crates/client/tests/rig_asset.rs` · `NOW.md` §0chr |

Two things could be wrong and both are gated (`tests/head_look.rs`, proven red under each): the **axis is derived from the rig's rest pose, never typed**, because this skeleton's neck is not axis-aligned and a typed `Vec3::X` swings the head sideways instead of nodding — the same measurement-not-convention lesson the retarget paid for an hour earlier — and the override **removes its own previous delta** before composing, so a future clip that does not animate the head cannot wind it into the ground. ⚠ **The gate's first fixture was itself broken in the instructive way**: it yawed the neck 90° to defeat a typed axis, which also put the gaze parallel to the pitch axis, so three tests failed against correct code. | 2026-08-17 | **The mannequin's whole animation library moves onto the new character — 46 clips, retargeted, no credits spent.** (*"hey can we somehow get all the animtions to stumpy now somehow"*.) The delivery had seven; the game now has **53**. `ci/retarget_anim.py` is the answer and it is a **rest-pose retarget**, which is the only kind that can work here: the two rigs disagree about what "no rotation" means — the source is a 53-joint Rigify skeleton in a T-pose rest, the target a 24-joint generator rig whose spine is named in the opposite order (`Spine02` is the *lowest*), each authored Z-up under a different stand-up rotation. So a channel is never copied; what transfers is the world-space deviation from rest, `D = Gt_src · G_src⁻¹`, re-anchored as `Gt_tgt = D · G_tgt` and localised. **Globals are accumulated from the SCENE ROOT**, which is what makes the two stand-up rotations cancel instead of needing a correction term somebody derives by hand and gets wrong. **Rotations only, and that is a rule rather than a shortcut**: a source bone's translation is *that skeleton's limb length*, so copying it stretches the target onto somebody else's build. The hips are the one exception — that channel is the body moving through the world — converted by the hip-height ratio and this rig's centimetre units. **The check that settles the transform is `A_TPose`, which retargets to an actual T-pose**; `Idle_Loop_alt` and `Walk_Loop_alt` land on top of the clips authored for this character, and `Swim_Fwd_Loop` puts it horizontal. ⚠ **And it was not sufficient — the first cut shipped with the arms 43° low and a person looking is what caught it** (*"with spell simple its right arm is inside the model"*). Transferring a delta from rest assumes both rigs' rests are the SAME POSE, and these two are not: source a true T (upper arm `[1, 0, 0]`), target an A. Measured per bone: spine 2–7°, legs 3–17°, **arms 36–43°** — so `A_TPose`, the walk, the sprint and every leg passed while every arm arrived 43° low, which reads as a hand inside the torso and looks like a proportions problem. Fixed by anchoring each bone on a virtual rest aligned to the source's bone direction (minimal-arc, so no twist is invented). **The transferable lesson is the shape of the false green**: a correct transform, a correct diagnostic, and an assumption underneath both that nothing was checking — and the check was one command, comparing the two rigs' rest bone directions. Nine seconds, **+1.0 MB**, 2.8 → 3.8 MB. **On a name collision the character's own clip keeps the bare name** and the retarget takes `_alt`, because a retarget of somebody else's idle is not an improvement on an idle authored for this body. Two consequences landed with it: `Clip::Sprint` points at a real `Sprint_Loop` and the one-afternoon 1.35× alias is deleted along with the whole `Clip::speed` hook, and **the mannequin is now kept for a stated reason** — a retarget is reproducible only while both rigs are in the tree. **What it cannot supply is a gather swing**: 46 clips contain no chopping motion, so the game's most-performed verb is still the one thing to buy (`WANTED.md` §11.1). | `ci/retarget_anim.py` · `assets/models/stumpy.glb` · `crates/client/src/render/anim.rs` · `assets/models/{MANIFEST,WANTED}.md` · `NOW.md` §0chr |

It loses **39 animation clips**: the delivery carries seven on its own 24-joint skeleton, so `Sprint` plays the jog at 1.35× and death, gather, hit, jump, swim and crouch have no clip at all. The mannequin stays in the tree unloaded, because it is those clips. | `crates/client/src/bin/modelview.rs` · `ci/import_char.py` · `crates/client/src/render/anim.rs` · `crates/client/tests/rig_asset.rs` · `assets/models/{MANIFEST,WANTED}.md` · `NOW.md` §0chr |

It runs `SITE_BLEND_M = 12 m` past it, because nothing ever required the two to be equal: `scatter_m` answers "does the grid stand anything here" and past the floor the answer is *yes*, so vegetation grows over the ramp and is what stops it being visible (`MONUMENTS.md` §3 — the reference blends with authored masks, not flattened circles). Swept over 128 seeds: 10 m is where every site flattens completely, 12 is the margin. (2) `max_cut` — the cut is clamped to what the ramp can carry (`band × CLIFF_SLOPE_RATIO × SITE_CUT_HEADROOM / 1.5`, headroom **0.5**, the rest of the cliff budget left for the raw ground the ramp adds to). **`WAYSTATION_RADIUS_M` is derived rather than picked**: its own floor (11.10 m, the canopy's footing) plus the pad's band, = 15.01 m, so both tiers blend over the same distance and it stays inside `HAVEN_RADIUS_M` — the existing "lesser tier is smaller" assert never had to move. **Measured, 128 seeds, at the shipped constants**: every site fully flat (worst floor 8.06 m → 0.000), worst gradient the carve ADDS 0.595 against its 0.596 budget — the clamp binding exactly where it was derived to. **`test_terrain_golden` moved and is regenerated in this commit** — `probe_sites` hashes windows over the pad and both waystations, all three of which stand on the road ring and therefore inside the carve, so a digest that had NOT moved would have meant the carve was not reaching its own sites. ⚠ An earlier draft of this row claimed the goldens were untouched; that was read off a `cargo test` run which had aborted before the golden's binary was scheduled. **So this IS a wipe** (2026-08-10's row). The operator's word wins; what the doc's reasoning still buys is the *ordering*, which does not change — weapons must carry a damage type before an armor protection vector has anything to key against, so that remains the first move rather than a deferral. **What this commits us to**, each with its wall: a `CONT_WEAR` container (wall 6 — `CONT_KIND_BITS` is 2 and all four values are spent, so the field widens to 3 bits and `PROTO_VER` bumps with regenerated goldens in the same commit); a wear array on `Player` (wall 5 — `state_hash` and the persist format both take it, and every byte-offset test in `persist.rs` regenerates); a `damage_type` column on every `content/weapons.toml` row and a per-type vector replacing `Armor::reduction_pct` (wall 7, and `balance.rs` anchor 2 has to keep holding across the vector); and a reduction arm in every route a player takes damage by, not only the melee swing. **The one thing the grid forces that neither the doc nor the question anticipated**: `combat.rs:36` says there are no hit areas — every hit is a body hit — so a five-area grid would ship four areas that can never be struck, which is the *paid nobody* failure `ARMOR.md` exists to name. A coverage model is therefore owed, and its arithmetic is a **proposed default in §open** rather than a spoken number. | `reference/ARMOR.md` §9 · `content/armor.toml` · `content/weapons.toml` · `sim-core/src/{inventory,combat,world,persist}.rs` · `protocol/src/{lib,event}.rs` · §open "equipment v0" |

| 2026-08-16 | **Discord presence may say what a player is doing in detail, and may offer a join — gated on the player enabling it.** (*"can we update it show it shows a player playing in decent detail? nothing wrong with that if the player enables it? or at least a join server button"*) This **overrules the deferral in the same day's §open row**, which shipped an address-free presence and left the join blocked pending this question. The condition is the design: *"if the player enables it"* is opt-in, so the disclosure is a **setting** rather than a refusal. **Two settings, because they are two different disclosures** (`SOCIAL` on the settings screen): `discord_presence` (default **on**) carries the verb, the biome and the party count — none of it locates a machine or names a person; `discord_share_server` (default **off**) adds the shard's name and address, which is what makes Discord's **Ask to Join** appear. They are a *consent*, not two knobs: sharing implies presence and turning presence off withdraws sharing, in `adjust` **and** in `from_persisted`, so neither a hundred clicks nor a hand-edited file reaches `share = true` under `presence = false` — a latent publish-my-address waiting for the master to come back on. Both directions are gated and each was proven red under its own planted defect. **The detail** is `Activity`: the verb + where on the island (`terrain::biome` off `Eye`, withheld until `Eye::placed` because before the first snapshot the position is the world origin, which is a real place and would name the wrong biome with confidence), the party count, and an elapsed clock. **Bounded inline strings, not `String`** — the Bevy side rebuilds `Activity` every frame to compare, so a `String` in it would be two allocations per frame forever; the allocating half is the worker's. **The join is Ask-to-Join, not a button**, because Discord's buttons take https urls only and ours is a `scry://` deeplink. The emitted half is `party` + `secrets.join`; the received half is a `SUBSCRIBE` to `ACTIVITY_JOIN` and a reader thread, and the address it hands back gets `shardlist::check_addr` — the same whitelist a stranger's pasted deeplink gets, because it is the same threat. An invite is honoured only from the menu: a player mid-session is not yanked out of a world by someone else's click. **Still dark without `GATES_DISCORD_APP_ID`**, and the fresh-launch half of Ask-to-Join is the existing deeplink plus one portal setting rather than any code here. | `crates/client/src/discord.rs` · `render/presence.rs` · `render/settings.rs` (`SOCIAL`) · `config.rs` · `NOW.md` §0dsc |

Each is a slice, not a keystroke, and they are queued in `NOW.md` §0ctl rather than bound to a dead key. **Two collisions were forced by the list and resolved toward it**: `G` was eat, which moves to **`J`** — the old binding's stated reason was that eat and drink sit under one hand, and `J`–`H` are adjacent exactly as `G`–`H` were — and `R` stays **repair**, because the reload that would have claimed it does not exist. **Three things landed that are not bindings and are the reason this was not a one-line pass.** (1) **Crouch crosses the wire and the sim ignores it, stated rather than hidden**: `BTN_CROUCH` has been in `BTN_MASK` since v0 and no code reads it, so the bit is accepted, prediction cannot drift on it (both sides run the same `movement::step` over a bit neither consults), no golden moves — and the settings row says *"sent, no effect until the combat pass"* instead of implying a stance. (2) **Free look is a strict body/head split, not a mode flag.** `Look::yaw`/`pitch` stay the BODY's and keep feeding `set_input`, `verbs::resolve`'s pick, the ghost, the compass and the map bearing; `Look::free_yaw`/`free_pitch` are the head's offset and exactly one line adds them in (`place_eye`, into `Eye`). So the invariant is a grep rather than an argument — a reader of `Look` follows the body, a reader of `Eye` follows the head — and `frozen` disables the mode outright, so **no capture frame can move**. (3) **The held map fixed a latch bug it would otherwise have made routine.** `set_input` is a latch `advance` re-emits every tick, and `map::enter` never zeroed it the way `pause::enter` and `death::enter` do — so opening the map while holding `W` walked the body forever. | 2026-08-15 | **East is `-X`. North stays `+Z`. The compass card is what has to be right, and `bearing == wire yaw` is retired.** (*"idk what to pick so just pick something"* — the choice `compass v0` posed the day before was handed back, so this row is the operator's call made by delegation and the reasoning is the assistant's.) The measured defect: the world is right-handed and Y-up, so facing `+Z` a body's right is `-X`, which `bearing_deg = atan2(fx, fz)` labels **west**. All four cardinals sit 180° from what a compass card wants — a reflection, not an offset — so **a player turning right watches the number fall** (probed: 22.5° → 9.8° → 357.2° → 344.5° → 331.9° → 319.2° over five 100 px pushes right). **What this spends is the property the old row was decided on**: the bearing becomes `(360 − yaw°) mod 360` rather than the wire yaw itself. Three reasons that is the right side to spend. (a) The identity was **already false in the tree** — `hud.rs:1712`'s raid readout computes its own `atan2` and never goes through `bearing_deg` — so "the same number" was being held by luck at one of two sites, which is the drift the old row feared, arriving anyway. (b) A conversion with **one named owner and a gate** does not drift; an identity maintained by coincidence does. (c) The alternative that keeps both — flipping `yaw_dir` in `sim-core` so yaw counts clockwise — buys a developer convenience with a **semantic wire change**: same layout, different meaning, so a stale client walks mirrored while `test_protocol_golden` stays green. Refused; a debugging nicety does not get to touch wall 5 or 6. **North was not moved instead** (`-Z` north keeps `+X` east and costs `(180 − yaw°)`), because it re-labels the sim's grid-edge constants the same way with no compensating gain. **Three riders, all decided here rather than left open.** The `-0.0` trap: `(-fx).atan2(fz)` is `-0.0` at yaw 0 and `f32::rem_euclid`'s guard is `r < 0.0`, so due north would print **`-00°`** at both `{:03.0}` sites — it is written `(360.0 - fx.atan2(fz).to_degrees()).rem_euclid(360.0)`. The **map moves in the same commit or not at all** — a north-up map has east on the right only if east is `-X`, so `ui::map`'s x term, `grid_label`'s column, `paint`'s write index and `LIGHT[0]` are one change with the two `atan2`s (half a switch is worse than none, `CLAUDE.md`'s mingw entry). And `capture.rs`'s vantages **are renamed**: `1-east` ↔ `3-west` swap labels and keep their indices, so the camera a past `-visual.md` cites is still findable while every future frame is labelled truthfully — the entire cost of this bug was a wrong label, so keeping a known-wrong one to protect citations is the wrong trade. `build.rs`'s `LOC_EDGE_N`/`_W` → `LOC_EDGE_ZLO`/`_XLO` stays a systems-lane edit and is not required by this row. **The sun was never wrong** and gap 2's "azimuth consumed twice" is retired with it: the disc lands at pixel 640 of 1280 in a frame shot at `RIG_SUN_AZIMUTH`, `269 ≈ 2 × 134.6` is coincidence, and the relation that holds is `360 − 134.6`. | `crates/client/src/look.rs:88` · `crates/client/src/render/hud.rs:1712` · `crates/client/src/ui/map.rs` · `crates/client/src/render/capture.rs` `VANTAGES` · `NOW.md` §0gj · `findings/note-20260815-the-compass-is-mirrored-not-the-sun.md` · `findings/note-20260815-the-sun-is-where-the-constant-says.md` |
| 2026-08-17 | **Input is latched between sim ticks, and the frame cap ships UNCAPPED** (*"cant we cap thigns at like 60 fps?"*, then, on being shown what a cap does and does not buy: *"i mean client can go faster than 60 we cant stop that. but we can handle input some regulated way? making sure i dont miss anything here"* — the second is the one that decided it). **The cap was aimed at a symptom.** What actually cost a fast client something was input sampling: `ClientCore::set_input` overwrote, and `advance` read it only at the 30 Hz boundary, so the one render frame that reached the sim was whichever fell last before a tick and **every press that began and ended between two ticks was silently dropped**. Measured on the tree that had it: a normal ~50 ms click survived 100 % at every framerate from 30 to 400 — which is exactly why nobody caught it — while a press lasting one render frame survived 100 % at 30 fps, 50 % at 60, and above that only by luck of phase. The loss is roughly `1 - press_ms / 33.3`, so **drawing faster made a fast click likelier to vanish**. `ClientCore::sticky_buttons` ORs every press seen since the last tick and the first tick consumes it, which makes a tap exactly one tick of the button — never none, never two. Buttons only, deliberately: the move axes stay level-sampled (an axis has no impulse meaning and synthesizing ~18 cm of travel from a 2.5 ms tap invents motion nobody held), and the view angles need nothing because `Look` integrates mouse delta into a free-running angle the tick already samples whole. **No wire or determinism surface** — it changes which byte goes into a frame the client already sent, so the server sims what it receives as before and the predictor uses the same frame it sends. Gated at 30/60/120/144/240/400 by `client-core/tests/input_sampling.rs`, all four directions (a tap arrives, arrives once, a hold stays held, and the sim tick count does not follow the frame rate), proven red under the defect. **With that fixed, frame rate and input fidelity are no longer traded against each other**, so the ceiling goes back to being the player's: `max_fps` defaults to 0. `limit_frames` and the ladder stay, because the one real cost the audit found is still real — with vsync off, a menu holding one still image is redrawn as fast as the hardware allows — and now it has a switch instead of a policy. Superseded row follows. | client-core `core.rs` · client `render/settings.rs` `config.rs` `render/mod.rs` |
| 2026-08-17 | ~~**The client caps at 60 fps by default**~~ — SUPERSEDED the same day by the row above; kept because the mechanism it describes still ships, only the default moved. (*"cant we cap thigns at like 60 fps? and in general check over all the timing things"*). Bevy's focused update mode is `UpdateMode::Continuous` — "as fast as it possibly can" — and `WinitSettings::default()` is `game()`, so **vsync was the only thing holding the render loop down, and vsync is a row on our own settings screen**: turning it off uncapped the client, and a menu with one still image on it would spin a core and a GPU at four figures to redraw it. `settings::limit_frames` runs in `Last` (a cap has to be the frame's final act; earlier it sleeps before the render it is pacing) and paces to a deadline the way the shard's tick loop does — advance from the target, not from "now", so the average rate does not drift slower by each sleep's overshoot, and **re-base rather than catch up**, because sprinting through uncapped frames to repay a stall is the behaviour a cap exists to prevent. Uncapped is the far end of `MAX_FPS_LADDER` (30/60/120/144/240/off) and, per the row above, the default. A hand-edited file may set an off-ladder rate (165 Hz is on nobody's ladder) but is floored at 30, because one frame a second is indistinguishable from a hung client. **Two consequences worth knowing.** It narrows the framerate-dependent-smoothing question in the row below to a bounded one: the reconciliation decay runs once per frame, so a default 60 makes it ~60/s for most players instead of whatever the panel does. And the per-frame streaming budgets (`props`/`terrain_mesh`/`clutter`/`structures`, one chunk each per frame) now build 60 chunks/s rather than however many an uncapped loop managed — a 25-chunk near ring fills in ~0.4 s at 60 and ~0.8 s at 30, both fine, and unchanged for anyone who had vsync on. **Not a fix for anything measured** — the audit that came with it found the shard's tick loop already deadline-paced, every server sleep either idle-gated or a backoff, and Bevy's *unfocused* mode already `reactive_low_power(1/60)`, so a backgrounded client was never the waste; a focused one on a menu was. | client `render/settings.rs` (`MAX_FPS_LADDER`, `limit_frames`, `FrameDeadline`) · `config.rs` · `render/mod.rs` |

| 2026-08-14 | **The seed stays 20260731 — the island was never the problem, the sweep window was** (*"and yea we need a better seed"*, asked on a finding that did not survive re-measurement, so this row records a **withdrawn** wipe rather than a performed one). The pass of 2026-08-14 reported the shipped island as the minimum of forty on all three axes — 46.32 m, slope 0.890, granite on 0.00% of its land — and that is what "we need a better seed" was answering. **Every one of those numbers came from a sweep of `-1024..1024`.** `terrain::continent` centres the island on `(ISLAND_SIZE/2, ISLAND_SIZE/2)`, so world coordinates run 0..2048 and that square's *corner* is the island's centre: one quadrant, 632 k m² of a 2.9 M m² island, and not the quadrant the capture camera stands in (x 1155). Every seed was swept the same way, so the comparison held and the conclusion did not. Over the whole square 20260731 reaches **106.00 m, slope 2.665, granite on 10.0% of its land** against a 44-island median of 106.0 / 2.585 / 7.2% — **upper third, not an outlier** — and within 300 m of the capture spawn it paints **8.9%** where the median island paints **0%**. So the public shard is not wiped, the replacement seed (20260806, which measures *worse* at the camera: 0‰ rock, 11 m tall, half of it sea) is not shipped, and what actually changed is the arithmetic: both island sweeps now cover the world square, `relief.rs` gained `the_sweep_covers_the_island_and_not_one_quadrant`, and `sim-core/examples/seed_scan` ranks 44 islands with an `--at x,z,r` mode for what one camera can see. **A frame with no granite in it is now the renderer's to answer for.** | `sim-core/tests/relief.rs` · `client/tests/ground_identity.rs` · `sim-core/examples/seed_scan.rs` · `ART.md` · `RENDER.md` §capture · `NOW.md` §0gi |

Asked in answer to the one honest cost of our determinism posture: the reference game can pin a generated map to a file and keep serving it, while our world **is** the code, so touching anything in `terrain.rs` moves the ground under every base at the same seed. The operator's read of the reference is right and is the reason this is cheap to accept — they force wipes on map-affecting changes and could not avoid it either, because no converter can move a foundation onto ground that changed shape under it. **The call settles the economics, and it is not the mechanism.** What landed with it is *detection*: the world file's header now carries `probe_terrain(seed)` — the same digest `test_terrain_golden` pins and the parity gate diffs — beside the seed and the content hash, so a build whose worldgen moved refuses the file at boot with a message naming the wipe, instead of loading it and putting bases in the air. `WORLD_FILE_FORMAT` 1 → 2; the field spends the padding the header was written with; computed once per boot, never per save. | 2026-08-10 | **Wipe weekly, and a golden regeneration is not a cost** (*"we have no worlds to wipe so thats fine. and also we wipe on updates so it is what it us. do it once a week."* · *"im fine with raising the sim or whatever we got the juice i think"*). Two calls in one breath, both answering `reference/PLANTS.md`. **First, the cadence moves monthly → weekly** (§open's "wipe cadence · BP survival" row and `DESIGN.md` §2/§14). The stated reason is the interesting half and it is an *alpha* reason rather than a design one: the map is already wiped on every update, so a monthly promise was describing a world that does not last a month anyway. **What deliberately does NOT move is BP survival** — blueprints still outlive one extra cycle — and at a weekly cadence that is a materially more generous rule than it was at monthly, which is a consequence to look at once players exist rather than a thing to pre-correct. **Second, the terrain goldens are cheap to regenerate.** `test_terrain_golden` and `test_replay` reddening on a worldgen change had been treated as a blocker across two passes; it is not one before there is a shard with players on it, and saying so out loud unblocks the scatter work. **Third, the sim's tree-clearance ceiling may rise** to fit a wider crown — `PINE_MAX_R` and the `SPAWN_CLEAR_M` derived from it. | 2026-08-10 | **Ranged combat tracks the reference game as closely as we can, and arrows come back** (*"why cant we get arrows back? why cant it be like rust?"* · *"in general yea we need to get it as close as we can to how rust works"*). Asked in answer to `reference/PROJECTILES.md`, which had just recorded five gaps between our ranged v0 and theirs. The call resolves all five toward theirs. **The one thing "as close as we can" cannot reach, and why**: their arrow is *simulated on the client* and audited by a thirteen-convar tolerance budget (§2/§3). Ours cannot be — a client-authored projectile cannot enter the WAL, cannot replay, and hands an RL agent the tolerances to optimise against (walls 5 and 1, `PLAYERS.md`). **That is a divergence in authority, not in feel**: the client already predicts movement, an arrow's path is a pure function of `(yaw, pitch, speed, drop)`, so the client can draw its own arrow the instant it fires and let the server's authoritative one correct it — identical to look at, nothing given up. Everything else in §9 is adopted: ballistics belong on the ammo, arrows are recoverable with a break chance and a lodge timer, headshots read the `headshot_mult` the content has always carried, and hit detection takes the most significant body part rather than the first intersection. **The numbers do not come with it** — `RIPLIST.md` §0's threat frame still binds, and their ~35 bow damage is priced against 85% arrow recovery, so it moves only after recovery does. | `reference/PROJECTILES.md` §9 · `crates/sim-core/src/ranged.rs` · `NOW.md` |
| 2026-08-10 | **The client validates certificates; loopback is the one carve-out** (operator, asked and answered: land it, with the `--cert-hash` dev path — a chosen option, not a spoken sentence, so it is not quoted here). `client_endpoint` called `with_no_cert_validation()` unconditionally, at all three call sites across the two player binaries (`client` and `gates`), including the one `ci/depot.py` ships — the code named itself a dev posture and said a shipping client validates, and this is that row. **The damage was never credential theft**: the nonce is per-connection and the key never enters the game process. It is that **SIWE has no channel binding** — the handshake proves the joiner holds a key and says nothing about which TLS connection the proof arrived on — so an on-path relay terminating the player's QUIC and opening its own to the shard forwards challenge and signature and is admitted **as the victim**, a live session hijack that leaves nothing to notice afterwards. Three postures now, chosen from the address dialled: a `--cert-hash` pin trusts that one certificate, **loopback trusts anything**, everything else gets the platform root store. **The carve-out is a deliberate concession and costs nothing against the threat above**: an attacker on the path between two sockets on this machine is already executing code on this machine. It is also what keeps every local dev flow and the gates-loop's `--capture` invocation working with no flag — that invocation lives outside this repo and cannot be edited from inside it, so a design requiring an edit there could not have landed. `--cert-hash` is refused without `--server`, because `--server` is what skips the menu: a pin on a run that reaches the menu would be a player believing they were pinned while the menu dialled unpinned, which is the one failure mode a security flag may not have. **No new machinery was needed and the doc said otherwise** — `NETCODE.md` §2.2 recorded the P-256/14-day rules as "Chrome's" and "nothing enforces them now", but the pinned wtransport enforces all three client-side and `Identity::self_signed` already emits exactly such a certificate, so the hash the shard has printed since the browser days stopped being vestigial without a line of crypto being written. `dangerous-configuration` stays in the client manifest for the loopback arm alone — and removing it would not be a compile gate anyway, since `--workspace` feature unification keeps the server's copy on. | `crates/client/src/lib.rs` (`client_endpoint`, `is_loopback_host`) · `crates/client/src/args.rs` (`--cert-hash`) · `crates/server/tests/tls_posture.rs` · `NETCODE.md` §2.2 |

The 2026-08-10 recycler pass shipped `item.obol` inert, reading `ALPHA.md` A1's *"OBOL faucets OFF"* as covering the in-world coin. That was the wrong read and the docs invited it by using one word for two things. **They are two things.** *Carried OBOL is an ordinary item*: paid by the recycler, dropped on death, raidable, spent on research — scrap's job exactly, with no wallet, no ledger and no chain anywhere near it. | 2026-08-07 | **Persistence copies the reference game: the body stays in the world** (*"i want what rust has pretty much so we cant go wrong, ill deviate after we get established"*). Asked how the reference game does it and the answer contradicted what had just shipped — `reference/SAVES.md` is the research. **There is no player save file there.** A disconnect does not remove the body and write it to a side file; the player object stays where it is and sleeps, metabolism still running (Devblog 7, which introduced sleepers and saving in the same post). So player persistence and world persistence are one file and one mechanism: you are saved because you are an entity, exactly as a wall is. Their `BaseNetworkable.ToStream(Stream, SaveInfo)` sits beside `SendAsSnapshot(Connection, …)` on one base class — so **the persistence boundary is the networked entity** — and the whole save system is one class with three hooks. **Devblog 7 also names our divergence as the thing they replaced**: "the old version of sleepers, where the player object gets removed and is replaced with a fake version of the player" is what `Command::Leave` does today, and it costs the genre's central consequence — a disconnect currently makes you *safe*, so offline raiding cannot exist. **What this decides:** the body stays (sleepers), the world is persisted with it, backups are numbered rotations at depth ≥ 2, and the player-keyed store stays as the thing that outlives a wipe — the same split they keep between the world `.sav` and `UserPersistence.db`. **What it does not decide, and must not be read as:** copying their *implementation*. Their save is a full-world stop-the-world walk whose only mitigation after thirteen years is "raise `server.saveinterval`" — a knob trading crash loss against a freeze that stalls every player. Our bounded one-slot-per-tick sweep off the sim thread is the thing to extend to the world stores, not to trade away, and their loader trusting the file outright is not copyable either (a save is the one non-command path into `World`, so it validates like a wire decode). They also have no replay to satisfy; wall 5 is ours alone, and how a *loaded world* keeps it is the one part of this with no reference answer. Sequence in `NOW.md` §0y; "I'll deviate after we get established" is on the record, so a later divergence needs no re-argument. | `reference/SAVES.md` · `NOW.md` §0y · `crates/server/src/store.rs` |
| 2026-08-04 | **The economy is the anti-cheat perimeter, and the official shards are the operator's alone** (*"eventually we should separate probably. cant have cheaters with real money value floating around. plus im the only one who can run the official ones with the crypto coin attached"*). One build, two populations: **official shards** — operator-run, A2/A3 armed, OBOL redeemable — carry whatever protection the stake justifies; **community and training shards** run unarmed (A1, no coin, no entitlements), stay open to self-hosting, and are where agent players live. The separation needs no new concept, which is why it is cheap: economy arming is already an operator-only act (`CLAUDE.md` wall 8), so the armed set *is* the perimeter, and an unarmed shard has nothing worth cheating for. It also dissolves the standing collision — the 2026-08-01 agent-training call and any anti-cheat posture stop competing once they are not on the same shard. Asked in the same breath why the genre carries so much anti-cheat: **full-loot persistence is the reason, not bad engineering.** A cheater in a round shooter costs you a match; in this genre they take a wipe's accumulated work, and coin makes that theft rather than grief. Worth copying from how Facepunch actually answered it — the measure that moved the needle was **server-side occlusion culling** (rolled out 2025, defaulted network-wide, "greatly limits ESP and other radar like features"), not the kernel driver. That is server code, it costs no client trust, it works in a browser, and here the occlusion grid is a pure function of the seed — bakeable at worldgen, a lookup in the tick, gold-testable beside `test_terrain_golden`. The custody rules are product, not preference: the seed phrase is shown **once** at creation and confirmed back before the wallet is usable; the key persists only as an encrypted keystore on the player's own box; it is never logged, never in the WAL, never in telemetry, never on the wire; **"connect an existing wallet" stays a first-class path** so nobody is forced into custody they did not ask for; and the launcher states plainly that a game wallet should be a small hot wallet, that losing the phrase loses banked OBOL and skins, and that **the operator holds no keys and can restore nothing** — which is `DESIGN.md` pillar 4 finally said to the player instead of only to us. | `DESIGN.md` §3.2 · §10 · `ALPHA.md` §4 |
| 2026-08-04 | **The client moves to `WebGPURenderer` + TSL, and it starts now** (*"for the record i am upgrading asap the graphics"*). **This is not the wgpu call in the row above** — that one replaces three.js with Rust and stays §open; this one stays in three.js and swaps which of its two renderers we use. The choice was never made before: WebGL arrived as the default of `import * as THREE from "three"` and every graphics decision since was made around it, with the only written traces being asides in the clipmap row and `shadows.js:33`. `MIGRATION.md` is the costed survey and it is now a **plan, not a question** — route **C** (node stack adopted), never route B (renderer swapped with the material path left behind), which pays the whole bill for none of the benefit. Three things bind the iteration. (1) **The probe harness is the migration, not the shaders**: 12 probes, 12 `getContext()` grabs, 43 `readPixels` sites, 126 references from `browser_smoke` — WebGPU has no GL context and no synchronous readback, so every probe needs an explicit render target and an async body, and `farShadowProbe` needs its frustum-corner math re-derived for clip-space Z `0..1`. A migration that lands the render path and leaves the probes behind ships a client with **no visual gates at all**, which is the one outcome forbidden outright. (2) **One owner, one lane**: it touches `scene.js`, `materials.js`, `shadows.js`, `terrain.js`, `main.js` and `browser_smoke.mjs` at once, so it cannot be split across parallel loops (`CLAUDE.md` §loop discipline) and it carries the coupled tonemap/sky/exposure/fog set with it. (3) **The prewarm COUNT must be re-derived before merge**, not after — a gate we cannot re-derive is worse than the stall it catches. Also spoken by the same act: the three bump `0.178.0` → `0.185.1` lands **first and alone**, because `shadows.js` throws at boot if three renames a shadow uniform and that throw is better read on a clean tree. | `MIGRATION.md` · `NOW.md` |

| 2026-08-04 | **The gates are tiered, and a pass pays for what it touched** (*"i think we need the tab stuff every few hours at this rate"*, *"pixel probe and we dont have mvp?"*). Measured the same night, and the numbers are the argument: `./ci/gates.sh` is **1,062 s** green end to end, of which the two renderer gates are nearly all of it, while every code gate above them finishes in **17 s** warm. A pass that changed a server config and three docs was rendering 3.6 million probed pixels four times to prove it. So the script now takes a tier: `all` (default, unchanged, what CI runs), `fast` (code gates only), and `auto` (code gates, plus the renderer tier only if the diff touches `web/`, `assets/textures/`, or either renderer gate). **A short run may never claim a long one's result** — `fast` and a skipping `auto` print their own final line and never "ALL GATES GREEN", because a builder pasting that from a 17 s run is the exact overstatement the merge rubric exists to catch. The deeper reason this was cheap to decide: the pixel probes answer *"is this pretty enough"*, which the 2026-08-03 ration already conceded is **an absolute bar that cannot be satisfied** — an unsatisfiable bar is a fine report and a bad merge gate, because it manufactures infinite work that always looks like progress. That is how eight passes went to chroma residuals while M1's survival verbs sat at item 13 of 20. The 2026-08-03 ration limited what the loop *picks*; this limits what every pass *pays*. Using it is the harness's half (operator act, outside this repo): the loop runs `auto`, and the renderer tier on a schedule rather than per pass. Landed with it, and the reason the tiering was findable at all: `ci/vantages.mjs` passed its 36 checks and then **hung for 3,113 s** — cleanup registered on `process.on("exit")` while the browser, server and shard children were the very handles keeping node alive — so `./ci/gates.sh` could not return on any machine. | `ci/gates.sh` · `ci/vantages.mjs` · `NOW.md` |
| 2026-08-04 | **Everything unmerged lands on `main` today, and the wind + felling lane lands with a red gate** (*"can we commit everything and get synced with main? eveyrthing need to get to main and we need to get all up today"*, then, told the gate was red and why: *"Push all four with the gate red"*). Recorded because `CLAUDE.md`'s loop discipline says a change that reddens a wall does not merge **ever**, and the operator's word outranks any doc including that one — but only when it is written down the same day, which is what this row is. **What is red, exactly**: `browser_smoke`'s prop probe reads pine `contrast x1.15` against `PROP_MIN_CONTRAST_RATIO = 1.15`, asserted `>=` on a value that rounds onto its own floor. Two independent runs, same value. **The cause is the denominator.** The ratio is `(baseline + added) / baseline` where the baseline is the flat state's own detail, pine geometry v1's five whorls add facet structure to that flat state, and the same field therefore divides by more: **x1.22 at mask 15.22% before the merge, x1.15 at mask 11.53% after.** The floor's own comment predicted it — *"a prop with structure of its own can never score what a smooth heightfield does"* — and was calibrated at x1.26 on the four-primitive canopy that this merge deletes. `gmHash4` was suspected and cleared: reverting it leaves the value at x1.15. **What was NOT done, and is the point of writing this down**: the floor was not lowered. Moving `PROP_MIN_CONTRAST_RATIO` to pass would be inventing a knob to clear a gate, which is the single move the merge rubric exists to catch; if 1.15 is genuinely the wrong floor for a whorled canopy that is a measurement and a spoken number, not an edit. The fix is the pine's field against its new silhouette, one owner, and it is `NOW.md` item 1 until it closes. **Also landed by the same act**: `origin/main` merged back (PRs #5–#7), the reference spawn docs, the death screen at wire v16 — itself a judged-FAIL branch merged on the same call, with its own unfixed defect carried in `NOW.md` rather than erased — and `gmHash4` salvaged off `loop/m1-surface-grain`, which stays unmerged because its own `BRANCH-NOTES.md` says it is red on purpose and marks its `browser_smoke` join rewrite "do not re-land". | `NOW.md` item 1 · `ci/browser_smoke.mjs` · `CLAUDE.md` §loop discipline |

Three parts. **(1) Guest play is demoted**, and the correction is that it was never elevated: it existed as exactly one sentence in `DESIGN.md` §10 and the `session{guest_uuid}` branch of §9's handshake — nothing in the pillars, the walls, or `ALPHA.md` rested on it. It had been cited in conversation as the reason the browser client must stay first class; that argument is void, and the two sites are now the whole of it. **(2) The house sells the game, and this clears the never-table rather than bending it.** §3.3 forbids selling **power** — damage, armor, speed, capacity, gather rate, upkeep and decay pauses, blueprints and tech, loot odds and map intel — and a uniform entry price grants no player an advantage over any other, so it is on no row. What is retired is the *framing* around it, which was scry's anxiety wearing a game's clothes: the queue row's "access is the last honest thing to sell and we default to not selling it" became a row about **priority over another player**, and the door-vs-better-door distinction is now written down instead of inferred — because "we already sell access" is precisely the crowbar a later pass would take to the priority-queue knob. That knob is unmoved and still defaults to never. `CLAUDE.md` wall 8 is restated from "money is appearance-only from the house" to **"the house never sells power"**, which is what it always meant. **(3) Web becomes second class**, native the product: the client tier is now a real axis, with the official armed shards and the full `ART.md` bar belonging to the native client, and the browser build serving as the demo and the board's playable link — allowed to sit visually lower, pointed at unarmed shards. This does **not** schedule a native renderer (still §open) and does not make one cheap: 9,955 lines of renderer in `web/src`, `browser_smoke`'s 5,179-line probe harness, and an `ART.md` bar measured off WebGL output are all unchanged. It changes only *who they are paid for*. **Deliberately left open below rather than invented here**: the price and its currency; what exactly the purchase gates (default proposed: the native client and the official armed set, with unarmed self-hosted shards free — the reading that keeps the 2026-08-01 agent-training goal and `CLAUDE.md`'s "an agent player pays the same doors" simultaneously true); and whether the `WebGPURenderer` + TSL migration spoken 2026-08-04 still starts now that its target has been demoted. | `DESIGN.md` §3.3 · §4 · §10 · §11 · §14 · `CLAUDE.md` wall 8 · `ALPHA.md` §4 · §open below |

Four `ci/vantages.mjs` captures were taken and compared against `Rust Images/genericview.jpeg` — the first time in this repo's history that a decision about the renderer was made by looking at its output rather than by reading a statistic about it. **What is actually on screen**: a heightfield, pines that are stacked cones, rocks that are flat-shaded dodecahedrons, a cloudless gradient sky. **What the reference has**: thousands of instanced grass blades as geometry, an understory of bushes and flowers, trees with branch structure and leaf masses, a stone building, water, a character. **The gap is mesh density and content, not pixel math**, which is why eight passes of surface-field work — 61 tuned constants, triplanar fields, analytic gradients, chroma neutrality to three decimals — never closed it and could not have. Three consequences. **(1) The renderer stack is not the constraint and neither candidate addresses it**: a wgpu port would faithfully reproduce cone trees and dodecahedron rocks in Rust, and TSL would hang volumetric clouds over a world with no grass in it. That cost is unchanged and still owed; what changed is that it is now worth paying. **It also moots `MIGRATION.md`** (`WebGPURenderer` + TSL, spoken the same day, plan of record): you do not port three.js to WebGPU *and* replace three.js. Same reasoning retires the open lighting red (`TONAL_MAX_P10`, p10 luma 112 against a ceiling of 60): it is that same coupled set, and a sequential single-owner pass spent on it in three.js would be thrown away by the port. **The pivot is cheap for a reason that was true all along**: `client-core` depends on nothing but `sim-core` and `protocol`, already builds as an rlib, and its browser-shaped half is exactly one file (`bridge.rs`, the raw C ABI). Windowing, input, ECS, asset pipeline and a wgpu renderer, none of which is where this project's value sits. **The boundary is the whole decision, and it is a rule, not a preference: Bevy draws, it does not decide.** `sim-core` is the authority and carries walls 1-5; `ClientCore` owns prediction and interpolation; the ECS reads those and writes transforms. Gameplay state living in a Bevy component would retire the determinism walls silently and no gate would catch it, so `no gameplay state in the ECS` is stated in `crates/client/src/bin/gates.rs` where someone about to break it will read it. **0.18, not 0.19**: 0.19 requires rustc 1.95 and this box has 1.94.1 — an upgrade is gated (wall 5 would catch a codegen change) but is its own act, not a side effect of picking an engine. **Bevy is an optional feature, default OFF**: it pulls ~565 lockfile entries and `clippy --workspace --all-targets` runs in the code tier on every health check in every lane. Measured: code tier 106 s with the feature off against a ~98 s baseline, i.e. unchanged; `--features render` is where the cost goes, and the renderer tier is where that belongs — the tier `browser_smoke` and `vantages` are vacating. **Named, because it is the pivot's real cost**: this box has no display and no compositor (`DISPLAY` unset, no `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`), so the loop can compile and gate the client but can never SEE it. That is the same hole the browser tier had (no GPU, SwiftShader) and the pivot does not close it — it moves it. | 2026-08-05 | **The desktop build ships through the scry launcher, and the whole chain is real** (*"we are pivoting into a rust desktop build for Linux. can we setup the scry launcher to support Gates? and I guess the backend of the site? and like hook up the scry sdk and finish all these loops out? make it a real downloader?"*). Four halves, and the fourth is the one that had never been true. **(1) Gates packages a depot.** `ci/depot.py` compiles `--features render`, stages the binary, hashes it and writes the index the launcher reads; `--self-test` is a gate in `ci/gates.sh` that needs no compiler. It deliberately does **not** compute the depot digest — `scry digest` does, because a second implementation of the number that gets notarized is scry's invariant 3 with money attached — and it does **not** publish, which is an operator act. **(2) The SDK is vendored, not reimplemented.** `crates/client/src/scry_overlay.rs` is byte-identical to scry's `sdk/rust/scry_overlay.rs`, pinned by sha256 with a test that goes red on a local edit; the wrapper is `scry.rs`. A launcher tells the game who is playing, and **an address is a claim, not authentication** — every rendering of it says "unverified", because `PROTO_VER`'s `Hello` carries no identity and wiring one in is a wire change, not this slice. No launcher running is a normal, playable state and there is no third branch that asks for a key. **(3) `--server`/`--identity` are the launch interface**, not a convenience: a depot's launch args are what starts this game, and an unfilled `{wallet}` arrives as an empty string that must read as absence. `args.rs` owns it, shared by both binaries; `gates --help` used to parse "--help" as a socket address. **(4) It was run end to end** on 2026-08-05 — real Bevy binary, real depot, real HTTP origin, `scry install gates` by slug, hashes verified, and the digest agreeing across packager, origin and client. Two findings came from running it and neither would have come from reading it: **the depot must ship `assets/`**, because the render slice loads textures at runtime and Bevy answers a missing one with a white fallback *and keeps drawing* — a binary-only depot installs perfectly, starts perfectly, and renders untextured; and **a dirty tree gave two different binaries the same build id**, which is a directory name on a player's disk, now keyed on the binary's own hash. (A third, the IPv6 endpoint bind that killed the packaged build at startup, was fixed independently on `main` in `3c50e35` while this was in flight; that fix is the one in the tree.) **Not done and named as such:** nothing is published to the live origin and nothing is notarized. | 2026-08-07 | **Identity is SIWE, and persistence was built on the wrong foundation first** (*"SIWE is what we are using now"*, *"we need to rework this so its normal and not odd"*, *"do everything you can so we dont fuck ourselfs in the future"*). Asked why persistence and auth had been so much harder here than in a WoW or Minecraft clone, the honest answer is two things and only one of them is this repo's walls. The walls are real cost — sim-core cannot do I/O or allocate in a tick, so there is no `serde`, and determinism is gated, so a load has to be *proven* not to break replay — and that is roughly 10× the code of the naive version, which is a price worth paying only while the determinism goals (bit-identical prediction, replay, the RL environment) are real. **The other half was sequencing and it was ours: we built persistence before identity.** Minecraft gets a stable UUID from Mojang auth before a byte is serialized, so persistence is a file format; here `PlayerKey` was a placeholder, and every awkward thing built in the preceding week — the sleeper index, the identity table bolted onto the world file, `PlayerKey::PLACEHOLDER`, the boot warning — was a workaround for its absence. SIWE removes the cause rather than the symptoms: an address is stable forever, public (so it is safe on disk, which is what `store.rs` demanded), and verifiable with no network call. The workarounds stay but stop being workarounds. **What this row authorizes and what it does not:** it supersedes the "scry session auth v0" §open row and the `auth.rs` header that said not to arm `require_auth` on a public shard — that warning existed because the validator was a stub, and it no longer is, so `bin/shard.rs`'s boot-time `AUTH WARNING` is deleted with it. It does **not** authorize the deploy; the config is committed and restarting the live shard is still an operator act. | `crates/protocol/src/auth.rs` · `crates/server/src/auth.rs` · `NOW.md` §0y |

| 2026-08-09 | **The mark buys speed, not yield — and a node holds its last fifth for whoever finishes it** (*"we need the marker to just be faster not more yield and we need to finish bonus"*, after `RIPLIST.md` §4.3 found both mechanics differ from the reference's). **Supersedes the weak-spot model shipped since gather v0.** A node's payout is now invariant at `hits × yield_per_hit[tool]` however it is struck: it holds `hits × HIT_UNIT` of budget, an unmarked swing spends `HIT_UNIT` and a marked one spends `HIT_UNIT + weak_spot_bonus_pct`, and pay is pro rata — so chasing the glint empties the node in **7 swings instead of 10** and banks the same 300. Facepunch's own words for why (Devblog 170): *"you will not actually earn more resources, but by using skill and good aim you can harvest the ore faster."* Ours paid 1.5× yield instead, which made the skilled player richer rather than quicker — a different game from the one being copied. New `finish_bonus_pct` withholds a share from every swing and pays it to whoever lands the exhausting one: **50 on the tree** (their Devblog 187 split a tree's wood half-standing, half-on-the-fall) and **20 on each ore node** (their Devblog 166, *"should mitigate cherry picking and leaving half finished nodes around the map"*) — a redistribution, never a bonus on top, so abandoning a half-struck node is what costs you. **The arithmetic is exact by construction and had to be**: the first cut paid `floor` per swing and lost 3 of 30 on a fixture node, so the running total is now the difference of two cumulative floors and the finisher's share is the exact remainder `total − pool`. Gated by `sim-core tests/gather.rs` (`the_mark_buys_speed_and_never_yield` proves same total in strictly fewer swings; `the_finish_share_moves_when_yield_arrives_not_how_much` proves the total is unmoved and the last swing beats every other), and the at-node ceiling in `balance.rs` re-derives to 2030/min. Neither mechanic moves a wire byte or the replay golden. | `reference/RIPLIST.md` §4.3 · `content/gatherables.toml` · sim-core `gather.rs` (`NodeDef::weak_pct`, `finish_pct`, `HIT_UNIT`) |

| 2026-08-09 | **A friend's link boots you onto their shard, and a listed shard's player count is live** (*"is Gates broadcasting servers however it needs to be? so the game can see it? … maybe even deeplinks if thats a thing? like someone can boot right up into a server their friend is playing"*). Two gaps, and the audit behind them is the useful half: **the seam was already built end to end and two pieces of it were dark.** The `scry-shardlist-v1` parser, the intro screen, `ci/shardlist.py`, the `--server`/`--servers` argv and the shard's own `GET /status.json` all existed and were tested; what did not exist was anything that *joined* the status endpoint to the list, and anything that turned a url into a join. **The count is POLLED, never baked** — a row may carry `status_url` (an additive, optional field; the kind stays `-v1` and older readers ignore it) and each reader asks that shard itself every `STATUS_POLL_SECS`. The alternative was one line in the generator and it is the wrong one: a `players` written at generation time describes the shard as it was whenever an operator last ran the script, and the document is then served for weeks, so a busy shard reads as empty — the exact defect the absent count was protecting against, arriving by a slower road. It also keeps scry's rule rather than bending it (the launcher "does not invent, cache or rank" and its broker refuses to proxy): nothing proxies, everyone measures. A shard that does not answer costs the row nothing — it keeps its last count, so a blip is not "everyone left" — and a shard that answers `0` states `0/100`, because that is a measurement and only an *unmeasured* count is `?`. **Deeplinks are `scry://join/<slug>/<host:port>`, and the platform owns the scheme** rather than the game: the launcher is the binary a player installs, it already substitutes `{server}` into a title's launch args and already holds the identity the shard will ask to sign, one scheme serves every title, and a link naming a title the player has not installed is still useful because the launcher can offer to install it — which a game-owned scheme could never do, the game being what is missing. `gates://host:port` is parsed too, for when the OS hands a url straight to this binary. **A link is a way of spelling an address, not a second way in**: it resolves to the same field, gets the same `check_addr`, and skips the same menu. It may carry a title and an address and *nothing else* — no identity, no token, no flag — and anything past the address is refused rather than ignored, because ignored is how an unnoticed field becomes a supported one. Registering the scheme with the desktop stays an operator/installer act. | `crates/client/src/{deeplink.rs,shardlist.rs,args.rs}` · `crates/client/src/render/menu.rs` · `ci/shardlist.py` · `shards.toml.example` · §open below |
| 2026-08-11 | **A copy of Gates is 0.005 ETH against a $10 headline, and ETH is the only rail open at launch** (*"what are we selling games in? eth?"* · *"id say 0.005 ETH as long as i can adjust it over time"*). Measured the day it was spoken: ETH **$1,882**, so 0.005 ETH is **$9.41** — which lands on the `$10 to start` the ticket's own NatSpec already carried, so the spoken number and the standing anchor agree without either being bent to the other. **The condition is satisfied**: `setPrices(usdCents, ethWei, scryWei, usdgUnits)` is `onlyOwner`, callable any time, no redeploy, and it emits `PricesPosted`. **What the condition does not say is the part that binds: there is no oracle.** The dollar figure and the ETH amount are posted independently and nothing on chain ties them, so the pair drifts on its own as ETH moves — at $3,764 the same 0.005 ETH is $18.82 against a $10 headline, a game silently costing 88% more than its own price tag. The meter's card already renders that ratio off the live tape (`_drift` → `usd_now`) so a buyer sees staleness before paying, which makes adjusting **maintenance rather than an option** — the sentence *"as long as i can adjust it"* is therefore a duty this row accepts, not a permission it grants. **SCRY and USDG ship closed** (`amount 0` ⇒ `r.open == false`): only ETH was spoken, no SCRY/USD figure has been said by anyone, and USDG — 6 decimals on 4663, so $10 is `10000000` — is the one rail that *cannot* drift from the headline and is the obvious first one to open when a stable door is wanted. To post: `setPrices(1000, 5000000000000000, 0, 0)`. **Nothing is set until `ScryGameTicket` is deployed for gates**, and its money destinations are welded at construction rather than by this row — `proceeds` takes ETH and USDG, `scrySink` takes SCRY, both immutable once deployed. **(knob: `usdCents` / `ethWei`, whether USDG opens, and the two sink addresses)** | `BUSINESS.md` · scry `contracts/src/ScryGameTicket.sol` (`setPrices`, `_post`) · scry `meter/tickets.py` (`_drift`) |

| 2026-08-11 | **Generated assets are in: Meshy for meshes, ElevenLabs for audio** (*"yea right up that we are ok with meshy and elevenlabs they can bitch later to us"*). **The rail gains a fourth basis, and it is not a licence at all.** CC0, CC-BY and the refused NC/SA are all *licences on somebody else's work*; a generated asset is commissioned output, so its rights come from a vendor contract, which is why the three-tier rail had no slot for it and why this needed a spoken call rather than a reading of the old one. **Both vendors' terms were read this session, and each carries a live condition.** *ElevenLabs*: §4(c)(ii) — "you retain all rights in and to your Output" — but §1(c) restricts free accounts to non-commercial use, so **anything that ships must be generated under a paid plan**; `BUSINESS.md` prices an entry fee, which is what makes that clause bite here exactly as it made NC bite in 2026-08-07's row. *Meshy*: a paid plan is full private ownership with no attribution owed, while **the free plan licenses output CC BY 4.0** — which is not a refusal, it lands on the rail's existing middle tier and costs one `NOTICE` entry. Rights attach at generation time and survive a later downgrade. Two conditions ride along and are cheap to keep: an asset must not be published to the Meshy Community feed, and a prompt may not launder someone else's copyright. **Unconfirmed, and worth one click**: this account holds 4,280 credits and working API access, both of which indicate a paid plan, but nothing in the API reports a tier and the ownership sentence above rests on it. **What this unblocks is a capability, not a permission** — 2026-08-07's row discovered that every 3D asset host is refused by this box's egress (Poly Haven, ambientCG, Quaternius, poly.pizza, itch.io, Sketchfab, OpenGameArt) and concluded that sourcing binaries is "an operator act from a machine that can reach them, not a loop's". **`api.meshy.ai` answers.** So a loop can produce a mesh again without an operator round-trip, and what is waiting is the ugliest surface in the game: all twelve deployables draw as one flat-coloured `Cuboid` (`structures.rs:420`), the pig is five boxes, the barrel a ten-segment cylinder. **ElevenLabs retires `sound/synth.rs`'s reason, not the synth.** `CLAUDE.md` says the bank is generated at boot "precisely to avoid this class of question" — SeedThree's and Eanpa-Sky's bird recordings being NC-SA or licence-unstated. That dodge is no longer load-bearing, and the synth is still right for anything synthesizable: deterministic, no depot bytes, cannot drift. Generated audio is for what a synth cannot reach. **The Facepunch rail is unchanged and now has a new surface — the prompt.** No proper nouns, no traced assets; and a generator will happily draw the reference game's silhouette if you name it, so a prompt describes the *thing* ("a crude stone smelting furnace") and never the *source*. Every generated asset carries vendor, mode, full prompt, task id and date in `assets/models/MANIFEST.md`, which is what makes that rule auditable after the fact instead of a promise. **The risk the operator accepted, stated rather than buried**: both grants are contractual, the training-data provenance behind these models is unsettled, and neither vendor indemnifies a challenge to it. The exposure is bounded by the property the texture rail already leans on — a generated asset is replaceable by a file swap with no code change — so being wrong later costs a regeneration, not a refactor. **Measured on the first one** (a stone furnace, 30 credits, ~4 min): 6,174 tris against a 6,000 target, albedo linear luma **0.2466** inside `ALBEDO_LUMA_BAND` [0.05, 0.55], roughness 0.636 / metallic 0.002 against the greybox's 0.70 / 0.0. **Three import constraints found, none of them licensing**: Meshy centres on origin (`min.y = −0.948`) where this repo authors feet at y=0 (`tests/mob_mesh.rs`); textures arrive 2048² at **9.3 MB per prop** against 6.5 MB for the entire current texture set, so twelve deployables is ~58 MB undownsampled (~15 MB at 1024², ~4 MB at 512²); and the albedo carries baked lighting, which will fight the light rig. | `ART.md` §7 · `assets/models/MANIFEST.md` · `crates/client/src/sound/synth.rs` · `NOW.md` |

| 2026-08-11 | **The client ships from GitHub, versions are three numbers not one, and a shard sets a floor on the release it admits** (*"how can we release client on github for download. and how can we manage versions better? we need that in the corner of builds. and the server needs to make sure client had the right version"*). **The audit is the useful half: there was exactly one version number and it was answering two questions.** `PROTO_VER` (34, now 35) gates the wire exactly and is deliberately stingy — it moves only on a layout change, so two releases a month apart share it. Nothing anywhere asked "which *release* is this": all six crates read `0.1.0`, no binary displayed anything, and `ci/depot.py`'s `<version>-g<sha>` build id was the only thing in the tree shaped like an answer — read by the packager and by nothing else. So the split is **three numbers with three owners** (`crates/protocol/src/version.rs` carries the table): the wire's `PROTO_VER` is exact and refuses `REFUSE_VERSION`; the release `VER` is a **floor** the operator sets per shard (`min_client`, default 0 ⇒ admits everything) and refuses the new `REFUSE_BUILD`; the commit `GIT_SHA` gates nothing and is evidence. **A minimum and not an equality, chosen against the product rather than against correctness**: equality on the build is the stronger claim and would lock out every installed client on every commit including a docs one — the reference game does force updates like that and ties them to its *protocol* number, which is what `PROTO_VER` already is here. `<`, so a client NEWER than the shard is admitted: a player who updated first must not be locked out of a shard nobody has restarted, and the incompatibility that would actually matter is the wire's to refuse one gate up. **The version is typed once** — `[workspace.package] version`, inherited by all six crates — and that one line moved `ci/depot.py` from reading a real number to silently returning `0.0.0`, which is the shape of this whole area: a build id that names no release, written into a directory name on a player's disk, with no error anywhere. It raises now. **Released as a draft, three platforms** (`.github/workflows/release.yml`, on a `v*` tag): the gates re-run on the tagged commit because a tag is not a promise main was green, the tag and the tree must agree on the version or the run fails, and `assets/` ships beside the binary because the client resolves its asset root there and a missing texture is a white fallback rather than an error. Publishing stays an operator act — the workflow drafts and stops, `ci/depot.py`'s posture about `published.json` exactly. **(knob: `min_client` per shard, and which platform builds are load-bearing)** | `crates/protocol/src/version.rs` · `crates/protocol/src/lib.rs` (`PROTO_VER` v35, `REFUSE_BUILD`) · `crates/server/src/config.rs` (`min_client`) · `.github/workflows/release.yml` · `shard.toml.example` |

| 2026-08-11 | **Gates is MIT, © MoreRight DAO — the code was never the moat** (*"lets work on the licence file"*, then, asked whether a permissive licence collides with selling the game: *"yea i mean only our premium servers are where u can earn coins LOL"*). **The state this corrects is a contradiction, not a gap.** All six crates declared `license = "MIT"`, `README.md`'s sixth line promised *"Open source, and built in public by agents"*, the repo is **public**, and it pays 100,000 SCRY per merged PR — while carrying **no `LICENSE` file at all**. GitHub reads licences from files rather than from `Cargo.toml`, so the repo displayed as *no licence*, which defaults to **all rights reserved**: every outside contributor the bounty attracted was working against terms that said the opposite of both the manifest and the README, and had no grant to contribute under. **The collision with `BUSINESS.md` is weaker than it looks, and the operator's sentence is the reason.** The copy check is **server-side** — an armed shard asks the chain whether the joining *wallet* holds one and SIWE proves the wallet (`entitle.rs`, `REFUSE_TICKET`), so a rebuilt client cannot talk past it — and shards that check nothing are already deliberate (2026-08-04, *one build, two populations*). Earning coins is gated to the premium shards. None of that travels with a fork, so keeping the source shut would protect nothing that is actually sold, and MIT retracts no promise already made in public. **Scope is stated rather than inferred, because a bare `LICENSE` at root would over-claim**: `LICENSE` covers the code, and `NOTICE` is the accounting for what ships under somebody else's terms — game-icons.net (CC BY 3.0), Roboto Condensed (Apache-2.0), `bevy_procedural_tree` (MIT OR Apache-2.0, a dependency whose code ships), Quaternius (CC0), and the Meshy meshes, whose rights come from a vendor contract rather than a licence at all (2026-08-11's row above). It also records what does **not** ship and therefore owes nothing, because "we read it" and "we shipped it" are different facts and that distinction is the whole IP posture. **Two defects fell out of writing it down.** `ci/depot.py` — the way a player actually receives this game — staged the binary and `assets/` and **no licence text**, which MIT requires to accompany every copy; it is fatal there now, and `--self-test` gates it (proven red with `NOTICE` removed). And the icon count was wrong in two places: `CLAUDE.md` said 62, the tree holds 65, and **one of them (`burnt_meat.png`) is ours** — so a notice claiming CC BY over all of them would have credited four strangers for our own file. 64 + 1, stated in `NOTICE` and corrected in `CLAUDE.md`. **(knob: none — dual-licensing with Apache-2.0 for its patent grant stays a one-file change if outside contribution ever makes it worth it)** | `LICENSE` · `NOTICE` · `README.md` §Licence · `ci/depot.py` · `.github/workflows/release.yml` · `CLAUDE.md` §Third-party credit |

## Open (defaults ship until spoken)

| knob | default | doc |

| hitscan v0 — the revolver fires: `MAX_HITSCAN_SAMPLES = 320` · `MAX_HITSCAN_MARK_SAMPLES = 64` (systems lane, 2026-08-19) | **PROPOSED, and the row is here for one invented number and one refusal.** The revolver was a **charged dead end**, which is worse than the inert data `NOW.md` called it: a barrel drop at weight 1, a recipe, a research rung behind gunpowder, and a round that both drops and crafts — a player could pay three times over and pull the trigger on nothing, because `bake_combat` dropped every row that was not melee, throwable or bow. It bakes now, through the same `bake_ranged` a bow does, with `RangedDef::hitscan` as the only difference: a bow's round carries `[[ammo]]` ballistics and a firearm's does not (`content/weapons.toml`'s own header contract), and `validate` now refuses **both** halves of that pairing so the sim never has to re-derive which it is holding. `ranged::hitscan` resolves the whole reach on the tick the trigger is pulled, through the *same two functions* the arrow uses (`world_stop`, then `nearest_body`), so a bullet and an arrow cannot disagree about what a trunk is. **`MAX_HITSCAN_SAMPLES = 320`** is `MAX_ARROW_SUBSTEPS`' idea against a different clock — 54.4 m at `ARROW_STEP_MM`, refused at boot rather than clamped, because a clamped reach is a gun that shoots through cover past a distance nothing writes down (the revolver needs 295). No shots-per-tick cap is owed: one player fires at most one shot a tick, so the shard-wide bound is `MAX_PLAYERS × MAX_HITSCAN_SAMPLES`, and a hitscan shot leaves no entity behind, so there is nothing to overflow. **`MAX_HITSCAN_MARK_SAMPLES = 64` is the only cosmetic knob in this file and it is measured**: the walk is a `terrain::ground` tap per sample at ~373 ns, so a hundred players holding the trigger at open sky on one tick was **20 ms of a 33 ms tick** — a decal owning two thirds of the budget. Bounding the walk a *miss* takes to 10.9 m makes it 5 ms, and the shot that hits is unaffected because that walk was already owed (`findings/hitscan-cost-20260819.md` has the table and the two fixes if it must come down further). **The refusal worth overruling is `DEATH_BY_BULLET`**: a firearm death reports `DEATH_BY_ARROW`, whose name now lies, because a seventh cause fits the 3-bit field and moves no layout and is *still* a wire change — an eighth pattern both ends refuse as forged becoming a live fact — and `protocol`'s `every_domain_fits_its_wire_field` refused it in exactly those words. The death screen already reads right ("shot you with the revolver from 12.4 m") because the weapon is a wire field. **Deliberately absent, and this is what a player will notice first: no `EV_SHOT`.** That payload is a muzzle speed and a drop in mm/tick which the client re-flies (`render/tracer.rs`); a hitscan has neither, and zeroes would hang a motionless tracer at the muzzle for four seconds. So a gun has no muzzle flash, no crack and no beam — only the decal where it stopped and the hitmarker where it landed — and giving it one is a new event or a spoken reading of `EV_SHOT`'s spare patterns, neither invented here. Also absent: structure damage (an arrow does not chip a wall either), falloff, and headshots. | `NOW.md` §0pvp item 3 · `findings/hitscan-cost-20260819.md` · sim-core `ranged.rs` `limits.rs` `combat.rs` `world.rs` · content `bake.rs` `validate.rs` · `sim-core/tests/gun.rs` · `sim-core/examples/shot_cost.rs` |
| armor reduction v0 — worn protection is applied: `WEAR_SLOTS = 2` · `ARMOR_MAX_PCT = 90` (systems lane, 2026-08-19) | **PROPOSED, and the row is here for two structural numbers and one ordering decision that went against a doc.** `content/armor.toml` had been priced, validated, hashed and balance-anchored since M1 with **nothing in `sim-core` reading a row of it** (`reference/ARMOR.md` §9.1).
