All three pieces are craftable and the road sign jacket is a research rung, so it was a **charged** dead end, not merely unread data — the revolver's class, one day later. `bake_combat` installs the table now and `combat::hurt` reads it: the shipped burlap shirt turns a rock's five hits into six. **`WEAR_SLOTS = 2`** is not a guess at a future roster — `ArmorSlot` has exactly two variants and `Player::worn` is indexed *by* slot rather than appended to, so there is no overflow arm to get wrong and a legs rung is a content schema change plus a widening here, in one commit. **`ARMOR_MAX_PCT = 90`** is deliberately **not a new number**: it is the same 90 `content/validate.rs` has refused a single row past since M1, applied to the *sum*, because a per-row ceiling cannot see a set. **The ordering call: one scalar now, damage types later, against `reference/ARMOR.md` §9.3 and `findings/armor-design-20260818.md` §7, which both put types first.** Three reasons and all three are about our data rather than theirs. (1) `reference/RIPLIST.md` §1h read their Protection tables at page tier for all three pieces we ship and found **Projectile and Melee equal on every row**, so a scalar expresses theirs exactly — `BALANCE.md` §6 is satisfied by *taking*, not by differing. (2) The weapon-side half of a type column — which of slash/blunt/stab each of twenty `weapons.toml` rows is — is in no source anybody here has read, so types-first means inventing twenty numbers, which §loop discipline forbids. (3) §9.3's stated benefit is *one content rewrite, not two*, and that benefit is already spent: `armor.toml` is scheduled for a rewrite by equipment v0 (spoken 2026-08-17) and blocked on §1h's take regardless, so types-first couples a landable slice to an unspoken one. **The model: the worn set is one number and both slots sum**, because aim is planar and there is no head to hit — crediting only the body piece would ship `item.armor_burlap_head` as craftable, priced and protecting nobody on the day armor started working, which is the audit repeating itself. **The arithmetic is `damage × (100 − pct) / 100`, floored on the damage and not on the absorption**, and that is load-bearing rather than cosmetic: the note's form (`raw − raw × pct / 100`) was tried and it **disagrees with `balance::hits_to_kill` on `item.hatchet_stone` at 35 %** — 6 hits against the band's 7. `content/tests/content.rs` pins the two equal for every (weapon, set) pair. **A corpse drops its plates** into the death bag through `drain_spill`, so a full pocket costs the killer a walk and never an item. **Not in this slice, and the first one is a product call the operator has to make**: nothing can *equip* armor (that is `CONT_WEAR`, `CONT_KIND_BITS` 2 → 3 and a `PROTO_VER` bump — all four container values are spent), no damage types, no hit areas, no condition on a worn piece, and `move_penalty_pct` still reaches no line of `movement.rs`. **And one thing deliberately left broken and written down rather than fixed**: `balance.rs`'s anchor 2 loops every armor row against a *body* hits-to-kill, so it credits a head piece against body hits, it is a ceiling with no floor, and it cannot see a set — head 10 % + roadsign 25 % is **+3 hits** on four weapons against `armor_extra_hits_max = 2`. Applying armor moves no content number, so `test_content` stays green while its meaning rots. Fixing it needs `armor_extra_hits_max` re-spoken (2 → 3) or the ladder re-priced, and that is an operator word, not a loop's. | `NOW.md` §0pvp item 4 · `findings/armor-design-20260818.md` · `reference/ARMOR.md` §9 · `reference/RIPLIST.md` §1h · sim-core `combat.rs` `world.rs` `persist.rs` `limits.rs` · content `bake.rs` `balance.rs` · server `store.rs` · `sim-core/tests/armor.rs` · `content/tests/content.rs` |
| aim staleness v0 — the shard measures how stale an aim is before anything rewinds: `AIM_STALE_BUCKETS = 8` (one bucket per tick for 0..=6, index 7 is “7 or more”) · `AIM_STALE_CEILING_TICKS = 80` (server lane, 2026-08-18) | **PROPOSED, and the row exists to record a MEASUREMENT rather than to ask for a number.** Slice 1 of `findings/lagcomp-design-20260818.md` §7. An input datagram carries `snapshot_ack = S` (“the newest world I had applied when I made these frames is server tick S”) and the server executes that frame at tick `T`; raw **`T − S`** folds RTT, input-buffer depth and the client's own scheduling into one honest number, and it is what every constant in slices 2–5 gets derived from. **Measured 2026-08-18 on this box** (4 cores, loopback, `shard` + `bots` release builds, load average 0.37–0.56 — uncontended, and the number is reported with that rather than as a clean benchmark): 100 bots × 60 s, **179,718 samples, mean 1.107 ticks (36.9 ms), max 3, and the distribution 0⇒21.5 % · 1⇒48.1 % · 2⇒28.5 % · 3⇒1.9 % · ≥4⇒0**; 153 unacked, 0 refused, 0 dropped ticks, `input_dg_bad` 0. A 10-bot × 30 s run first read mean 1.234 / max 3, so the two populations agree and neither loaded the box — **this measures the floor, not the ceiling.** ⚠ **The prediction held and one reading of it is wrong.** §0.1 predicted ~0.5–1.5 ticks of raw `T − S` at LAN latency and 1.107 sits inside it — but loopback RTT is ~0 and the number is still 1.1 ticks, so **raw staleness is not RTT**: it is the input buffer plus the age of the newest snapshot a client has applied (`SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL_TICKS = 2`). That is a floor no network improvement removes, and it is the half `§0.1`'s RTT table cannot see. Adding §2.2's other two terms gives a favour of 1.107 + `INTERP_DELAY_TICKS`(4) − `REWIND_ACK_BIAS_TICKS`(1) ≈ **4.1 ticks / 137 ms**, against §0.1's 4.6 at 20 ms RTT. **The published number is RAW and says so in the code**: `INTERP_DELAY_TICKS` still lives in `client-core/src/interp.rs` and mirroring it here by hand is the `props.js` drift `CLAUDE.md` opens with — add it when slice 2 gives it one home, and **do not double-count it**. **Two constants are genuinely new and neither is a balance number.** `AIM_STALE_BUCKETS` is 8 because 7 is §1.4's proposed `REWIND_MAX_TICKS`, so the top bucket answers exactly one question — what fraction of aims a 7-tick clamp would fail to reach (**0 of 188,691 here, which says the clamp is roomy on loopback and nothing at all about a 200 ms link**). `AIM_STALE_CEILING_TICKS` is **derived, not picked**: `SENT_SNAPSHOT_RING`(32) × `SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL_TICKS`(2) + `INPUT_BUFFER_CAP`(16) = 80 ticks — the oldest ack the server could still corroborate out of its own sent ring, plus the longest a frame can wait in the buffer. Past it the sample is **refused and counted** (`aim_stale_refused`, also in `anomaly::WATCHED`), because `snapshot_ack` is a client claim and one datagram naming an hour-old snapshot would own `aim_stale_max` forever. **Nothing else moved**: no `PROTO_VER` bump, no golden, no `state_hash`, no `SAVE_FORMAT`, and `sim-core` is untouched. | `findings/lagcomp-design-20260818.md` §2.2/§7 · `NOW.md` §0pvp item 5 · `crates/server/src/{client,core,stats,status,anomaly}.rs` · `crates/server/tests/lagcomp_measure.rs` |
| attacker-side flinch v0 — a hit body recoils, and **only the attacker sees it**: `FLINCH_CLIP_S = 0.33333` · `FLINCH_BLEND_S = 0.16667` (client lane, 2026-08-18) | **PROPOSED, and the asymmetry is the thing to overrule.** `Hit_Chest` has shipped in `stumpy.glb` since the rig landed and nothing played it; the victim's id has been on the wire since `EV_HIT` existed and `client-core` threw it away with `let _ = victim`. Keeping it costs **no packet and no `PROTO_VER` bump** — which is the whole reason this is cheap and the whole reason it is lopsided. ⚠ **`EV_HIT` is unicast to the attacker** (`world.rs` `EV_HIT`; `server/core.rs` routes it by `a`), so the body flinches on **one screen in the world — yours**. A bystander watching two players fight sees no recoil on either. That is defensible as *strictly more information for the person who needs it*, and it is a deliberate refusal of the alternative: a broadcast flinch is one message per landed blow per player with no AOI filter, on the hottest path in a fight, and `NOW.md` §0sw already owes that fan-out a soak it has not had. **Reverse it by saying so** — the symmetric version is a new broadcast event, not an edit here. **Two knobs, and neither is picked.** `FLINCH_CLIP_S` is `Hit_Chest`'s own length read off the shipped file (0.33333 s), the `ANIM_RIG_H_M` habit; unlike `SWING_CLIP_S` there is nothing to cut it to fit, because the sim has no cadence on *being* hit. `FLINCH_BLEND_S` is the clip's **apex**, measured out of the binary chunk: the largest joint rotation away from the first keyframe climbs 0.04° → 19.92° and peaks at t = 0.16667 s, then settles to 14.32°. The general `ANIM_BLEND_S` (0.18 s) is *past* that peak, so reusing it would draw the apex at partial weight — the body least bent at the one frame that carries the information. **The ranking is the design call.** Death outranks a flinch (a corpse does not flinch, and the gait carries `Clip::Death` already), and below that the flinch and the swing are **one slot with the newest winning** — each clears the other's clock. That costs the attacker a truncated swing arc where everyone else sees a whole one, which is a **second asymmetry** and is the honest price: the alternative, ranking the swing above, drops ~83 % of flinches in a melee duel, because the sim allows a swing every 1.267 s and the clip runs 1.053 s of it. Gates: `rig_asset.rs::{the_flinch_clip_is_the_length_the_client_thinks_it_is,the_flinch_blend_lands_on_the_clips_own_apex}`, seven units in `anim.rs`, the wiring scrape `anim.rs::the_flinch_is_wired_from_the_feed_to_the_body`, and the widened `pop_hit` in `client-core/tests/wire.rs`. **Nobody has seen the pose** — the standing caveat `NOW.md` §0sw and §0chr both carry. | `NOW.md` §0pvp item 1 · `crates/client/src/render/{anim,bodies,feed}.rs` · `crates/client-core/src/core.rs` (`NO_VICTIM`, `pop_hit`) |
| remote swing v0 — another player's arm is audible and positional (client lane, 2026-08-18) | **PROPOSED — nobody has heard it**, audio v0's standing caveat, and `remote footsteps v0`'s shape one verb over. `EV_SWING` has been broadcast since wire v47 and the arc has been drawn in silence; reusing `Cue::Swing` for it was the trap `NOW.md` §0sw named, because that row is **non-positional** — full gain at both ears with no pan and no falloff, so every swing on the island would have arrived as if it were in your hands. So `Cue::RemoteSwing` is appended after `Growl` (the enum's append-order rule — cue noise is seeded off the discriminant), **byte-identical** to the local cue by delegation in `synth::render`: what makes a swing remote is its def, never its sound. Cue row (`RSWING`): radius and gain read off the local `SWING` row (**20 m / 0.45**) rather than restated — that radius has been in the table since it was written and **has never been read**, because `positional: false` makes `radius_m` dead, so this is where the number becomes true instead of a new one being invented beside it. What differs is deliberate: **positional**; **priority 4** against the local 3, the impacts' register, because another player's swing is intel and your own is a keystroke you just pressed; and a **40 ms cooldown** rather than 120, since the cooldown is per-CUE and therefore the crew's stagger and not one arm's — ⚠ **it binds inside a frame** (`a_cooldown_binds_within_one_frame`), so two raiders swinging on the same frame are still one voice, and 40 ms is what keeps the second one from waiting an eighth of a second. The producer (`render/audio.rs::remote_swings`) reads the same `feed.swings()` slice `bodies::stream` animates from and the transform that system just wrote, so the sound and the arc cannot disagree about who or where; querying the drawn bodies is also what makes two culls free — your own swing has no entity (`stream` skips `player_id`, so the local `Cue::Swing` still fires exactly once) and a swinger outside AOI has no position to guess at. Gates: `tests/sound.rs::{a_remote_swing_is_the_same_arm_heard_where_the_body_is,a_remote_swing_is_heard_at_the_body_and_culled_by_distance,a_second_swinger_is_not_locked_out_by_the_first,the_remote_swing_has_a_producer_and_the_local_one_is_not_it}` plus the bank's physical checks via `Cue::ALL`. **A positional HIT sound was deliberately not taken with it**: it needs a flesh-impact waveform nothing in `synth.rs` generates, which is a second design and not a fallout. | `NOW.md` §0pvp item 2 · §0sw · `crates/client/src/sound/{mod.rs,synth.rs}` · `render/{audio.rs,mod.rs}` |
| remote death fact v0 — a killed body falls: one bit on `EntityState`, `PROTO_VER = 48` (client+wire lane, 2026-08-18) | **PROPOSED.** A corpse keeps its slot, its position, its facing and its interest entry until its owner leaves the death screen (`world.rs` `die` — the screen is waiting on the body), and not one bit of that record said the person was out of the fight, so the client drew a killed player standing at idle. Three choices and each is a refusal as much as a pick. **(a) A wire bit, not a derived one.** `EV_DEATH` is already broadcast and carries the victim, so a client could latch it for free — but nothing on the wire ever says *alive again*, so a player respawning inside your view would draw as a corpse forever. A fact the wire cannot retract is not a fact a client may infer. **(b) Unconditional beside `sleeping`, not delta-gated**, which is v26's argument unchanged: it flips twice per death, so a change flag would spend a bit to save a bit and add a state the decoder can fall out of step on. **(c) It is a STATE, not an event**, and that is what separates it from `EV_SWING` — a swing is an instant and a death is a condition that lasts, so a body entering AOI after the kill has to be able to learn it, which an event already spent cannot tell it. **Laying the body down is safe in a way the sleeper pose is not**, and that is the one claim worth checking rather than believing: `combat::strike`, `ranged` and the blast all skip `hp == 0`, and players do not collide with each other, so a corpse sits inside no volume the server tests — which is the whole of the objection that keeps a *sleeper* standing. **The disclosure cost, said out loud**: the bit rides the snapshot, so it is AOI-scoped and tells you only about bodies you can already see — strictly less than `EV_DEATH`, which is broadcast to the whole shard today. Nothing open. | `NOW.md` §0chr · protocol `lib.rs` `goldens.rs` · server `core.rs` · client-core `interp.rs` · client `render/{anim,bodies}.rs` · `server/tests/client_loop.rs` |
| player rig v0 — the numbers the character import invented (client lane, 2026-08-17) | **PROPOSED, shipping.** ⚠ **(1) is retired the day it was written and is kept as the record of a stopgap that lasted an afternoon.** ~~Sprint playback 1.35×~~ — there was no sprint clip, so `Clip::Sprint` played the jog faster at a rate deliberately *below* the 1.8 speed ratio, on the grounds that a gait cycle at 1.8× reads as a cartoon. `ci/retarget_anim.py` then gave the rig a real `Sprint_Loop`, so the alias, the rate and the whole per-clip `Clip::speed` hook were deleted rather than left as scaffolding. **What survives is the reasoning**, for the next rig that arrives short: an alias plus a rate is the honest stopgap, and it is cheaper to delete than a duplicated clip would have been. (2) **Bark roughness 0.85**, imposed at import over the generator's 0.5528 — a wooden character at half roughness reads as plastic. Not measured against a reference photograph, which is what would retire this row. (2b) **`ANIM_HEAD_PITCH_MAX` = 0.9 rad (~52°)**, against a wire that carries ~88°. **A clamp and not a scale**, because the two fail differently: a scale makes every glance an understatement, while a clamp is only wrong at the extremes — where a head-only look is *already* wrong, since a person looking at their own feet bends at the chest. The remainder is dropped rather than distributed across the spine, and distributing it is the named follow-up. (3) **The sleeper tint, `srgb(0.34, 0.38, 0.46)` over the model's own albedo, roughness +0.1.** It replaces a flat `srgb(0.24, 0.26, 0.30)` that used to overwrite the material entirely; the constraint it keeps is the one `BodyShades` states — colder and darker, never brighter, because a sleeper is the thing you sneak up on and making it the most legible object on the beach hands the raider more than the wire does. **All three are look, not balance**: no gate scores them, and the honest test is the operator booting the game. | `crates/client/src/render/anim.rs` (`Clip::speed`, `build`) · `assets/models/MANIFEST.md` · `NOW.md` §0chr |
| class-S interest v0 — the placed-piece walk is aimed: `PIECE_SCAN_BATCH = 256` store entries scanned per client per tick (server lane, 2026-08-18) | **PROPOSED, and it is the only invented number in the slice — the two radii are derived and say so.** `PIECE_INTEREST_CM` is `AOI_EXIT_CM` and the re-arm threshold is `AOI_EXIT_CM − AOI_ENTER_CM`, because `NETCODE.md` §7 asks for ONE spatial truth and a second set of radii would be two. What is genuinely new is a *rate*: a filtered walk looks at more entries than it says, so the work needs its own cap (wall 4) and the wire batch (`PIECE_SYNC_BATCH`, protocol) stays what it was. The floor is not taste — a walk that cannot finish before a sprinter travels the re-arm threshold is re-armed forever and never converges, which is `reference/NETWORK.md` §9.2.1's livelock with a new cause: `MAX_PIECES / B / TICK_HZ × SPRINT_SPEED < PIECE_REARM_CM` ⇒ **B > 47**. 256 clears it by 5.4× (a full-store walk is 32 ticks, in which a sprinter covers 5.9 m of 32 m) and bounds the scan at 256 integer distance tests per walking client per tick — zero in steady state, where every walk has finished. Measured on a 2,291-piece island with 454 pieces inside the radius: a joiner's walk went **2,291 → 454 records, 11,384 → 2,258 bytes, complete at tick 72 → 19**. `crates/server/tests/piece_interest.rs` holds the inequality against the constants it is derived from, and both directions of the radius (too wide leaks, too tight breaks the promise). | `NETCODE.md` §7 · `reference/NETWORK.md` §9.2.1 · `crates/server/src/interest.rs` |
| trust log v0 — `EV_TRUST` is sim-side and nothing encodes it; presence is three values, not a bool; a verb on your own record is silent (systems lane, 2026-08-18) | **PROPOSED.** `PLAYERS.md` wall 3 asks every trust-bearing verb for an event carrying whether the counterparty was online. Three calls in it are the operator's to overrule. **(a) It does not cross the wire.** The obvious alternative — a bit on `EV_DOOR` — would broadcast *this base's owner is asleep* to every client in range, which is a fact a human has to walk to a base and watch for. Handing it out free fails `PLAYERS.md` wall 1's affordance rule on the human side before an agent ever reads it, so the row is the server's to keep and the wire is untouched (no `PROTO_VER` bump). The cost is that nothing reads it yet: `ShardCore`'s drain hits `_ => {}` and a server lane owes it a sink. **(b) Presence is `AWAKE` / `ASLEEP` / `GONE`.** A sleeper standing in the world and an evicted body are different facts about a counterparty, and folding them would let the record claim a body was there to be raided when its slot had been freed; a corpse on the death screen reads AWAKE, because the question the field asks is whether the act had a witness. **(c) `owner == actor` is silent**, along with `owner == 0` and a `mob::mob_id` (a carcass bag carries the animal's tagged id where a player's goes). Logging your own door would bury the measurement under ordinary play. **What it costs:** one extra seat in the 256-event ring per trust verb, on a tick whose command cap is also 256 — so the ring was already the tighter bound and this halves the headroom on a saturated one. Unlike every other event, a dropped trust row cannot be re-derived by a resync: it is a fact about a moment. Gated by six checks in `sim-core/tests/event_roles.rs`, each proven red under its own mutant. |
| barrel proportions v1 — the road barrel is the measured drum: `OCCUPANT_R_M[BarrelSlot] = 0.2925` · `OCCUPANT_TOP_M[BarrelSlot] = 0.88` · `archetype_lift(BarrelSlot) = 0.44` · `Cylinder::new(0.2925, 0.88)` (client+sim lane, 2026-08-18) | **PROPOSED, and every digit is read rather than chosen.** A 55-gallon drum is **0.585 m across by 0.88 m tall**, and two independent sources agree on that pair: the reference set's `barrelroad`, and Meshy's `auto_size` vision estimate, which returned 0.880 × 0.585 unprompted (`DECISIONS.md` 2026-08-11, deployable proportions v1). The arithmetic is only halving: radius = 0.585 / 2 = **0.2925**, half-height = 0.88 / 2 = **0.44**, and the lift IS that half-height, so the drum's base is the slot's ground and its top is its own **0.88** — which is what `OCCUPANT_TOP_M` means by "lift + half-extent", and the convention `CrateSlot` (0.4 + 0.4) and `CacheSlot` (0.275 + 0.275) already keep. **What it replaces was a guess carried forward**: 0.45 / 0.975 cited `CylinderGeometry(0.45, 0.45, 0.95)`, the deleted browser client's geometry — 0.9 m across by 0.95 tall, ratio 1.06, near-spherical and ~44 % too fat, where the real object is 1.50. It also stood 2.5 cm proud of its own base (lift 0.5 over a half-height of 0.475), which the new pair closes to zero before `SINK_M`. **This is the collision half of a row that already claimed to be done.** 2026-08-11's deployable-proportions row records the barrel as *"now 0.585 × 0.88"*, and it was not: the mesh moved on a branch and the merge reddened `greybox.rs`'s `every_drawn_archetype_fits_the_volume_the_sim_blocks`, because the sim still blocked 0.1575 m wider than the client drew — past `SLACK_R_M`, i.e. an invisible collision skirt. The mesh could never move alone; the pair moves together or not at all. **Its cost is two goldens**, regenerated in the same commit (walls 5/6): `test_replay`'s `GOLDEN_FINAL_HASH`, because a body's collision against a barrel is replayed state. **One behaviour changes downstream and it is the floor doing its job**: `skirt_base_r` is `max(r, SKIRT_MIN_R_M = 0.30)`, so the barrel's clutter skirt now takes the 0.30 m floor instead of its own radius — the case that floor was written for, and clutter is neither hashed nor networked. | `crates/sim-core/src/terrain.rs` (`OCCUPANT_R_M`, `OCCUPANT_TOP_M`, `occupant_volume`) · `crates/client/src/render/props.rs` (`archetype_mesh`, `archetype_lift`) · `crates/client/tests/greybox.rs` · `crates/sim-core/tests/replay.rs` |
| durability pip v0 — the bar a worn item draws in a cell: `PIP_H_PX = 3.0` px tall · `PIP_FILL = VITAL_HP` · `PIP_TROUGH = VITAL_TROUGH` (client lane, 2026-08-17) | **PROPOSED, and the shape of the thing was already spoken — only its look is here.** `ui::slots::pip_fraction` and its four gates landed with the durability wire slice and pinned all three states (a pristine item draws nothing, a worn one draws its fraction, a dead one draws the trough empty); wire v46 gave it the ceiling to divide by. What was left was pixels, and they are **reused rather than picked**: the pip is a fraction of a maximum, which is the statement the vitals stack already makes, so it takes the two colours measured off `crafting.png` for that job instead of a third pair nobody measured. **One colour, no warning band** — a green-to-red ramp at some fraction is the obvious embellishment and it would be an invented knob with a threshold in it; nothing in `reference/DURABILITY.md` sources one, and the state that matters is already distinct without it, because a pristine item draws no bar at all. Height is the only number with a consequence: at 3 px the hotbar's count badge lifts off the cell edge by that much to clear it, so raising it moves the badge. Drawn by four cells (hotbar, your grid, the container's, the drag ghost) through one `pip_fraction` call each, and `tests/ui.rs` §Q scans the two files for the call — the defect it is proven against is a panel that draws stacks and divides nothing, which is exactly what shipped for four wire versions. | `NOW.md` §0dur item 1 · `crates/client/src/ui/slots.rs` · `render/panels/mod.rs` |
| equipment v0 — the wear grid, the damage types, the hit bands, the coverage shares, the layer rule, the condition ratio and **three new balance bands** (sim+wire+content+client lane, 2026-08-17) | **PROPOSED. Rewritten 2026-08-17 against a mapped tree** — the first draft of this row was written before the code was read and got two things wrong, both recorded below. **The model, in one line: the hit AREA is geometry the sim already computes, not a roll.** The operator took the reference's five-area grid, and `combat.rs:36` says the sim has no hit areas at all — so something had to resolve one, and the two candidates were a per-hit RNG roll (legal under walls 1/2/5 — `loot.rs:158`'s stateless `cell_hash` form is the precedent) and a geometric band on the capsule. **Geometry wins on three counts**: a roll makes `hits_to_kill` an *expectation* while `balance.rs` anchor 2 asserts on an integer; `PLAYERS.md`'s agent player facing a rolled area learns a slot machine where one facing a geometric band learns to aim; and the arrow path **already computes the hit height and throws it away** (`ranged.rs`, whose own comment says "No headshot, so the whole body is one target"). Four struck bands on the 1.7 m capsule (feet/legs/chest/head), hands reachable by the arrow annulus only — melee never lands on hands, and that asymmetry is **stated rather than papered over with a fudge factor**. Weighted coverage shares are used in exactly one place, the blast, because an explosion genuinely does reach every area at once. Layers absorb **outer-first, absorb-and-subtract**, which makes `raw == dealt + absorbed_over + absorbed_under` an exact integer identity. **TAKEN from the reference, recorded not invented** (`BALANCE.md` §6.1): the **25%** broken floor; the condition debit being the damage *absorbed* rather than a flat tick; **player damage only** wears worn items; the part multipliers head ×2 / chest ×1 / limbs ×0.5 (`PROJECTILES.md`, and head ×2 is already `headshot_mult` in content); the hitbox never changing with what is worn — ours by construction, **written down because a bulky-armour flag will be proposed**; and the 40% movement penalty charged for entering the category rather than per piece, **deferred to its own slice**. **OURS, and every one is a number nobody can hand us**: the three band heights — head from **1.52 m**, chest from **0.95 m** (the hip, so LEGS is genuinely legs and the pelvis counts as torso, which is what makes the reference's limbs-×0.5 portable to a band split), legs from **0.12 m** (the ankle); the melee origin, which is **derived** as the midpoint of the head and chest floors rather than typed, so it survives a band edit — the spoken rule is *a level swing between two bodies of equal height lands mid-chest*; the limb annulus at **0.55** of the squared capsule radius, **the marginal call in this design** — refuse it and hands pay only against blast, which makes that slot nearly decorative in v0; the blast shares **head 10 / chest 30 / legs 44 / feet 6 / hands 10**, derived from the band heights, with the residual going to chest so the five parts sum to the raw damage exactly; the condition ratio at **1.00** condition per hp absorbed, which is chosen so that an item's *lifetime* becomes `items.toml`'s existing `condition_max` — a content number, wall 7 whole; the damage-type set at **six** — slash, blunt, stab, bullet, explosion, bite — because `weapons.toml` has no type column to inherit from and radiation/cold/heat/electric/falling have **no source of damage in this world**, so shipping columns nothing can key is worse than omitting them; bite resolving to the legs (the strongest single lever for making boots a decision, and it teaches something legible — *wolves go for your legs*); starvation and the keypad shock taking **no** reduction and **no** wear, each owed a one-line comment at the site because an omission reads as an oversight; the eight wear slots and their (area, layer) table; and the per-type protection vectors for the armor rows, which `ARMOR.md` §7 establishes cannot be taken because every reference number is an undated snapshot across at least three reworks. ⚠ **THREE NEW BANDS, and they are the load-bearing half of this row** — each closes a hole where `test_content` stays green over a broken game. (1) A **head TTK band**: `balance.toml`'s existing `headshot_mult` band says in its own words that it exists *"so a data edit can't quietly make headshots one-tap past the TTK band"* — and it pins the multiplier while pinning no TTK, because nothing ever applied it. The day the multiplier goes live the crossbow at 35 × 2 kills in **two** head hits against 100 hp while `ttk_bow` stays green. (2) An **armor minimum**: anchor 2 is a ceiling only, so a full worn set that adds *zero* hits satisfies it perfectly — armor could be entirely decorative with every gate green, which is the *paid nobody* failure this whole slice exists to fix, recreated one level out. (3) A **full-set ceiling**: `armor_extra_hits_max` is per *piece* and cannot see two layers stacked, and two 45% layers leave 30% of the damage. **Two corrections to this row's first draft**, both from reading the code: the wear grid **can** reuse `encode_event_cont_sync` — it already takes a kind and already sizes its bound with `slots_in(kind)`, so no new event subtype is owed — but the *subscription bookkeeping* around it is single-slot on both ends (`open_cont_kind` server-side, `cont_kind` client-side) and `CONT_SELF` on that message is reserved as the CLOSE sentinel, so wear needs its own always-on shadow beside the open-container one or opening the wear grid evicts the box you are dragging out of. And the wasm shadow-stack trap is **not** tripped here: eight wear slots is +48 bytes per player, taking `World` from ~302 to ~307 KiB against a 1 MiB stack — 1.6%, measured, not the thing that tips it. `crate::boxed_array` **cannot** be used regardless, because `Player` derives `Copy` and is copied by value in `World::die`. **What the operator is being asked:** the three bands above, which are the ones that decide whether this ships as a game or as a spreadsheet; the limb annulus, which is the one slot that is thin without it; and the question already open from this morning — whether the under layer ships in v0 at all, given the reference's under layer carries comfort and cold and we have no temperature, so a burlap shirt and a burlap vest are the same item at two prices until `survival.rs` grows one. | 2026-08-17 spoken row · `reference/ARMOR.md` §3/§4/§5/§6/§9 · `reference/PROJECTILES.md` · `content/{armor,weapons,items,balance}.toml` · `sim-core/src/{inventory,combat,ranged,charge,world,persist}.rs` · `protocol/src/{lib,event}.rs` |
| melee mark v0 — a landed swing scuffs what it hit, on the existing `EV_IMPACT`: `STRIKE_WAIST_FRAC = 0.5` of a short occupant's height (systems lane, 2026-08-18) | **PROPOSED, and the wire cost is zero, which is the whole finding.** `NOW.md` §0mk item 1 said a melee mark needed the same missing fact as §0sw's remote animation. It did not: `EV_IMPACT` was never an arrow's fact but *a surface was struck at this point* — already broadcast, already carrying a quantized point and a surface class, already the only thing `render/decal.rs` reads — so `gather::swing` pushes it and the mark costs no wire byte, no `PROTO_VER` bump and no client line. **One invented number, and it is here because the rule says so rather than because it is interesting.** A strike lands at the swinger's eye height or at `STRIKE_WAIST_FRAC` of the occupant's height, whichever is lower. Half is the middle of the thing rather than a tuning — you cannot strike the centre of a knee-high rock from eye level, and for anything taller than you, which is every tree, the eye wins and that is where a swing at a trunk lands. It was measured into existence: clamping to the occupant's TOP put the mark on the rim of the boulder beside spawn (y 13.43 against an eye at 13.87), where the mark's own normal is horizontal and the surface curves away under it. **Refused on purpose**: a swing the node refuses scuffs nothing (the push sits below the tool arm), a bush gets no mark because `occupant_volume` gives it no skin, and flesh stays unmarked because `SURF_BITS` has exactly one spare code and spending it on blood is a deliberate act nobody has asked for. ⚠ **Unverified visually and it cannot be verified headless**: no `ForwardDecal` renders under lavapipe at any size or alpha, so neither this mark nor the arrow marks of 2026-08-16 have been seen by anybody. One boot on a GPU settles it. | `NOW.md` §0mk · sim-core `gather.rs` `world.rs` · `sim-core/tests/gather.rs` |
| remote swing fact v0 — a body that swings is a body other people can see: `EV_SWING = 40` (broadcast, outcome-free) · `SUB_SWING = 52` · PROTO_VER 47 at the time (48 since the death bit — see the 2026-08-18 corpse row, which is where the registry's live declaration moved) · `SWING_RING = 8` · `SWING_CLIP_S = 1.05333` s (was 1.0 with `Punch_Cross`; see the 2026-08-18 swing row) (systems+wire+client lane, 2026-08-18) | **PROPOSED.** The operator asked for *"some animation atleast showing the rock swing"*; the first-person half landed 2026-08-16 and the other half could not, because there was nothing on the wire to animate from. `EV_HIT` is unicast to the attacker and drops field `a` at encode, and a **miss** — the commonest swing in the game — crossed nothing at all, so another player swinging at you was a body standing perfectly still. Three decisions here and each one is a refusal as much as a choice. **(a) It carries no position.** Every body's place is already in the snapshot; the one thing a client cannot derive is that the arm moved, because it never receives another player's input frame. **(b) It is outcome-free and fires on a whiff**, pushed from `gather::swing`'s cadence gate — the only line in the tree that runs exactly once per swing regardless of what the swing finds. A fact that fires only on a hit is a hit fact, and this lane has one. **(c) `b` and `c` are zero and stay zero.** The held item would be cheap in bits and would make an a/b transposition detectable, but nothing draws a different arc for a rock than for a hatchet yet, and a field nobody reads is a field nobody maintains. **The disclosure cost, written down rather than left unsaid**: the event lane has no AOI filter at all — chat's radius is the only distance test in `pump_events` — so a swing reaches every connected client, exactly as `EV_SHOT` and `EV_IMPACT` already do. It says a player is awake and swinging, and nothing about where. **Open for the operator, and it is the only open thing here**: the clip is `Punch_Cross` and the ask was a rock SWING. That is arithmetic, not taste — `SWING_INTERVAL_TICKS` 38 at `TICK_HZ` 30 lets a player swing every 1.267 s, and `Sword_Attack` is 1.500 s, which with `ANIM_BLEND_S` on top is 1.68 s: every arc would be cut off by the next one and no body would ever finish a stroke. `Punch_Cross` is 1.000 s and fits. The two honest exits both need a word — accept the punch, or shorten the sword clip. | `NOW.md` §0sw · sim-core `world.rs` `gather.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` `goldens.rs` · server `core.rs` · client-core `core.rs` · client `render/{feed,anim,bodies,mod}.rs` · `sim-core/tests/event_roles.rs` · `server/tests/gather_wire.rs` · `client-core/tests/wire.rs` · `client/tests/anim.rs` |
| surface marks v0 — an arrow leaves a mark: `MARKS = 48` drawable at once · `LIFE_S = 45.0` s at full strength then `FADE_S = 6.0` s of tail · `SIZE_M = 0.22` footprint · `DEPTH_FADE_M = 0.35` blend reach · `PREWARM_FRAMES = 8` at `PREWARM_ALPHA = 0.02` · `IMPACT_RING = 8` · wire v45 (sim + client lanes, 2026-08-16) | **PROPOSED — the operator asked for marks (*"when i hit a tree it needs a mark on it, when i shot the ground it needs bullet holes"*), and this is the half the sim could already answer.** `ranged::step` has always computed the exact point an arrow stops and which of its three predicates stopped it, and then thrown both away; `EV_IMPACT` is that fact, and `render/decal.rs` is the first decal in the tree. **The wire numbers are not new**: the point crosses in the entity lane's own quanta (`POS_XZ_BITS`/`POS_Y_BITS`/`POS_Y_BIAS`), so the window check is `write_bag`'s and nothing was invented for it — the one new width is `SURF_BITS = 2`, registered in `event.rs`'s wire-domain table and derived from `ranged::SURF_BUILT` so a fourth kind fails to compile rather than truncating into a live one. **Every constant above is a client cosmetic** and none reaches the sim: `MARKS` is a *view* cap the way `TRACERS` is, and its overflow policy is drop-**oldest**, the inverse of the tracer's, because a tracer that vanishes mid-flight reads as a bug while the oldest mark is the faintest thing on screen. `DEPTH_FADE_M` is the one with a visual argument rather than an arithmetic one: it decides whether a mark on a trunk smears onto the ground behind it, which is `ART.md` rule 2's "clean intersection edge" arriving as a literal decal. `PREWARM_*` is not a taste knob at all — it answers `CLAUDE.md`'s shader-prewarm trap, which survived the port, is worse natively, and has had **no** gate since `browser_smoke` went with the browser. **What is deliberately not here**: ~~a mark from a melee swing~~ — **that landed 2026-08-18 and the reasoning here was wrong.** This row said it needed the missing fact that blocks §0sw; it did not. `EV_IMPACT` is not an arrow's fact but *a surface was struck at this point*, so `gather::swing` pushes the same event on a landed node swing and the mark cost no wire byte and no new constant. Still not here: a mark on a built PIECE from a swing (behind the address question below), a mark on flesh, a mark that persists across a session, and spray paint, which is not a decal feature but a deployable. **v45 and not v44**: this was written as 44 on the branch while the damage band took 44 on the trunk — the third branch/trunk number collision this wire has had, cured the way `protocol/src/lib.rs` has cured the other two, by leaving the trunk's number alone. The two changes touch nothing in common, which is why both stayed green apart. **Open for the operator**: the lifetime band, and whether a built piece's address should ride `EV_IMPACT` so a mark on a wall can face the wall rather than its cell's dominant axis. | `NOW.md` §0mk · sim-core `ranged.rs` `world.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` `goldens.rs` · server `core.rs` · client-core `core.rs` · client `render/{decal,feed,mod}.rs` · `sim-core/tests/event_roles.rs` · `client-core/tests/wire.rs` · `client/tests/decal.rs` |
| discord rich presence v0 — the application id (`GATES_DISCORD_APP_ID`), the art key (`LARGE_IMAGE`, shipping as `gates`), the six presence lines, and the update floor (`MIN_UPDATE`, 5 s, under Discord's published 5-per-20-s limit) (client lane, 2026-08-16) | **PROPOSED, and shipping DARK: there is no id in this tree and no default, so nothing connects until an operator sets one.** Prompted by the operator seeing the game named in Discord and asking how it knew. **The answer is measured rather than assumed, and it rules out the obvious explanation**: Discord matches running executables against `GET /api/v9/applications/detectable` — **22,455 entries on 2026-08-16, none of them `gates` or `gates.exe`** — so the sighting was not verified detection. It was Discord's *manual* "Add it!", which registers any unrecognised windowed process and thereafter shows the bare process name, which is exactly the lowercase `gates` our launch contract ships (`ci/depot.py`). **Two things follow and only one is code.** Getting into that database is a submission to Discord and therefore an operator act; rich presence is not, needs no database entry, and overrides the bare name with a title, two lines and an elapsed clock. ⚠ **Do not read the 22,455 as a cap on who may do this** — it is the curated list for *passive process detection*, not the population of applications that may push presence, and conflating them is the reason this looked gated. Rich presence takes an application id, which any developer creates in the portal with no review. The proof is in the same data: **VS Code, Spotify, Photoshop, Figma, Blender, OBS, IntelliJ and Neovim are all absent from the list**, and every one of them shows up in Discord statuses daily — as rich presence under its own id. **The application's NAME in the portal is the word drawn after "Playing"**, so naming it `Gates` is what retires the lowercase `gates`, and that is the visible win rather than the payload. ⚠ And the submission route in the other direction is **unverified**: self-serve game selling is deprecated and no current form was found, so the detectable-list act is a question to put to Discord rather than a step anyone here has confirmed exists. **What is deliberately NOT published**: the shard address and the player's identity. A presence line is visible to everyone who can see the profile, so an address in it hands a stranger the box a friend is on — `reference/VOICE.md` §9.1 is that bug with a different transport, and it took the reference a devblog and a forced migration to undo. `no_payload_carries_an_address_or_an_identity` is that rule as a test rather than a paragraph, and it is the one that fails when somebody adds a shard name and reaches for the address beside it. **No dependency**: the framing is two little-endian `u32`s and the payloads are four fixed shapes, which does not earn serde — `crate::config`'s call about TOML, same crate, same reason. **What the operator is being asked**: (1) whether to create the Discord application at all, which is what turns this on; (2) whether to also submit `gates`/`gates.exe` to the detectable database, which is a *separate* act and the only one that helps players who never touch rich presence — and which is worth weighing against how generic the name is, since the same submission is what stops some other program named `gates` matching ours; (3) the six lines of copy, which are plain sentences on purpose because they are read by people who have never heard of the game; (4) whether art is uploaded under the `gates` key (until something is, Discord draws no image, which is harmless). **What is not built and is the honest next slice**: the join button. `scry://join/<title>/<host:port>` already exists and is exactly the shape Discord's party/join flow wants (`crates/client/src/deeplink.rs`), but a join secret is a new security surface — it publishes an address to a stranger by design — and it needs the address question above answered first rather than second. There is also no in-game on/off toggle: the player's control today is Discord's own activity-privacy switch, which is real but is not ours. | `crates/client/src/discord.rs` · `crates/client/src/render/presence.rs` · `NOW.md` §0dsc |
| free look v0 — how far the head may turn from the body while `Left Alt` is held: `FREE_LOOK_YAW_LIMIT = 2.094_395_1` rad (120°) (client lane, 2026-08-16) | **PROPOSED, shipping at 120°, and it is OURS rather than taken.** The 2026-08-16 control row copies the reference's bindings key for key, and this is the one number that copying does not supply: the reference offers free look as a verb and publishes no angle for it, so `BALANCE.md` §6's take-theirs default has nothing to take — and §6 governs sim values in any case, where this is a client cosmetic that never reaches the wire. **What the bound is actually for**: the yaw is an offset from the body, and the body keeps walking and aiming where it was, so the only failure mode is a head turned far enough that the player loses track of which way they are going. 120° is wider than a shoulder check in either direction and stops short of the full half-turn where the camera faces the body's back — at which point a player running forward is watching themselves recede and has no on-screen cue that the mode is even on. **The pitch half is not a new number**: the offset is clamped against the body's own pitch so the summed view stops at the existing `PITCH_LIMIT`, which is what keeps the yaw basis from degenerating at the pole. **What the operator is being asked**: whether 120° is the number, and separately whether the mode should have a visible cue at all — the reference tilts the viewmodel; ours does not, and the held item currently sways with the camera because the viewmodel is parented to it (`viewmodel.rs` reads `eye.yaw`). That is the one cosmetic seam the split leaves, and it is a slice rather than a constant. | `crates/client/src/look.rs` (`FREE_LOOK_YAW_LIMIT`, `free_yaw_after`, `free_pitch_after`) · `crates/client/src/render/input.rs` · `NOW.md` §0ctl |
| build base lattice v0 — pieces line up: `BUILD_BASE_Q_M = 0.5` (the vertical build lattice, `LEVEL_H_M/6`) · skirt `SKIRT_SINK_M = 0.35` / `SKIRT_MAX_M = 4.0` · aim march `AIM_STEP_M = 0.25` / `AIM_RANGE_M = BUILD_REACH_M + 3` (client+sim lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED — the operator's 2026-08-15 screenshots ("bad news about the building system and pieces lining out"): stepped foundations with cracks, a wall ghost z-fighting inside a standing wall, `SPOT TAKEN` on a ghost parked behind the gap the crosshair was on.** Three mechanisms, three fixes. **(1) Every column sampled its own terrain**, so adjacent foundations stood at two heights on any ground that was not flat and the wall between two cells met neither neighbour flush. Now `build::column_floor_y` — the ONE height implementation; `collide::col_base_y`, the renderer's `level_base_y` and `deploy::box_drop_pos` all call it, retiring two hand-kept formula copies (one had already drifted: the box-drop bag sat 0.3 m inside the slab it claimed to land on) — snaps cell-center terrain to a 0.5 m lattice **with the lift folded into the snap** (0.3 is not binary-exact; added after, `k·q + 0.3` rounds per-magnitude and a one-band step measured 1.499999). In one band flushness is bit-equal `==`; between bands the step is an exact multiple of q. **Why 0.5 and not finer/coarser**: exactly representable in f32 (flushness needs no epsilon), `q/2 + PIECE_LIFT_M = 0.55 ≤ STEP_UP = 0.6` so a lone foundation is always steppable, and q < STEP_UP so even a band step is walked, not jumped; coarser widens plates but strands builders at their own slab. Measured on the shipped island: 71.5% of adjacent land pairs in an 80×80-cell centre window are bit-equal flush (was 0% by construction). Gate `sim-core/tests/base_lattice.rs` (4, the flush identity proven red under the raw formula). **(2) Foundations drew as 0.3 m plates**, floating over the downhill side of their cell. `structures::foundation_part` — per-ADDRESS where `shape_parts` is per-shape, one emit for the piece AND the ghost — extends the slab down as a skirt to `SKIRT_SINK_M` below the lowest of five ground samples under the cell, capped at `SKIRT_MAX_M`; the top face never moves (`tests/ghost.rs` holds top-at-plane and bottom-in-ground). Drawn, not collided — the residual is in `NOW.md` §0bl. **(3) The aim ignored the look ray**: `feet + yaw·3.5 m`, pitch discarded, so the ghost could not be steered by looking at a spot. `place::aim_from_look` marches the eye ray (the tracer's own convention — the ghost sits where a shot would land) against terrain and the predictor's piece surfaces (`ClientCore::cols`), planar-clamped to `BUILD_REACH_M`; the 3.5 m projection survives as the sky/out-of-range fallback. Pure and headless-gated in `place.rs`. **No wire change, no save change, no `PROTO_VER`** — height stays derived from (seed, cell), which is what made this landable in one pass. **Open for the operator:** the 0.5 (0.75 widens plates but needs a jump at every band step and can strand at a lone slab); whether an edge piece at a band boundary should base on the LOWER of its two columns instead of its canonical cell (today a boundary wall can hang one band over the lower plate — an arrow-sized slit); and the real v1 this defers, the reference's model — a plate height chosen at the FIRST foundation and latched by neighbours (too high/too low refusals, stilts past one band) — which is a stored height, i.e. a wire field, a save format bump and a mirror change, priced in `NOW.md` §0bl. | `NOW.md` §0bl · sim-core `build.rs` (`column_floor_y`) `collide.rs` `deploy.rs` · client `render/structures.rs` (`foundation_part`, `level_base_y`) `render/ghost.rs` `ui/place.rs` (`aim_from_look`, `target_at`) · `sim-core/tests/base_lattice.rs` · `client/tests/ghost.rs` |
| structure damage v0 — a raided wall looks raided: `DMG_BANDS = 8` (3 bits, `DMG_BAND_BITS`) · band rounds loss **up** · `DMG_DARKEST = 0.55` · `DMG_ROUGHEN = 0.12` · **`PROTO_VER` 43 → 44** (client+server+wire lane, 2026-08-16) | **PROPOSED — and like the row below it, the first half is a defect report.** Piece and deployable hp were **never on the wire**: `write_piece_rec` wrote address and row and stopped, `read_piece_rec` filled the rest from `..default()`, and nothing in `client-core` ever wrote the mirror's `hp`. So the client's copy was a permanent 0, and `ui::structure::Target::damaged()` — `hp_max > 0 && hp < hp_max` — answered **true for every structure in the world**. The one thing it gates, `hammer.rs`'s `Act::Say("not damaged")`, was therefore unreachable: every repair swing at an intact wall took a server round trip to come back refused, by a client-side guard that had never once fired. Nothing caught it because both halves are individually sane and the field was write-only. **What the wire carries is a BAND, not the hp**, and that is a design call rather than a saving: exact hp is 16 bits against 3, it changes on every swing where a band changes on one in eight, and it would hand a raider a readout of precisely how many more hits a wall needs — a mechanic nobody has spoken for. 3 bits also means **the width is the range check**, so a forged value is impossible rather than refused; that is why the band is 8 and not a rounder 10. **The band is derived at the encode boundary and stored nowhere**, which is the load-bearing choice: a stored band must be updated at every site that moves `hp` — a swing, decay, upkeep, a repair — and the one that gets forgotten is a wall drawing clean while the sim knows it is nearly through, with every gate green. Two things fall out of deriving it, both confirmed against the tree rather than assumed: `state_hash` walks an **explicit 12-byte field list**, so `dmg` cannot move a hash and a shard that never fills it is bit-identical to one that does; and `worldsave.rs` reads field by field, so **the save format did not grow and there is no format bump**. The sync walk alone would leave the band correct exactly once, at the moment you arrived, so `PieceSet::set_dmg` is fed by the `EV_STRUCT_HIT` and `EV_PIECE_REPAIRED` events already on the wire — no new byte for the live half. The render response is a scalar darkening plus a roughening over 4 tiers × 8 bands = 32 materials built once, which is the other reason the band is 3 bits: a continuous damage value means a material per PIECE, and a base is thousands. **`DMG_DARKEST = 0.55` is bounded by `ART.md` rule 3, not taste** — a damaged wall's shaded face pays the darkening AND `FACE_BOTTOM` AND the side ramp's foot, all multiplied, and 0.55 × 0.86 × 0.90 = 0.43 against rule 3's 0.30 floor; 0.35 would break it. `tests/pieces.rs` §D asserts the product, not the term. **Open for the operator:** whether 8 bands is the resolution (a player cannot count them, so the question is really how many visually distinct states a wall should have); the two response constants, which are the material's whole vocabulary until a cracked/scorched overlay is sourced — that is the right answer and it needs a map nobody has; and the two things this deliberately does not do, both in `NOW.md` §0ps — **deployables get the wire fix and no damage visual** (their materials are baked into the `.glb`, so a band variant means cloning loaded assets; the band is deliberately left OUT of the deploy redraw comparison rather than respawning a furnace on every swing for an identical picture), and nothing shows WHICH face was struck. | `NOW.md` §0ps · sim-core `build.rs` (`damage_band`, `DMG_BANDS`, `PieceRec::dmg`) `deploy.rs` (`DeployRec::dmg`) `worldsave.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` (`PROTO_VER`, `DMG_BAND_BITS`) `event.rs` (`write_piece_rec`, `write_deploy_rec`) + 94 regenerated `v44_*` fixtures · server `core.rs` (`piece_hp_max`, `deploy_hp_max`, the two sync fills) · client-core `core.rs` (`PieceSet::set_dmg`, the `StructHit`/`PieceRepaired` arms) · client `render/structures.rs` (`damage_mix`, `DMG_DARKEST`, `DMG_ROUGHEN`, `Live::dmg`) `ui/structure.rs` (`Target::dmg`) · `crates/client/tests/pieces.rs` §D · `sim-core/src/build.rs` §tests |
| piece surface v0 — a built piece wears a photograph: tier maps twig→`bark` ×1.6 · wood→`wood` · stone→`stone` · metal→`metal` (all ×1.0) · `PIECE_TILES_PER_M = 0.55` · face tint `FACE_TOP 1.14` / `FACE_SIDE 1.00` / `FACE_BOTTOM 0.86` with side ramp `SIDE_FOOT 0.90` → `SIDE_HEAD 1.10` · `SKIRT_STEP_M = 0.25` / `SKIRT_STEPS = 16` (client lane, 2026-08-16) | **PROPOSED — and the first item is a defect report, not a knob.** `structures::TIER` had **three** rows against the sim's **four** materials (`MAT_TWIG..=MAT_METAL`) and `spawn_piece` indexed it `.min(2)`, so every piece in the game drew one rung off: wood in the stone grey, stone as a `metallic: 0.85` conductor, metal right by accident, and **twig with no row at all** — which since twig v0 (2026-08-10) is the state every piece is FIRST seen in, because placement and grade are two acts and nothing enters the world as anything else. It shipped 2026-08-10 and was found 2026-08-16. Nothing caught it because a colour table has no gate and a clamp is not a compile error; `TIER` is `[_; N_TIERS]` now, so a fifth material is a build failure, and `tests/pieces.rs` §A holds the half a length cannot check (each material resolves to its own row, naming files that exist). **The rest is `ART.md` rule 1 on the largest flat surface in the game.** Pieces were the last thing in the client still drawing as a flat `base_color` — `props.rs` and `viewmodel.rs` have sampled these same CC0 maps since 2026-08-11 — so a wall now wears albedo + normal off `assets/textures/`, no new file and no manifest row, and the handles are the ones `PropMaps` already holds (the asset server keys on path plus settings). **Three prerequisites, each of which fails silently**: 0..1 UVs became metre-scaled (a stretched tile reads as no texture, the failure `textures.rs` records for the terrain), meshes gained tangents (without them Bevy ignores `normal_map_texture` outright), and they gained a vertex tint. **Every number here is scalar for one reason**: `ART.md` §7 bounds a per-channel gain to stretching a source's colour deviation by ≤ ×1, and a scalar's span is 1.000 by construction — the argument `GROUND_DETAIL_GAIN` already rests on. Twig's ×1.6 is the only gain: it wears `bark` (the literal surface of a stick) which measures luma 0.107 against `wood`'s 0.141, where the reference reads a twig frame as the PALER of the two; ×1.6 lands it at 0.171, between wood and stone, clipping 0.004% of texels (measured on the shipped file). The face tint is a mean-1 luminance field averaging exactly 1.0 over a box's six faces, so it modulates the photograph instead of replacing it — its job is that the reference's foundations do not read as boxes because their cap is a different surface from their sides, and value separation is most of that read and free in a vertex. The side ramp is rule 2's contact term. **`SKIRT_STEP_M` is a consequence, not a taste**: UVs do not scale with a transform, so the skirt's per-address depth had to become discrete before it could be textured at all — it rounds **up**, never down, because a shallower skirt leaves the ground `tests/ghost.rs` requires it to be buried in. **Open for the operator:** the tile density (0.55 is the authored structures' 0.5 nudged off a whole number of tiles per 3 m cell, so a tile boundary does not land on every piece edge); twig's gain and whether `bark` is its identity at all — a straw/lashed-pole set is not in `assets/textures/` and would be sourced through `CANDIDATES.md`; and the two things this deliberately does NOT do, both in `NOW.md` §0ps — per-address variation (a base is still a hundred identical walls at one rotation, `ART.md` rule 7) and trim parts (the lashings, plank seams and capstones that are most of what the reference images actually show). | `NOW.md` §0ps · client `render/structures.rs` (`TIER`, `tier`, `part_mesh`, `box_mesh`, `tri_prism_mesh`, `face_tint`, `skirt_step`, `footing_of`, `piece_span`) · `crates/client/tests/pieces.rs` · `ART.md` rules 1/2/7, §7 · `assets/textures/MANIFEST.md` |
| transport truth v0 — the socket buffer, the admission gate, the controller and the telemetry: `UDP_BUF_BYTES = 8 MiB` asked both directions · `ADMIT_RETRY_AT = 2 * MAX_PLAYERS` · `ADMIT_REFUSE_AT = 4 * MAX_PLAYERS` · `NET_SAMPLE_EVERY = 500` writer polls (~1 Hz/connection) · `cc = "cubic"` (server lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED, and the defect is not a wrong number — it is that `NETCODE.md` §2.2 is headed *config of record* and three of its rows described code that did not exist.** Found by grepping the table row by row instead of reading it, which is the only reason it was found at all: four rows check out, the MTU row is honest that it takes quinn's defaults, and the remaining three were intentions written in the present tense. The socket-buffer row had carried a ⚠ since 2026-08-11; the other two had not. **(1) The socket buffer is asked for AND read back, and the readback is the actual feature.** `setsockopt(SO_RCVBUF)` does not fail when the kernel refuses — it clamps to `net.core.rmem_max` and returns success — so a shard could ask for 8 MiB, run on 208 KiB, and no number anywhere would disagree. `ShardStats` now carries `net_rcvbuf_asked` beside `net_rcvbuf_bytes` (and the send pair) so the two can contradict each other out loud. Measured on this box: `rmem_max` is 4 MiB, so the 8 MiB request is clamped and the pair says so. **The gate deliberately does not assert 8 MiB** — a wall that only passes on a tuned box teaches people to skip it. **(2) The admission gate is the DoS-shaped one and it was fully absent.** Every refusal we had (`refused_version|build|auth|ticket|full`) fires *after* a completed handshake — QUIC, TLS, CONNECT, SIWE, and an entitlement round trip to scry — so a full shard was an amplifier. `IncomingSession` is `quinn::Incoming` and exposes `retry()`/`refuse()`/`remote_address_validated()`, so this needed no new dependency and, notably, **did not need WebTransport dropped** (`NOW.md` §0wt asked whether the wrapper hid it; it does not). Retry before refuse, both past a full shard, asserted at **compile time** (`const _: () = assert!(…)`) rather than in a test, because every term is a constant and a wrong one should fail the build. ⚠ `retry()` **panics** if the address is already validated — wtransport's own doc says so — which is why the call is guarded rather than optimistic. **(3) The controller is selectable at last.** §2.2 promised a `--cc` flag from the day it was written; it is `shard.toml` `cc = "cubic" | "bbr"`, CUBIC still the default, an unknown value **refused at boot** rather than defaulted (an A/B that silently runs the other arm reports a result for a controller it never ran). **(4) Telemetry, which is what makes the other three measurable.** Nothing in `crates/` had ever called `stats()` or `rtt()`, so every congestion claim in `NETCODE.md` was unfalsifiable and the 100-bot soak's 16.5 kB/s/client was a ceiling somebody computed. `writer_task` now samples quinn's own `ConnectionStats` through wtransport's passthrough — **no wire field, no `PROTO_VER`, no golden** — as deltas rather than gauges, so a shard total survives connections coming and going. Sampling starts on the FIRST poll, not a period in, or a connection that lives under a second would contribute nothing and that is exactly when loss matters. **Open for the operator:** all five numbers. `UDP_BUF_BYTES` is quinn's README's advice and not a measurement of ours; the two admission multiples are arithmetic over `MAX_PLAYERS` picked to sit clear of a legitimate rush and nobody has seen a real flood; `NET_SAMPLE_EVERY` trades resolution against per-connection work and 1 Hz is a guess. And the one this unblocks rather than answers: **whether `cc = "bbr"` is better here, which is now runnable and has not been run.** | `NETCODE.md` §2.1, §2.2 · `NOW.md` §0tx · server `net.rs` (`bind_udp`, the accept gate, `writer_task`) `config.rs` (`Congestion`) `stats.rs` · `crates/server/tests/transport.rs` · `net.rs` unit tests |
| item durability v0 — four numbers, none of them ours to pick: **(Q1) a tier-1 tool's maximum condition** · **(Q2) does the rock wear** · **(Q3) where a worn tool is repaired** · **(Q4) what a tool at zero condition does** (systems lane, 2026-08-15) | ⚠ **Q1 AND Q2 ARE ANSWERED AND THIS ROW'S RECOMMENDATIONS FOR THEM ARE SUPERSEDED — see the dated 2026-08-15 row "Item condition is per-item and the rock wears". Q1 is not 100-as-derived, it is 100/50/400 as TAKEN, per item; Q2 is YES, not the "recommend no" below. Read the rest for the blast radius and the ordered cut, which are unaffected; read Q1/Q2's reasoning only as the argument that was made before the number was found — the Q1 derivation independently landed on 100, which is corroboration and not licence to keep deriving. Q2's stranding hazard is REAL and survives the answer: it is now a design consequence to handle the way the reference does (a rock on every spawn, which `World::wake`'s re-grant already ships), not a reason to differ.** **LANDED 2026-08-15/16 WITH RESIDUALS — the dated rows above hold all four answers** (Q1/Q2 taken from the reference; Q3/Q4 adopted as recommended, *"just pick the best and go"*). Steps 1–6 shipped as designed: `cond: u16` last on `ItemStack`, `PROTO_VER` 42 with 93 goldens, `SAVE_FORMAT` 3 / `WORLD_SAVE_FORMAT` 7, all eight gates in and each proven red, plus the gather refusal (`EV_GATHER_REFUSED`, §0kit item 2) in the same wire window. Residuals: ~~no durability bar in any panel~~ — **drawn 2026-08-17** in all four cells that hold a stack (§open "durability pip v0"); the repair bench stays v1 exactly as Q3 says. The paragraphs below are the design as it was argued and stand as history: **Q1 — recommend 100 points (10 000 hundredths), one value for every tier-1 tool.** Derived rather than chosen, from three sides that agree: at `SWING_INTERVAL_TICKS = 38` / `TICK_HZ = 30` a swing is 1.267 s, reference loss is 0.3/hit, so 100 points is 333 hits ≈ 33 nodes ≈ 7.0 min of continuous swinging ≈ **~18 min of real farming** — a session-shaped consumable, where 30 points re-crafts every five minutes (durability becomes the game) and 250 is met once a wipe (decoration); §3's repair table is **20% *of maximum*** per repair, which on a 100-point scale is exactly five repairs and 66⅔ hits of future life each, integers instead of a rounding argument; and `u16` hundredths tops out at 655 points, so a metal tier at 250 fits with 2.6× spare. Worth knowing before answering: **durability is not an economic lever in this economy, it is a friction lever** — a stone hatchet costs 200 wood + 100 stone and pays that back in under four swings, so at any plausible maximum the tool returns ≥25× its craft cost. The honest question is how often a player must stop and re-craft. **Q2 — recommend no, and this one is coupled to §0kit rather than to fidelity.** `DURABILITY.md` §4 reading (b): the Condition Loss column is inherited from the shared gather schema and is inert for an item whose condition is disabled, consistent with the same page's `Repairability: False` and its absent maximum. The sharp edge is ours: with the `hand` rows deleted 2026-08-15 the rock is the **only** path from naked to anything, `item.stone` comes off one node whose rows are the rock and the two pickaxes, and `grep 'item.stone\|item.rock' content/loot.toml content/mobs.toml` is empty — so a player holding a dead rock with under 10 stone has no gather verb, no craft and no exit but death, which makes suicide a stone dispenser and is a worse loop than the one §0die closed. "No" costs nothing and is enforced rather than remembered: `item.rock` declares no `condition_max` and validation rule V3 refuses any `condition_loss` row naming it. Same for `item.torch`. **Q3 — recommend nowhere in v0**: re-craft *is* the repair, exactly as the reference's own rock is, and §4 is the proof that an unrepairable tool is a complete design. For v1 recommend the **workbench we already ship** (`Station::Workbench1..3`, `content/deployables.toml`) over a new deployable — §1.3 asks only that repair be a place you go with a blueprint check on the player, and both are state we hold. Do **not** take §3's 0.20 repair ratio into `content/`: it is DISPUTED against every guide's 0.50 and §3 says so. **Q4 — recommend "stays, and stops being a tool."** §9.3 lists only three questions; this is the fourth and v0 cannot ship without it, because a 0-condition tool that still gathers is no durability at all. One guard in `gather::swing` reads `held` as `NO_ITEM` when the held stack's `cond == 0`, so `NodeDef::yield_for` falls back to the hand row — no new branch, no new event, no new refusal, and §0kit makes it free: with the hand rows at 0, "falls back to the hand row" already *is* "gathers nothing". **The v0 cut, ordered; steps 1–5 are one commit because they cross the wire together.** (1) `ItemStack` gains `cond: u16` last, so `item` and `count` keep their bit positions — **30** production struct literals against **327** test ones, and the test half is a `sed` where every miss is a build error. That failure mode is the whole argument for the field over `DURABILITY.md` §9.2's side table, and it is *not* the argument §9.2 gives: §9.2 cites our item-move trap, which `plan_move`/`resolve` taking `ItemStack` by value and every write funnelling through `World::cont_slot`/`set_cont_slot` already forecloses. A missed site under the field is a compile error; a missed site under a side table is a wrong condition, green on the goldens, green on replay, green on clippy — the three-green-gates shape. A side table also touches ~24 production mutation sites (not fewer) and has no honest cap: its key space is 100×30 + 256×12 + 256×30 + 64×30 = 15 672. (2) **Box `Backpacks::entries` and `BoxStore::entries`/`ovens` in the same commit** (`backpack.rs:193`, `deploy.rs:745`) — both are stack-built and `Backpacks` goes 37 888 → 53 248 B, past the line that turned `test_parity_wasm` into an out-of-bounds read with every native test green in 2026-08-08. `WorldConts` is already boxed and is the model. (3) Content: `condition_max` on `Item` (absent ⇒ 0 ⇒ never wears and can never be repaired, which is §4's rock-and-torch rule expressed as a schema default rather than a rule anyone remembers) and `condition_loss` on `Gatherable`, a `BTreeMap` keyed per (tool, node) exactly as `yield_per_hit` is — **the table IS the wrong-tool predicate, there is no predicate to port**. Seven validation rules; two carry the slice. **V7** (`condition_max > 0` ⇒ `stack = 1`) is what guarantees no merge path is ever asked to reconcile two conditions, which is what lets `plan_move` and `inv_add` keep their arithmetic unchanged — **land it first**. **V4** (a non-`hand` tool carrying condition must have a `condition_loss` row on every node it pays on) is a *set* check, the class this repo keeps getting bitten by. `canon.rs` must hash both fields, or a WAL replays under a wear table it was not played under. `bake_spawn_kit` must set the kit stack's `cond = condition_max_of(item)`, **not 0** — inert while the kit is a rock and a torch, and a one-line trap with a two-week fuse the day a hatchet goes back in it. (4) Wear in `gather::swing` after the payout, `saturating_sub`, **on a landed node hit only — never on `Swing::Free`, never on `smash`**, because a whiff that wore would put a new `SUB_INV` message on the wire on every swing of every player forever. `inventory::resolve`'s `ItemStack { item, count: dst.count + count }` splice drops `cond` and **compiles either way** — the item-move trap's exact anatomy, and it gets its own gate. Four `state_hash` scratch buffers go `[0u8; 4]` → `[0u8; 6]`; no pinned digest constant exists, so nothing goldens the hash and native/wasm move together. (5) Wire: `InvSlot`, two encoders (`event.rs:874`, `:1842`), two decoders (`:2086`, `:2799`), `PROTO_VER` 41 → 42, all 92 `v41_*` fixtures renamed and regenerated. **No new `EV_*` is owed** — condition reaches the client on the existing `SUB_INV` diff, because `server/src/core.rs:2621` compares whole `ItemStack`s. Cost is +2 B per slot: `SUB_INV` 141 → 201 B at 30 slots, `SUB_CONT_SYNC` 21 → 27 B at 3 and 146 → 206 B at 30, worst case against `MAX_EVENT_MSG_BYTES = 320`, on the **reliable bidi stream** where no datagram clamp applies (snapshots carry no `ItemStack`). Free while regenerating: four orphan `v37_*.bin` fixtures and their unreachable `match` arms. (6) Persistence, **separate commit**: `PLAYER_SAVE_BYTES` 196 → 256, `SAVE_FORMAT` 2 → 3, `WORLD_SAVE_FORMAT` 6 → 7, `WORLD_SAVE_MAX_BYTES` 581 528 → 612 872. **The canonical-empty extension is not optional and nobody would think of it**: `persist.rs:380` and `worldsave.rs:619` refuse `count == 0 && item != 0` and must also refuse `count == 0 && cond != 0`, or a slot emptied by a path that forgot to zero `cond` hashes differently from the same empty slot the sim produced — a difference nothing can see, which those two comments already name as wall 5's failure mode. **Eight gates it earns, each with the defect it is proven against**: a moved tool keeps its condition (revert the `resolve` splice — reads 0, and it compiles); a swing wears by the node's own rate *and does not wear on a node whose table omits the tool* (make `wear_for` per-tool instead of per-(tool,node)); a tool at zero gathers at the hand rate (delete the `cond == 0` guard); wear is in `state_hash`, with the stronger half that two worlds differing only in one tool's condition must hash differently (omit the bytes from any of the four loops); V7 and V4 as content refusals; a saved tool comes back worn and an empty slot may not carry condition; and the container sync carries condition (zero the `cond` write in the encoder — the decode reads 0 while `test_protocol_golden` stays green, because the golden was regenerated from the same broken encoder). **Must NOT move, and if one does the slice is wrong**: `test_terrain_golden`, `test_alloc_zero`, `test_replay`, `event_roles.rs`'s count, `every_encoder_has_a_golden`, and `inventory_move.rs`'s `REFUSE_M_*` set. `ci/parity.mjs` vs `probe.rs` **must** change on both sides identically, so the *diff* stays empty — that is the gate that catches a float sneaking into the wear arithmetic. `ci/knob_registry.mjs` is not triggered and must not be: these are content numbers and belong in `content/*.toml`, never as Rust constants (wall 7). | `NOW.md` §0dur · `reference/DURABILITY.md` §3/§4/§5/§9 |
| sun arc v0 — how much compass bearing the sun sweeps between sunrise and sunset: `RIG_SUN_ARC` (`crates/client/src/render/rig.rs`) (client lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED, shipping as π rad (180°) and carrying its derivation at the definition.** Opened by the operator's playtest: *"the sun donest move"* — it did, up and down only, because `to_sun` read `RIG_SUN_AZIMUTH = 2.35` as a constant and its own doc argued the v0 case ("a sweeping azimuth would drag every shadow through a half-turn a cycle for no legibility gain"). The operator scored that from the player's side, so the bearing sweeps. **π is derived, not chosen**: `sun_elevation` puts the sun on the horizon at dawn, at 35° at noon and back on the horizon at dusk, symmetric about noon — which is the **equinoctial** path, and at the equinox the sun rises due east and sets due west at every latitude, a sweep of exactly π. The same curve's 35° peak fixes the matching latitude at 90° − 35° = 55°, the band `ART.md` §1's temperate pine-and-granite island already implies, so both halves of the sun's path agree about where this island is. **Measured on the shipped tree**: sunrise compass 135.4° (SE) → noon 225.4° (SW, unmoved) → sunset 315.4° (NW), 5.71° of shadow rotation per minute of daylight. **What a smaller value buys**, which is the actual choice: a shorter arc holds a shadow's direction more nearly constant and crowds sunrise and sunset toward the noon bearing; below ~1.58 rad (91°) both fall on the same side of the noon meridian and the sun reads as wobbling rather than crossing the sky. **Noon is the anchor and did not move** — `sun_azimuth(CAPTURE_DAY_FRAC) == RIG_SUN_AZIMUTH` bit-exact, `DayPin::capture()`'s tick 14350 reads `day_frac` of exactly 0.35, so every frame a visual judge has scored stays comparable and `ART.md` §1's 30–40° band is untouched (elevation never entered the change). **Two residuals the operator may want to speak separately.** The noon bearing 2.35 rad is **southwest**, not due south, so the shipped path is SE → SW → NW rather than the classic E → S → W; correcting it moves noon and retires every judged frame, so it is its own pass with a re-capture rather than a drive-by. And the cloud deck now turns one full revolution per cycle about the zenith (`sky::deck_rotation`, the coupled-set fix) — exact, gated as arithmetic, and a visible change nobody asked for; the physically right answer is advection plus a lit term evaluated at sample time rather than baked, which is a real slice. `DAY_TICKS` was deliberately not touched by the lane that built the sweep — and was **spoken separately the same day** (dated row above, 80 min), so the arc now spreads over 70 minutes of daylight rather than 31.5: **2.57° of shadow rotation per minute, not 5.71°**, which is the number the "what a smaller value buys" argument above should be read against. ⚠ For whoever writes the registry row: π is spelled `std::f32::consts::PI` in source, so the backtick `NAME = value` declaration form would be a promise `ci/knob_registry.mjs` enforces against a literal that is not there — prose about it is safe. | `NOW.md` §0sun · `crates/client/src/render/rig.rs` · `crates/client/tests/sun.rs` |
| dev spawn kit override — `dev_spawn_kit` in `shard.toml`, `"item.id:count, …"` (server lane, 2026-08-15) | **Unset, which means the content's own kit — a rock and a torch.** A new shard-local key in `dev_spawn`'s exact shape, opened because 2026-08-15 replaced the nine-entry `[[spawn_kit]]` (plan, hammer, stone hatchet, stone pickaxe, torch, 3 bandages, 900 wood, 500 stone, 100 metal frags) that existed only so the build and hammer verbs could be exercised at all — a naked spawn made the build wheel read `350 Wood (0)`. It carries **no invented gameplay number**: the counts are typed by whoever sets it, and `shard.toml.example` reproduces the retired kit verbatim as a commented line that two gates keep parseable and resolvable against shipped content. It is deliberately not a boolean naming a second kit baked into the binary — that would put balance numbers in `crates/` (wall 7). **It inherits `dev_spawn`'s caveat exactly and the doc comment says so**: nothing records it, so a shard run with it set is not reproducible from seed + content hash alone. Never on a public shard. ⚠ **One consequence worth speaking rather than discovering**: `World::wake` re-grants the kit on every respawn as of the same day, so whatever is typed here is minted **per death**, not once per character — the documented example re-mints 900 wood, 500 stone and 100 metal frags every time a tester dies. Deliberate on a dev box and stated at both call sites; it is the reason not to reach for this key on anything a stranger can join. | `crates/server/src/config.rs` · `shard.toml.example` · `NOW.md` §0kit |
| a rockless living player is stranded until death — spoken call, not a number (content lane, 2026-08-15) | **Shipping as-is: the respawn re-grant is the only route back, which is the reference's own shape.** Verified against the shipped tables rather than inferred: `item.stone` comes only off `gather.stone_node`, whose yield rows after 2026-08-15 are the rock and the two pickaxes; `recipe.rock` costs 10 stone; and `grep 'item.stone\|item.rock' content/loot.toml content/mobs.toml` returns **empty** — no barrel, crate or mob pays either. So a living player who moves their rock into a box, with fewer than 10 stone, has no gather verb and no exit but dying. The reference has the same shape, which is why it hands you a rock on every spawn, so this is probably right — but it is a consequence of the kit change that nobody spoke, and it is what makes Q2 of "item durability v0" (does the rock wear) a gameplay question rather than a fidelity one. **Both options if the operator wants it closed are content, not code**: a `item.stone` row on a barrel loot table, or a rock that cannot be moved out of the belt. The grep that proves it is written into `content/balance.toml`'s kit comment. No partial hand yield was invented to paper over it. | `content/balance.toml` · `NOW.md` §0kit |
| screenshot key v0 — the key is `F12`, the late check is `VERIFY_FRAMES = 20` frames, `PENDING_CAP = 8` shots may await that check, and `PER_STAMP = 64` names exist per second (client lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED. None of the four is spoken and none is measured; they are documented defaults, which is what this section is for.** Asked for because the operator could not screenshot on Linux, and the mechanism is why the fix is in the game rather than on the desktop: X11 grabbers read the root window and are blind under Wayland, whose answer is a portal a bare WM or a container need not have, and a fullscreen surface on direct scanout can hand a compositor a black frame — a working key that captures nothing. A client reading its own swapchain is blind to every branch of that, and this client already does it six times per capture run. **`F12` is convention, not derivation** (and this client binds no other function key at all, so it collides with nothing). **`VERIFY_FRAMES = 20` is borrowed rather than chosen** — it is `capture::TAIL_FRAMES`, the only readback budget anything here has ever measured, and it is a frame count for that file's reason: the readback is asynchronous in frames, and elapsed milliseconds measure the box. **`PENDING_CAP = 8` is wall 4's cap on the one path a player drives as fast as they can press**, and is effectively unreachable — `just_pressed` does not repeat, so the list drains faster than a hand fills it. **`PER_STAMP = 64` bounds the collision walk** so a held key cannot become an unbounded `stat` loop on the frame it was pressed. The shape that is NOT a knob and should outlive the numbers: **the press is announced at once and only a failure is announced again**, because `save_to_disk` logs an IO error and returns with no path back to the caller — a toast written only after a successful `stat` would lag the keypress, and one written and never re-checked would be the trap list's worst class, a pass it did not earn. | `crates/client/src/shot.rs` · `crates/client/src/render/shot.rs` · `crates/client/tests/shot.rs` · `render/capture.rs` (`TAIL_FRAMES`) |
