| ground identity separation v0 — two of the four ground identities may not be the same paint: `SHARE_MIN = 0.05` (an identity's share of the island's land, below which it is not part of the rule) · `TWIN_RATIO_MIN = 1.25` (Rec.601 sRGB luma ratio a qualifying pair must clear) (client lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED. Neither number is `ART.md`'s, and the reason is that §3 has no rule about *pairs* — it has a table of four surfaces and says one thing about one pair (granite is "roughly 2×" turf). The defect this gate holds needed a rule about pairs.** `pass-20260815-042118-10-visual.md` gap 1 measured the delivered ground at hue 33–37° and luma 96–113 at every sample of six frames, ~0.4% of pixels reading as granite where `ART.md` §0 records 8.9% of the land within 300 m of the capture spawn carrying it. **The cause was not the classifier**: a probe over 34,806 land samples at that spawn found `terrain::splat_from` delivers near-pure identities (max weight p50 = 1.000, 92.2% of samples above 0.8) and reproduces §0's granite share to the digit (8.89%). The cause was that **forest litter and granite were 1.0° apart in hue, 0.5 points in saturation and 1.059× in value — 6.7 luma out of 255 — while owning 89.4% of the land inside that radius.** Four identities, three paints. Every existing per-identity gate passed throughout, and so did `the_island_is_more_than_one_surface`, because the island's *hue* spread is genuinely fine: grass is far from the warm three. Nothing could see a pair. **`TWIN_RATIO_MIN = 1.25` is read off §3's own table rather than chosen**: the tightest pair in it that the document names as two different substances is beach sand (117 luma) and granite (147), at 1.256×. §3's dirt-path/granite pair is 1.058× and is deliberately **not** the anchor — a path is a ribbon, and the identity that borrowed its row is a third of the world. **`SHARE_MIN = 0.05` is the one genuinely arbitrary number, and it is arbitrary in a way that can be stated**: the four shares are sand 1.1%, grass 51.8%, litter 37.9%, granite 9.2%, so **any cut between 1.1% and 9.2% selects the same three identities**. The gate asserts it selects exactly three pairs, so a future `GROUND_MIX` that moved the shares under it goes red rather than quietly testing nothing — that failure (a wall going green because its cases were deleted) happened in this repo on 2026-08-15 and is the reason the count is asserted. Sand is excluded on its share and because `wetted` darkens the band it lives in anyway. **What the operator is actually being asked**: whether a pair rule belongs in `ART.md` §3 at all, or whether the honest fix is to *measure* a forest-floor row off the reference set — §3 has no litter row, which is why litter's value is the one of the four that is derived from the held mean rather than read from the document. A measured row would retire `SHARE_MIN` entirely. | `ART.md` §3, §8 · `crates/client/tests/ground_identity.rs` `granite_stands_clear_of_the_ground_it_shares` · `crates/client/src/render/terrain_mesh.rs` `GROUND_ALBEDO` · `findings/pass-20260815-042118-10-visual.md` gap 1 · `NOW.md` §0gp |
| standing litter v0 — the forest-litter channel grows something instead of only dropping something: `FROND_H = 0.19` (metres, at scale 1) · `FRONDS_PER_CLUMP = 3` · `FROND_TIP_GAIN = 1.45` (multiple of the root albedo) (client lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED. Three numbers, and the reason to read this row is the mismatch that produced them.** `sim-core`'s density law names two of the four ground identities as living — `clutter_richness_at`'s doc says *"Grass (channel 1) and forest litter (channel 2) are the ground identities that grow things; sand and rock do not thicken"*, and the code is `let grow = w[1] + w[2]`. The client then drew every element that law thickened with `chip()` at 16 × 2.2 × 3 cm: aspect ratio 7.3, the flattest mesh in the file, the same builder sand and rock get. **One side of the seam said understory and the other said gravel**, and it is not abstract — `NOW.md` §0gp measured the capture camera standing on 93 % litter with grass and granite at exactly zero within 60 m, so on the frames the visual judge scores, essentially every clutter element was a 2.2 cm sliver. The judge read it back as "`4-near` is entirely close ground with flat twig decals and not one 3D clutter mesh". A litter element now draws a **clump**: the fallen stick it always had, unchanged and emitted first, plus `FRONDS_PER_CLUMP` standing stalks — the same relationship a tuft already has to one grass element (seven blades from one placement), and at 2.44 elements per m² one element covers 0.4 m², which is a clump-sized patch of floor. **`FROND_H` is not derived from `ART.md` and this row is where that is admitted.** §1's 20–40 cm band is quoted about GRASS and §3 has no litter row at all, so there is no measured height to take; 0.19 m is standing debris under a canopy — dead stalks, bracken, a frond — deliberately shorter than `TUFT_H = 0.34`, and the gate holds it in a band relative to that one measured neighbour rather than pretending to an absolute. **`FROND_TIP_GAIN` is a multiplier and not a colour, which is the load-bearing part.** The stalk's root colour is `GROUND_ALBEDO[2]`, the island's own litter identity, so a stalk is the colour of the ground it grew out of and the two cannot drift. That seam was open: every other clutter colour here is a hex authored beside the ground rather than from it (`PEBBLE_C`, `TWIG_C`, `SHARD_C`, the tuft ramp), so a shard rolled off the rock channel is painted `0x7d7a73` while the rock under it is `GROUND_ALBEDO[3]`, and nothing in the tree measures the gap — `terrain.rs`'s "THE MIX IS THE SPLAT" argument covers which kind is drawn where and does not reach what colour it is painted. **Open for the operator:** all three numbers, none of which anyone has seen rendered — this pass could not capture a frame, and what it gates is the correspondence (a channel the sim calls growing draws standing geometry, one it calls inert does not), never that the result looks right. Also open: whether the other three kinds should take their colour from `GROUND_ALBEDO` the same way, which would close the rest of that seam and is one row per kind. | `NOW.md` §0gp · `findings/pass-20260815-042118-06-visual.md` gap 1 · `ART.md` rules 1–4 · client `render/clutter.rs` · `crates/client/tests/cover.rs` |
| clutter contact v0 — the near-ground chip is a volume pressed into the ground, not a plate standing on it: `CHIP_VOLUME_BLEND = 0.55` · `CHIP_SINK = 0.30` (fraction of the chip's own height) (client lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED. Two numbers, and the reason to read this row is the third thing in it, which is a correction rather than a knob.** The defect is `findings/pass-20260815-042118-03-visual.md` gap 2 and its blind reader: "stray flat blue triangles poking through it — an engine test surface", plus the ranked ask to delete or texture "the flat-shaded pebble primitives". **Blue was the diagnosis and nobody had read it as one.** `chip()` built pebble, twig and shard with `volume_center: None`, so all twelve vertices of a 5 cm stone carried one of exactly four facet directions; a facet turned away from a 35° sun is lit almost entirely by `fill.rs`'s sky half (0.80, 0.85, 0.95 sRGB), so a grey stone comes back blue-grey and does it in four hard steps. `CHIP_VOLUME_BLEND` pulls each vertex normal toward the chip's own centroid — the idiom already in this file for needles and blades — and is **partial on purpose**: 0.0 is the facet set, 1.0 erases the chip's angularity into a sphere's normal field and `ART.md` rule 1 wants that near-field grain kept. `CHIP_SINK` answers the other half of the same gap, `ART.md` rule 2's "a clean intersection edge reads as a decal": the base ring was coplanar with the ground, which IS that edge by construction, so it is pushed under and only the taper crosses. It is a **fraction of the chip's height rather than metres**, so it scales with the element and never buries a small one — the gate asserts more of every chip is drawn than hidden across the 0.75–1.25 scale band. **It is deliberately geometry and not an occlusion term.** The routed skill (`threejs-screen-space-ambient-occlusion` §9) is explicit that a visibility scalar belongs to the reconstructed indirect slot only and that multiplying scene colour by it makes "sunlit surfaces become gray" — SSAO already owns that slot at `rig.rs:223`, and a vertex-colour AO would have bought "grounded" at the price of "washed out" one pass later. **The correction, which outlives both numbers:** `clutter.rs` justified forcing every blade normal fully vertical with the claim that "a blade's two triangles wind opposite ways, so one of them took the sun and the other went black". They do not — `crates/client/tests/contact.rs` computes both facets over a 128-case yaw × lean sweep and holds them within dot 0.99. The real blackener is the tile material's `double_sided` flip (Bevy negates the shading normal on a back-facing fragment, and seven blades at seven yaws put half of them there), and the false reason concealed the cost: **+Y is the ground's own normal, so a blade takes the same sun cosine and the same `fill_at` sample as the dirt it stands in**, and albedo is all that separates grass from ground. That is the judge's "reads as paint" as arithmetic. **Open for the operator:** the 0.55 and the 0.30, neither of which anyone has seen rendered — this pass could not capture a frame; and the blade fix itself, which is a per-vertex root-to-tip normal ramp needing `Soup::tri` to take a blend *function*, and which must not land blind (`NOW.md` §0gc). | `NOW.md` §0gc · `findings/pass-20260815-042118-03-visual.md` gap 2 · `ART.md` rules 1–2 · client `render/clutter.rs` · `crates/client/tests/contact.rs` |
| hemisphere sky fill — the fill is a function of the normal: `fill_at(n) = mix(bounce, sky, 0.5 + 0.5·n·Y)` · `SKY_FILL_LUX = AMBIENT_DAYLIGHT × 1.7` (carried across) · `FILL_FACE = 32` (client lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED, and the honest summary is that it registers a MODEL rather than a magnitude — every number in it is derived from something already in the tree, which is why it could land at all.** The defect is `bevy_pbr`'s and it is structural: `pbr_ambient.wgsl`'s `ambient_light()` takes a `world_normal` parameter and **never reads it**, so an `AmbientLight` delivers identical irradiance to the underside of a boulder and to open ground facing the sky. No brightness value fixes that; the term is direction-free by construction. `ART.md` §4 asks for the opposite in as many words — "**Sky fill**: hemisphere, sky half cool blue, earth half warm — this is what keeps rule 3's 0.30 floor reachable and what makes shadowed grass go cool" — and `rig.rs` has carried the debt in a comment since the Bevy port ("the down-facing-prop-face half of that split is owed to a later slice"). **The mechanism is that no custom material was needed, and `RENDER.md` twice said one was.** `environment_map.wgsl` samples its diffuse cubemap **by the world normal** (`var irradiance_sample_dir = N;`), so a cubemap holding `fill_at(n)` *is* a hemisphere light — exactly, not approximately. That matters because `StandardMaterial` is `#[bindless]` in 0.18 (a custom ground material is a slice, not a knob) and because the gate box renders on a CPU rasterizer where a compute pipeline is a risk worth not taking. It also lands on the right side of §4's occlusion law for free: `pbr_functions.wgsl` accumulates `environment_light.diffuse * diffuse_occlusion`, and the SSAO the rig already carried is folded into that by `min()` — indirect-only and occlusion-weighted without writing either. **Nothing here is a chosen number, and that is the safety argument for landing a lighting change with no frame in front of anyone.** The sky half is the shipped uniform term carried across *unchanged*, so an up-facing surface in the open receives exactly what it did before and the frame's level, exposure and highlight placement cannot have moved — only the *distribution over normal direction* changed. The earth half is the island's own albedo under the island's own irradiance (`DIRECT_SUNLIGHT·sin(RIG_SUN_ELEVATION) + SKY_FILL_LUX`). It comes out **warm on its own** — litter is 38 % of the land and hue 38° — so §4's "earth half warm" is a derivation rather than a hand-picked colour. ⚠ **`GROUND_MIX` corrected 2026-08-15 and this row's magnitudes with it.** It was `[0.008, 0.619, 0.373, 0.0]`, lifted from "over 39,521 land samples at seed 20260731" — and that window was `-1024..1024` on a world `terrain::continent` centres at (1024, 1024), i.e. the quadrant `CLAUDE.md`'s trap list retracted for the relief sweep on 2026-08-14 (`0..1024` alone returns the identical 39,521; the negative half is sea). Whole-island, 157,198 land samples: `GROUND_MIX = [0.011284, 0.518242, 0.378859, 0.091615]` — granite is **9.2 %** of the island, not 0.0000, and `relief.rs` (10.0 % legible rock) and `seed_scan --at 1155,140,300` (8.9 %) both already said so. Gate: `crates/client/tests/ground_mix.rs`, 4 of 5 red on the old constant. Measured result, corrected: sky half 11,689 lux (**unchanged** — the sky half was carried across and this correction does not touch it), earth half 7,036 → **8,028** (+14.1 %), **ratio 1.661 → 1.456**; `peak_lux()` stays 15,130 (the sky's blue channel), so `EnvironmentMapLight::intensity` and every up-facing surface are untouched and the change is confined to down-facing faces, which is the same safety argument the original row made. **What it costs elsewhere:** `GROUND_ALBEDO`'s granite was authored free *because* this said its weight was zero, and the area-weighted mean linear luma that re-placement was pinned to (0.09390) is **14.4 % low** against the island's own 0.10746. Those four albedos did **not** move here — brightness is the coupled owner's and §3's hue/saturation bands bind that edit too. **What it does NOT claim, stated because the visual judge will measure it next**: the p10 did not move and was not expected to. Cast shadow on *open* ground is an up-facing surface and no hemisphere darkens it; the darks this restores are on down-facing faces. The remaining half of that gap is the **transfer**, and it is deliberately not in this row — `rig.rs` derived its floor as an illuminance ratio (`fill = 0.30 × sun_on_flat`, giving a delivered linear 0.229) while `ART.md` rule 3 is a *pixel* ratio the judge measures at 0.725, and the two readings point opposite ways. That one needs eyes on a frame and one sequential owner (`CLAUDE.md`'s 60→66 datum). **Open for the operator:** the `0.5 + 0.5·n·Y` blend itself (analytic for two uniform halves, but a real sky is not uniform); whether the sky half should now be **re-chromed cooler** — it was warmed to fix a NAVY shaded face that was a *down*-facing face being fed blue sky, and that cause is now gone, which makes §3's "grass shadows go COOL, hue 70° → 172°" reachable; and whether the bounce should track the hour rather than being baked at the noon register. | `ART.md` §4 · `findings/pass-20260815-042118-01-visual.md` gap 2 · client `render/fill.rs` `render/rig.rs` `render/sky.rs` (`cube_dir`) · `crates/client/tests/fill.rs` · `RENDER.md` §0 |
| triangles v0 — the grid change: four `LOC_TRI_*` half-cells + two `LOC_DIAG_*` slots · three shapes at §7b.3's ratios (0.5 / 0.25 / 0.5) · `MAX_PIECE_DEFS = 48` · wire v40 (systems lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED — `reference/BUILDING.md` §9.14, the one item the research priced as a grid change, costed as one: the address space itself grew.** A cell body now holds a full plane, OR up to two **half-cell triangles across one diagonal** (NW+SE or NE+SW), and each diagonal is an edge slot only the WALL fits — so honeycombed corners, diagonal enclosure and octagonal towers exist here the way round towers exist there. **The deliberate deviation, stated in full: ours is the right half-cell, not their equilateral.** An equilateral cannot address into a square lattice at all — their catalogue runs on a free socket graph, which is why their privilege needed OBB physics and a building id — and the half-cell keeps every wall this repo is built on: cell addressing, O(1) occupancy, bounded flood fills, the claim BFS, `collapse_from`'s ≤7-address `dependents`. `BALANCE.md` §6.2's one admissible ground to differ is a **mechanism** difference, and the grid is the mechanism; the *prices* are still theirs digit for digit (§7b.3: tri foundation and tri roof 0.5, tri floor 0.25 — including tri roof NOT halving, their own refusal), and the area ratio lands better than theirs (our half is exactly the ½ the price says; their equilateral is 0.43). **What holds it up**: a triangle stands on either of its two side edges or on the **diagonal wall under its own hypotenuse**; the diagonal stands on the body it crosses or the diagonal below; straight edges accept a touching half as ground the way they accept a plane (`bears_edge`); `collapse_matches_a_naive_fixed_point` now grows fixtures WITH triangles and diagonals, so the supported↔dependents inverse is gated, not asserted. **Collision**: a tri plane is ground over its own half and air over the other; a diagonal wall blocks bodies and shots along its line (the rotated-frame `diag_meet`, wall-1 floats only); both walks gained the per-cell diagonal pass. **Wire v40**: `SHAPE_BITS` 3→4 (the saturation catalogue v1's pin priced), `BUILD_LOC_BITS` 2→4, and every loc read now range-checks against the STORE it addresses (`loc_max`) — pieces reach the diagonals, deployables never do, and a deployable still requires a FULL plane, so a tri cell refuses furniture (stated, not a gap). **Render**: the four halves and both diagonals are ONE prism and one turned wall slab under root quarter/eighth-turns (`base_transform`), the ghost previews the same prism from the same `part_mesh`, and no new pipeline specializes (same vertex layout — the prewarm trap stays shut). **Deliberately absent**: diagonal doorways/windows/frames (each wants its insert story), stairs on triangles (risers need the full plane), tri-half hard/soft render identity, and any equilateral ambition — if the operator ever wants their 60° look, that is a socket-graph rewrite and this row is where its price is written. **Open for the operator**: whether the wall on a diagonal should cost more than a straight wall (it is ~1.41× the length; today the socket prices it, which is §7b.3's own half-wall logic), and the wheel at 11 wedges (usable, snugger than 8). | `NOW.md` §0ac · `reference/BUILDING.md` §9.14, §7b.1/7b.3 · sim-core `build.rs` `collide.rs` `claim.rs` `combat.rs` `limits.rs` · `content/building.toml` `CONTENT.md` §1 · content `schema.rs` `bake.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` `goldens.rs` · client `ui/{build,place,icons}.rs` `render/{structures,ghost}.rs` `render/panels/ring.rs` · `ci/bake_icons.py` |
| hard/soft v0 — every edge piece has a soft face: `PieceRec::facing` · `HARD_SIDE_STRUCTURE = 1` · wire v39 · save format 6 (systems lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED — `reference/BUILDING.md` §7b.5 taken as a mechanism, on the operator's "work on all of it": one rule that turns placement *orientation* into skill, and the reason a base can be weaker than its bill of materials.** The soft side **faces the placer** — you build from inside, so a correctly built base is hard-side-out with no extra verb — and a melee swing on the hard face of a wall, doorway, window or frame lands `HARD_SIDE_STRUCTURE = 1` whatever the tool, where the soft face pays the tool's whole structure column. The 1 is the reference's own rule as a raider meets it (their hard side is not a farm), and it is the invented number here; the facing itself is geometry. **What deliberately does not have sides**: planes and risers (a floor's sides are above/below, and no swing here carries a vertical direction yet — stated, not smuggled), deployables (a door is breached, not oriented), and **explosives** (`charge.rs` untouched: a blast is a point, not a stance, which matches the reference and keeps the satchel the answer to a hard-side base). **No rotate verb**, on purpose: inside the demolish grace window a wrong facing is a free re-place, and a rotate outside it would re-aim a base mid-raid; if wanted later it is one command and one §open row. **Legibility is one shared comparison**: `build::soft_side` prices the swing in `combat::raid` AND labels the HUD's centre prompt (SOFT SIDE / HARD SIDE via `ui::structure::Target::side`), so the label cannot disagree with the bill. **Wire v39** (the facing bit on both piece messages — the first layout change since v36) and **save format 6** (a byte between `row` and `hp`); the bit is in `state_hash`, and the `EV_PIECE_PLACED` broadcast now reads the RECORD off the store rather than rebuilding it from the event payload — the two-lanes drift (broadcast vs sync walk disagreeing about a facing) closed at the single source instead of patched in both. **Open for the operator**: the 1 (a percentage would scale with tools; flat is the reference's feel), whether bullets/arrows should ever respect sides (today arrows do no structure damage at all), and floor sides when a vertical attack direction exists. | `NOW.md` §0ac · sim-core `build.rs` `combat.rs` `world.rs` `worldsave.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` `goldens.rs` · server `core.rs` · client `ui/structure.rs` `render/hud.rs` `render/verbs.rs` · `sim-core/tests/{event_roles,worldsave}.rs` |
| catalogue v1 — the window and the wall frame: `WINDOW_SILL_M = 1.0` · `WINDOW_HEAD_M = 2.2` · `FRAME_RIM_M = 0.15` · `DOOR_HEAD_M = 2.1` · wire v38 (systems lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED — the two socket shapes of `reference/BUILDING.md` §9.13, landed on the operator's "work on all of it".** Prices needed no knob: §7b.3 already decided them (window 0.7 of the wall — the doorway's column digit for digit; frame 0.5 — the floor's), and hp is the material's alone, so `content/building.toml` grew 8 rows with no new number in them. **The four constants above are the invented half, all geometry.** The window's collision contract is `NOW.md` §0ac's stated answer — *blocks a body and not a bullet* — realised as an aperture: 1.2 m wide (the doorway's own posts, reused so the two openings stay one family) × 1.2 m tall (sill 1.0, header 2.2), which a 1.7 m capsule can never fit and an arrow threads. That forced the fix `ranged.rs`'s doc had owed since ranged v0: **an arrow now flies at its own radius** (`collide::shot_blocked`, a point at its altitude) instead of as a 1.7 m capsule, because "passes a bullet" is unbuildable while every bullet is shaped like a body. Two shapes got more honest with it — the **frame blocks exactly its drawn rim** (0.15 m jambs and top beam; a painted jamb a body ghosts through is the lie `RENDER.md` §8 forbids), and the **doorway's drawn lintel finally stops an arrow** (`DOOR_HEAD_M`; bodies never reached it, so movement is untouched). Drawn-vs-sim is gated the doorway's way: `window_solid_at`/`frame_solid_at` are the one definition and `client/tests/ghost.rs` samples the parts table against them in (t, y). **Wire v38**: no field moved; `SHAPE_BITS`' last two codes stopped decoding as `Malformed`, the domain pin moved 5 → 7 and the field is **saturated** — §9.14's triangles cannot land without widening it. **Deliberately absent**: the inserts themselves (bars, glass, shutters, the garage door — §7b.4's second purchase, each its own deployable pass) and any door mounting in either shape (`place_deploy` still requires `SHAPE_DOORWAY`). **Open for the operator**: the sill/head band, the rim, and whether the window's aperture should be narrower than the doorway's posts once inserts exist to size against it. | `NOW.md` §0ac · sim-core `build.rs` `collide.rs` `ranged.rs` `claim.rs` `combat.rs` · `content/building.toml` · content `schema.rs` `bake.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` `goldens.rs` · client `ui/{build,place,icons}.rs` `render/structures.rs` `render/panels/ring.rs` · `client/tests/ghost.rs` · `ci/bake_icons.py` |
| bench ladder v0 + tech tree v0 — WB2 = 500 frags + **125 obol** (hp 750, crafted at WB1) · WB3 = 1,000 frags + **300 obol** (hp 1000, crafted at WB2) · tier moves: gunpowder/revolver/pistol ammo/roadsign plate → workbench2, satchel → workbench3 · tree edges: revolver ← gunpowder, satchel ← gunpowder, roadsign ← medkit; arrow_metal, medkit, gunpowder roots · one cost column serves both verbs (systems lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED under the spoken era** (the era itself is Spoken, 2026-08-15 — these are the numbers filling it in). **The obol prices are derived, not picked, and the derivation is the row**: their pre-Oct-2025 ladder in *barrels* is what was taken — WB2 = 500 scrap ≈ 6.7× a T1 research (75), WB3 = 1,250 ≈ 16.7× — and ours restate the same multiples over our own T1 (20 obol): 125 and 300, ≈ 425 through the ladder against research prices of 20–75, so the benches dwarf any single blueprint exactly as theirs do. Their 20/100 **HQM** line is refused, not substituted — no HQM tier exists here (`RIPLIST.md` §3's class) — and the frags line carries their frags line as written. **The hp rise (500→750→1000) is ours**: no pre-Oct-2025 bench hp survives in the sources. **The tier moves are ours** in the small: our catalogue is 41 recipes against their hundreds, so "WB2 is the powder gate, WB3 is the raid gate" is the arc transcribed rather than a row-by-row match. **One cost column for table and tree** is the pre-2025 reading (the tree charges more only as the path total — satchel 115 via tree against 75 with a sample nothing drops); their current game prices the two separately, and if that is ever wanted it is a schema column, not a re-speak of this row. Moves with the OBOL faucet counts or the whole anchor breaks — same clause as "OBOL faucet v0", third file on the one ratio. **Open for the operator:** the 125/300; the hp rise; whether pistol ammo (ungated, WB2) should be craftable at WB1 with the revolver researched; and whether the tree should someday charge its own prices (the fragment-era split), which would want the era re-spoken first. | `content/{recipes,deployables,research}.toml` · `reference/RIPLIST.md` §2 row 7 · `reference/BALANCE.md` §6 · `NOW.md` §0tt |
| the capture probe's spawn — pin where the visual gate stands: `capture_spawn = <x,z>` in the capture shard's `shard.toml`, feeding the `dev_spawn` override that already exists (client lane, 2026-08-14) | **PROPOSED, no default, and it is a harness knob rather than a game one — nothing about a player's spawn changes.** The defect: `render/capture.rs`'s `VANTAGES` is six yaw/pitch pairs with **no translation**, so all six frames are shot from wherever the probe spawned, and `World::spawn_pos` draws a *bearing* from the player id the shard happens to hand out. Meanwhile the island is a two-population mosaic — `SPLAT_MOIST_BAND` is 0.08 wide across a moisture field spanning ~0.9, so 48.8% of land reads as grass alone and 34.7% as litter alone, 30.5° apart in hue. Measured over 16 bearings at 30 m, the near-band green-dominant share is **0.0%, 78.6%, 81.2%, 85.2%, 85.3%** — bimodal. **So the visual gate's most-cited input is a draw, redrawn every run**, and the judge drew the litter side three reports running and concluded the island is "one painted material" from one tile of it. This is the same class as the unpinned sun elevation the visual judge already carries as its own defect 17, and the two compound: a pass is currently scored at a random hour on a random one of two ground colours. `World::spawn_pos_n` already honours `dev_spawn` (`world.rs:1223`) and `NOW.md` §0p3 already carries the recipe for standing the camera anywhere, so the mechanism is built and what is missing is the *choice of place* — which is a taste call about what a representative frame of this island is, and therefore the operator's. Three candidates, none picked here: the shipped spawn of one fixed id (comparable, but keeps whichever population that id draws), a point chosen to straddle the grass/litter boundary (shows both, flatters neither), or two pinned points captured as separate vantage sets (doubles the frames). Gate and measurements: `crates/client/tests/ground_where_the_green_goes.rs`, `gates-loop/findings/note-20260814-where-the-green-goes.md`. |
| shard population v0 — a shard seats its own inhabitants: `population = 0` (off) · `MAX_POPULATION = MAX_PLAYERS - 1` · `DEFAULT_SHIFT_SECS = 300` · `RECONNECT_BACKOFF_MS = 2_000` (server lane, 2026-08-14) | **PROPOSED. The defect is the merge-gate judge's ranked gap 1 in THREE consecutive reports (`findings/pass-20260813-230343-{14,16,18}-judge.md`), and the third ranked it first *because* the raid work kept landing**: four passes took the raid from "reaches the wire" to "can afford to act" to "the verb is correct" to "the wire's own arrangement provably raids", and a human who joined the alpha shard met a silent island in all four. Every opponent in the tree lived inside a `#[test]` or needed an operator to start `bin/bots` by hand. **The fix is a config key, not a system**: `shard.toml`'s `population = N`, seated by `server::population::seat` after the bind. **Over the wire, deliberately, and that is the design call.** An inhabitant is not a second producer feeding `ShardCore::push_input` — it dials the shard's own bound port with the same `botclient::run_bot` a load test uses, through the full QUIC/WebTransport handshake as `Address::GUEST`, so the server cannot tell one from a player. A shortcut into the core would have been a *second implementation of the input path*, and the bugs the trap list actually names (a container refusal computed on values the client did not predict, the item-move verb that fails as a disconnect) all live in the half it would skip. **Resident, not a fleet, which is the whole difference from `bin/bots`**: that binary hands each bot a year and never looks again, so a bot whose connection dies silently shrinks the population and nothing notices. Here a slot is a *post* — bounded shift, `BotReport` absorbed into a gauge, re-manned until the shard's own shutdown flag, which is the same flag that stops the sim thread so a shard has exactly one stop. The shift is raced against that flag rather than run to completion: five minutes is longer than a `systemctl stop` should wait. **`DEFAULT_SHIFT_SECS = 300` is bounded on both ends by something real** — well over the 300-tick fuse (10 s) and over `RAID_CYCLE`, so a shift contains whole raids rather than clipping one, and short enough that a wedged post shows up in the report line within five minutes. `RECONNECT_BACKOFF_MS = 2_000` is not a latency number; it exists so a shard that cannot reach itself spins at 0.5 Hz instead of as fast as the reactor allows, and the success path never waits. **Two refusals at boot rather than a surprise later, both wall 4's shape.** The cap is `MAX_PLAYERS - 1` and is *refused, not clamped*: a shard whose every slot is a bot hands a human `REFUSE_FULL`, which is the silent island arriving as a locked door, and an operator who typed 200 must not read it as the number that is playing. And `population > 0` beside `require_auth = true` is refused with both keys named, because an inhabitant handshakes as a guest — an authenticating shard would refuse every one and the supervisor would re-dial its own front door forever, a reconnect storm a shard runs against itself. **`dial_addr` is the one non-obvious line**: a public shard binds `0.0.0.0`, which is a wildcard to listen on and not a host to connect to, so the bound address is repaired to loopback before a post dials it — without it the whole population fails every shift on the only config that is not a test's. **Zero is the shipping default and the row says why: this is scaffolding, not a feature.** A population is for a world that does not have enough players yet; an operator who has players does not want bots eating their slots. **Open for the operator:** the 300 s shift and the 2 s backoff; what N an alpha shard should actually run; whether the owner/attacker split should stay `index % 2` (today a post keeps its role across shifts, which is what lets a base outlive the raider shooting at it); and the one this pass deliberately did not touch — an inhabitant plays on the shard's shipped spawn kit and nothing else, so what it can afford is exactly what a player who joined beside it can afford, which is judge -18 §B.2's live half. | `NOW.md` §0pop · `findings/pass-20260813-230343-{14,16,18}-judge.md` gap 1 · server `population.rs` `config.rs` `bin/shard.rs` `bin/bots.rs` · `crates/server/tests/population.rs` · `shard.toml.example` |
| site guards v0 — the destination is contested: `GUARDS_PER_SITE = 2`, so `SITE_GUARDS` is 6 (derived: `(1 + WAYSTATIONS) * GUARDS_PER_SITE`, arithmetic over the knob rather than a knob, which is why the registry pins only the first) · a guard's leash is `SiteFootprint::scatter_m` · a guard's placement floor is `LAND_MIN_H` (systems lane, 2026-08-14) | **PROPOSED. The defect is the merge-gate judge's ranked gap 1 in two consecutive reports (`findings/pass-20260813-230343-{03,05}-judge.md`), and the second one ranked it first *because* the crate opened**: the road → barrel → cache → crate gradient was complete, gated and measured — `ci/haven_prize.mjs` proves the pad out-pays the road 33.07 to 13.50 — and there was no guard at the pad, so a player who knew where the crates were could clear them on a timer forever. A destination priced at time alone is a loot route, not a game. **A guard is not a species, and that is the design call.** It is a *wolf slot* with two facts changed: its home is inside an authored site, and its leash is that site's footprint instead of `MobDef::roam_cm`. Both are pure functions of the slot ordinal, which is the property `kind_of` already relies on (`protocol` v29 refused a `kind` field on `EntityState`; the client recomputes species off the slot inside the mob id). So **nothing crossed the wire, no golden moved, no save format changed and the client was not touched** — and that last one is not luck: every species match in the client is a `_ =>` fall-through to the pig (`render/mobs.rs::of`, `full_mps_of`, `voice_h_of`, `sound/voice.rs::cue_of`, `ui/death.rs`), so a third *kind* would have compiled green and drawn a guard as a pig. **`roam_cm` was the whole problem.** A wolf roams 90 m and the pad is 16 across: left on the species number a guard spends most of its life somewhere else, and the crates are unattended exactly when somebody walks up to them. Bound to `SiteFootprint::scatter_m` it holds the ground it was placed on — the same disc the clutter sweep already clears. It does **not** shorten a chase: `think` skips the leash entirely while roused, so a guard still charges at the wolf's full 30 m spook and still commits for `flee_ticks` past the last moment you were close; it simply comes home afterwards. **The placement floor is the one number that had to differ from the free roster's, and it is a measurement, not a loosened check.** `home_of` rejects the beach band (`BEACH_MAX_H`, sea + 2 m) as a *margin* — it keeps wanderers away from water nothing here can swim in. But the haven is scored for low relief beside the coast road, and on real seeds the pad lands inside that margin: **seed 1's pad centre is 1.28 m**, with the crates and the shelter standing on it regardless, and measured it had *no* admissible bearing at any radius while seed 42 had roughly half. Rejecting the apron for that refuses to guard exactly the destinations the solver likes best. `LAND_MIN_H` (sea + 0.6 m) is the question actually being asked — terrain.rs calls it "the land line: ground below this is water's edge, not somewhere a thing stands or a road runs", and scatter and the coast road already share it. The same substitution has to happen in `think`'s beached override or a guard standing at its own post reads as beached and steers home forever from home. **Guards stand on the swept apron**, the annulus between `SiteFootprint::swept_m` and `scatter_m`, drawn uniform by *area* (on r², or they crowd the inner edge) on the yaw LUT's bearings so no trigonometry enters wall 1's float list. Outer bound is the leash; inner bound is the floor the sweep clears for the crate ring and the shelter, so a guard cannot hatch inside a container it would be shoved out of every tick. An unfilled waystation keeps no guard — the slot stays unhomed, the same honest empty a slot that found no land gets. **Deliberately absent: the guard has no loot tier of its own.** The judge asked for one so that killing it is also a reason to go; it drops a wolf's meat and fat, and the reason to fight it is the crates behind it. The tier wants a third species with its own `drops` — which is the five client arms above — and it cannot be smuggled through `content/loot.toml`, because `validate` requires `hits > 0` ("swings to open") and a mob has no meaning for that field. **Open for the operator:** the 2 per site (6 of 16 wolves — 10 still hunt the open island); whether a waystation deserves the same count as the pad when it pays a quarter as much; and whether a guard should respawn on the wolf's 300 s like any other animal, which today it does, so a cleared pad is re-guarded five minutes later. | `NOW.md` §0wc · `findings/pass-20260813-230343-{03,05}-judge.md` gap 1 · sim-core `mob.rs` `terrain.rs` · `sim-core/tests/guard.rs` · `reference/ANIMALS.md` §9 |
| world containers v0 — the crate opens: `CONT_WORLD = 3` · `MAX_WORLD_CONTS = 64` · refill on the barrel's window · wire v37 · save format 5 (systems lane, 2026-08-14) | **PROPOSED. The defect first, and it is the merge-gate judge's ranked gap 1 (`findings/pass-20260813-230343-04-judge.md`): the entire destination gradient was built, gated, measured — and paid nobody.** The coast road, the bay barrels, two waystations, the haven pad's five crates, `LOOT_CRATE`/`LOOT_CACHE` baked and validated, `ci/haven_prize.mjs` proving 33.07 expected items in a crate against a barrel's 13.50 — and `loot.rs:33` said in the tree *"No verb opens one yet."* `roll_into` had exactly one caller and it was hardcoded to `LOOT_BARREL`. A player could walk the road, survive the wolf, reach the pad, and find scenery with a collision radius. **The fix is a fourth container kind, not a verb.** `CONT_KIND_BITS` has been 2 since v18 with three kinds in it; value 3 was forgeable and refused at both ends, and it is now the authored container. `plan_move` did not move one line, the panel needed one `container_title` arm, and the per-tick `ContSync` diff needed one resolve arm — a crate is a box that terrain placed. **Three things a fourth kind gets wrong that the first three could not, and each is a design call.** (1) **Its contents do not exist until somebody opens it**, so this is the only container verb that reaches the sim: `ActionMsg::Container` stays a pure subscription for a bag and a box (`server/core.rs`'s `continue` arm, whose comment said a future reader could not add a command without deleting the sentence — this is that reader) and mints `Command::OpenWorldCont` for kind 3, because the roll is state and must reach the WAL and the hash or a replay rebuilds a shard whose crates are all still full. (2) **The client names a cell and terrain owns what is there**: the handle is `gather::cell_key(cx, cz)` and `worldcont::open` re-derives the occupant through `terrain::scatter`, so a handle naming an empty meadow mints nothing. That scatter is paid **once, on the open**, never in `drip_client`'s per-tick re-resolve — ~60 `noise2` evaluations per open panel per tick is the cold-scatter spike `occupy.rs` exists to refuse. (3) **It refills**, being furniture that outlives being emptied, and the refill is **lazy, inside `open`** — no sweep, so the store costs the tick exactly nothing. The window is `gather::RESPAWN_{MIN,RANGE}_TICKS`, **reused and not re-spoken**: the "node/barrel respawn 20–45 min" row already names two things and a crate is the third, on its own noise channel so a cache and a barrel in one cell do not share a jitter draw. The timer arms on the *transition* to empty only, or a player camping a crate could hold its refill off forever by putting one item back and taking it out. **`MAX_WORLD_CONTS = 64` is a proposed default and its overflow policy is refuse**, unlike `MAX_BACKPACKS`' evict: an emptied record carries its refill deadline, so dropping one and re-minting it on the next open re-rolls the loot immediately — the cap would become a dupe rather than a limit. The island authors nine, so the branch is unreachable, and `the_cap_is_above_what_terrain_authors` is what keeps that true when a monument lands. **Wire v37, 86 goldens, three of them new** (wall 6): no field widened and no existing fixture's bytes moved — what changed is the *accepted value set* on three messages, which is a one-directional compatibility break and exactly what the version is for. Three new fixtures rather than one, for the reason `goldens::action_move_box` records in its own doc: when the third kind landed only its *open* was pinned, and the bytes meaning "take it out" went a whole version unchecked. `event.rs`'s domain pin moved 2 → 3 and the field is now **saturated** — there is no forgeable container kind left, so the forged-kind case in `container_action_round_trips…` was replaced by the assertion that says why it cannot be written. **Save format 5** carries the section, because a shard reboot that re-rolled every container on the island is the loot equivalent of the world save not existing; the decoder range-checks the cell, refuses a table no container rolls, refuses two records on one cell, and **refuses a stand position outside the cell it claims** — without that last one a hand-edited save moves the pad's crate to a player's feet and the walk, which is the entire price of the loot, is gone. **Deliberately absent:** no lock (a crate is anyone's, matching a bare box and an unlocked door); no guard (the judge's other gap — it wants a leash exemption and its own pass); no smash path (a barrel is hit until it bursts, a crate has a lid, and `gather::target_index` and `worldcont::table_of` split them with neither a subset of the other); and no client prediction, since the roll is the server's and the client has no seed to guess it with. **Open for the operator:** the 64; whether the refill should be the barrel's window at all or slower, since a crate is a destination and a barrel is roadside furniture; and whether an emptied crate should *say* so at a distance — today the only way to learn the pad is farmed out is to walk to it. | `NOW.md` §0wc · `findings/pass-20260813-230343-04-judge.md` gap 1 · sim-core `worldcont.rs` `inventory.rs` `world.rs` `worldsave.rs` `limits.rs` · `crates/protocol/src/{lib,event,goldens}.rs` · server `core.rs` · client `ui/interact.rs` `ui/slots.rs` `render/verbs.rs` · `sim-core/tests/worldcont.rs` |
| the 100-bot soak baseline — measured 2026-08-12 on this box (netcode lane) | **RAN, and it is the first time.** `NOW.md` §0q item 4 had asked for this since the queue was written: `NETCODE.md` §9's budgets had never met 100 real connections. One shard (release, `content/` armed, world file + player store + anomaly log all configured), 100 `bots` clients walking, held ~25 minutes; the shard itself was up ~34 and taken down with SIGTERM so the flush path ran too. **Every number is a COUNT, never a wall-clock assert** (`CLAUDE.md`'s clock rule), which is why the headline is a zero rather than a millisecond: **`dropped-ticks 0`** — across ~61,500 ticks the sim thread never fell the 8 ticks behind that `MAX_TICK_BACKLOG` abandons a backlog at, so the 33.3 ms budget held at full population without a single skipped tick. The rest, all zeros where zero is the good answer: **4,498,556 input datagrams accepted, 0 bad, 0 ring-dropped**; **2,250,001 snapshots sent, 0 ring skips, 0 send errors**; **17,503,297 AOI entities offered and 17,503,297 carried — 0 shed**, so the interest set never once exceeded what a datagram could hold, which is the `AOI_RANK_EXIT`/`MAX_SNAPSHOT_ENTITIES` band doing exactly what its row says at a population it had never seen; **35 world saves written, 0 failed**, and 100 clean joins → 100 clean leaves → 100 sleepers standing. **The snapshot cadence is confirmed from the outside**: 2,250,001 over 100 clients over ~1,500 s is 15.0/client/s, which is the 15 Hz the design states rather than a number anybody typed here. **What the soak does NOT have, stated rather than implied**: (a) **bytes**. Nothing counts them, so per-client bandwidth is inferable only as a ceiling — 15 datagrams/s × `DATAGRAM_BUDGET_BYTES` 1100 = ≤16.5 kB/s down per client, ≤1.65 MB/s at 100 — and a real byte counter is owed before that ceiling is quoted as a measurement. (b) **tick jitter as a distribution**; `dropped-ticks` is a threshold crossing, not a histogram, and a histogram needs a clock this box may not assert on. (c) **an hour**; §0q asked for one and this is 25 minutes of load, so the slow-leak class (a store that fills, a ring that creeps) is not yet excluded. (d) **contention** — 100 bots walk, they do not raid, so the caps that a raid storm drives are still gated one site at a time (`CLAUDE.md` wall 4's own ⚠). **The anomaly log's whole path was proven in the same run, and deliberately**: it sat at zero lines for 13,000 ticks — correct, because no watched counter moved — so 8 extra bots were connected against a full shard to make one move, and the file gained exactly `{"tick":13230,"kind":"counter","counter":"refused_full","delta":8}`. Counter → sweep → ring → log thread → file, end to end, with the right tick and the right delta. **An empty log is now evidence rather than an absence**, which is what `ALPHA.md` §6's "zero silent failures" needs it to be. | `NOW.md` §0q item 4 · `NETCODE.md` §9 · `ALPHA.md` §6 · `crates/server/src/bin/bots.rs` · `crates/server/src/anomaly.rs` |
| admin v0 + the anomaly log — `admin_wallets` · `anomaly_file` · `MAX_ADMINS = 16` · `MAX_BANS = 256` · `ANOMALY_RING_CAP = 256` (server lane, 2026-08-11) | **PROPOSED — and the transport decision is the interesting half: an admin command is a chat line, so no wire byte moved.** `ALPHA.md` §3 asked for a wallet-allowlisted admin lane; a `KIND_ADMIN` message would have spent a `PROTO_VER` on making the client responsible for a syntax the server must re-validate anyway. Instead a `/`-prefixed chat frame is intercepted before the fan-out, parsed pure in `protocol::admin`, and dispatched sim-side where the wallet, the world and the send closure all already are. Consequences worth having: **the client needed no build**, an admin on an older client is still an admin, and it is the reference's own shape reached from the other end — their console takes text, because a command is a sentence a person types. **Six verbs**: `/kick` `/ban` `/say` `/tp` `/give` `/save`, plus `/bug` for everybody (`ALPHA.md` §4). Targets are **ids, not wallets** — a 42-char address does not fit a 48-byte line beside a verb, and an admin reads an id off their own screen — while a **ban is remembered as the wallet**, because an id means nothing after a reconnect. **Permission is the SIWE-proved key** (`auth.rs`), never a client claim, and there is deliberately **no in-game promotion verb**: a list that grows at runtime is a shard where one compromised key is every key. Unset ⇒ nobody is an admin, `entitle.rs`'s one-build-two-populations posture applied to privilege; a bad entry **refuses the boot whole**, because a typo'd admin address is a privilege somebody thinks they have. `/tp` and `/give` are **`Command`s the wire cannot reach** (`Command::Evict`'s precedent), so an admin act lands in the stream a replay reads — which §3 asks for in the same breath as the lane; kick and ban cross an rtrb ring to the accept loop that owns the sockets, and a full ring **refuses out loud** rather than queueing a kick nobody remembers ordering. **No slash line is ever relayed to the room**, good or mistyped: `/kik 259` must not announce to a griefer that somebody is fumbling for the kick command. **The anomaly log is the alpha gate's own evidence** (§6: "zero silent failures"). Fifty-odd counters existed and *nothing read them* but a console line nobody keeps — a counter with no timeline is a silent failure with a number attached. One JSONL file, appended, on its own thread (`store_thread`'s shape). Its one idea: the sim thread **samples the watched counters each second and logs the deltas**, so all thirty-odd become logged events without touching one of their forty call sites, and tomorrow's counter is watched by adding a table row rather than by remembering a new call. The cost is stated — a delta says how many, never which client — so lines that need a subject (an admin act, a `/bug` with its tick and position) are written directly. Formatting is the log thread's; the record crossing the ring is integers and a fixed byte array, so wall 3 holds. Overflow **drops with a counter**: a diagnostic that could stall the sim thread would be worse than the bug it was recording. A configured log that cannot be opened **refuses the boot** rather than downgrading silently. **Open for the operator:** whether a ban should survive a restart (it does not — that wants its own file and its own format version), whether `/tp` should also be able to bring a player *to* the admin (refused at v0: no counter-play), and the log's rotation, which is an ops question and not a code one. | `protocol/admin.rs` · server `admin.rs` `anomaly.rs` `core.rs` `net.rs` `config.rs` `stats.rs` · sim-core `world.rs` · `tests/admin_wire.rs` · `shard.toml.example` · `ALPHA.md` §3/§4/§6 |
| day/night v0 — **80-minute cycle · 87.5 % day** · night ambient 60 lux (client+sim lane, 2026-08-11; cycle length SPOKEN 2026-08-15) | **The cycle length is no longer proposed — see the dated row above; the rest of this row still is. The design call is that time of day carries NO wire byte.** `ALPHA.md` §1 asked for a 45-minute cycle and `NETCODE.md` §3 files time of day on the G channel; both are satisfied without a field, because the snapshot header already carries the tick on every datagram and the client already keeps a smoothed estimate of it (`client-core/clock.rs` `server_est`). So the clock is `sim_core::world::day_frac(tick)` — one modulo, one divide, pure, in the crate both sides share — and two clients at one campfire agree because they agree about the tick. `DAY_TICKS = 144_000` is the **80 minutes spoken 2026-08-15** (was 81_000 / 45 min); `DAY_PORTION = 0.875` splits it 70 min day / 10 min night (was 0.70, ~31.5 / ~13.5); `DAY_PHASE_TICKS = 31_500` boots tick zero into **mid-morning**, which is not cosmetic — every `--capture` probe and the art rubric score daylight frames, and a fresh world starting on the dawn terminator would tank the visual bar for a reason that is a clock. **What it costs is a set-time admin verb**: with no offset field anywhere, moving the clock means moving the tick, which nothing may do. That is one wire field away if ever wanted, and this row is where to say so. **The renderer was the only reader until 2026-08-14** (`render/rig.rs` — the coupled-set owner, so the cycle lives there by `ART.md` rule 5): one `daylight(frac)` scalar drives the sun's illuminance, the ambient lerp toward the night floor, and the cloud deck's brightness, so the three cannot disagree about the hour. The sun arcs on a **fixed azimuth** (a sweeping bearing drags every shadow through a half-turn a cycle for no legibility gain), peaks at the authored `RIG_SUN_ELEVATION` exactly, and dips `NIGHT_DIP = 0.35` rad under the horizon at deep night with shadows off — a light at zero illuminance still schedules cascades. `NIGHT_AMBIENT_LUX = 60` is a **look, not a photometry claim**: real moonlight is ~0.25 lux and the exposure here never adapts (fixed ev100 14.2), so 60 is the number at which shapes read at arm's length and the ridge line is gone. **Nothing in the sim read the clock when this row was written, and that stopped being true on 2026-08-14** (`nocturnal senses`, above): a predator's notice radius is the hour's, so a wrong curve is a divergence surface now and not only a picture. The door is `world::is_night` — added so the boundary is one comparison rather than a literal each caller writes, and the bird gate below was moved onto it. The gameplay-adjacent consumer that landed *with* this row was audio: `render/audio.rs` gates the bird layer to daylight, repaying the refusal `sound/birds.rs`' header recorded ("the client has no day/night cycle, so dusk would be a cause we invented"). Crickets are the night companion and still owed. **The 45 minutes and the 70/30 split were the two things this row held open, and both were spoken on 2026-08-15** (dated row above): 80 minutes, 70 day + 10 night. ⚠ **What nobody re-derived is what that did to NIGHT** — 13.5 min became 10, so every consumer tuned against the old darkness lost a third of it: the nocturnal-senses radius above, `render/audio.rs`'s bird/cricket gate, and any window a player waits out after dark. None of those numbers moved with it. **Still open for the operator:** how dark night should actually be — that one wants somebody booting the game at midnight, because no arithmetic here can answer it (`CLAUDE.md`: the visual gate is a person), and it is now a shorter night to judge. | sim-core `limits.rs` `world.rs::day_frac` · client `render/rig.rs` `render/feed.rs` `render/audio.rs` · `tests/daynight.rs` · `ALPHA.md` §1 · `RENDER.md` §2 |
| mob attack v0 — the pig fights back: bite 15 · reach 2 m · every 2 s · brave above 50 % (systems lane, 2026-08-11) | **PROPOSED — the reference boar's mechanism, and the one number in it that is recollection says so.** The shape is theirs at tier 3 (search summaries, the tier rows 1c/1d landed at): a boar is aggressive when a player strays too close and breaks off once hurt past half health. Ours is exactly that, as one rule: proximity or a hit **rouses** the animal (the renamed `flee_until` — one timer, both directions), and courage decides which way the rousing points — at or above `brave_pct` of max hp it charges the nearest player at its fast gait, below it the identical state is the flight it always was. **The bite is phase-locked, not timed**: it lands on ticks where `tick % attack_ticks == slot % attack_ticks`, so the cooldown needs no per-mob timer, adds nothing to `state_hash`, and replays for free — `MOB_THINK_TICKS`' own trick. Bites park in a bounded per-tick buffer (`MAX_MOB_BITES_PER_TICK = 8`, derived: only thinking animals bite, ≈ 4/tick ceiling; overflow drops the bite — one merciful tick, never a queue) and `world::tick` applies them with `combat::strike`'s exact liturgy, minus the hitmarker a pig has no screen for. A kill is `DEATH_BY_MOB` — the fifth cause, the one wire v36's 2 → 3 bit widening was minted for — with the roster's **tagged** id as the killer, which is how the death screen says "a pig gored you" instead of printing a number. **The damage number is tier 4** (recollection: under the wolf's commonly-cited 20; ~10 bites on an AFK naked): re-verify against the page if egress opens, `RIPLIST.md` §1e's standing caveat. `attack = 0` keeps a pacifist species expressible and validate refuses every half-armed state. **Open for the operator:** the 15, and whether the charge should also cost hunger to hold (their predators eat; ours does not). | `content/mobs.toml` · sim-core `mob.rs` `world.rs` `limits.rs` · `tests/mob.rs` · client `ui/death.rs` · `reference/ANIMALS.md` §7/§9.5 |
| nocturnal senses — the predator's notice radius reads the clock: `night_spook_m` per species, wolf 30 m by day → **15 m by night**, pig 12 m unchanged (systems lane, 2026-08-14) | **PROPOSED, and it answers `predator v0`'s own "Open for the operator" question — *whether a wolf should hunt harder at night* — with the opposite of what that question assumed.** It hunts WORSE. The direction is sourced and it was the surprise of the pass: the reference game's 2024 predator rework **cut** wolf sight at night, on the stated grounds that the old animal "feels a bit like a landmine… that issue is of course amplified in the pitch black night" (tier 1, devblog, fetched clean; "their sight has been reduced at night" is tier 2, patch-note summaries). Night danger in this genre is a problem of what the **player** can see, and sharpening the animal on top of that is an ambush the player was never given the information to avoid. **No game in the survey publishes a day→night sense ratio above 1× for one creature** — Minecraft and Valheim make night dangerous by *spawn gating* instead, and The Long Dark states the negative outright (30 m sight, "not by time of day or weather"). The one published 2× is a night-only **variant**, which is a different row in `mobs.toml` and not this field. `reference/BALANCE.md` §6 therefore takes theirs: no mechanism case exists to differ, and effort is not one. **What is ours, and the honest list is one number**: the ratio. Nobody publishes one, so 0.5× is borrowed from the best-supported analogue in the survey — two games independently use it for the *crouched player* case (30 m → 15 m), which is the same "this creature senses less right now" shape. 30 → 15 rather than a third invented figure. The pig's two radii are **equal on purpose**, not stubbed: the reference reworked its predator and said nothing about its boar, and §6.2 refuses a difference with no mechanism — `the_clock_moves_the_hunter_and_not_the_prey` is what makes changing that cost a test. **The field is a second radius rather than a multiplier and validate takes no view on the direction**, so a nocturnal species stays a content row and not a code change (wall 7). **What it buys:** the first decision the clock makes — crossing open ground after dusk is a real tactic against the thing that hunts you, and the cost is that you cannot see either. Before this, `world::day_frac` had *no caller in the sim at all* and its own doc said so; `world::is_night` is the door and `mob::think` is the one line through it. **Open for the operator:** the 0.5× itself (a playtest number, like the 1-in-4 mix); and whether the follow-on is the sourced one — a night-only variant row, which is how the genre actually makes the dark cost something, rather than more tuning of this field. | `content/mobs.toml` · sim-core `world.rs` (`is_night`) `mob.rs` (`spook_at`) · `content/src/{schema,bake,validate,canon}.rs` · `sim-core/tests/mob.rs` · `content/tests/content.rs` · `server/tests/hunt.rs` · client `render/audio.rs` |
| wolf voice v0 — the roster speaks by species: `HOWL_PERIOD_S = 75.0` at 88 m · `GROWL_PERIOD_S = 2.5` at 14 m · snort unchanged (client lane, 2026-08-14) | **PROPOSED. The defect first: `render/audio.rs::pigs` played `Cue::Snort` for every drawn animal and never asked the species, so all sixteen wolves snorted.** The client already knew — `mob::kind_of` is pure and seedless and the wire deliberately carries no species field (protocol v29 refused one on cost) — the render half simply never asked. `sound/pig.rs` is now `sound/voice.rs`: `Voices::due` reads the slot itself and returns the *cue*, so a caller cannot pick a sound the clock was not timed for, and a third species is a change there and nowhere in `render/`. **The register switch is not a knob and that is the design's one nice property**: `voice::switch_m()` is `CUES[Growl].radius_m` read back out, so a growl can never be chosen and then culled by the mixer's falloff, and a howl can never play from an animal near enough to bite. **What the growl does NOT mean is aggression** — `Mob::roused_until` and `brave_pct` are sim-side only and no mob-def lane exists on the wire, so the register is *distance*, which is the honest half: a growl is a close-range threat call and a howl is a long-range contact call, whatever the animal is deciding. **The synthesis is source–filter off the literature, and the research changed the design in four places rather than confirming it**: the formants are *derived*, not picked (measured growl formant dispersion 671 ± 253 Hz → a 26 cm tract → F1–F4 at 340/1020/1700/2380, shared by both voices); the howl's contour is **asymmetric** because max F0 lands in the first quarter of 79 % of real howls and min F0 in the last quarter of 78 %, so it is a fast rise and a long terminal fall and not an arc; its waver is **1.6 Hz**, an order under the 5.5 Hz I first wrote (1–15 inflexions across a multi-second call); and the growl is **tonal-with-noise through those formants**, not filtered hiss, because formant spacing is the body-size cue dogs actually read and that is impossible if the source is aperiodic. Two defects in my own filter bank were found by *mutation-testing the gate*, not by listening: `b0 = 1 − r²` is not a normalization (a two-pole's gain rises as bandwidth narrows — F1 peaked at 20.6 against F4's 3.0, and my −6 dB/oct tilt then multiplied a spread already there, 48:1, so the doc claimed four formants over one), and once that was fixed the growl came out *brighter* than the howl because the source was a hard-edged pulse with no glottal rolloff and the noise was summed over the **output** instead of at the source. **The knobs that are ours, and none is measured:** the two cadences, the two radii, the two gains/priorities. `HOWL_PERIOD_S = 75 s` is **halved from the sourced ~165 s**, and the case is a mechanism we lack (§6.2's admissible kind): 165 s is the interval between *pack bouts*, and we have no bout — every wolf keeps its own clock, so the island would go quiet for minutes. **Three sourced follow-ons this pass did not take:** (1) a real howl carries ~10 km and our AOI is 208 m, so 88 m is a compromise and the research's answer is a floored-falloff world layer rather than a mob emitter — that forks the mixer's one distance law and wants its own pass; (2) the cadence *shape* should be Minecraft's refractory-plus-ramping-hazard (min 4.0 s, median 5.9 s) rather than uniform ± 50 %, which permits 4.6 s after 4.5 s and 13.5 s twice running — it is a drop-in for pig, birds and wolf together; (3) **the growl's 14 m sits inside the wolf's smallest notice radius (15 m at night) on purpose, and nothing enforces it** — the client has no `content` dependency and no mob-def lane, so a `mobs.toml` edit will not redden anything. **Open for the operator:** all six numbers, and they want ears rather than arithmetic — `bin/soundbank.rs` dumps the bank to WAV. | client `sound/voice.rs` (was `sound/pig.rs`) `sound/mod.rs` (`Cue::Howl` `Cue::Growl`, `CUES`) `sound/synth.rs` (`Res` `WOLF_FORMANTS` `formant_gain` `howl` `growl`) `render/audio.rs` (`voices`) `render/mobs.rs` (`voice_h_of`) · `tests/sound.rs` |
| predator v0 — the wolf hunts: `WOLF_SLOT_EVERY = 4` · hp 100 · notice 30 m · bite 20 · 85 % sprint · courage floor 0 (systems lane, 2026-08-14) | **PROPOSED — and it answers a question `animals v0` left open below: "whether a second species should be a content row or wait for the aggression path".** It is a content row. The aggression path landed as `mob attack v0` and generalised for free, which was the surprise worth recording: **nothing in `mob.rs` branches on species.** One rousing timer, and `brave_pct` decides which way it points — a floor of zero is a floor no wound drops under, so the state that makes a hurt pig run makes a wolf charge at 1 hp. Prey and hunter differ by two content numbers (courage 0 against 50, notice 30 m against 12) and by nothing in code. What was NOT free is the **roster**: `MOB_KINDS` was 1 and `Mobs::new` wrote `MOB_PIG` into all 64 slots, so the file header's three-day-old promise that "a second species costs a content row and nothing else" was two thirds true. It costs a row, a bake arm and an ordinal now. **`kind_of(slot)` is why no wire byte moved**: v29 rejected a `kind` field on `EntityState` on cost, and that rejection is only affordable if both sides can derive the answer — so the species is a pure function of the slot, a stride rather than a hashed draw, which makes the predator count a number a gate counts (16 of 64, every seed) instead of a distribution it samples. The seed still varies where they live. **The stride found a real defect on its first tick** and this is the half worth reading: a charging animal held `flee_gait` at any distance, so one in reach overshot, turned on its next think and ran back — a 30-tick orbit. The bite is phase-locked to `attack_ticks` (60), and 60 is a multiple of 30, so **the bite sampled the same point of that orbit forever**: a slot whose phase landed outside reach could never bite that player, not rarely, never. Slot 0's phase landed inside; every bite gate hunted slot 0; all of them were green for three days. The first pig to hatch anywhere else — slot 1, once `kind_of` gave slot 0 to the wolf — could not bite at all. Fixed by standing still once closed, which is also what the animal should do, and gated as *settling* (`a_closed_charge_settles_instead_of_orbiting`). **Sourced numbers name their source**: hp 100 is the reference roster table that broke the boar's 80/150 tie (`BALANCE.md` §6 rung 3); bite 20 is this repo's own tier-4 recollection, already written into the pig's row as the figure its 15 sits under. **The rest are proposed defaults, and the honest list is**: the 1-in-4 mix, 85 % of sprint (the ceiling is the pig's defect running backwards — at 100 a fleeing pig could never be *caught*, at 100 a hunting wolf can never be *escaped*), 6 s of commitment, 90 m of range, and the drops (meat 4 + fat 8, deliberately less than the pig, which is the reference's own shape: you kill this because it is killing you). **Open for the operator:** the mix — 16 predators on one island is the one number a playtest answers and no arithmetic here can; whether a wolf should hunt harder at night, which is the thing that would make `day/night v0` cost something; and whether it should pay a hide/bone item, which this row refused because two new items would drag recipes and `ui::icons::STEMS` into a slice about the roster. | `content/mobs.toml` · sim-core `mob.rs` · `content/src/bake.rs` · `tests/mob.rs` · `server/tests/hunt.rs` · client `render/mobs.rs` `ui/death.rs` · `reference/ANIMALS.md` §9.5 |
| deploy collision v0 — six solid archetypes, tops are ground (systems lane, 2026-08-11) | **PROPOSED — which furniture stops a body, and the volumes are the client's own authored table.** Until this landed no deployable blocked movement at all (`NOW.md` §0n2's "drawn geometry with no blocked volume") and nothing scattered or built could be stood ON (§0q item 3). Now: **hearth, box, furnace, workbench, recycler and research table block**, as axis-aligned boxes at the cell centre with the piece formula's base, sized digit-for-digit from `render/structures.rs`'s `DEPLOY` rows — real-world dimensions, so the sim blocks exactly what the client draws and `tests/greybox.rs` holds the two tables equal, the comparison §0n2 said could not exist. **Four archetypes stay walk-over on purpose**: the bag (a 0.32 m mat, and eight of them must not wall a doorway for cloth), the fire (the genre walks over its campfire; the burn it owes a body is a damage feature, not a wall), the door (blocks as an edge through the shut bits it always had) and the lock (never a record). The mechanism is the shut bit's twin — a 4-bit archetype nibble per (column, level) in `ColMasks`, kept in lockstep by `place_deploy`/`drop_deploy`, re-derived on load by `rebuild_doors`, mirrored client-side through `seal_for` — so `movement::step`'s signature never moved and prediction inherits everything. **Tops are ground under the step-rule lid** (`piece_ground`'s solid arm, `slot_ground` for occupants): a jump lands ON a box instead of falling through it into the volume, the reference's box-stair works, and the shelter's plinth is finally standable. Being inside is never absorbing — the occupant escape latch applies to deploys separately. `test_replay` regenerated (verb landing, determinism green); nothing joined `state_hash`. **Open for the operator:** whether the fire should hurt instead of block, and whether a sleeper should block (unanswered since persistence v0, unchanged here). | sim-core `collide.rs` `deploy.rs` `movement.rs` `occupy.rs` `terrain.rs` · client-core `core.rs` · `tests/solid_deploy.rs` · client `tests/greybox.rs` §D |
| the public shard's status port — `status_addr = "127.0.0.1:8431"` (platform lane, 2026-08-11) | **PROPOSED, and it is a port number nobody spoke.** The *shape* was spoken (loopback, fronted by nginx — the row in §Spoken); 8431 is arithmetic and belongs here rather than only in a config file. Two constraints picked it and neither is taste: `shard.toml.example` suggests 8080 and **8080 is taken on this box** by another service, and a status bind failure REFUSES THE BOOT (`config.rs` — a shard whose config names an endpoint it silently does not serve is the defect the parser refuses everywhere else), so a collision here is a public shard that will not start. It is loopback-only, so it collides with nothing outside this machine and a second shard on another box may reuse it. **What is NOT open**: that the endpoint is fronted rather than bound publicly, and the published url. Change the number by editing `shard-public.toml` and the nginx `location` together — they are two halves of one address and nothing gates the pair. | `shard-public.toml` · `/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/game.moreright.xyz` · `crates/server/src/status.rs` |
| the compiler — `rust-toolchain.toml` `channel = "1.97.1"` (platform lane, 2026-08-11) | **PROPOSED, and it is a number nobody had ever typed.** Until today this repo did not say which compiler built it: CI installed whatever `stable` was on the day a run fired, and this box was on whatever it last updated to. **The cheap failure is what found it** — those were 1.94.1 (March) and 1.97.1 (July), 1.97 added `clippy::manual_option_zip`, the gate runs `-D warnings`, and so **every `gates` run on `main` had failed for days while `./ci/gates.sh` was green locally**, including every PR merged through #56/#57/#58. The merge gate had stopped gating and nothing said so, because everyone ran the gates locally and they passed. **The expensive failure is the one this row is really about**: wall 5 is `same build + seed + WAL → same state hashes`, `test_replay` and `test_terrain_golden` pin those hashes, and a compiler is free to change float codegen between releases — so two machines on two compilers can disagree about a number while both believe they are running the same build. "Same build" had no definition. **Pinned forward rather than back**, deliberately: 1.94.1 would have gone green instantly by keeping a five-month-old compiler and dodging a valid lint, which is the shape of a fix that removes the symptom and keeps the hole. **Measured when it landed, and it is the reassuring half**: a full clean rebuild under 1.97.1 passes all ten gates and `test_replay`, `test_terrain_golden`, `test_protocol_golden` and `test_parity_wasm` are all **unchanged** — a three-release jump moved no state hash. That is the answer wall 5 wants and it had never once been asked. **How to raise it**: bump the channel, run `./ci/gates.sh`, and expect new lints — that is the point, they arrive on a commit somebody is looking at rather than on whichever run happens after a Rust release. **If a golden moves when this moves, stop**: that is not a fixture to regenerate, it is wall 5 reporting that the compiler changed an arithmetic result. **Open for the operator:** the cadence. Nothing here says how often to bump it, and a pin nobody raises is a repo that ages onto an unsupported compiler. | `rust-toolchain.toml` · `.github/workflows/{gates,nightly,release}.yml` · `CLAUDE.md` wall 5 |
| the world's register — Gates as a threshold dimension, obsidian/lapis/gold ruins, a broken-by-default world, extraction windows, and an optional ward (proposed 2026-08-10 over two conversations, **unspoken**) | **PROPOSED, and the default is the status quo: nothing changes until the register itself is ruled on.** From two explicitly exploratory operator conversations (*"help me think"*, *"help me consider and log all of this"*). `WORLD.md` carries the whole of it; this row exists so it is not mistaken for a ruling. **Three framing calls came with it and each bounds the rest**: it is a **roadmap, not a v1 spec** (*"early on we dont have to do this, it might be paths over time that evolve the game"*), so nothing competes with `DESIGN.md` §2's alpha; it is a **ceiling** (*"i think this is the max deviation"*), so classes/levels/spells are outside it by the operator's own line; and the risk is low *because* the ancient tech is environmental rather than inventory (*"some secret magitek from this civ isnt going to break much"*) — which is `WORLD.md` §7.1, the rule that keeps player gear crude forever. **The keystone is the name.** *"its called Gates so its some threshold dimension… a broken place in time"* — and it earns its keep by making mechanics that already exist legible instead of needing new ones: you respawn because a threshold does not let you leave by dying, the world wipes because the gate cycles, and **banked OBOL survives a wipe because what you send out leaves the broken place while what you carry stays in it**, which is `DESIGN.md` §3.1's carried/banked split with a reason attached rather than a new mechanic. **Two design ideas are new since the first conversation and both are improvements.** (a) **Extraction**: the bank terminal is gated — either a per-player cooldown (*"once every 24 hours"*) or a server-opened window (*"the server needs to work on activating something for the rest that allows everyone to extract"*). They solve different problems — a valve versus a convergence — and `WORLD.md` §4.3 recommends the window as primary with a **guaranteed floor and a contested ceiling**: the gate opens on the world's own posted cadence so nobody can be locked out of their economy, and activation buys extra or longer windows. It is the purest PvP driver in the design and costs **no PvE at all**, which is the line the operator drew (*"trying not to push too much pve but also this is total pvp bait"*); the fight is the walk in and the walk out, never inside the haven, which stays a no-damage zone. (b) **The inversion**: *"maybe the default state is debuffs are on and players need to turn them off over the course of the reset."* This **retires the griefing hole the first version had** — you cannot grief with a debuff that is already the default — and buys three things: the wipe cycle gets an arc (Rust's day 1 and day 28 are the same world), a repaired monument becomes a PvP objective that is not somebody's base, and repairs are a public-goods problem so the politics fall out of the incentives. Two problems it creates and their answers are in `WORLD.md` §5.2: grade the world's condition by register so the coast stays survivable on wipe day, and make repair **open doors rather than remove threats** so a tamed late cycle is not an empty one. **The ward is explicitly undecided** (*"idk if we will even do the shields but its on the table"*) and nothing else depends on it — which is fortunate, because it is the one item carrying a real gate collision. **Three engineering findings, none of them taste**: a ward would invalidate `CONTENT.md` §4 anchor 2 *without reddening `test_content`* (the bands compute against `globals.player_hp = 100` and would silently describe a different fight) while `reference/BALANCE.md` §6.2 **permits** it as the admissible mechanism difference; the tree has **no fall damage at all**, so "no fall damage" is the status quo and the proposal is the opposite; and **extraction and world states are one system or they are two** — an opened gate and a repaired monument are the same bounded, tick-expiring, hashed, broadcast object, so whichever lands first should be the general table even carrying one state (the terminal arrives at A2, `ALPHA.md` §2). **What still needs the word** is `WORLD.md` §10, and the urgent one is unchanged: `ART.md` is measured off `Rust Images/` and `gates-loop/art/RUBRIC.md` is checksummed outside this repo, so **an obsidian world is scored as a defect by construction and no builder can fix it** — 2026-08-01's art row already named the exit (*"Revisit when a concrete palette is spoken; that changes the style section of the visual rubric only, never the ten criteria"*), and it needs three acts: a palette, a reference set, and the rubric's style section. §9.1 is the timing that follows: **decide the register early, build it late** — deciding costs a sentence today, while art made for the wrong register has to be remade. | `WORLD.md` · `ART.md` · `DESIGN.md` §3.1 · `ALPHA.md` §2 · `CONTENT.md` §4 · `reference/BALANCE.md` §6 · `NOW.md` §0wd |
| tree species v0 — `SPAWN_CLEAR_M = 4.5`, `BROADLEAF_MAX_R = 2.9`, `SEEDS_PER_SPECIES = 3` (client + sim lane, 2026-08-10) | **PROPOSED — a second species, and the sim number it forced.** The pool was one species at three seeds (`tree.rs`'s own words); `TreeType::Deciduous` was a variant of a crate enum we already depended on and had never used, so a broadleaf is a parameter block rather than an asset (`reference/PLANTS.md` §3.1, §6.1). It is deliberately **shorter and much wider** than the conifer — 5.4 m against 6.6, 2.9 m of crown against 1.7 — because a pine is a spire and a broadleaf is a dome, and two species sharing a silhouette envelope is one species with two names. **`SPAWN_CLEAR_M` 4.0 → 4.5 is the consequence, and it is derived rather than picked**: the widest ceiling (2.9) at the largest scale `scatter` rolls (`SLOT_SCALE_MAX` 1.1) is a 3.19 m canopy edge, plus `CAPSULE_RADIUS_M` 0.4 is 3.59 m of touching distance, plus 0.85 m of standing room so a spawn does not open with a trunk filling the frame. Authorised by the operator in advance (2026-08-10, *"im fine with raising the sim or whatever we got the juice i think"*). **The reason this needed a spoken permission at all is that nothing was checking it**: `world.rs` credited `ci/pine_shape.mjs` with closing that arithmetic and that gate went with the browser client, so the derivation had been a dead citation — `crates/client/tests/tree.rs::a_fresh_spawn_stands_clear_of_the_widest_tree` closes it in Rust, in the crate that can see both the mesh and the constant. `SEEDS_PER_SPECIES` is the old `CONIFER_POOL` 3 restated per species, so the pool is now 6 and grows with the table. **Open for the operator: the broadleaf has never been looked at.** This repo's only visual gate is a person booting the game (`CLAUDE.md`), every automated check here is arithmetic, and arithmetic cannot say whether the thing reads as a tree. | `crates/client/src/render/tree.rs` · `crates/sim-core/src/world.rs` · `crates/client/tests/tree.rs` · `reference/PLANTS.md` §6.1 |
| felling v0 — `FELL_FALL_S = 1.6` (client lane, 2026-08-10) | **PROPOSED — one number, and the interesting half is what is deliberately NOT a knob.** How long a chopped tree takes to go from standing to flat. The browser's `FELL_TICKS` was 33 at 30 Hz (1.1 s) and then *sank the trunk out of the world* over 60 more ticks, so its fall only had to hold the eye until the vanish covered for it; ours has to survive being looked at afterwards, and 1.1 s reads as a swat rather than as a tree. **The fall bearing is derived, never rolled** — `fell_bearing(key)` is a pure function of the sim's own `gather::cell_key`, so the trunk and the canopy (siblings, not parent and child) agree without sharing state, and two clients watching the same tree see it land the same way.
