A bearing rolled at fell time would differ per client, which is the kind of divergence nothing would ever fail on. **The curve is not a knob either**: `p²` over the duration, one expression with no state. A rigid rod under gravity obeys `θ'' = (3g/2L)·sin θ`, and integrating it per frame would buy a stiffness knob and a state variable for a motion that lasts 1.6 s. **What the trunk does after it lands is the actual design call and it is free**: it stays. A felled tree keeps the mesh it already had, so a downed forest costs exactly what a standing one costs — the operator's read of the reference (2026-08-10: *"all they do in rust is fall over and nothing lol then dispear"*) is a despawn we have no reason to copy. The log does **not** collide; that is a sim change and is not claimed. **Open for the operator:** whether a settle bounce is wanted, and whether a downed trunk should eventually decay rather than persist to the slot's 20–45 min respawn. | `crates/client/src/render/props.rs` · `crates/client/tests/fell.rs` · `reference/PLANTS.md` |
| interest rank cap v0 — `AOI_RANK_EXIT = MAX_SNAPSHOT_ENTITIES` (64), `AOI_RANK_ENTER = 45` (netcode lane, 2026-08-10) | **PROPOSED, and only the band width is really open — the exit rank is arithmetic.** `MAX_SNAPSHOT_ENTITIES` is a per-datagram wire count that two places read as a world cap: `update_interest` marked everything inside 176 m interesting (up to `MAX_PLAYERS + MAX_MOBS` = 164), and the client's `Interp` was sized on it while being filled *cumulatively*, so the 65th distinct id since the last zero-state was accepted by `ClientView`, refused by the interpolator, and never drawn. The exit rank is therefore fixed by the constant it is making true: a set capped at 64 is what the sentence in `limits.rs` already claimed. **The band width is the number, and it is derived rather than picked**: clearing an interest bit is welded to a wire removal — destroyed interpolation history, a 133–200 ms frozen body, a rebuilt mannequin — so a single rank threshold would make two bodies a metre apart do that to each other at 15 Hz. Ranks need the hysteresis distance already has. At uniform density the count inside a radius goes as its square, so the band with *the same forgiveness as the 176/208 m band* is that ratio in area — `AOI_ENTER_CM² / AOI_EXIT_CM²` × 64 = 45. A body must improve 19 places to re-enter, the rank equivalent of walking 32 m closer. **The cost is stated, not hidden**: on a shard denser than this a client sees 45–64 of its neighbours and not the rest. Say *near*, not *nearest* — hysteresis makes the set path-dependent by design, so an incumbent at rank 60 is held while a newcomer at rank 46 is refused, and only a body that improves 19 places displaces one. Measured 48 of 99 in the clustered scene. The reference game solves the same problem with per-entity radii and server-side occlusion (`reference/NETWORK.md`), neither of which exists here. **Measured, before anyone retunes it:** `AOI_RANK_EXIT` at 63 — the tighter reading — turns `the_shed_counter_sees_the_budget_refuse` red, because that gate needs the interest set to be able to offer more than one datagram can carry. **Open for the operator:** whether 45 is the right floor for a fight, and whether the ranking should weight by class (a player at 100 m may matter more than a pig at 40 m) rather than by distance alone. | `crates/sim-core/src/limits.rs` · `crates/server/src/core.rs` `update_interest` · `crates/server/tests/snapshot_budget.rs` |
| title manifest v0 — `MAX_MANIFEST_BYTES = 16384`, keys `servers` / `news` / `store` / `workshop` (client lane, 2026-08-10) | **PROPOSED, and the open half is the schema rather than the number.** The launcher answers `Overlay::title(slug)` with a **url**, not a document — it does not proxy a game's own files, a rule the SDK states twice — so the game fetches the manifest itself, capped and refuse-don't-truncate like every other network read here. `servers.url` is the key the platform already publishes; the other three are named by us and **nothing publishes them yet**, which the screen says in those words rather than drawing a dead row. Each section may also carry a `label`, so the platform can rename a section of its own site without a client release. Two refusals are load-bearing: the document must be a JSON **object** (a struct whose fields all default also deserializes from a JSON *array*, so `[]` read as a valid manifest naming nothing — a host serving the wrong document would have presented as a platform that had published nothing), and a link must be **https** with a host and no whitespace, because the verb on the other end is the launcher opening it on the player's desktop. **Open for the operator:** whether these are the right three keys and whether they live in the title manifest at all, which is a scry-side call. | `crates/client/src/manifest.rs` · `crates/client/src/ui/hub.rs` · `MENUS.md` §3 |
| menu backdrop v0 — one still at `assets/menu/backdrop.jpg`, scrim alpha `0.82` (client lane, 2026-08-10) | **PROPOSED — and the knob the operator actually owns is whether it moves.** The reference plays a *video* behind its main menu (operator, 2026-08-10: *"rust uses a video for the background"*), which retired the note that preceded this one: rendering the island live behind the menu is the expensive way to buy the cheap thing — no camera to drive, no streaming ring to feed, no `WorldId` to insert and tear down. Bevy decodes no video and this repo is not adding a decoder for a title screen, so the shipped form is a still, and the code points a frame index at a handle so a sequence is a drop-in. **The sizes are the decision**: a 1280×720 JPEG is ~70 KB, so a three-second loop at 20 fps is ~4–12 MB in the depot for a screen a player looks at for four seconds. The still that ships is a `--capture --no-hud` plate of our own island — reproducible from the seed, so a better one is a command rather than an art commission. Absent is a supported state (a source checkout, a trimmed depot): Bevy draws nothing for an unresolved handle and the flat `BG` shows through. The scrim's alpha is the whole contrast budget of the front end and the panels sit at 0.90/0.86 over it, which is why the sea reads through the server list instead of only round the edges. **Open for the operator:** motion, and which vantage. | `crates/client/src/render/ui.rs` · `crates/client/src/render/capture.rs` · `NOW.md` §0s |
| boot splash v0 — `Screen::Boot`, no number in it (client lane, 2026-08-09) | **PROPOSED, and the knob is the absence of a knob.** The splash ends when both its waits are answered — every `Startup` asset handle settled, and the scry handshake returned — and there is no minimum display time, no fade and no frame count anywhere in it. That is the whole call: every commercial splash this copies (Rust's, League's) holds for a *duration*, and a duration is the thing `CLAUDE.md` forbids a gate or a player to wait on. So the only tunable a reviewer might expect is deliberately not there, and if the screen flashes past on a fast box that is correct behaviour rather than a bug to pad. **What it covers, stated honestly**: from window creation onward — `Startup`'s four loaders, the generated sound bank, the ten rasterised rings, the two TTF parses, the 57 icons, the launcher round trip, and (on a launcher join) the connect. **What it cannot cover**: the wgpu adapter enumeration and window creation *before* the first Bevy frame, measured at ~3 s under llvmpipe on this box. Covering that needs a second process or an OS-level window, which is a real option and is not taken here. **Open for the operator:** whether a minimum hold is wanted after all for brand reasons — that is a product call, not an engineering one. | `crates/client/src/ui/boot.rs` · `crates/client/src/render/boot.rs` · `MENUS.md` §3 |
| server browser v0 — `MAX_FAVOURITES = 128`, busy-first sort (client lane, 2026-08-09) | **PROPOSED — one wall-4 cap and one ordering, against the reference's PLAY GAME frame.** `MAX_FAVOURITES` bounds a list that is written to the settings file and read at every launch, so unbounded is a file that grows forever; 128 is comfortably past `shardlist::MAX_SHARDS` (64), because starring every shard on a full list must never be what hits the cap or the cap would be a rule about the list rather than about the file. **The search box's cap is not a second knob and this row used to claim it was** — the browser landed with its own `MAX_QUERY_CHARS`, at 40, beside the craft panel's shipped 32, and `ci/knob_registry.mjs` refused it: a name that means two things is a name the registry cannot be authoritative about. There is one now — `crate::ui::MAX_QUERY_CHARS`, at 32, the value already in players' hands, and it stays out of the registry in the panel's own words — it exists because an unbounded string fed by a keyboard is an unbounded string. **The sort is the reference's own** — its list reads 145, 110, 101, 54, … so population descending — and the tie-breaks are ours and are the interesting half: name second so two identical polls draw the same order, and an *unknown* count sorts LAST rather than as zero, because `Option`'s natural ordering puts `None` first and an unpolled shard would otherwise head the list as if it were the busiest thing on it. **What is NOT a knob here is the rail**: the reference's Official / Community / Modded / Friends categories are facts about an ecosystem `scry-shardlist-v1` does not publish, so the rail is All + Favourited, on `settings.rs`'s standing policy against drawing a category with nothing behind it. Its WIPE SCHEDULE group and region chips are absent for the same reason. **Open for the operator:** whether the document should ever carry an operator/modded distinction — that is a schema call with a scry side to it, not a client one. | `crates/client/src/ui/servers.rs` · `crates/client/src/render/menu.rs` · `MENUS.md` §3 |
| shard status poll v0 — `STATUS_POLL_SECS = 10.0`, `STATUS_TIMEOUT_S = 4` | **PROPOSED.** How often each reader re-asks a listed shard's `/status.json`, and how long one ask may take. Chosen against what the number is *for*: a player deciding which of four shards to join needs "busy or empty", not a scoreboard. Ten seconds against the 64-row cap is at most 6.4 requests a second across a full list from one client, and the endpoint is a plain TCP GET a shard answers serially on one thread (`status.rs`). **The timeout is shorter than the interval on purpose** — a round must finish before the next is due, or a slow shard stacks rounds until the list polls itself in a loop; `begin_status_poll` refuses a second round as well, and `the_poll_interval_outlasts_its_own_timeout` pins the constants so the two cannot drift into agreement-by-accident. Four seconds is comfortably past a transatlantic round trip, and a shard that misses it reads as `?` rather than as zero. **What is deliberately not here**: `ping_ms`. It is a measurement only the reader can make — the distance from a player to a shard is not a property of the shard — so neither the file nor the generator can state one, and no reader invents it. The wall-4 caps beside these (`MAX_URL_BYTES`, `MAX_STATUS_BYTES`) carry documented defaults in source rather than rows here, which is what the sibling shard-list caps already do. | `crates/client/src/render/menu.rs` · `crates/client/src/shardlist.rs` · `shards.toml.example` |
| join-link schemes — `scry://join/<slug>/<host:port>`, `gates://<host:port>` | **PROPOSED, and the open half is not the spelling.** The parser, the argv seam and the refusals are built and gated (`deeplink.rs`); what nobody has spoken is **who registers the scheme with the desktop and when**. That is an act on a player's machine — a `.desktop` file plus `xdg-mime` on Linux, a registry key on Windows — so it belongs to the launcher's installer, not to a game that was merely run once, and nothing in this repo performs it. Until it happens a link works when pasted into a launcher or passed on an argv, and not when clicked in a chat window. **Also open**: whether a link may name anything beyond a title and an address. Today it may not, and the refusal is deliberate rather than unimplemented — a link is untrusted input from a stranger by construction, so a field that could set an identity would be a phishing primitive. Adding a spectate flag or a password would each need a sentence here first. | `crates/client/src/deeplink.rs` · `crates/client/src/args.rs` |
| the board's playable link (**opened by the 2026-08-06 browser cut**) | **UNANSWERED.** `DESIGN.md` §11's board delivery is "repo + playable link + a recorded round whose replay hash checks", and the playable link was the web demo. With the browser client cut there is no artifact that a board visitor can click and play. Three candidates, none chosen: the depot download behind the scry launcher (a real install, not a link — raises the bar to click-through-to-play); a recorded round played back in the browser off the WAL, which is not *playable* but is honest and reuses `replay`; or the board accepts repo + replay hash and drops the link. Each changes `ALPHA.md` §4's landing page differently, and the second button on that page is dead until this is spoken. | `DESIGN.md` §11 · `ALPHA.md` §4 |
| the product is a Rust clone, not a proving ground (**operator, 2026-08-04**) | Playability outranks provability when the two compete for a pass. The sim ships 15 verbs, 48 items and 36 recipes that the client cannot reach: the hotbar renders six slots as text and there is no inventory grid, container view or item-move. **No wall moves and no gate is weakened** — gates ride along with the feature they protect — but a gate is not a valid item by itself unless a red wall demands it. Supersedes nothing in the walls; it reorders NOW.md. The RL-training-environment goal (2026-08-01) stands as background, not as a driver. | `NOW.md` item 1 · lane charters |

| **the economy has never been measured against the sim** (**opened 2026-08-08**) | **The comparison, the ceiling and the decomposition landed 2026-08-09; the semantics call is still the operator's, and the proposal below is now well evidenced rather than a guess.** `balance.rs` refuses a `farm_per_min` above the sim's at-node ceiling (2030/min at tier 1, weak mark included, 38-tick cadence), gated by `content.rs::the_declared_farm_rate_cannot_beat_standing_at_the_node` plus the hash probe the rate never had. `server tests/farmwalk.rs` measures the rest: **969 wood/min, 71.6% duty** against the kit hatchet's 1353 ceiling (8 trees, 2,970 ticks, seed 20260731) — an **unthreatened solo** walk, since nothing here contests a node or kills a farmer. **The decomposition is the finding** (`RIPLIST.md` §0/§5, researched the same day): the reference's own gather ladder falls ~30× from at-node to best real farming **with no threat in it at all** — node density, travel, carry limits, deposit trips, respawn, smelt latency — and only ~2–5× more to contested play. Ours measures a **1.40× logistics term and a 1.0× threat term**. Apply their decomposition to our 1353 ceiling and you get 9–69/min: **the declared 50 sits inside that band**, so the 20× gap is not evidence the constant is wrong — it is evidence the island charges almost none of the friction that would make it right. **PROPOSED, on that evidence**: keep `farm_per_min` as a friction-inclusive session rate, leave 50 where it is, and treat the gap as a debt owed by the WORLD (`RIPLIST.md` §2 rows 5–6: logistics friction first at ~10–30×, mob→player damage second at ~2–5×, and threat modelled as trip shape and load loss, never as a flat rate multiplier). The four anchors (`starter_minutes` 85.6, `satchel_minutes` 29.6, upkeep 8.56/day, `wood_wall_minutes` 7.0) are model prices under either reading. Also still open: whether `CONTENT.md` §3 becomes a band or stops claiming minutes — its header no longer claims those rows are tested. | `reference/RIPLIST.md` §0/§2/§5 · `reference/BALANCE.md` §4.3 · `CONTENT.md` §3/§4 · `content/balance.toml` · server `tests/farmwalk.rs` |
| tools as weapons — should the band widen? (**opened 2026-08-08**) | **PROPOSED: leave `ttk_melee = [3, 5]`.** (Numbers moved under this row the same day: after the reference alignment tools are 20–30, the wooden spear is 20, and a rock now ties it — so "one hit worse than a spear" became "the same as a spear", and reach is the only separator left. That makes the question below sharper, not softer.) The alternative is widening to something like `[3, 8]` so a rock takes 8 hits and a crafted weapon is worth crafting — which is the better *game* and a real balance pass, because widening the band also lets a bad weapon exist at all and re-prices every TTK assertion that currently holds. Not taken by a loop: a band is the thing `CONTENT.md` §4 exists to make un-driftable, so moving one is spoken, not inferred. Also parked here: the torch, which the reference arms at 13 and our band therefore forbids. | CONTENT §4 · `content/balance.toml` |

| beach spawn ring | the spawn zone is the **beach**, as `TERRAIN.md` §1 stage 6 and `DESIGN.md` §2 already said; the retired placeholder sampled the interior 224–1824 m, which *is* the forest band, so fresh spawns stood inside scatter · 48 hashed candidates, each one bearing off the shared 256-entry yaw LUT (no trig, wall 1) plus a 12-step bisection along that ray for the shoreline crossing at **1.2 m** — above the 0.4 m wade line so a spawn is on sand, below the 2 m beach mask so it is beach and not shelf · bracket 640–1024 m from island center: land is solid inside 640 and the sea floor is under the target before 1024, which is also the largest radius keeping every bearing's outer probe inside the 2048 m square · accept = under the beach mask **and** slope < 1.0 (the foundation/walkability shape) **and** 4 m clear of every scatter slot center — the widest archetype the client draws is the tree cone at r 1.7 m × 1.1 max scale ≈ 1.9 m, plus the 0.4 m capsule, so 4 m stands clear of one rather than merely outside it · the clearance scan covers the 3×3 cell block, conservative for any clearance under 9 m (a slot two cells out is ≥ 16 − 4 − 3 m away) · two fallbacks, both gated unreachable: the first merely-walkable shore point, then island center · measured on the way in: the worst spawn over 400 seeds × 64 ids consumed 7 of the 48 candidates, so nothing is near a fallback · `spawn_ring_lands_on_a_clear_beach` re-derives beach/wade/slope/clearance from terrain over 32 seeds × 64 joins and scans 5×5 cells where the selector scans 3×3, so a clearance that outgrew the scanned block reddens instead of passing · **`dev_spawn` still overrides everything** (row below) | sim-core `world.rs` · `terrain.rs` |

| client netcode fill-ins (NETCODE §3/§4 speak dilation ~5% · smoothing 0.95/0.85 · interp 133 ms) | resync run-ahead 3 ticks · catch-up cap 4 steps/frame · server-est gain 0.1, snap past 6 ticks · error hard-snap 4 m · smoothing blend 0.25–1 m, dead zone 1 cm, per render frame · interp history 16 samples · buffer-dry ⇒ freeze (Hermite/extrapolation await wire velocity, M2) · input datagrams at 30 Hz (per client tick, not per render frame) · predictor ring 32 seqs · bridge in-buf 2 KiB · advance dt clamp 1 s · ⚠ **the smoothing rate is per render frame and therefore framerate-dependent** — 0.95/frame converges ~2.4× faster at 144 fps than at 60, so two players on one shard smooth a correction over different wall-clock times. Known and unaddressed: the rate is NETCODE-spoken as a per-frame figure, and making it a per-second rate is a change to a spoken number, not a fill-in. Raised 2026-08-17 while fixing the decay that was never called at all (below) | client-core `predict.rs` `interp.rs` `clock.rs` `core.rs` `bridge.rs` |
| reconciliation offset decay ownership (2026-08-17) | `Predictor::decay_error` is called from `ClientCore::advance`, not from the render loop that documents it. **It had no caller in `crates/client/` at all** — the only invocations were eight `server/tests/*_wire.rs` harnesses hand-rolling *"the render loop's once-per-frame call"*, so `err` (written `+=` per mispredicted reconcile) accumulated for a whole session and put the camera up to `SNAP_AT_M`'s 4 m from the body the world collides against, then teleported. Reported from play as a tree trunk offset from the thing that stopped you. Placement in `advance` is the fix: it is the one entry both `Session::pump` and every harness already go through, so no caller can forget it — the `pop_*` single-drain rule's shape. Measured at 5,400 ticks: 0 mispredicts on a clean wire, 1 at 2 % loss (0.224 m, permanent without the drain), 71 at 30 % (0.875 m). Gated by `client_loop.rs::the_correction_offset_does_not_accumulate_over_a_long_lossy_session`, proven red under the defect | client-core `core.rs` `predict.rs` |

| door v0 + door wire v6 | a door places **closed** and seals its doorway; the **use** action toggles it, and any player within the 5 m build reach may — *superseded by the lock v0 row below: a door now places locked and answers only to its owner until its owner unlocks it; unlocked, this row's behavior is exactly what remains* · the sealed/open bit lives in the collision index beside the piece masks, kept in lockstep by `deploy.rs` on placement, toggle and removal, and re-derived client-side after anything that rebuilds that index · a use naming an address that holds no door refuses with reason 11 · `PROTO_VER` 6, goldens regenerated: C→S action subtype 5 = use (cell 10 + 10 · level 3 · loc 2 — address only, so two toggles racing can't fight over a state) · S→C event subtype 22 = door (address + absolute open bit, broadcast like a placement) · every placed-deployable record on the wire gained its open bit, so the join walk carries which doors stand open · your **own** door swings on the press, not on the reply (NETCODE.md §6.1): the client mirror and the predictor's shut bit flip optimistically, one prediction outstanding at a time so it never compounds, resolved by the next action outcome — the announcement is absolute so it confirms or corrects, and any deploy refusal rolls it back (refusals are sender-only, but with an 8-deep action ring the bounce may belong to an earlier queued action; an early rollback is harmless because a use that does land announces right behind it) · a rollback raises the deploy-applied flag, so the leaf the renderer swung on the press swings back — nothing else would ever tell it to, the sim's state having never moved · a sync walk re-derives regardless · web: E is the use key — the nearest door within **the sim's own 5 m cell-center reach** toggles, and only with none in reach does E fall through to feeding a hearth | sim-core `deploy.rs` `collide.rs` `build.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` · server `core.rs` · client-wasm `core.rs` `bridge.rs` · web `src/*` |
| lock v0 + lock wire v8 | a door places **locked to its placer** and only its owner may use it — every other hand bounces with reason 12 ("not your door"), which the placement/sync record and the door announcement let a client *see* coming (the locked bit is on the wire) without letting it *decide* (the owner id is not) · the **lock** action sets the bit absolutely rather than toggling, for the same reason the use action carries no state: two presses racing must agree on the result, not swap it · owner-only, build-reach-gated, and door-only, bouncing with the same reason 11 / reason 4 the use action does — the shared `door_in_reach` is what keeps the two verbs' refusals identical · locking never moves the leaf (a door locks open; its owner is then the one who can shut it) and one EV_DOOR carries open + locked together, absolute, so a client never holds half a door · `PROTO_VER` 8, goldens regenerated: C→S action subtype 6 = lock (address + 1 bit) · S→C event subtype 22 grew its locked bit · every placed-deployable record grew one too (31 bits now), so the join walk carries which doors stand locked · **no `mine` bit**: the client predicts its own press either way and a refusal rolls the leaf back through the door v0 path, which is DESIGN §5.6's own mispredict example — a foreign locked door flashes for one RTT and corrects, and the alternative (a per-recipient record) was judged not worth making a broadcast differ per viewer · **no shared access this slice** — one owner id, no codes, no crew list, matching the hearth claim's own owner-only rule; whether sharing arrives as a code lock, a hearth auth list that doors inherit, or crews is the open question, and it is the operator's to answer · no lock item: the lock is a property of the door, not a deployable, so nothing in `content/` moved · web: L locks or unlocks the nearest door in the sim's own 5 m cell-center reach, and a locked leaf renders banded iron (cosmetics row) | sim-core `deploy.rs` `world.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` · server `core.rs` · client-wasm `core.rs` `bridge.rs` · web `src/*` |
| lock v1 — the code lock (supersedes lock v0's access model) | **`reference/DOORS.md` §9, built.** A door places **bare**; a **code lock** (`item.lock_code`, 100 metal frags at workbench1 — the reference's own price) bolts onto a door's address and lives in a dense side-store keyed by it, exactly as a hearth's stock and a box's contents are (`MAX_LOCKS = 512`, refuse-on-full). It carries a four-digit main code and an optional guest code; **entering the correct code does not open the door, it authorizes you** — the lock remembers you and thereafter the door simply works, which is the mechanic the whole document is about. Guests get the door verb and nothing else (Devblog 149's own list: no code change, no unlock, no taking it off). **Setting the main code is the only eviction**: both lists clear and the setter is re-remembered, so the control verb cannot lock you out. `LOCK_AUTH_CAP = 8` and `LOCK_GUEST_CAP = 8`, two lists so a crowd of guests cannot crowd out owners, and the overflow policy is **refuse, never evict** — a door that forgot its owner is the one failure a cap may not have. **Brute force costs, both ways at once** rather than in the reference's six-year two-step: `SHOCK_STEP_HP = 5` per consecutive miss, forgiven after `SHOCK_RESET_TICKS = 300` (10 s), and `LOCKOUT_TRIES = 8` misses shut the keypad for `LOCKOUT_TICKS = 27000` (15 min) — during which a *correct* code is refused too, which is the point. **One divergence, stated**: the shock floors at 1 hp and never kills, because this crate keeps one kill site per module that can kill and a third is a third chance for the death count, the event, the bag and the respawn to disagree. **Knock** is the other half of a refusal (`EV_KNOCK`, broadcast) — the reference shipped it in Nov 2014 and it is the only channel a locked-out player has to the person inside. **Wire v28**, goldens regenerated: `ACT_LOCK` trades its one bit for a 3-bit op + 14-bit code (six verbs on one action code, because `ACT_MAX` was full at 15 and a sixth action would have moved every C→S message by a bit); `SUB_DOOR` and the placed-deployable record each grow a `has_lock` bit; two new S→C subtypes, `SUB_KNOCK` (41, broadcast) and `SUB_AUTH` (42, own-fact — a grant is true of one recipient, so a broadcast would publish a base's access list); `PLACEMENT_BITS` 2 → 3 for `PLACE_DOOR`. `DeployRec`'s two lock bits are a **mirror** of the store, re-derived by `rebuild_doors` at load for the reason `Pieces::cols` is not in the save at all. `WORLD_SAVE_FORMAT` 1 → 2 for the lock section. Client: `L` opens a keypad **HUD line**, not a panel — a screen that takes your mouse to type four digits is a screen you die in. | `reference/DOORS.md` · `sim-core/lock.rs` `deploy.rs` · `protocol/lib.rs` `event.rs` · `content/deployables.toml` · `client/ui/keypad.rs` |
| hearth crew v1 + the shared roster (supersedes the hearth claim's owner-only rule) | **`reference/BUILDING.md` §9.1–2, built.** `Deploys::foreign_claim` was `h.owner == placer` — one id, no list, no way to share a base — which is *lock v0's bug living in the building system*, under `build::place`, `build::upgrade`, `build::repair` and `place_deploy` at once. A hearth now carries a **crew** and the claim asks the crew. Placing it joins the crew (the reference's own rule: the act of putting the cupboard down is the act of joining its list), so a live hearth is never crewless. `HEARTH_CREW_CAP = 10`, the reference's own vanilla cap. Three ops, self-service and therefore unforgeable — **the only player a crew op can name is its sender**: join (refused unless the crew is empty or already holds you), leave (never gated — a refusal would tell a stranger whether they were on the list), and clear (to the clearer alone, in one operation, because a crew that passed through empty is a crew anyone could have joined in the gap). **`sim-core/roster.rs` is the shared list** and the reason it exists is the reference's own hook table: they built one access-list pattern and hung it on `BuildingPrivlidge`, `AutoTurret` and `VehiclePrivilege`, three unrelated classes. Ours is now the lock's two lists and the hearth's one; writing it twice is how two copies acquire two eviction rules that no gate compares. Its rules, stated once: bounded, **refuse never evict**, idempotent add, order-preserving remove, and a **zeroed tail** — `state_hash` folds the whole array, so two rosters holding the same members must be byte-equal whatever route they took, and `the_zeroed_tail_makes_two_equal_lists_equal` is that sentence as a test. **Wire v29**: `ACT_LOCK` is now `ACT_ACCESS` and its op field widened 3 → 4 bits — the nine ops are one space because they are one question (*who may do this here*) and because `ACT_MAX` is still full at 15, so a second action code did not exist to spend. Which store an op addresses is the **sim's** branch (`deploy::op_is_crew`), never the wire's. `WORLD_SAVE_FORMAT` stays 2's shape with the crew appended per hearth. Client: `L` joins, `K` leaves, both on the hearth's own prompt. **Measured while landing, and the second time in one day**: the crew pushed `Deploys::new` past wasm32's 1 MiB shadow stack and `test_parity_wasm` died as an out-of-bounds read inside a constructor with every native test green. `crate::boxed_array` is the fix and now every large array in the deploy store is built on the heap. | `reference/BUILDING.md` · `sim-core/roster.rs` `deploy.rs` `lock.rs` · `protocol/lib.rs` · `client/render/verbs.rs` |
| privilege v1 — the claim is a volume the base emits (supersedes the hearth's circle for the build verbs) | **`reference/BUILDING.md` §3/§9.3, built.** The claim was a `HEARTH_RADIUS_M` circle from the hearth's cell centre: it over-claimed open ground on every side a base does not extend to, and under-claimed the far end of any base longer than 24 m. That is the shape the reference shipped and **replaced in Devblog 185** with privilege *emitted by the building blocks*, for the reason that reads as obvious once said: a circle centred on a point is the wrong shape for a building. Ours is now **a point is claimed when a piece of a hearth's base stands within `PRIV_CUSHION_M = 16` m of it**, connectivity by flood fill over the build grid (`claim.rs`). **The grid makes this cheaper for us than it was for them**: their query is `GetBuildingPrivilege(OBB, …)` against physics, which is why it needed a persistent building identity with `Merge`/`Split` to afford; ours is a breadth-first walk over a hash-indexed grid — no physics, no building id, no merge, no split, deterministic because the queue and neighbour orders are both fixed. **The walk's direction is the performance argument**: seeding from each *hearth* would be one walk per hearth on every placement, so it seeds from the pieces near the *point* and flood-fills outward to see which hearths it reaches — one walk per query whatever the shard holds. `PRIV_BFS_CELLS = 256` bounds it (wall 4) and the overflow policy is **the walk stops**: it can under-claim and never over-claim, so the worst case is somebody building against the far wall of a 768 m base and it can never lock a player out of open ground. **One rule fell out rather than being written**: `any_claim` is the same walk without the crew test, so the hearth-spacing refusal now means *one hearth per building*, however far apart two stand along it — the reference's own rule (§2), got for free from the shape. `HEARTH_RADIUS_M` is **not** retired: it is still the upkeep sweep's coverage test and still the number `covering_hearth` asks, because "what does this hearth pay for" and "whose base is this" are different questions and the sweep runs per tick where the walk runs per keypress. That split is named in `NOW.md` rather than hidden. | `reference/BUILDING.md` §3 · `sim-core/claim.rs` `build.rs` `deploy.rs` |
| demolish v1 — the grace window, and pickup with it | **`reference/BUILDING.md` §6/§7 verbs 9 and 11, built.** Two verbs under one command, split where the reference splits them. A **piece** comes down only inside `DEMOLISH_WINDOW_TICKS = 18 000` (10 min at 30 Hz, their figure for a foundation) and refunds its cost rows **whole** — an undo, not a salvage; a fraction would make misplacing a foundation a tax and they do not charge one either. A **deployable** comes back up at any time, because a box is furniture and a wall is a base, which is their asymmetry and not ours. Both ask the same `claim::foreign_claim` every other build verb asks, so on unclaimed ground **anyone in reach may take it** — `DOORS.md` §5's rule, and the reason a hearth is worth placing. Three things the pickup path refuses rather than does: a container with anything in it (`REFUSE_D_NOT_EMPTY` — a box that vanished into an inventory too small for it would be silent loss caused by the player's own verb), a stocked hearth for the same reason, and **a door whose lock does not know you** — without that check every code lock in the game is defeated by lifting what it is bolted to. A door's lock comes up as a *second item* rather than being destroyed with the frame, because it is a separate thing bolted on. **The window needed a new kind of state**: a placement tick per piece and per deployable, in parallel arrays for `bag_ready`'s stated reason (the records are what the sync packets mirror). **Wire v30**: the seventeenth C→S action, and the one that finally paid the price `ACT_THROW`'s comment quoted at v23 — `ACTION_SUB_BITS` widened 4 → 5, so every action message moved a bit and every C→S golden regenerated. The two verbs before it dodged that bump by folding into `ACT_ACCESS`'s op field; this one could not, because it addresses two stores with a leading bit rather than asking an access question. The lane has fifteen codes spare now. **The removal budget moved**: `MAX_REMOVALS_PER_TICK` is minted at the top of `tick` rather than after the commands, because a *command* can now seed a cascade — two budgets would be two caps and therefore no cap. Client: `Backspace`, a key nothing else is near. | `reference/BUILDING.md` §6 · `sim-core/build.rs` `deploy.rs` `world.rs` · `protocol/lib.rs` |
| upkeep/decay v1 — per material, and a ladder (supersedes upkeep/decay v0's all-or-nothing charge and flat rate) | **`reference/BUILDING.md` §4/§5/§9.5–6, built.** Two changes, both small and both the reference's. **(1) Upkeep is charged per material.** v0 looked for the first hearth in radius that could cover a piece's *whole* charge, so a hearth holding stone but no wood protected **nothing** — half a stock did half of nothing. Each cost row now finds its own payer and the piece is protected only if every row found one, which is the reference's own sentence: *if your stone runs out, only the stone parts of your base lose health.* A piece that pays three rows and misses the fourth has still spent the three, which is the honest reading of a partial payment. One property of v0 is deliberately kept: **no hearth in radius means no protection at all**, checked before any row is priced — without it a piece that costs nothing would be free forever. **(2) Decay is a ladder, not a flat rate.** `content/balance.toml` `decay_pct_per_period = { wood = 34, stone = 20, metal = 13 }` — 100 / the reference's own 3 h / 5 h / 8 h, so the tougher the grade the slower it rots and **an upgrade buys time as well as hp**. It replaces one flat 5 % that made a metal base rot at a wooden one's rate and took twenty hours to do it. `validate` refuses a ladder that is **not monotone** (a tougher grade rotting faster would make an upgrade cost materials to shorten a base's life — the one relationship among these three numbers a reader cannot check by eye) and refuses a 0 % rate (turn decay off with `upkeep_pct_per_day`, not by rounding). Content that prices no ladder falls back to the flat rate, so an older `balance.toml` plays exactly the game it played — a new table may add a rule, never silently change one (wall 7). Deployables keep the flat rate and always will: a box has no build material for the ladder to key on. | `reference/BUILDING.md` §4 · `content/balance.toml` · `sim-core/deploy.rs` · `crates/content/src/validate.rs` |
| upgrade v0 + upgrade wire v9 | a standing piece climbs the material ladder in place: same shape, same address, same collision, `content/building.toml`'s own words that a piece's `cost` is both its build cost and its **upgrade-into** cost — so wood → stone is priced at the stone row entire, and the ladder is a rung named, not a step taken (wood → metal in one press is legal wherever the table holds the row) · **damage carries as a fraction, never heals**: a wall at half hp becomes a stone wall at half hp (`old_hp × new_max / old_max`, floor, never below 1), so an upgrade is not a free repair mid-raid; the upkeep clock is left where it stood, an unpaid period still owed · sideways and downward refuse with reason 8 (`REFUSE_B_TIER`), as does a shape whose rung the table lacks · privilege is the placement rule unchanged — a foreign hearth's claim refuses (reason 7), and outside every claim anyone may upgrade anyone's wall, which is the same door the place verb already leaves open (pieces carry no owner; ownership is the hearth's job) · **no new S→C message**: an upgrade re-rows an address, which is exactly what the piece-placed broadcast says, and every mirror downstream already upserts by address — so the client's collision index swaps rows through the path the piece-defs drip already uses (NETCODE §6.2's structural log names `upgraded` as an event kind of its own; that is the chunk-versioned lane's shape, not today's flat broadcast — the kind splits out when that lane lands) · `PROTO_VER` 9, goldens regenerated and rekeyed `v9_*`: C→S action subtype 7 = upgrade (address + material 2 bits; three materials in two bits, so the fourth is forgeable and the decoder refuses it), the last code the 3-bit subtype field had left — subtype 8 costs a width bump · content gains a wall: the ladder must be whole (a stone rung with no wood one under it is a piece nothing can ever be upgraded into), refused at boot by `test_content` · no prediction rides along — an upgrade never moves collision, so there is nothing for the predictor to be wrong about · web: U climbs the nearest piece within the sim's own 5 m anchor reach one rung, and the tier color already reads the new material · gate coverage: `probe_parity` (bot 1 pokes the whole ladder every 16 ticks), `test_replay` (a scripted second wall reaches the stone rung and is refused both ways off it, asserted from the store), `test_alloc_zero` (a command arm of its own, over the builder bot's own cell so the paid write paths — insert, payment, re-row — are what the counter sees; two asserts there now refuse a run that only ever drove refusals), `upgrade_rides_the_wire`, `test_protocol_golden` + the forged-material domain test, `test_content`'s whole-ladder rule | sim-core `build.rs` `world.rs` `probe.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` · server `core.rs` · client-wasm `bridge.rs` · web `src/main.js` · content `validate.rs` |
| chat v0 + chat wire v10 | text on the bidi lane, two channels: **local** reaches everyone within `CHAT_LOCAL_CM` = 20 m planar of the speaker (the spoken §open "local chat" row, now a constant in `limits.rs`), **global** reaches every connected client — the speaker included either way, so the echo is the delivery receipt and a client never renders its own line on faith · **chat is not sim state**: no `Command`, no `World`, no WAL, so a replay reproduces a round without reproducing what anyone typed and determinism cannot depend on it (`test_chat_never_touches_the_state_hash` compares a chatting shard against a quiet one) · **the one player-authored payload**, so it is sanitized at both edges and the encoder refuses what the decoder would: trimmed of ASCII whitespace, non-empty, ≤ **48 UTF-8 bytes** (the cap the server's 64 B `read_frame` acceptance leaves room for), valid UTF-8, **no control characters** (a newline in a chat line is how one gets into a log), and a *non-canonical* line — one sanitize would have changed — is `Malformed` rather than repaired, so one line has exactly one encoding · profanity untouched (ALPHA §1) · **rate limit** per connection in the reader task: token bucket, burst 3, one token per 2 s (~30 lines/min sustained); refused lines are counted (`chat_rate_limited`), never announced — the limiter is the shard's business, not a conversation with the spammer · per-conn chat ring 4, **drop newest** counted, the opposite of the action ring's backpressure on purpose: chat is not a transaction, and stalling the shared stream on it would park a spammer's own build and craft lane behind their own typing · a chat frame that fails to decode is counted (`chat_bad`) and swallowed, where a malformed *action* still drops the session — chat text is the one payload a client bug can plausibly malform · sim drains one line per client per tick and the fan-out always takes it (said or lost, never deferred); a speaker the world has no body for is dropped and counted, and a recipient's full event ring loses the line without an `ev_resync`, because no sync walk would bring it back · `PROTO_VER` 10, goldens regenerated and rekeyed `v10_*`: C→S `KIND_CHAT` = 7 — **the last code the 3-bit kind field holds**, spent on chat because chat is text and not a sim command and the action lane's subtype space was full anyway; the next lane must subtype an existing kind, since widening `KIND_BITS` would widen every input datagram and every snapshot · S→C event subtype 23 = chat (speaker id 32 bits + global bit + the line) · client rings: wasm chat ring 16, drop oldest (deeper than the toast ring — a lost line is the one loss a player notices) · web: T or Enter opens a composer that owns the keyboard while open (no walking on a letter), Enter sends, Escape cancels, a leading `/g ` sends global, and the log sits bottom-left holding 8 lines that fade in 12 s unless the composer is held open; lines render through `textContent`, never `innerHTML`; the composer's `maxlength` counts UTF-16 units where the cap is UTF-8 bytes, so a refused line toasts rather than vanishing · gate coverage: `chat_wire.rs` (routing, the radius exact to the 3 cm quantum both sides, the state-hash comparison, the bodiless speaker), `test_protocol_golden` + `test_chat_text_domain_is_enforced` (both edges, forged frames both directions), `chat_limiter_spends_a_burst_then_drips`, `ci/client_smoke.mjs` (the raw C ABI both ways), `ci/browser_smoke.mjs` assertions 2 and 7 (typed into a real composer in one browser, read off the other's DOM — heard at the spawn, silent at 30 m+, global through) | protocol `chat.rs` `event.rs` · server `net.rs` `core.rs` `client.rs` `slot.rs` · sim-core `limits.rs` · client-wasm `core.rs` `bridge.rs` · web `src/*` |
| deploy wire v5 | `PROTO_VER` 5, goldens regenerated: C→S action subtype field 2 → 3 bits — deploy-place (row 4 bits, wire-exact · cell 10 + 10 · level 3 · loc 2) and feed (cell + level) · S→C event subtype field 4 → 5 bits, new subtypes: deploy-placed broadcast (address + row; owner stays sim-side until respawn-on-bag needs a lane) · deploy-sync join walk batch 24 · deploy-refused reason · deploy-def catalog batch 8 (arch/placement/hp/item) · piece-removed / deploy-removed broadcasts (address only) · stock ack (feeder-only: address + ≤ 4 rows of item + u32 units) · web: the build wheel extends past the piece table into the deployable rows, right-click places, E feeds the nearest mirrored hearth in reach, deployables render as per-archetype colored boxes (cosmetics row) | protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` · server `core.rs` `client.rs` · client-core `core.rs` `bridge.rs` · web `src/*` |
| craft wire v3 (the action lane) | `PROTO_VER` 3, goldens regenerated: C→S `KIND_ACTION` rides the handshake bidi stream in the same ≤ 64 B frames as the hello (craft: recipe 8 bits refused ≥ 64, count 8 bits refused 0 or > 99 · cancel: index 3 bits refused ≥ 4); a malformed action frame drops the session (framing trust is gone) · per-conn action ring 8, overflow **backpressures** the stream reader — the reliable lane never drops · sim drains ≤ 1 action per client per tick into the command buffer; a full buffer defers to the next tick · S→C `KIND_EVENT` subtypes: craft-queue diff (dense live jobs + head-unit eta u16, shadowed per client like the inv diff; ev_resync forces a resend) · craft-done toast (item + added) · craft-refused reason · recipe-catalog drip (batch 4, craft time as raw ticks in 24 bits — no tick-rate coupling) · web panel: C toggles (exits pointer lock), click crafts, shift-click ×5, queue strip cancels on click, eta countdown re-anchors on every announce · client craft-toast ring 8 / refusal ring 4, drop oldest (cosmetic, like the gather toast ring) | protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` · server `net.rs` `core.rs` `client.rs` · client-core `core.rs` `bridge.rs` · web `src/*` |
| piece collision v0 | capsule r 0.4 m · h 1.7 m (render capsule 0.4, eye 1.6) · piece vertical base = cell-center terrain sample + 0.3 m lift + 3 m/storey — the renderer's formula, now sim truth (lift, wall slab 0.24 m, doorway posts 0.9 m graduate from the cosmetics row) · planes (foundation/floor/roof) walk at their base; stairs are a ramp rising +Z through the storey · walls block their edge for the storey they span (capsule-height overlap; slab inflated by capsule r) · doorway blocks only its posts — the 1.2 m opening passes, the lintel can't matter at 1.7 m until a jump exists · a **closed door** in a doorway blocks the whole edge like a wall, an open one passes like an empty doorway (door v0 row) · stepping onto a built surface uses the 0.6 m step rule alone (the terrain cliff ratio is terrain's) · wade tests the effective ground, so a floor over shallows is dry · edge z-gate tests the endpoint, not the exact crossing (≤ 0.19 m steps: a fingertip of post corner, accepted) · column index: open-addressed 16 384 slots (2 × piece cap), linear probe, backward-shift delete, derived state — never hashed, rebuilt client-side when late piece-defs arrive (unknown rows collide as nothing until then; a reconcile absorbs it) | sim-core `collide.rs` `movement.rs` `limits.rs` · client-core `core.rs` `predict.rs` |
| door v0 + door wire v6 | a door places **closed** and seals its doorway; the **use** action toggles it, and any player within the 5 m build reach may — *superseded by the lock v0 row below: a door now places locked and answers only to its owner until its owner unlocks it; unlocked, this row's behavior is exactly what remains* · the sealed/open bit lives in the collision index beside the piece masks, kept in lockstep by `deploy.rs` on placement, toggle and removal, and re-derived client-side after anything that rebuilds that index · a use naming an address that holds no door refuses with reason 11 · `PROTO_VER` 6, goldens regenerated: C→S action subtype 5 = use (cell 10 + 10 · level 3 · loc 2 — address only, so two toggles racing can't fight over a state) · S→C event subtype 22 = door (address + absolute open bit, broadcast like a placement) · every placed-deployable record on the wire gained its open bit, so the join walk carries which doors stand open · your **own** door swings on the press, not on the reply (NETCODE.md §6.1): the client mirror and the predictor's shut bit flip optimistically, one prediction outstanding at a time so it never compounds, resolved by the next action outcome — the announcement is absolute so it confirms or corrects, and any deploy refusal rolls it back (refusals are sender-only, but with an 8-deep action ring the bounce may belong to an earlier queued action; an early rollback is harmless because a use that does land announces right behind it) · a rollback raises the deploy-applied flag, so the leaf the renderer swung on the press swings back — nothing else would ever tell it to, the sim's state having never moved · a sync walk re-derives regardless · web: E is the use key — the nearest door within **the sim's own 5 m cell-center reach** toggles, and only with none in reach does E fall through to feeding a hearth | sim-core `deploy.rs` `collide.rs` `build.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` · server `core.rs` · client-core `core.rs` `bridge.rs` · web `src/*` |
| lock v0 + lock wire v8 | a door places **locked to its placer** and only its owner may use it — every other hand bounces with reason 12 ("not your door"), which the placement/sync record and the door announcement let a client *see* coming (the locked bit is on the wire) without letting it *decide* (the owner id is not) · the **lock** action sets the bit absolutely rather than toggling, for the same reason the use action carries no state: two presses racing must agree on the result, not swap it · owner-only, build-reach-gated, and door-only, bouncing with the same reason 11 / reason 4 the use action does — the shared `door_in_reach` is what keeps the two verbs' refusals identical · locking never moves the leaf (a door locks open; its owner is then the one who can shut it) and one EV_DOOR carries open + locked together, absolute, so a client never holds half a door · `PROTO_VER` 8, goldens regenerated: C→S action subtype 6 = lock (address + 1 bit) · S→C event subtype 22 grew its locked bit · every placed-deployable record grew one too (31 bits now), so the join walk carries which doors stand locked · **no `mine` bit**: the client predicts its own press either way and a refusal rolls the leaf back through the door v0 path, which is DESIGN §5.6's own mispredict example — a foreign locked door flashes for one RTT and corrects, and the alternative (a per-recipient record) was judged not worth making a broadcast differ per viewer · **no shared access this slice** — one owner id, no codes, no crew list, matching the hearth claim's own owner-only rule; whether sharing arrives as a code lock, a hearth auth list that doors inherit, or crews is the open question, and it is the operator's to answer · no lock item: the lock is a property of the door, not a deployable, so nothing in `content/` moved · web: L locks or unlocks the nearest door in the sim's own 5 m cell-center reach, and a locked leaf renders banded iron (cosmetics row) | sim-core `deploy.rs` `world.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` · server `core.rs` · client-core `core.rs` `bridge.rs` · web `src/*` |

| lighting v0 + the shadow gate | **Superseded in part by the lighting v1 row at the foot of this section (2026-08-03), which owns the transfer, the exposure, the hemisphere's earth half, the fog distances and the whole sky dome. What stays this row's, unchanged and re-measured green under v1: the sun's azimuth and elevation, the key's intensity, the hemisphere's sky half, every shadow-rig number, and the shadow gate's floors.** **One key, one fill, one bounded shadow map, one tone map** — the client had no shadow map at all and a 0.95 hemisphere doing all the work, so nothing cast, nothing received, and every object read as an untextured silhouette. **Key**: directional, azimuth 2.35 rad / elevation 0.36 rad (~21° — low, so everything rakes), colour `0xffe1b8`, intensity 3.0. **Fill**: hemisphere `0xa9c3e2` over `0x6b5f4a` at 1.15 — the whole ambient budget, and deliberately below the key (the gate asserts `key > fill`, because a fill that strong is the flat state this work ends). **Transfer**: `NeutralToneMapping` (Khronos PBR Neutral) at exposure 0.8, owned by the renderer, set on no material and followed by no post stack. Not ACES: its toe crushed the dark-albedo scatter (`0x2f6b33` pine) to near-black, which is a different bug from a dark scene — Neutral is identity below ~0.8 linear and only rolls off highlights, so the shadows this slice adds are the thing making the image dark. **Air**: fog `0x808f9c` 180–1000 m (was `0x8fb4d6` 250–900), and the sky is now a 24×16 back-face dome of radius 10 parked on the camera, vertex-coloured from the fog colour at the horizon to `0x2c4463` at the zenith on a `y^0.62` ramp. Geometry rather than a clear colour for one reason: fog is shaded and therefore tone-mapped, a clear colour is not, so painting the sky as geometry makes the horizon seam exact by construction instead of by tuning two numbers against each other. **Shadows**: `PCFSoftShadowMap`, 2048 px over an 80 m radius (texel 0.078 m), light parked 260 m back along the sun ray, ortho depth 1–520 m (relief is ~90 m, TERRAIN §6), depth bias 0 and normal bias **1.2 texels** (0.094 m) — in texels because the acne it fixes is a texel-footprint artefact. The box centre is snapped to its own texel grid **in light space** every frame (`threejs-shadow-systems`, clipmap ref §4) or the grid slides under the world and every silhouette shimmers; Z is quantized at 40 m because it moves depth coverage, not the projected grid. **The bound is real**: one map, so a caster more than 80 m from the player casts nothing. That is the one case the skill allows a single map, and the cascade/clipmap that would lift it is a later slice, not a claim made here. **Casters/receivers**: near ring both · far mesh receives only (it is the 8 m LOD of the ground the near ring already casts from — two disagreeing silhouettes in one map, for 131 k tris a frame) · scatter pools both · pieces, deployables, remotes both · water, sky dome, ghost and weak-mark neither. **`renderer.info.autoReset` is off** and `render()` resets it itself: three resets the counters *after* the shadow pass, so the defaults silently exclude every shadow draw — the exact half of the budget this rig adds. **Gate** (`ci/browser_smoke.mjs` assertions 9–11, all counted, none timed): the rig's flags; then the dev-only `shadowProbe` renders the live scene twice per yaw — key casting, then not — reads both frames back with `gl.readPixels` and requires the shadow pass to have darkened ≥ **1.5%** of the swept pixels by > **6/255** luma, over 4 yaws at pitch −0.45 from the pinned `dev_spawn` after a 4 s stream-in settle (pinned position on purpose: after the walk the frame depends on how much wall clock a shared box gave the input pump). Measured there: **9.39%** darkened, per-yaw 16.8 / 5.2 / 2.8 / 12.8, stable to the decimal across runs (materials v0's surfaces later moved this to **10.49%**, per-yaw 16.3 / 7.1 / 3.6 / 15.0 — same rig, brighter specular; the floors are unaffected). Two more floors exist because the aggregate alone was **shown not to bite**: dropping `castShadow` from the terrain and the scatter pools still scored 6.12%, because the other tab's avatar stands on the shared spawn and under a 21° sun throws a long enough shadow to carry two of the four yaws on its own. So every yaw must clear **1%** (that mutation left two at exactly 0.0%), and the shadow pass must submit ≥ **8 draw calls** (it submitted 2 — the avatar's two meshes — against 25 intact, the seven `frustumCulled=false` scatter pools among them). The same two renders are what let the next assertion put DESIGN §9's budget under a gate for the first time: **58 draw calls, 483,550 triangles** both passes, against < 300 / < 1.5 M. A third level would redraw that identical set at a third of the resolution and darken no new pixel; the table is generated, so the day the horizon casts, the levels come from a constant. **Ortho depth** is `half + margin(100 m) + below(260 m)`, a stated deviation from the reference's `margin + 2·half`, which leaves only `half` of coverage below the centre — 80 m at level 0, under the island's ~90 m relief. Lighting v0 carried 260 m and this keeps it. **Snapping**: each level quantizes its centre in light space by its OWN texel width (Z at half-width/2, coarse on purpose — it moves depth coverage, not the projected grid). **Normal bias** is **1.2 texels** at every level, so 0.094 m and 0.563 m in metres — the reference's §9, and the gate asserts the texel count is equal across levels rather than the metres. **Guard band 0.15, blend ratio 0.15**: containment runs in each level's own normalized shadow coordinates, so the sampled edge is 0.85 and the cross-fade the last 15% of it, and no per-level uniform can disagree with the committed centre. Weight is accumulated finest→coarsest and the leftover resolves to **unshadowed**, so past the coarsest level shadow fades out rather than ending. **Update policy**: level 0 dynamic (every frame, never touches the budget); coarse levels cached, **budget 1/frame**, **max age 64 frames**, ages staggered, and `invalidate(sphere)` force-dirties past the budget on near-chunk stream-in/out (its own bounding sphere) and on any piece or deployable appearing or vanishing. Measured over the gate's settle: level 0 **87** renders against level 1's **5**. **How it is built in WebGL** (the skill's API is WebGPU/TSL): N DirectionalLights, all casting, added finest-first so `directionalShadowMap[i]` is level i; **only level 0 carries intensity** (the coarse levels are depth textures that light nothing, or the key would be counted once per level); `shadow.autoUpdate = false` on every level, which makes three skip `updateMatrices` on the same test it skips the render — so `shadow.matrix`, and therefore the shader's containment box, is frozen to the last completed map by construction; and a patch on every `MeshStandardMaterial` that replaces three's per-light `getShadow(...)` in `lights_fragment_begin` with one `gatesClipmapShadow()` reading all levels. The stock call is read off the INSTALLED chunk and required to appear exactly once — a three upgrade that renames a shadow uniform throws at boot instead of quietly handing every light its own map. **Per-level filtering**: level 0 keeps three's `getShadow` (PCFSoft, **16** fetches — this row said 36, which was quoted from an older three and is wrong for the installed r178; corrected by the **fragment budget v0** row below, where the count is read off three's own chunk instead of retyped); coarse levels take ONE comparison, sampled unconditionally and selected afterwards (stricter than three's own, which branches around its taps). | the horizon casts + the horizon gate | **The ground casts at all, the far mesh casts outside the near ring, and the level table takes its constant** — `NOW.md` item 1, and the first half of it was not the level table. **The ground was never casting.** three derives the shadow pass's material from the surface one and flips the side (`shadowSide[FrontSide] = BackSide`), which is the closed-mesh answer to acne and exactly wrong for a heightfield: every terrain triangle faces the sky, the sun is in the sky, so all of them were culled out of every depth pass they were submitted to. Hills cast nothing at ANY distance; the only shadows in the frame came from the scatter, the pieces and the players. Fix is one line — `material.shadowSide = THREE.FrontSide` — and the acne the default was avoiding is what the per-level normal bias is already for. Measured: the shadow sweep goes **10.5% → 24.0%** of 3,686,400 pixels (worst yaw 3.6% → 20.4%). **The far mesh casts**, through a `MeshDepthMaterial` that discards every fragment whose world XZ is inside the near ring's current footprint, so each XZ column of the world has exactly one caster — 1 m mesh inside the ring, 8 m mesh outside it. The footprint is the box of LOADED near chunks (not the desired set: a queued chunk casts nothing, and stream-out keeps chunks one ring past the desired set and every one of them casts), republished on chunk arrival and teardown and never on the RAF path, and a move force-dirties the cached levels. **Skirt**: the far caster is sunk **3 m** below its own surface, which is `TERRAIN.md` §4's near↔far skirt in the only place a shadow can see one — the two LODs disagree by a couple of metres at the seam and at a 21° sun one metre is 2.6 m of shadow, so a sunk caster casts ~8 m late instead of painting a band along the hole's edge that slides with the player. **Levels**: max half-width **240 m → 720 m**, so the generated table is **80 / 240 / 720 m**, all at **1024 px** (texels 0.156 / 0.469 / 1.406 m). 720 because fog closes at 1000 m and 720 m of light-space half-width is ~2000 m of ground along the sun's bearing. **Map size 2048 → 1024 at level 0**: that 2048 was sized against a caster set with no ground in it, so the map was nearly empty and its resolution was free; a map that now gets FILLED every frame is 4.2 M depth fragments against 1.05 M, and a level cannot resolve detail its casters do not have — the finest caster here is a 1 m terrain grid and a 0.32 m pine trunk, and 0.156 m texels are six across the trunk. **Ortho depth is now derived, not flat.** A level bounds light-space X and Y; the ground inside those bounds spans `half·cot(elevation)` of light-space Z each way — 2.66 m per metre of half-width at this sun — so the shipped flat `half + 100 + 260` could not contain level 2's box (1913 m needed, 260 m carried) and clipped the far half of every level along the sun's bearing. Now `2·(half·cot(el) + relief/sin(el) + 100)`, with relief 90 m (`TERRAIN.md` §6) and the far cap raised 3000 → 8000 m. **Per-level caster sets**, because a caster only matters to level i if some pixel that READS level i shares its light-space column: the near ring and its scatter stop at level 1 (they span ±192 m; level 2 is read only past 204 m), the far mesh starts at level 1 (level 0's box is entirely inside the hole, so it would fill a map only to discard it — costing the band from the hole edge out to 227 m along the sun's bearing, which the ring already mostly covers and which every pixel past 68 m reads level 1 for). Built on `onBeforeShadow`/`onAfterShadow` collapsing the geometry's draw range, since three has no per-light caster filter. Submitting everything to everything measured **1,483,382 triangles** a frame against DESIGN §9's 1.5 M. **Two shader fixes the level count forced**, both pre-existing and both multiplied by it: `gatesClipmapShadow()` is now evaluated once per FRAGMENT rather than once per light (its call site is inside three's unrolled directional loop, so the clipmap was read once per LEVEL — 74 depth fetches a fragment at two levels where 37 would do, and 114 at three), and the loop's `RE_Direct` is selected to iteration 0 at compile time (`#if UNROLLED_LOOP_INDEX == 0`), since levels 1.. carry zero intensity and were running a full GGX lobe to add nothing. What is quotable about that is **counted, not timed**: per fragment, 2 lights x 37 fetches = 74 before, 38 after at three levels (114 un-hoisted), and one GGX lobe where there were N. The milliseconds are NOT quotable and no number here rides on them — the gate box is shared and its frame time swings 530-680 ms across runs on the SAME tree, which a re-judge measured directly (536 on the builder's run, 664 on the judge's). What is real and not a stopwatch reading: before the hoist, this slice starved a third browser tab of enough CPU to miss a 60 s WebTransport handshake, repeatably; after it, the public tab joins. **Gate** (`ci/browser_smoke.mjs`, all counted, none timed): the shadow-sweep floors raised **1.5% → 15%** aggregate and **1% → 10%** per yaw, so a terrain material that forgets `shadowSide` now fails outright (10.5% / 3.6%); a per-level assertion that each ortho depth covers its own box at the published sun elevation; the far caster's structure (`shadowSide` is FrontSide, the far mesh casts, it casts through a custom depth material, the skirt is non-zero, the hole is a real chunk box that CONTAINS every loaded chunk and the player, the near ring stops short of the coarsest level and the far mesh starts past level 0); and the **horizon probe** (4 yaws, lifted 80 m, pitched −0.42, ordinary near plane and FOV — no near-plane trick is needed here), which holds the frame fixed and removes the caster — opening the hole to swallow the world discards every far-mesh fragment, which is exactly the state that shipped before this slice. It is a claim about distance for free: the hole means every pixel it darkens was cast by geometry more than the ring's footprint away. Measured **0.54%** of 3,686,400 swept pixels at mean Δluma **45-48/255** (the same order as the near shadows), on **2 of 4** yaws — floors 0.15% aggregate and 2 directions above 0.1%, deliberately not every yaw, because past the ring the only caster is the ground itself and where the island has relief is worldgen's fact, not the caster's. Plus a control per yaw (two renders of one state must differ nowhere) and a lifted-pixel ceiling (removing a caster cannot darken). Budget after: **147 draw calls, 1,053,442 triangles**, peak, all passes, against < 300 / < 1.5 M. The probe is dev-only and the public-shard tab asserts its absence alongside the other four (`horizonProbe` added to assertion 12’s hook list in the same commit — a probe that force-redraws every level and reads back twelve full frames is not a thing to ship to a public shard). | web `src/shadows.js` `src/terrain.js` `src/materials.js` `src/scene.js` `src/main.js` · `ci/browser_smoke.mjs` · TERRAIN §4 |
| materials v0 + the surface gate | **Four authored identities, one shared field, every channel from the same causes** — lighting v0 gave the world shape and it still had no *surface*: everything was `MeshLambertMaterial` at a flat colour, so nothing had roughness, nothing had detail, the ground was a per-vertex biome colour plus a ±0.035 per-vertex tint jitter, and the scatter pines were 0x2f6b33 repeated three hundred times. This is the splat material `TERRAIN.md` §4 always specified, built with **no textures at all**. **Identities** (the retired vertex palette's own colours, so the surface changed and the art direction did not): sand `0.78/0.71/0.52` rough 0.92 · grass `0.35/0.49/0.23` rough 0.82 · litter `0.15/0.33/0.16` rough 0.88 · rock `0.50/0.48/0.46` rough 0.70, with bump **strengths** 0.35/0.6/0.9/2.2 — unitless, because the two bump octaves are 5.6× apart in wavelength and one amplitude in metres cannot serve both (0.06 m over a 9.5 m wave is a slope of 0.006, which is a surface nobody can see; that mis-scaling was found by the probe below, at 1.2%). **Weights** ride the geometry as a 4-byte normalized `splat` attribute (4 B where the baked colour cost 12), written by the terrain worker from the sim's own (height, moisture, slope) as soft ramps **centred on `sim-core` `biome()`'s own hard edges**: beach 1–3 m (edge 2), alpine 44–60 m (edge 52), forest moisture 0.01–0.09 (edge 0.05), cliff 0.8–1.2 × tan 50° — a one-hot attribute would put a biome seam on the vertex grid that no in-shader noise can hide. **The field**: one hash-without-sine value noise, quintic, sampled three times per pixel at 1/48 · 1/9.5 · 1/1.7 m and shared by every channel (the skill's first failure mode is a PBR stack where each channel samples its own noise). It breaks the weights up by ±0.34 before a square-sharpen and renormalize, mottles albedo by ±0.16/0.11/0.07 per octave, varies roughness by ±0.18, and drives a surface-gradient bump (no tangents, no UVs — there are none) at **0.55 m and 0.09 m per octave**, both faded out by pixel footprint (meso 2→7 m, micro 0.3→1.1 m) so no normal survives below a pixel, then a specular-AA term at gain 0.5 widens the lobe by the perturbed normal's own variance. **Causal modifiers, each driving colour AND roughness together**: waterline wetness (−0.4→1.6 m, ×0.68 darker, roughness→0.28) which is what retired the separate sea-floor palette entry — 0.68 × sand lands on it — snow on high rock (52→80 m, to the retired `C_PEAK` `0.72/0.72/0.75`, roughness→0.55), and cliff darkening ×0.86 ramped over normal.y 0.78→0.60 (the sim's 50° threshold is 0.643). **Off the ground**: authored response bundles rather than colours — wood 0.95/0 · stone 0.88/0 · metal 0.42/**0.8** · foliage 0.86/0 · rock 0.85/0 · ore 0.55/**0.45** · cloth 0.78/0 · water **0.14**/0, so the build-tier read is sheen as much as colour (`bases.webp`) and a locked door wears the metal response with the iron colour. **Scatter** keeps one draw call per archetype and stops being one flat colour anyway: a merged trunk + skirt + crown pine with baked vertex-colour ramps (trunk 0x3a2a1e→0x53402d, skirt 0x1e4423→0x39733a, crown 0x2c5c2c→0x4d8845), plus a per-instance tint hashed from the slot's own cell (amplitude 0.17 pine / 0.19 bush / 0.10–0.12 nodes / 0.08 barrel, value and warm-cool on independent bits) — derived, never stored, so a chunk that streams out and back gets the same forest. **Gate** (`ci/browser_smoke.mjs` assertions 13–15, all counted, none timed): the structural facts (standard material, splat uniforms installed, four identities, ≥3 distinct roughnesses and ≥3 distinct bump strengths, three distinct tier roughnesses, metal a conductor and wood/stone not, water < 0.3, every scatter pool standard + vertex-coloured + instance-tinted with at least one metallic and at least one non-empty); then the **splat census**, ~106 k near-ring vertices read straight off the geometry, because a shader can blend four identities perfectly and still paint one if what it is fed is constant — measured spreads **1.00/1.00/0.00/0.16**, present 1.9/99.9/0.0/0.1%, deepest second identity **0.50**, **1.5%** of the ring a genuine blend (floors 2 channels ≥ 0.15 spread, 2 identities ≥ 1% present, second ≥ 0.35, mixed ≥ 0.5%; pinning the weights to a constant vector takes all four spreads to 0.000). Deliberately **not** an assertion about which biomes are in view: the moisture channel's features are ~700 m across, so a 320 m ring is usually one biome, and that is worldgen's fact, not the material's. Then the **surface probe**, the same 2N-frame shape as the shadow probe: the live scene rendered twice per yaw with `uSurface` at 1 and 0 over 4 yaws at pitch −0.7, holding the vertex weights, the four identities and the causal modifiers fixed and removing every contribution of the noise field (weight break-up, mottling, roughness variation, bump), so the delta is the field and nothing else (steeper than the shadow probe's: this one is about the ground). Measured **12.81%** of 3,686,400 pixels moved by > 6/255 luma, per yaw 2.4 / 6.8 / 8.4 / 33.7, stable to the decimal. Floors 4% aggregate and 1% per yaw — and a third, because those two were **shown not to bite**: collapsing the noise scales so the whole ring lands in one lattice cell moved **66–96%** of the pixels and sailed past both, because a constant field is a uniform darkening. So every yaw must move pixels **both ways** ≥ 0.2% (that mutation scored +0.00% up on two of four yaws; the worst real yaw is +0.5%). Budget after: **58 draw calls, 492,118 triangles**, both passes, against < 300 / < 1.5 M. Both probes are dev-only and the public-shard tab asserts their absence alongside `shadowProbe`. | web `src/materials.js` `src/terrain.js` `src/terrainWorker.js` `src/scene.js` `src/main.js` · `ci/browser_smoke.mjs` · TERRAIN §4 |
| fragment budget v0 + the cost probe | **What a fragment costs, counted — and what this box can and cannot time** — `NOW.md` item 1's blocker. Grain (`loop/m1-surface-grain`, unmerged) measured well and did not merge because the browser gate's third tab missed its 60 s join, and the two suspects named were per-fragment cost and program size. Nothing in the tree could weigh either: assertions 9–15 all prove a system *reaches* the image, and every one works by weighting a term to zero with a uniform — which removes no instruction. **The near filter's cost was quoted, not read.** `shadows.js` `LEVEL_FILTER_TAPS` was a hand-typed `[36, 1]`, and 36 described three r1xx (nine `texture2DShadowLerp`, four fetches each). The installed **r178 compiles a different kernel: sixteen `texture2DCompare` over a 4×4 footprint, weighted 1/9**. It is now derived from the installed `shadowmap_pars_fragment` and throws if that branch is renamed — the same rule the `getShadow(…)` call site was already held to. So the clipmap costs **18** depth fetches per shaded fragment (16 + 1 + 1), not 38, and the headroom `NOW.md` pointed at was less than half what it assumed. **Program size is mostly three's.** Terrain fragment program, `#include`s expanded: **80,563 chars**, of which **73,375 (91.1%) is three's stock `MeshStandardMaterial`** — captured before the first replace, so it is the template three handed over and not something inferred — and **7,188 (8.9%) is everything this repo added to the ground**: splat graph, four identities, causal modifiers, both bump octaves and the clipmap patch. Within that, the clipmap shadow GLSL is 2,503 chars (`ship` − `noshadow`) and the field's three sample lines are 617 (`ship` − `nofield`); neither is a whole subsystem's source, because `gmNoise` and every expression consuming it stay in every variant. **The first cut of this row said 78,060 was stock and 2,503 was everything of ours, which is wrong and was caught in judging**: 78,060 is the `noshadow` VARIANT, still carrying the splat graph and the field. A variant is the shipped program minus one term; it is not the unpatched one. The gate measures the unpatched template directly now and asserts it is both non-zero and smaller than the patched program. **The probe**: `GameScene.costProbe` compiles the ground five ways (`ship` / `nofield` / `near1` / `noshadow` / `noskip`, `materials.js` TERRAIN_VARIANTS) plus a **control** — the shipped program swept a second time at the far end of the run, so the probe publishes its own resolution. Per run: compile timed on the first render through each program; fill from a 3-scale viewport sweep (1 / 0.5 / 0.25, projection untouched so coverage is fixed), min of 4 single-frame samples per scale, least squares ms against Mpx; and each variant's frame read back and differenced against two references from the shipped program. **The control is the load-bearing part, and what it mostly reports is that this box cannot do this.** Over five runs, two sweeps of *identical* work differed by **0.3%, 2.7%, 3.8%, 10.5% and 51.1%** of frame — the floor is itself a one-sample estimate and swings 170×, so "× the floor" is indicative and never a confidence bound. Read against it, exactly one thing measured with a consistent sign: **the whole shadow term, 28 / 176 / 81 / 86 / 150 ms — 2.9–17.1% of frame, right sign 5/5** (though on the noisiest two runs it sat under that run's own floor). Level 0's 16-fetch PCF did **not** converge (+18 / −98 / −42 / −584 / −567 ms), and twice it was self-refuting: `near1` still takes 3 fetches to `noshadow`'s 0, so the PCF delta cannot exceed the whole shadow term's in the same run, and it was 7× and 3.8× it. The noise field did not resolve either — wrong sign in 3 of 5 runs. Compiling one of these programs is **order a second**: 2.0–4.0 s of first render against a 0.5–1.7 s already-compiled floor. **Every one of those is TIMED on a shared 4-core box through a software rasterizer and is not a reference-hardware claim** (DESIGN §14); the counted numbers above are. The consequence for `NOW.md` item 1: the ~9% frame delta grain rejected itself on is the same order as this floor, so that rejection was never a measurement. **Taken while here**: the micro octave is skipped where its own footprint fade has already retired it — every use of it is multiplied by a fade that is exactly zero there, so it is bit-exact, and the `noskip` variant exists to gate that rather than argue it (**0 px differ, max 0/255**). **Gate** (`ci/browser_smoke.mjs` assertion 16): counted — ≤ **24** depth fetches/fragment (18 today; the cap leaves room for a fourth level or a wider coarse kernel and still fails a near filter that becomes the most expensive thing in the shader) and ≤ **96,000** chars of resolved GLSL (80,563 today, ~19% headroom, survives a three minor bump and catches a doubled program), the clipmap's published total equal to the sum of its per-level taps, the ground rendering the `ship` variant and not a probe's, and the `#include` expansion having actually expanded. Then attribution, because a time between two programs is worth nothing without the image between them: the probe's zero point measured (`ship` vs its own reference frame, **0 px**), `nofield` landing on the `uSurface = 0` image within 1/255 (stated before the run — two separately compiled programs may schedule identical arithmetic differently), `noskip` identical to `ship` at **0/255 exactly** (a stricter tolerance, and it earns it: where the fade is nonzero both programs evaluate the same expression, and where it is zero the octave is multiplied by exactly 0), `noshadow` darkening **0** pixels (removing a shadow term can only brighten) while brightening some, every variant submitting **identical draw calls** at every scale, and an **instrument self-test**: a synced frame must be ≥ **2×** unsynced draw submission. That last one is there because the first cut of this probe used `gl.finish()` as its barrier — through Chrome's command buffer it returns once the commands are consumed, not once the pixels exist, so every variant “measured” 0.2 ms and the gate's own fit caught it (a frame whose cost did not rise with its pixel count). The barrier is a 1-pixel `readPixels` now and the ratio is ~1900×. No timing threshold is asserted anywhere in this gate. Probe is dev-only, builds its variant programs only on a dev shard, and the public-shard tab asserts its absence alongside the other five. | web `src/shadows.js` `src/materials.js` `src/terrain.js` `src/scene.js` `src/main.js` · `ci/browser_smoke.mjs` · DESIGN §9 |
| materials v1 — grain + the contrast gate, and the join instrument | **A fourth octave that only exists at arm's length, priced against the run's own floor — and the gate's own eyes, fixed.** Materials v0's finest octave is 1.7 m across, so at arm's length the ground was smooth-shaded mottling with no *texture* at all. Grain is one more sample of the same shared field, at a wavelength the **identity** chooses: sand 25 /m (4 cm), grass 8.3 /m, litter 11 /m, rock 16.7 /m, with a per-identity **ridge fold** (0 = soft stipple, 1 = `1-|2g-1|` crystalline speckle: sand 0.15, grass 0.55, litter 0.45, rock 0.80) and a per-identity **contrast** (0.10/0.20/0.22/0.14). One tap, not four — scale, shape and contrast are each a dot product against the same splat weights, so a biome boundary *morphs* one grain into another instead of cross-fading two. It also drives bump (**0.008 m**, a surface slope of 0.07–0.29 against its own wavelengths — the same band the two octaves above were chosen in) and roughness (**±0.10**), so it is a surface and not a decal. **Retired by pixel footprint in CYCLES PER PIXEL** (`0.03 → 0.09`), not metres, because the wavelength is a per-identity number and one metre threshold cannot serve four: every identity's grain dies on one curve, each at its own distance, and retiring at ~11 px/cycle means the octave is gone while still comfortably resolved rather than at the moment it stops being.
