The fade needs the **world** footprint (`fwidth(vGmPos)`), not the horizontal one — a pixel on a steep face barely moves in XZ while covering metres of surface — and the whole block is skipped behind the cheap horizontal footprint, which is a lower bound on it. `uGrain` is its own probe handle, separate from `uSurface`, and ships at **1**; the gate reads the live uniform back. **The cost question `NOW.md` item 1 asked, answered.** Grain's first attempt (`loop/m1-surface-grain`, unmerged) rejected itself on a ~9% frame delta measured against nothing. `costProbe` now compiles a **`nograin`** variant — the octave's instructions gone, not weighted to zero — beside `nofield`/`near1`/`noshadow`/`noskip`, and the gate asserts it lands on the `uGrain = 0` image (≤ 1/255 luma) so the millisecond difference is grain's and no other edit's. **Every run of the gate takes this reading again, and it has never once come out the same way.** The six taken while this slice was built: **+14 ms (0.3× the floor), −74 ms (1.7×), −64 ms (1.3×), −110 ms (2.2×), −104 ms (8.2×), −62 ms (0.1×) — five of six the WRONG SIGN, less work measured slower.** The ×-the-floor figures are indicative only, because the floor is a one-sample estimate too and swung **13–603 ms** across those same six runs (once 54% of the frame). Whatever the next run prints is a seventh reading, not a correction to these. So **this box cannot price the grain octave**: it lands exactly where level 0's PCF landed, inside the run-to-run noise a two-sweep control measures at 44–89 ms. That is the answer, not a failure — the decision moves to the **counted** budget, where grain is quotable anywhere: terrain fragment program **80,563 → 81,520 chars** (of which the octave is **638**; three's unpatched `MeshStandardMaterial` is still 73,375 = 90.0%), **4 noise sample sites per fragment against 3**, depth fetches unchanged at 18/24. All under budget. **The grain gate** (`ci/browser_smoke.mjs` assertion 15b) is a **contrast** measure, because 15's pixel count cannot tell a fourth octave from a fourth tint — a wash moves every pixel it touches. `contrastProbe` renders three frames per view (on, the same state again as a CONTROL, off) and scores mean neighbour-to-neighbour luma difference over the pixels the toggle moved, in both states. Measured at the pinned spawn: near view (pitch −1.05) **10.01% of the frame moved (+6.41/−3.60), contrast 0.17 → 1.02 luma/px = ×6.12**; far view (lifted 60 m, pitch −0.42) **0.000% moved**; **control noise 0 pixels at both**. Floors: 5% moved near, ×2.0 contrast, 0.5% each direction, ≤ 0.5% far, control exactly 0 — plus structural ones (≥ 3 distinct wavelengths, ≥ 3 distinct ridge shapes, every scale > 4× the micro octave's 0.588 /m, the fade rising and retiring at ≤ 1 cycle/px). **What this pass did NOT do:** grain is sampled on world XZ, which is stretched by `1/upness` on a slope — invisible at 1.7 m, a combed hillside at 4 cm. Triplanar for the grain alone is the fix and it is `NOW.md`'s next surface item; the shape that worked (measured on the abandoned branch, surviving in no tree) is the ridge fold applied **per plane** before the blend and the blend's deviation restored by `1/|w|`. **The join instrument.** The first grain attempt died on "the browser gate's third tab missed its 60 s join" and so did this one, twice — until the failure path was made to say *where*: `#start` hidden, `__gatesDebug` present, client in the world, and **2 polls in 62.6 seconds**. The gate had spent its whole window asking. Two repairs, neither touching an assertion or the timeout: the look is **one** `page.evaluate` carrying only `inWorld`/`snapshots`/`starterr` instead of two carrying every fact the client publishes (which every slice since has grown), and looks are **launched on a 250 ms cadence with up to 4 outstanding** instead of each waiting for the last — so the join is seen within one round trip of becoming true, not one round trip of the next poll's turn. Measured effect on the same box: 2 looks → 13–19, and both the poll count and the page state now print on the passing path too, because an instrument starving is a slow failure. | web `src/materials.js` `src/scene.js` `src/main.js` · `ci/browser_smoke.mjs` · TERRAIN §4 |
| materials v1 — the projection: grain, triplanar | **The grain octave is laid ON the surface instead of stamped through it from above, and the gate that says so measures COARSENING across a tilt, not pixels.** The defect the two passes above left behind: a world-XZ field on a face of upness `u` is stretched by `1/u` along the fall line and untouched across it. At the micro octave's 1.7 m that is a smear nobody reads as wrong; at grain's 4–12 cm it is a hillside combed downhill, and grain is the only octave a player ever sees at arm's length. **The fix is triplanar for the grain and nothing else** — every other octave keeps world XZ — and the shape is the one the abandoned branch measured, both halves load-bearing: the **ridge fold applied per plane, BEFORE the blend** (the average of three ridged fields is not the ridge of an average — fold last and rock's crystalline speckle turns soft on every face that is not axis-aligned) and **the blend's deviation restored by `1/|w|`** (three independent fields under convex weights carry `|w|` of one field's spread — 1.0 on an axis, 0.577 at the 45-45-45 corner — so a stock blend does not reproject the octave, it fades it out on exactly the faces it was reprojected for; the abandoned branch measured **×0.56** the contrast that way). **No sharpening exponent**: the weights are raw `|n|` normalized to sum 1, because a `pow(k)` is a knob nobody has spoken and neither measured half needs one. On level ground the weights are `(0,1,0)` exactly, `|w|` is exactly 1, and the tap is bit-for-bit the one it replaces — the change is confined to slopes by construction. **The price, counted** (`NOW.md` item 1 rules the timed question out on this box by name, five of six readings the wrong sign): grain's noise sample sites **1 → 3**, so the ground pays **6 per fragment against 4**, and the terrain fragment program **81,520 → 81,820 chars** (the octave 638 → 938; three's unpatched `MeshStandardMaterial` still 73,375 = 89.7%). Depth fetches unchanged at 18/24. A **noise-sample budget of 8/fragment** joins the depth-fetch and program-size caps, one octave of headroom over what ships. **The gate** (`ci/browser_smoke.mjs` assertion 15c) is a within-run comparison of two compiled programs, because an absolute number about a surface's shape is not something this box can be trusted with: **`flatgrain`** is the shipped program carrying materials v1's world-XZ tap and nothing else changed, and `projectionProbe` renders both at two cameras — square-on to a slope, square-on to level ground — toggling `uGrain` at each and scoring the octave's own difference image. The face is **found, not written down** (a seed that moved the spawn onto a meadow would otherwise aim the probe at level ground and pass by default): vertices within 150 m are binned on a 4 m grid, and the steepest bin that is **coherent** (mean-normal length ≥ 0.9) and **lit** (`dot(n, sun)` ≥ 0.35 — an unlit face puts the octave's albedo swing under the probe's own threshold, which cost a run to discover) wins, deterministically, chunks walked in sorted key order. The measure is **`(gradX + gradY) / (2·amp)`** — the octave's detail, direction-averaged and normalized by its own amplitude — at both cameras, per program; the ratio between them is how much that program's grain coarsens when the ground tilts, and every confound (distance, splat identity, fade, light, curvature, mask selection) sits in both programs' numerator and denominator alike. Measured on the 46.6° face this spawn offers (upness 0.687, a **×1.456** stretch): world XZ coarsens **×2.017**, triplanar **×1.397** — a gain of **×1.444**, floor 1.15, where a program that lost the fix scores exactly 1.000 (verified by neutering the tap's normal read: both programs then read ×2.017 and the gate fails). Amplitude **×1.044** against a 0.9 floor, which is what refuses the mush a missing `1/|w|` would buy. The probe measures the **albedo channel alone** (`uGrainAmp` held at zero): a bump reaches the image through `−∇h·L`, so its screen anisotropy belongs to the sun, and at 21° elevation it buried the projection under itself. **Every bar 15c holds, in full, because a registry row that lists most of a gate is how the one it omits goes unexamined:** grain must reach ≥ **2%** of the face view and ≥ **1%** of the flat one in both programs (measured 23.4/12.4 and 2.7/2.4) or the ratios are taken over nothing; the face must be ≤ **0.9** upness and ≥ **0.9** coherent and ≥ **0.35** lit, found by binning vertices within **150 m** on a **4 m** grid at ≥ **8** vertices a bin; stretch gain ≥ **1.15** (measured ×1.444); amplitude ≥ **0.9** (×1.044); the flat pair must agree within **15%** (6.4% — "level" near this spawn is 6° of slope, not 0°); two renders of one state must differ **nowhere** (0 px at every view, every program); where the octave is retired 60 m up the two programs must differ on ≤ **0.02%** of the frame (14 px of 921,600 — the same allowance `COST_IDENTITY_MAX_DELTA` makes for separately scheduled arithmetic); and the **confinement pair**, which is the "level ground is unchanged" claim measured instead of derived — the two projections must sit ≥ **0.15** of the octave's own amplitude apart on the face (**0.534**, max 16/255) and ≤ **0.10** apart on level ground (**0.022**, max **1/255** over 14.1% of the frame). Two bars there and not one ratio, because a tap that ignores the normal makes both programs identical and any ratio bar passes `0 ≥ 0`. **The first cut of that pair was wrong and was caught in judging**, which is why the numbers above are the second set: `projectionProbe` kept ONE reference frame across its view loop, so each program's face and flat frames were compared against the other program's *retired* frame — a camera 60 m up — and the assertion was reading the difference between two landscapes as the difference between two projections (4.867 and 2.494, where the octave itself bounds a correct reading at ~2). The reference is now one buffer **per view**; the corrected readings are the ones quoted. **What is reported and deliberately NOT asserted:** the screen-axis split `gradX/gradY`. It looks like the directional measure a directional defect deserves and it is not one — the level control scores 1.11 in both programs and the face scores 0.39–0.42 in both, a 2.5× bias shared by both programs that belongs to a 107° frustum over curved terrain and is four times what the projection is worth. Direction-averaging drops it; asserting on it would have been asserting on the frustum. **Still open:** `gmHash4` (four lattice corners in one `vec4` body, image-identical by construction, gateable the way `noskip` is via a `noh4` variant) — `NOW.md` item 1 keeps it. | web `src/materials.js` `src/scene.js` `src/terrain.js` `src/main.js` · `ci/browser_smoke.mjs` · TERRAIN §4 |
| melee combat v0 + combat wire v11 | **The swing that fells a tree now lands on a person, and the numbers are all `content/`'s.** Until this row nothing in the world could hurt anything: piece and deployable hp decayed, `content/weapons.toml` was data nothing played, and a locked door was furniture (the merge-gate judge's ranked gap 1, both rounds, `findings/pass-20260802-001255-01-judge.md`). **Every number comes from content, none from code**: damage and reach are `weapons.toml`'s melee rows (`spear_wood` 25 / 2 m, `spear_metal` 30 / 2 m), max hp is `balance.toml` `globals.player_hp` = 100 — the same value the TTK anchor divides by, so the band the data declares (melee 3–5 body hits) and the band the sim plays are one number; `bake_combat` refuses a melee row that deals nothing or reaches nowhere, and `content.rs`'s `bake_combat_plays_the_band_the_data_declares` re-derives hits-to-kill from the *baked* table and asserts it inside `ttk_melee`. **Reused, not re-spoken**: the swing cadence (38 ticks, gather verb v0 — which is the melee rows' own ~47/min), the 30° planar aim cone, the ±3 m vertical window and the 0.04 m² point-blank bypass, all shared with `gather::swing` so one arm has one aim; `gather::swing` gets first claim on every swing and hands on only a whiff (a tree in reach always outranks the person behind it). **Proposed defaults, this row's own**: hp is granted at join and announced there (a bar you first see when something hurts you is no bar); **death v0 = immediate respawn at the spawn ring's next generation, inventory and craft queue destroyed** — the ring's candidate sequence is walked forward by the death count, so no second selector and no second constant, and destruction rather than a drop is the honest reading until a ground container exists (`Player.deaths` is sim state and hashed). **Deliberately absent, each for a stated reason, none of them an oversight**: no unarmed damage (`weapons.toml` has no unarmed row and inventing one is inventing a number — a bare hand and a stack of wood are the same swing: nothing) · no headshots (aim is planar until M2's rewound raycasts, so there is no head) · no armor (`armor.toml` bakes into nothing yet) · no per-weapon cadence · no structure damage (no melee-vs-structure column exists, and inventing one would move the raid-ratio anchor `test_content` asserts) · no backpack (the next slice; until it lands a kill takes but does not pay). **Wire v11, goldens regenerated in the same commit** (wall 6): three S→C event subtypes — hit (attacker-only, the hitmarker), health (own, absolute hp + max, so a missed one repairs itself), death (broadcast, victim + killer, the kill feed's raw material). No datagram layout moved: hp is an own-fact and a one-shot broadcast, so it rides the reliable lane when it changes instead of costing every entity record a field every tick; remote health bars are a snapshot bump of their own. **Gates added**: `probe_combat` — a third parity export, 500 three-bot brawls × 256 ticks, native and wasm byte-equal, its own probe rather than three lines inside `probe_parity` because a brawl empties the pockets that probe's build/craft/deploy coverage depends on; a duel inside `test_alloc_zero`'s counted window with a `deaths > before` assert, so the damage/death/respawn paths are counted and not merely reachable; ten sim tests (`tests/combat.rs`), two content tests, four bridge-smoke checks that hand-frame all three messages through the raw C ABI, and a browser assertion that reads the vitals stack out of the DOM and compares it against `content/balance.toml` parsed by the gate — content → bake → sim → wire → wasm → HUD, end to end, with no number typed into the gate. | CONTENT §4 · sim-core `combat.rs` · `NOW.md` item 1 |
| death backpack v0 + backpack wire v12 | **A kill pays now: what you carried stays where you fell, and someone can pick it up.** Melee v0 could take a life and nothing off the body — `world::respawn` destroyed the inventory, because the world had no ground container (the merge-gate judge's ranked gap 1, two passes running, `findings/archive-prestamp/pass-20260802-025624-01-judge.md`). **One bag per body, not thirty items** — NETCODE.md §6.4's shipped consolidation, minus the ragdoll (a cosmetic mesh, content for later). **Despawn is content's, proposed here**: `content/balance.toml` `[backpack]` — `despawn_base_min = 5` × the rarity multiplier of the rarest item inside (`mult_common/uncommon/rare/very_rare = 1/4/8/12`), which is exactly the 5/20/40/60-minute ladder NETCODE §6.4 cites, resolved per item by `bake_backpack` against the rarity column `items.toml` has always declared "drives the despawn multiplier". `validate::structural` refuses a zero base and a ladder that does not rise strictly — a falling ladder would make a rare bag despawn *sooner* than a common one. **Reused, not re-spoken**: reach is `build::BUILD_REACH_M` (5 m), the same arm a hearth feed, a door use and a lock already share; the take announces itself with `EV_GATHER`, so loot pays in the `+N Item` toast gathering already pays in. **Proposed defaults, this row's own**: `MAX_BACKPACKS = 256` with overflow **evicting the bag nearest its own despawn** — NETCODE §6.4's "oldest-lowest-tier first" collapsed into the one key that already encodes both, counted by a `BAG_GONE_EVICTED` removal, and never *refusing* (a death that silently kept its inventory would make the cap a way to dodge the loss); the take is **all-that-fits from the nearest bag in reach**, with what does not fit staying in the bag and an emptied bag leaving at once; the loot action is **payload-free** — the sim picks, so nothing spoofable crosses; the craft queue is still destroyed (its inputs were spent when it was queued, and refunding them into the bag would pay the killer twice for one farm). **Deliberately absent, each for a stated reason**: no per-slot container UI (its own slice; a half-container that can only be emptied is honest where a broken grid is not) · no bag hp, so a bag cannot be destroyed without opening it · no ground drops for a full inventory (`gather::inv_add` still loses that overflow; its own lane) · no rarity tier on the wire (the client needs to draw a bag and reach for it, not to know what is in one). **Inert content (`base_ticks == 0`) still destroys the inventory outright**, exactly as before this row: a new table may add a rule, never silently change one. **Wire v12, goldens regenerated in the same commit** (wall 6): the loot action — the ninth, which widened the C→S action subtype field 3 → 4 bits, so **every action message moved by one bit** — and three S→C event subtypes (bag dropped, join sync, removed-with-reason). No datagram layout moved: a bag is class-S furniture on the reliable lane, like a placed deployable. The event subtype space is now 30 of 32. **Gates added**: 11 sim tests (`tests/backpack.rs` — the drop and its position, the take, the partial take, reach in and out, the despawn tick not one early and not one late, the rarity raise, the inert path, the naked death, and that a bag is hashed state); 2 content tests (the ladder bakes from the shipped set, item by item, and the three refusals); 3 wire tests (`backpack_wire.rs` — kill → broadcast → loot → removal through real encoded bytes, a late joiner repaired by the sync walk, and a 20 m loot refused by distance alone), each asserted twice, once on the client mirror and once on the decoded bytes the server actually sent; `test_alloc_zero`'s duellists now loot every counted tick and the window asserts a bag was dropped AND one emptied by a take; `probe_combat` arms the ladder and one bot reaches for a bag every tick, so the scan, take, removal and despawn sweep are byte-identical native and wasm; four golden fixtures; ~20 bridge-smoke checks hand-framing all three subtypes through the raw C ABI; and a browser assertion that the loot key runs in a real browser and the bag set holds at zero. `test_replay`'s golden regenerated in the same commit — that script arms no combat, so what moved is the two new zero-length fields entering the digest. | NETCODE §6.4 · DESIGN §2 · sim-core `backpack.rs` · `NOW.md` item 1 |
| ground drops v0 — a full pack stops destroying what it is paid (systems lane, 2026-08-14) | **PROPOSED, and it invents no number.** The merge-gate judge's ranked gap 2 (`findings/pass-20260813-230343-07-judge.md`): *"`gather::inv_add` still loses overflow, so a full pack quietly eats the pickup"* — and `backpack.rs`'s own header had carried it as the last line v0 did not do, while the "gather bounds & overflow" row above deferred it to *"until ground drops land with the death/backpack slice"*. That slice landed 2026-08-06, so the condition had been met for a week. **No new archetype**: `Backpacks::stand_up` is already the one insert path for a death bag, a smashed barrel and an animal corpse, and its doc says a spill is the same object. **The mechanism is a spill buffer, not a store reference**: `gather::swing` and `craft::step` write the remainder into a caller-owned `[ItemStack; INV_SLOTS]` and `world.rs` drains it once per player per tick, which is how gather and craft keep their promise to own no container store — exactly the split `Swing::Smashed` already makes between the barrel's bit and the barrel's loot. **One drain point, at a fixed place in the tick**, because two producers each standing their own bag up is CLAUDE.md's single-consumer trap one level over. **Three design calls, each reusing a number rather than adding one.** (1) **The merge is what bounds it**: a spill lands in the nearest bag already within `LOOT_REACH_M` before it mints one, so a player swinging at a full pack costs one bag however long they swing rather than one every 38 ticks — without it, `MAX_BACKPACKS = 256` churns in about five and a half minutes of one player farming and evicts other people's death bags to do it (256 bags × `SWING_INTERVAL_TICKS` 38 ÷ `TICK_HZ` 30 = 324 s; this row said "about three minutes" and `backpack.rs` said five, one derivation disagreeing with itself inside one commit — corrected 2026-08-14 off the merge-gate judge's ranked fix 1, pass -08). The radius is not a new knob: the arm that decides you may open a bag decides your spill can reach it, and the pick is `loot_nearest`'s own nearest-first, ties-to-lower-index rule. (2) **A growing bag's clock is pushed out and never pulled in**, or dropping wood into a partly-looted bag would shorten the clock its rare contents bought — the despawn ladder paying backwards. (3) **The inert ladder still disarms it whole**: `base_ticks == 0` destroys the overflow exactly as before, so content that never armed the module gets the pre-backpack world and no shard grows litter it did not ask for. **Wire-free and save-free**: no new event, no `PROTO_VER` bump, no format bump — a bag is a bag, the per-tick sync already carries a content change, and `GOLDEN_FINAL_HASH` did not move because the replay probe's bots never fill a pack. **Armed on the two paths that PAY a player first, and on the four that give one back later the same day**: a demolish refund (`build.rs`), a deployable pick-up and a lock removal (`deploy.rs`, `lock.rs`) and a craft cancel's refund (`craft.rs`) all take the same buffer now, drained by `World::drain_spill` — six producers, one drain, the owner named in code. **The address question `NOW.md` §0sp2 held them for is answered by arithmetic rather than taste**: all six verbs refuse beyond `BUILD_REACH_M`, `LOOT_REACH_M` *is* `BUILD_REACH_M`, and that is the merge radius, so the object's own address is inside merge reach of the feet by construction and the two candidate fall-points cannot produce a bag a player would look for elsewhere. The feet win on cost: no piece-anchor geometry at the drain, and every give-back in one tick merges to one bag. **Two latent defects went with it** — `spill_at` did not clear the caller's buffer on the mint path (`stand_up` copies it wholesale), which a second caller turns into items duplicated into the world (judge, pass -08, ranked fix 2); and `craft::cancel`'s chunk loop **broke** at the first chunk that did not fit whole, losing that chunk's tail and every chunk after it, so cancelling a large queue at a nearly-full pack destroyed most of a refund silently. **One defect fixed on the way**: `inv_add` at a ceiling of zero set `s.item` before computing a take of zero, stamping `{ item, count: 0 }` across every empty slot — non-canonical empties that `world.rs`'s hash reads, so two shards handling a zero-ceiling item differently diverged on state no player can see. Six of the nine callers did not guard it by hand. **"Whether a spill should say so" is answered for the whole-spill case and did not need a wire field** (2026-08-14, same lane): the zero was always on the wire — `EV_CRAFT_DONE`'s doc line has declared *"0 = full inventory; the loss is announced, never silent"* since it landed — and the client threw it away, the gather arm on an `if added > 0` and the craft arm by formatting it into `crafted 0 × Stone Hatchet` and showing that. Making the zero mean one thing cost one guard in the sim (`gather::swing` pushes no `EV_GATHER` for a swing the cumulative schedule owed nothing, which used to produce the identical sentence), and the client turns it into *"pack full — Wood dropped at your feet"*. **Still open, and now answered by reading the encoder and the four give-back call sites rather than guessed (nothing was *measured* — the merge-gate judge's ranked fix 3b, pass 20260813-230343-10):** a wire field buys exactly two things a client-side read cannot reach — a PARTIAL spill (some fit, some did not; the shortfall never leaves the sim) and the four give-backs, which emit no payout event at all. Also still open: whether the merge should respect ownership rather than merging into whatever bag is nearest. | `NOW.md` §0sp2 · `findings/pass-20260813-230343-07-judge.md` gap 2 · sim-core `backpack.rs` `gather.rs` `craft.rs` `world.rs` `lock.rs` · `sim-core/tests/spill.rs` |
| piece damage v0 + raid wire v13 | **A locked door is no longer a vault: the swing that lands on a person also lands on a wall.** Melee v0 and the death backpack made a kill pay, but only outdoors — no damage verb touched a piece or a deployable, so anything behind a locked door was safe forever and `CONTENT.md`'s raid bands were data nothing played (the merge-gate judge's ranked gap 1, `findings/archive-prestamp/pass-20260802-035930-01-judge.md`). **The column, not a constant:** `content/weapons.toml` gains `structure`, per-weapon damage against a structure (spear_wood 3, spear_metal 4, bow/crossbow/revolver 1, satchel 500 — the satchel's is its old `damage`, so the raid ratio is arithmetically unchanged and its band holds). **The re-derived anchor:** the raid ratio now divides by `structure`, and it gains a melee face in swings — `bands.door_breach_swings = [30, 80]` (the wood door falls to spear_metal in 50) and `bands.wall_breach_swings_min = 150` (wood 188, stone 438, metal 750, rising), both **proposed defaults**, plus two ordering laws with no number in them (no weapon's `structure` above its own `damage`; the raid tool's above every melee's). **The verb:** target order is node -> player -> structure, each nearest-in-cone at the weapon's own reach; reach is measured to `build::anchor`, the exact point placement measures build reach to, and the vertical rule is the movement collider's storey overlap — *you can hit what could block you*. A deployable always wins the tie with the piece at its address, so the door is struck, never the frame it hangs in. **Bounded:** the piece scan is 9 columns x 4 locs through the column index, never the 8,192-entry store; the deployable scan is one pass over its 1,024. **Deliberately not here:** no throwable verb (the satchel is priced, not armed), no repair, no structural collapse (a broken foundation leaves the wall standing; only the deployable at the exact same address falls with it), no building-privilege exemption (you may break your own), no piece hp on the snapshot. | `content/weapons.toml` · `content/balance.toml` · `CONTENT.md` §4 · `sim-core/combat.rs` |
| prop surfaces v0 — everything that is not the ground gets a surface | **The field the ground has, extended triplanar to every `surfaceMaterial`, with an ANALYTIC gradient so it cannot be the ground's dither.** The visual judge's pass 20260802-163821-02 put its ranked gap 1 here and measured it: a 4,386-pixel boulder facet in `05-held-level.png` at luma sd 0.96 with four widely separated sample points returning the identical byte triple, against `spawnedrock.jpg`'s rock interior at sd 26.31 and `gameplayfoundbase.jpeg`'s log wall at 18.18 — 27x and 20x our spread on the same objects at the same apparent size — and "no amount of further terrain work reaches criterion 2 without this". Until this row every non-ground object in the world was a roughness, a metalness and a flat colour. **What ships.** Two octaves of the same value noise the ground uses, sampled triplanar (a prop has no UVs and is not a heightfield, so a top-down projection smears exactly the boulder overhangs and trunk flanks the report photographed), blended on the face normal with the fold per plane and `/length(w)` restoring the deviation the convex blend removes — `gmGrainTri`'s two corrections, verbatim, because they are the same argument. Per class it drives albedo (a scalar swing that moves VALUE and a luminance-neutral deviation that moves HUE — materials v3's division of labour), roughness, a crevice darkening on the ridge fold's low side, and a normal perturbation. `field: null` for water, which branches out on a uniform and pays nothing. **The gradient is analytic and that is the load-bearing choice.** Value noise with a quintic fade has an exact derivative out of the four corner hashes the value already costs, so this bump carries its own gradient and there is no `dFdx` anywhere in the patch. The pass before this one proved what the alternative costs: a reconstructed gradient is constant across the rasterizer's 2x2 quad by construction, measured at 1.9 luma/px of neighbour contrast within a quad against 21.4 across one, which is the per-pixel dither a blind reader named in 5 of 6 frames (§open, "the quad-constant gradient"). Nothing here can be quad-locked; there is no derivative in it. **Structure separates a rock from a log, not amplitude.** `ridge` folds the field toward `1 - |2n-1|`, which turns a blob field into a crack network; `crevice` darkens the fold's low side so a crack reads as depth rather than paint; `scale` is a per-axis vec3, so wood's field is stretched 4.8x along world Y and its fissures run UP the trunk. Seven classes, seven distinct structures, and `browser_smoke` 15f asserts the count — a table with one row copied would satisfy every pixel assertion and none of the ask. **The frequencies are set by the OBJECT and this was measured, not reasoned.** The first cut authored them at the ground's scales (foliage 5.5 /m, rock 3.1 /m) and the gate's own pine view at 10 m scored **0.00% of the frame moved**, on the class with the strongest field in the table: an octave at 5.5 /m retires at `0.09 / scale` = 7.7 m at this frustum. The coarse octave is now about a third of the object it sits on (canopy 1.0 m, boulder 0.8 m, bark 0.5 m across the grain, ore 0.67 m) and the detail octave a quarter of that, which retires at 21-42 m — the band the report is about ("simplified cards in the mid-ground and dark specks on the far ridge") rather than arm's length. Slopes: `2*PI * amp * scale`, all seven inside the 0.03-0.25 band the ground's octaves were authored against, all under `BUMP_MAX_SLOPE` and asserted against both. **One fade law, not two.** `PROP_FADE_CPP` and `PROP_BUMP_FADE_CPP` are both `FADE_OCTAVE_CPP`. The first cut halved the bump's band on the argument that a normal is one derivative up from its value; measured on the pine at 4.9 m it retired the detail octave's RELIEF to 7% while its albedo stood at 90%, and the frame's neighbour contrast came back x1.13 — a canopy whose colour varied and whose surface did not. What covers the sparkle is `SPEC_AA`, applied to exactly this perturbation, which is what the ground's bump runs the full band under. Materials v2's own words: the law is not grain's, it is the field's, and two bands is two hand-derived thresholds to get wrong. **Knobs**: `PROP_GAIN` (2.5, retired with the browser client) (`TINT_GAIN`'s argument verbatim — value noise's deviation about its midpoint is 0.2262, so a gain of 2 makes the authored contrast a place the material never goes), `PROP_DETAIL_MUL` (4.0, retired with the browser client), `PROP_DETAIL_SHARE` (0.6, retired with the browser client), `PINE_RAGGED = 0.34`; the seven per-class rows live in `SURFACES` in `web/src/materials.js` with each number's derivation beside it. Deviation lengths are ~0.2, the band the ground's four identities already sit in, and each has its luminance projected OUT so neutrality is exact by construction rather than to three decimal places by hand — the first cut shipped ~0.08 and measured a chroma spread of x1.09 against a x1.10 floor, a hue octave that had to be looked for. **The pine's silhouette**: each canopy ring pulled IN per vertex by a hash over its own quantized angle and height (so a shared edge cannot open, and a chunk that streams out and back is bit-identical), 7 -> 9 segments, 40 -> 48 triangles a pine. IN only, and that is a sim constraint rather than taste: the beach-spawn row derives its 4 m clearance from "the tree cone at r 1.7 m x 1.1 max scale ~= 1.9 m", so a canopy that could grow would invalidate a spoken number from the renderer. Frame peak measured 1.057-1.065 M triangles against DESIGN §9's 1.5 M, unmoved. **The gate** (`browser_smoke` 15f): a structural half off `propFacts()` — class count, distinct structures, one fade law, every octave under `NYQUIST_CPP`, every slope in band and under the cap, every deviation's luminance residual under `TINT_LUMA_NEUTRAL`, the toggle shipping at 1 — and a rendered half at two views aimed at instances `terrain.nearestProps` finds (a probe that framed no prop is a FAIL, never a skip). Its sharp assertion is the field's OWN difference image: neighbour variation as a share of magnitude, where a wash scores **exactly 0** by construction. That measure replaced a ship-vs-flat contrast ratio as the wall because that ratio's denominator carries the mesh's facet edges and colour ramp, which the toggle does not remove — one field scores x1.62 on a pale faceted boulder and x1.26 on a dark canopy. Measured at this gate's own dev spawn (1024,1024): rock at 17.3 m mask 6.5% (+5.05/-1.46) structure 0.050 chroma x1.12, pine at 2.3 m mask 10.4% (+8.65/-1.71) structure 0.041 chroma x1.13, control noise 0 px on both. **Not in this row**: a program-size or noise-site budget for the prop shader (the ground has both; `propFacts().noiseSamples` publishes 6 and the wall is unwritten), needle cards, a second species, and any authored albedo for bark or granite. |
| survival clock v0 + wire v14 | **Nothing in this game ever said *now*.** You could log in, stand still for an hour, and be in precisely the state you started in — gathering fed crafting fed building that protected a body nothing threatened, and `content/consumables.toml` authored five rows that were parsed, validated and hashed into the WAL header by a sim that never read one (the merge-gate judge's ranked gap 1, `findings/archive-prestamp/pass-20260802-163821-05-judge.md`: "there is no clock and no pressure, so the loop has no engine"). This is its own named smallest slice: a hunger/thirst pair on the vitals wire, a slow health drain past a timer, and the consumables wired to reset it. **Content, proposed here**: `content/balance.toml` `[survival]` — max_food 100, max_water 100, food_minutes_to_empty 60, water_minutes_to_empty 40, starve_hp_per_min 3, dehydrate_hp_per_min 5. The **shape** is what is claimed, not the exact numbers: water is the shorter fuse and the harsher penalty, and an untended fresh spawn has ~40 min before the first point of damage and dies around the 60-minute mark — long enough that gathering and building are still the session, short enough that standing still is never free. `validate::structural` refuses every shape that would make the clock silently inert (a zero meter, a zero span, a zero rate, food emptying before water, a pair that kills a full-hp body in under 5 min, and a heal with no span to arrive over), because silence — not a crash — is this module's failure mode. **Exact integer arithmetic, and that is a determinism requirement rather than a taste**: one shared rational accumulator per meter (add the numerator, divide out whole units, keep the remainder), so a meter reaches zero on exactly the tick its span names and a replay resuming mid-span cannot drift. No floats, no clock, no unbounded loop; constant work per tick, so walls 1–3 hold and the accumulators are hashed state. **Minutes become ticks exactly once**, in `bake_survival` at the content boundary — the sim owns a tick counter and never multiplies a clock. **Tick order is drain → hurt → heal**, each for a stated reason: draining first means the tick a meter empties is the first tick it costs hp (no free tick), and healing last means a bandage started this tick still pays out this tick, so a player racing starvation with food in hand can win. Both meters empty **stack** — the rates add before the accumulator sees them. A refill **forgives** the partial minute rather than banking it. **The eat verb** takes one unit from an inventory slot, applies food and water at once and delivers `health` over the row's `seconds` — the ramp is the reason `seconds` is read at all — and it **refuses rather than destroying the item** when the pair is full and the row heals nothing (`REFUSE_C_NOT_FOOD = 1`, `REFUSE_C_FULL = 2`, announced, never silent). A second consume folds the first's remainder in rather than queueing behind it. Death by clock names the victim as its own killer, which `EV_DEATH`'s doc comment already anticipated. **Wire v14, goldens regenerated in the same commit** (wall 6): the eat action is the **tenth** of the sixteen v12's widening bought and the three events (`EV_VITALS = 21`, `EV_CONSUMED = 22`, `EV_CONSUME_REFUSED = 23`; subtypes 31–33) are inside the sixty-four v13's widening bought — so **no field widened and not one message moved by a bit**, which is the whole return on those two bumps having been taken early. No datagram layout moved: the meter pair is an own-fact on the reliable lane like hp, absolute so a missed reading repairs itself on the next, and for hp's exact reason — it changes on a drain step, not on a tick. Field widths `ACTION_SLOT_BITS = 5` and `REFUSE_C_BITS = 4`. **Inert content disarms the module entirely** (`max_food` 0 ⇒ no meter granted, nothing drains, nothing starves), so a shard booted without a `[survival]` section plays exactly the game it played before — a new table may add a rule, never silently change one. **Deliberately absent, each for a reason**: no temperature and no wetness (the `WET 36%` chip wants weather first) · no comfort · no metabolism from activity, so sprinting costs what standing does · no food spoilage · no stomach cap beyond the meter ceiling · no day/night, whose half of `DESIGN.md` §2's pair is blocked behind the ground's structure moving from bump into albedo. **Gates added**: 6 sim unit tests (the span is exact to the tick, both meters stack and kill, a refill forgives the partial minute, the heal arrives over its seconds, a full pair refuses without destroying the item, and inert content is a no-op); 2 content tests (the spans the sim plays are the file's minutes at the sim's rate, every authored consumable reaches the table keyed to its own item and nothing else in the table is food, and the kill floor is checked against the same `player_hp` the TTK anchor divides by — so the survival band and the combat band cannot drift apart; plus six refusals); 4 golden fixtures; and — the one that matters most — **the clock is armed inside three walls, each in its own fixture**. The first draft of this row claimed that installing it in `probe_combat` bought all three; it bought one, and the merge-gate judge failed the pass for the gap between the sentence and the diff (`findings/pass-20260803-041958-01-judge.md`, checks 9 and 10). What is true now, gate by gate. **`test_parity_wasm`**: `probe_combat` installs `probe_fixture`, whose spans are seconds, so meters drain, empty, starve and are granted again inside the probe window and one bot eats every tick — folded native and wasm and asserted byte-identical. **`test_alloc_zero`**: the gate installs the same fixture itself, on all 100 bodies, and asserts inside the counted window that the drain witness's pair reached zero, that a staged body starved (an `EV_DEATH` naming one id twice) and was granted a fresh pair by its respawn, and that the eat verb both landed (100 consumes, the stack shrank) and refused (200, on a non-food slot and a forged index). It also now asserts the tick's event ring **dropped nothing** across the window: measured at a 210-of-256 peak with the clock installed, and that test's bag, raid, starve and eat counters all read off a drop-newest ring whose late pushes are the very events they count — so the drop counter is asserted rather than assumed. (The meter asserts read player state and do not need it.) **`test_replay`**: the script installs it on 64 bodies with the spans widened past the 900 ticks it runs — a body that starves respawns elsewhere and would move the builder off the arc the script pins — and two bots eat, so the regenerated golden is a function of the drain arithmetic rather than of its absence. Each gate arms the module in its own fixture; none inherits it from another, and the four prose sites that said otherwise were corrected in the same commit. The browser assertion was widened from one bar to three, each read off its own row and each compared against `content/balance.toml` rather than a typed number. `test_replay`'s golden was regenerated twice across the two commits, and the second regeneration is the one that carries meaning: the first moved because `state_hash` widened over fields the script left at zero, the second because the clock now actually runs on that surface. **Also closed, found next door**: `[backpack]`'s despawn ladder reached the sim and was not reaching the content hash, so two contents that despawned bags at different rates canonicalised identically and a replay could be handed a WAL whose header said the ladder matched when it did not. | DESIGN §2 · CONTENT §1 · `content/balance.toml` · `content/consumables.toml` · `sim-core/survival.rs` · `NOW.md` item 1 |
| food you can get + the clock's death | **The clock shipped over an island that paid no food.** `content/consumables.toml` authored berries, mushrooms and corn; `content/items.toml` listed them; nothing in the world yielded a single unit of any of the three, so the loop the last pass armed was: spawn, gather for forty minutes, take 5 hp/min from thirst, die at ~60 — and, because a clock death never moved `deaths`, respawn on the *identical* beach to do it again (the merge-gate judge's ranked gap 1, `findings/archive-prestamp/pass-20260803-041958-02-judge.md`, and `NOW.md` item 1's own next two wants). Two halves, two commits. **A death by the clock is a death**: one line at the death site, which is where `combat::strike` already counts its own, and the consequence is `spawn_pos_n(id, deaths)` walking the spawn ring forward a generation so the body wakes up somewhere else. `test_alloc_zero`'s staged starve now asserts the count moved, and new `crates/sim-core/tests/survival.rs` owns the half the module cannot see (`World::respawn` and the ring): the count lands once, the body is standing on `spawn_pos_n(id, 1)` and not where it fell, a second death walks the ring again, and the respawn hands back a full pair with hp in it. `test_replay`'s golden did **not** move, and that is a fact about the script rather than the change — its fixture widens both spans past the 900 ticks it runs precisely so no body starves inside it. **A node may pay two things.** `NodeDef` gains `secondary`, one flat `(item, units)` pair, and `[gatherable.secondary]` in `content/gatherables.toml` is where it comes from: **the bush pays 5 berries beside its 10 cloth**. Flat is the design and not a shortcut — no tool row, no weak-spot bonus, because picking is not chopping; the primary keeps both, so the tool ladder and the glint still mean what they meant. It rides its own `EV_GATHER`, so the toast stack reads `+10 Cloth` *and* `+5 Berries` rather than one line that lies about half of what arrived. **Proposed numbers**, `DESIGN.md` §2's shape rather than the digits: 5 berries per pickup is 75 food and 25 water against the shipped 100/60-min and 100/40-min meters — 45 minutes of hunger and 10 of thirst per bush, so hunger is answered by the world and thirst is answered *slowly*, which is what leaves the drink verb (`NOW.md` item 1) worth building. Mushrooms and corn stay unreachable on purpose: they want a forest-floor pickup and a farming lane, and inventing either here would be inventing content to satisfy a wall. **The wall: a clock must have an answer.** `validate::structural` already refused every shape that made the clock silently *inert*; it now also refuses one that is silently **unanswerable** — hunger or thirst that drains while nothing on the island pays a consumable restoring it. Reachable means *a gatherable pays it*, because gather is the only payout path the sim has: loot tables deliberately do not count, since nine barrel entries are parsed and hashed and no verb opens a container (that judge's ranked gap 2), and counting them would be exactly the lie the check exists to catch. When the open verb lands, the set widens in that commit. Beside it, `test_content` prices the answer in the clock's own units — the best food a node pays must buy ≥ 20 min of the hunger span and the best water ≥ 5 min of the thirst span, computed from `content/balance.toml`'s own numbers — so a content that satisfies the boolean with one berry still goes red. **And the value reaches the content hash**: `canon.rs` hashes the secondary (absent as a 0 tag), because a field that reaches the sim and not the hash lets two contents that play differently canonicalise identically — the defect `[backpack]`'s ladder carried until 2026-08-03, found again here before it shipped. `hash_moves_with_values` asserts it on the shipped rate. Refusals added: a secondary paying zero, a secondary repeating the primary output, a secondary naming no item, the bush's berry row deleted, and berries that feed but do not water. **One hardening from the same judge's notes**: `consume` added `p.food + def.food` in u16 and clamped afterwards, so the clamp could only fix a sum that was already right — saturating now, with a test, because "unreachable through today's validator" is not "cannot happen" and this is one word. (Its other note, that `decode_action` reads the consume slot without the `>= INV_SLOTS` check, does not reproduce at HEAD: the arm carries it, and with `INV_SLOTS` 30 against a 5-bit field it refuses the two forgeable values.) **Still open on this item, and the piece that makes thirst answerable rather than merely survivable**: the drink verb. It is a wire bump (`ACT_DRINK`, eleventh of sixteen — no field widens, but 54 goldens rename and regenerate), a sim verb against `terrain::height`, a `[survival]` content pair, server routing, the wasm bridge and a keybind, so it is its own pass and not a tail on this one; `NOW.md` item 1 carries the shape. | DESIGN §2 · CONTENT §1 · `content/gatherables.toml` · `sim-core/gather.rs` · `sim-core/survival.rs` · `content/validate.rs` · `NOW.md` item 1 |
| the daylight register (sky · air · ambient floor · one owner) | **Superseded on its constants by the lighting v1 row below (merged 2026-08-04); its gate, assertion 16, still runs.** The two passes re-metered the same coupled set within eight hours of each other on branches that never saw one another — the parallel-owner failure `CLAUDE.md`'s coupled-lighting trap names — and v1 is the owner that ships, so every knob this row proposed now reads as a mention rather than a declaration. What this row still owns is its *findings*, and two of them outlived their numbers: the ambient floor's measured block against the prop chroma wall, and the fog seam being off by the transfer (v1 shares one `THREE.Color` between fog and dome, which makes the two inputs identical and leaves the two outputs a transfer apart — recorded here, and in `scene.js` at the probe that reads it, as owed). **The sky was the darkest large surface in the frame, there was no aerial perspective at all, and half of every object was an unlit black silhouette.** Three findings, one owner, because `CLAUDE.md`'s trap list says so — *"tonemap, sky, exposure and fog are one owner; split across parallel passes they break each other's assumptions faster than they improve"* — and because they turned out to be one register. From the visual judge's ranked gaps 3 and 2 (`findings/pass-20260803-064506-01-visual.md`), and from the **prop surfaces v0** row above, which measured the third one, wrote out its arithmetic, and said in its own words that it was handing it here: *"the whole indirect budget is one `HemisphereLight` at 1.15, and the fix is the coupled sky/sun/exposure/fog edit `CLAUDE.md` reserves for one owner in one iteration."* **The sky** is a fragment program on a stock `MeshBasicMaterial` now, not a 24×16 vertex ramp (which banded in ~7 px steps across the largest flat region in every frame and could not hold a disc a third of a degree across): the ramp, a sun disc, its halo and a dither, all per fragment from the view direction, with three's own `tonemapping_fragment` and `colorspace_fragment` still downstream so the renderer keeps single tone-map ownership. Proposed defaults, all since **superseded by lighting v1** (below) — horizon `FOG_COLOR` at `0xc9d8e4` (v0 `0x808f9c`; v1 renames it `SKY_HORIZON`), `SKY_ZENITH` at `0x4a7cc0` (v0 `0x2c4463`), `SKY_CURVE` at `1.6` (v0 0.62 — **above** 1 is the haze band, the first few degrees of sky holding the horizon colour before the blue takes over; under 1 does the exact opposite and is what pulled the zenith blue down onto the ground line), `SUN_DISC_RAD` at `[0.012, 0.03]` rad with `SUN_DISC_GAIN` at `9.0`, `SUN_GLOW_POW` at `220.0` (v1 renames it `SUN_GLOW_POWER`), `SUN_GLOW_GAIN` at `0.9`, `SKY_DITHER` at `0.006` of linear (~1.5/255 through the transfer's local slope, hashed on `gl_FragCoord` **only** — every probe in this file renders one frame twice and requires the pair to be identical). **The air**: `FOG_NEAR` at `20` (v0 180 m, against a 5×5×64 m near ring that is 160 m of visible ground — the term existed only in the source, and the judge measured near-to-far grass at 68→73 luma, flat) and `FOG_FAR` at `1400` (v0 1000). Not shorter, deliberately: fog is the one change here that can only ever REMOVE contrast at distance, and the far-shadow and horizon gates measure shadow at 200–500 m; 1400 m puts 14–34% of haze there against their mean deltas and a 6/255 bar, and both cleared unmoved at **0.72%** and **0.48%** against 0.25% and 0.15% (the horizon probe's mean Δluma 33.7 → 43.1, i.e. up, because the register is brighter). The near shadow probe went 24.0% → **23.17%** against its 15% floor. **The fog colour is handed to `THREE.Fog` pre-transfer** — exposure applied, then Khronos PBR Neutral applied in JS (`toneMapNeutral`, three's own chunk transcribed) — because three mixes fog AFTER the tone map and the output transform, so the fog colour is the one shaded quantity the renderer's transfer never touches. Lighting v0's claim that the dome and the fog "share a horizon colour, so the seam is exact by construction" was therefore already off by the transfer; at v0's register the two landed within ~10% and nobody saw it, and at a daylight one the same hex would have put distant ground ~28/255 **above** the sky over it — the inverted hierarchy rebuilt at the horizon line. **The ambient floor did NOT move, and that is this row's largest finding rather than its omission** — `FILL_SKY` at `0xa9c3e2` over `FILL_GROUND` at `0x6b5f4a` at `FILL_INTENSITY` of `1.15`, all three exactly v0's. **`EXPOSURE` did not move off `0.8`**, and that is a measurement: this slice ran at 0.7 for three rounds as the front-end compensation for a fill of 1.9, the fill ended at 1.3 so the compensation stopped being owed, and 0.7 cost something on the way out — the darkest pixels are where 8-bit chromaticity is most quantized, and at 0.7 the boulder's delivered p05 fell to 22/255 and took the prop gate's chroma ratio with it, to x1.0995 against its x1.10 floor. Two properties of the pair are load-bearing and both were forced by a wall, not chosen. **Bright**, because the number that had to move is the DOWN pole: the prop row's arithmetic delivers a down-facing prop face at `FILL_GROUND × FILL_INTENSITY × albedo ÷ π × EXPOSURE`, which put the pine canopy's underside at RGB (2, 6, 1) against the judge's measured (2, 6, 0); the pole goes 0.132 → 0.75 of linear luminance, **5.7×**. **The block is a WALL, not a difficulty, and it was found by building the fix six ways and measuring every one.** The prop gate's assertion is the ratio of a prop's chromaticity spread with the material's procedural field ON against OFF, shipping at **x1.12 (boulder) and x1.13 (pine) against a x1.10 floor** — 0.02 of headroom. Every unit of ambient lands in that ratio's DENOMINATOR and none in its numerator, because light added to a face that was rendering as near-black noise is new chromaticity in the frame the material did not put there. Measured: fill 1.9 with poles 0.143 apart → boulder **x1.07** (and the shadow gate at **14.57% against its own 15%**); fill 1.5, same pair → **x1.09**; fill 1.3 with a near-achromatic pair 0.051 apart → **x1.0995**; the same with `EXPOSURE` 0.8 → 0.7 → **x1.0986**; both poles on the key's own chromaticity → **x1.067**; the key walked toward the fill instead (`0xfff1de`, 0.069 away against v0's 0.132) → **x1.06**. The last configuration is the one that settles it: sky pole left at v0 and the BOUNCE alone raised 3.4x (0.132 → 0.454 linear luminance) — the boulder **recovers**, and the **pine goes x1.07**, because the two poles fail through two different props. The sky pole lights the up- and side-facing majority of a boulder; the bounce lights a canopy's underside; each breaks the prop whose faces it reaches, and there is no split of this budget that raises the floor and leaves that ratio alone. Both escapes are worse rather than better, and that is the measurement the conclusion rests on: a coloured light does not only cost spread, it AMPLIFIES a mineral's chromatic deviation, so walking the key toward the fill dropped the NUMERATOR 38% while the denominator held. **So the floor waits for the numerator, which is somebody else's slice** — `NOW.md`'s "nothing that is not the ground has a surface" is the pass that raises a prop's authored chroma, and with it the same ambient buys a ratio that clears x1.10 and this becomes a two-line change. Raising it now would mean lowering a wall to fit. **`SUN_COLOR`, `SUN_ELEVATION` and `SUN_INTENSITY` did not move**, and the elevation is the one item here taken out rather than shipped. The report asks for "sun elevation up to a near-midday register"; the shadow gate's floors (15% of the sweep, 10% on every yaw) were measured against terrain self-shadowing under a 21° sun, where a hillside shades 2.66 m of ground per metre of relief, and at 45° that is 1.0 m — most of the measured 24.0% gone. A floor a change would breach means the change is not done. Proposed for a later pass, with its own work: `SUN_ELEVATION` 0.36 → ~0.8 rad **together with** a re-derived shadow floor measured under the new sun, or a second shading term that keeps hillside relief readable when the sun is high. **Gate** (`ci/browser_smoke.mjs` assertion 16, all counted, none timed): a structural half — the near plane inside the ring, the dome patched and no longer vertex-coloured, a dither, a disc with an inner and outer radius and a gain — then `scene.daylightProbe`, four renders and four readbacks per yaw, each removing exactly one part of the register. **(a) The sky is above the ground.** The dome is hidden over a magenta clear, which the tone mapper never touches, so "sky" is the pixels that come back exactly (255, 0, 255) — a mask by construction. Measured sky **173–183** luma against a median ground of **76–102**, ratio **x1.79–x2.28** at the eye (floor x1.15); the defect was 55–114 against 160–200. **(b) Distance lightens and washes out.** The fog is pushed past the frame by its own near/far uniforms (never `scene.fog = null`, which is a `USE_FOG` define and would relink every material), and the per-pixel delta divided by `hazeLuma − pixelLuma` recovers the fog FACTOR — a depth channel this client has no other way to read. The raw delta is NOT that channel and the first cut of this probe used it: a delta is `f × (haze − the pixel's own colour)`, so it sorts by how dark a pixel is as much as by how far, and the "far" tercile came back as the frame's dark half with a luma ramp running *backwards*. Terciles of the factor, over pixels the haze actually moved (an 8-bit frame rounds everything inside ~30 m to zero lift, and there are enough of those to own a whole tercile). Per yaw, on the lifted sweep: the haze moves **1.59–9.76%** of the frame (floor 0.5%), lightens **100.0%** of what it touches (floor 98% — a haze darker than its ground would score 0), the far third carries a mean factor of **0.073–0.163** (floor 0.03), and each band's own luma LIFT (1.00→1.65→4.40 … 1.02→2.83→17.16) and saturation DROP (0.0062→0.0160→0.0405 …) climb on every step. The lift and the drop are asserted per yaw and the RAW ramp only over the pooled sweep, and that split is a measurement rather than a convenience: a band's raw luma is mostly what its ground is made of, and one lifted yaw whose far third is darker rock reads x1.008 while the yaw beside it reads x1.37 — pooled, the far third reads **x1.162** luma and **x0.713** saturation against the near third (floors x1.05 and x0.90). **(c) The ambient floor.** The key is taken to zero intensity and `luma(fill only) / luma(full)` per ground pixel is the share of that pixel's light that survives losing the sun, read at the 5th percentile — the darkest ground pixels are the most-lit ones, which is where a floor is actually tested. Measured **20.8–41.2%** on the shipped rig, and the floor is set at **0.15** rather than at the judge's 0.30 — deliberately, and this is the one number in this row that is a REGRESSION wall and not an achievement. 0.30 is the judge's bar in the judge's own 8-bit output space (it measured lit-vs-unlit faces of one object at 89.5/9.1 and 57.2/6.1) and the rig cannot reach it while the prop wall stands; asserting it anyway would be a gate the tree cannot pass, and asserting nothing would let the next slice spend the floor without noticing. So it asserts the share cannot FALL, at 1.4x under the worst yaw. Note also what the metric is and is not: it reads the fill's share on non-sky pixels, which at these vantages are mostly ground. It is sensitive to both poles and measured so — raising the bounce 3.4x with the sky pole untouched moved the worst yaw 20.8% → 28.2% — but a ground pixel faces up, so the one case it will never see is the one the judge measured, a canopy's underside at (2, 6, 0). That needs an object-face probe this pass did not build. **Swept twice, and the two sweeps are not redundant**: at the eye (pitch −0.12, height 0) for (a) and (c), because that is where a player plays and where the capture harness shoots; lifted (pitch −0.18, +40 m) for (b), on the argument the far-shadow and horizon probes already make for their own 80 m — at 1.6 m the ground 100 m out sits 0.016 rad below the horizon, which is 1.2% of a 75° frame's height, and the eye sweep does still read **0.00%** air on the one yaw whose ground is all foreground. Probe is dev-only and the public-shard tab asserts its absence. **Not in this row**: the sun's elevation (above), contact AO where objects meet terrain, the local player submitted to the shadow pass (there is no first-person body to submit), cloud, and any second scattering term — the dome is a ramp with a disc on it, not an atmosphere model. | web `src/scene.js` `src/main.js` · `ci/browser_smoke.mjs` · DESIGN §9 · `findings/pass-20260803-064506-01-visual.md` |
| the drink verb + wire v15 | **Thirst had no answer, and the ocean you spawn beside was scenery.** The clock shipped a 40-minute water meter and one way to fill it — a bush, 5 berries at 5 water each, 10 minutes a pickup against a 45-minute food payout on the same bush — so the fastest-draining meter was answered slowly and indirectly by the action that answers hunger, while a player standing knee-deep in the sea could not touch it. That is a survival loop with one input, which is not a loop (merge-gate judge's ranked gap 3, `findings/pass-20260803-064506-02-judge.md`; open in `NOW.md` for two passes). `ACT_DRINK = 10` is the eleventh action of sixteen and `SUB_DRANK = 34` the 35th event subtype of sixty-four, so **no field widened and no message moved by a bit** — the fifty-four existing goldens are byte-identical after regeneration and only `v15_hello.bin` differs, because `PROTO_VER` is inside it. **The sea is salt**, and that is the design rather than a tax: the ocean is the answer that is always there and never free, so the bush stays worth walking to. `content/balance.toml` `[survival]` gains `drink_water` and `drink_hp_cost`, proposed at **25 water / 2 hp** and derived against the answers already shipping rather than picked — 25 is exactly one bush's worth of thirst (10 minutes of the 40-minute meter) delivered in one press with no walk and no bush, and 2 hp is priced against the repair on the same shelf: a bandage is 20 cloth, a bush pays 10 cloth, so 2 bushes = 1 bandage = 20 hp = ten mouthfuls, against ten bushes to drink the same water by hand. The sea is 5× the farm time cheaper and it bleeds. **The verb reads the world, not the inventory** — the first one here that does: five `terrain::height` taps (the feet and the four cardinal points at `build::BUILD_REACH_M`, reused rather than given a reach of its own) and any one below `SEA_LEVEL` is water in reach. Five and not a ring because trig is banned in the sim (wall 1), and five is enough for the only water this world has: a half-space whose coastline wanders tens of metres per cycle cannot thread between taps 5 m apart. Payload-free on the wire for a stronger reason than `Loot`'s — the heightfield is a pure function of the seed both sides already hold, so there is no position to forge and no way to drink an ocean from a hilltop. **It can kill you**, and the death goes through the module's one kill site (`died_by_the_world`, factored out of the starve path in this commit so the two ways the world can kill cannot disagree about what a death is): the count moves, `EV_DEATH` names one id twice, and `World::respawn` walks the spawn ring. A full meter refuses rather than paying hp for nothing, and a dry press is announced as `REFUSE_C_NO_WATER = 3` on the one refusal channel the whole survival module shares; the landed drink rides `EV_DRANK = 24`. Those four codes are declared here in the registry's own form for the same reason the clock's row declared `REFUSE_C_NOT_FOOD = 1` and `EV_CONSUMED = 22`: they are discriminants rather than knobs, so nothing forces it, but a code that is named in prose without its value is a claim the registry gate cannot check, and the two neighbouring rows should not disagree about whether it bothers. **`validate::structural` widened in the same commit**: an armed drink is the second way to answer thirst, so a content set may drop the berry's water — but not both, and `test_content` pins exactly that (the drink alone boots; disarming it as well is refused). Two new bounds beside it: a cost with no water is a tax with no purchase, and a mouthful costing a whole body is a suicide button. **Three gates arm it, each in its own fixture**: `test_alloc_zero` stands a seventh bot on a heightfield-scanned shoreline and presses drink every counted tick — the landed drink, the salt death, the respawn's grant and the dry refusal all inside the window, at zero alloc delta; `test_replay` puts bot 21 on the same scanned shoreline pressing every seven ticks, and the pinned hash moved to `0xDB77_3653_6575_F530` **as a function of the verb's own arithmetic** (its cadence is 7 rather than 29 precisely so most presses find a full meter, which is how the full refusal reaches the replayed surface); `test_parity_wasm` presses drink on every bot every tick in `probe_combat`, because the answer is a float comparison against `terrain::height` and that is exactly the arithmetic that goes different ways on two targets. The replay deliberately does NOT carry the salt death — it installs no `CombatContent`, so every body there has `hp_max == 0` and a cost clamped to a body's own hp is zero; an assert on `hp_max` is what keeps that comment from going stale. **Not in this row**: fresh water (there are no rivers or lakes in the generator, so "is this salt" is not a question the world can yet ask — when there are, the same five taps answer it and content gains a second row), a held container to carry water in (the reference frames' `ml` quantities are M2's), a drink animation or any world-space effect, and thirst from activity — the span is still wall-clock. | `crates/sim-core/src/survival.rs` `world.rs` · `crates/protocol/src/lib.rs` `event.rs` `goldens.rs` · `crates/content/src/{schema,bake,validate}.rs` · `content/balance.toml` · `crates/server/src/core.rs` · `crates/client-core/src/{bridge,core}.rs` · `web/src/main.js` · `ci/client_smoke.mjs` · DESIGN §2 · `findings/pass-20260803-064506-02-judge.md` |
| respawn on bag v0 | **Death evicted you from your own base, and there was no way back.** `item.sleeping_bag` was authored, craftable, deployable, given its own archetype `ARCH_BAG`, capped per owner by `BAG_CAP`, decayed by upkeep and hashed into the WAL — and **nothing read one at a death**: `World::respawn` sent every body to `spawn_pos_n(id, deaths)` unconditionally, a blind ring point with no map, no compass and no marker to walk back by, so every session ended where its first death landed (the merge-gate judge's ranked gap 1, `findings/archive-prestamp/pass-20260803-064506-04-judge.md`: *"the one mechanic the genre uses to make a base worth building is placed, capped, hashed and inert"*). `ALPHA.md` §1 had specified the whole rule a month earlier — "respawn-on-it with a per-anchor cooldown (~5 min **(knob)**)" — so this row invents nothing; it implements a spoken one. **The knob is the registry's own** (`bag cooldown · cap`, 5 min · 8): `BAG_COOLDOWN_TICKS = 9_000` is those five minutes at the 30 Hz tick, written as the literal the knob-registry gate can pin with `the_cooldown_is_five_minutes_of_ticks` holding the arithmetic against `TICK_HZ`. **The rule**: on death the store is scanned for the dying player's own `ARCH_BAG` records whose cooldown has lapsed, and the **nearest to where the body fell** answers — so a death defending a compound wakes inside that compound, not on a bag across the island. Ties go to the earlier entry, which is insertion order and therefore replayable. The chosen bag is *spent* for `BAG_COOLDOWN_TICKS`; a player killed twice inside five minutes walks to their next bag and, with none ready, back to the ring — which is what makes `BAG_CAP` a cap on **how many deaths in a row a defender can answer** rather than a number about storage. One pass of the store, squared planar distance (no `sqrt`, no trig), no allocation, and no iteration order that is not the store's own (walls 1–3). **The cooldown is sim state and hashed** — two shards disagreeing about which bags are spent disagree about where the next death wakes, and a digest that could not see it would call them the same world. It lives in a **parallel array** in the store rather than as a field on `DeployRec`, and that was decided by a wall rather than by taste: `DeployRec` is the struct the deploy-sync packet mirrors, so eight sim-only bytes on it rode 24-deep in `EventMsg::DeploySync` and grew the client's event enum from 578 to 770 bytes — `clippy::large_enum_variant` red, with boxing unavailable (the type is `Copy` by wire necessity). The fix was not to silence the lint but to put the timer where it belonged: the client draws a bag, it does not adjudicate one. `insert` and the swap-remove move both halves together. **The bag is asked before the ring, and it wins over `dev_spawn`**, whose own doc pins the *ring*: a shard pinned for a browser test still honours a bag its player placed. **No wire change, and that is a decision rather than an omission**: the client learns the new position from the next snapshot, so nothing about the mechanic waits on a packet, and the subtype a respawn *would* spend belongs to the **respawn screen** (`ALPHA.md` §1's "choose beach or a bag"), which is its own slice with its own action — spending it now would pin a layout to a guess (wall 6). `EV_RESPAWN = 25` announces `(player, woke-on-a-bag)` sim-side on **every** death, ignored by the server's existing event catch-all; the three walls read it directly. **Deliberately absent, each for a reason**: no pick-your-bag choice (needs the death screen and an action subtype) · no bag on *join*, so a reconnect is still a ring spawn (that is the sleeper/haven lane, `NETCODE.md` §6.3) · no bed and no halved cooldown (ALPHA §1 files it as "later") · no respawn-time bag destruction, so raiding a bag is still exactly killing the deployable. **Inert content disarms it entirely** — a `DeployContent` with no bag row scans, finds nothing, and every death is the ring it always was: a new rule may add an answer, never silently change one. **Gates added**: 4 sim unit tests in the module that owns the scan (nearest wins over first-placed, spent for exactly its cooldown and awake on the tick it names — not one early, a foreign bag is not an answer and a refused scan spends nothing, a workbench is not a bed) and 6 in `crates/sim-core/tests/bag_respawn.rs` at the level the module cannot see (a death wakes on the bag and still counts, grants hp and refills the pair; no bag is still the spawn ring at the generation `deaths` names; a second death inside the cooldown falls back to that ring; a second bag answers it instead when there is one; the bag wakes again once its five minutes lapse; and the cooldown moves `state_hash` with nothing else in the world changed). **Armed inside two walls in their own fixtures, and structural in the third** — the distinction is the one the last clock row was failed for, so it is stated exactly: `test_alloc_zero` installs the deploy fixture itself, places a bag for the staged starving body through the real verb, and asserts inside the counted window that a respawn announced a bag AND put the body on that bag's own cell, that other deaths still fell through to the ring, that the cooldown was stamped, and that the delta is still 0/0. `test_parity_wasm` gains a fourth probe, `probe_bags` — its own world rather than three lines in `probe_combat`, because that probe pins `dev_spawn` to a shoreline for the drink verb and a ground-class bag wants 1.5 m, so borrowing it would have traded the salt death out of the digest for bags. It returns `wakes << 32 \| digest32`, printed as two fields, and **`ci/gates.sh` fails on a zero count**: two targets can agree byte-for-byte about a path that never ran, so the digest is evidence of parity and the count is evidence of coverage (measured 63 wakes over 24 sequences). `test_replay`'s golden regenerated in the same commit and **structurally**: that script installs no `CombatContent`, so no body on it can die and no cooldown can be written — what moved is the cooldown array entering the digest over the deployables the script stands, which is the property a WAL resumed mid-cooldown depends on. | ALPHA §1 · DESIGN §2 · sim-core `deploy.rs` `world.rs` `probe.rs` · `ci/gates.sh` `ci/parity.mjs` · `findings/archive-prestamp/pass-20260803-064506-04-judge.md` · `NOW.md` item 1 |
