| pine geometry v1 (whorls) + the shape gate, and the sim coupling it closes | **`ART.md` rule 6 has said the shape is wrong since the art bible was written, and no amount of material work could reach it.** *"Silhouette before surface. If a prop is not identifiable as a black shape against the sky, no material work will save it. Pines in the references are tall, thin, and ragged-edged; a smooth cone is wrong at any texture budget."* What shipped was a trunk and two cones — 48 triangles whose profile is two straight lines — and every pass since has been surface work on top of it: an authored PBR response, triplanar bark, an albedo band, a ragged per-vertex pull. The visual judge's ranked gap 1(c) named it exactly (*"a clean polygon edge with no needle cards, no alpha detail, no sub-branch break-up"*) and a blind reader called it *"flat-shaded cones stacked on a cylinder trunk"* in 3 of 3 frames showing a tree. **What landed:** conifers put branches in WHORLS — rings at intervals up the trunk, longest low, shortest high, each drooping below its own attachment — so the canopy is now five short capped cones on a tapered full-height trunk, each independently ragged (its own seed, so they do not align into flutes) and each with a drooping rim (`PINE_DROOP = 0.18` m). **102 triangles** against 48; 6.60 m tall by 2.74 m wide against 5.2 by 3.4, so slenderness goes **1.53 → 2.41**; 44 distinct silhouette radii against ~18; 23% of the tree's area now faces DOWN, which is `ART.md` §5's *"the underside must still read — it is the face every judge has caught"* and the face `scene.js`'s fill and bounce poles were tuned against. Caps are the reason for that number and are not optional: an open cone shows backfaces from below, which is to say nothing. Two greens kept (§5's floor), each ramping over the span of the whorls that wear it rather than per whorl, so the gradient does not reset five times up the tree. Fleet cost ~127k tris in a forest ring against ~60k, on a measured 1.06 M peak of DESIGN §9's 1.5 M. **`web/src/props.js` is new** and the split is what makes the gate possible: it imports THREE and nothing else — no materials, no textures, no DOM — so `ci/pine_shape.mjs` can import the **shipped** builder and measure the real buffer instead of scoring a copy, which is the "gate that matches nothing" failure the trap list calls the worst bug class here. `bump_basis.mjs` has to mirror GLSL and then pin the mirror; geometry can just be run. **The assertion that is not about art at all:** `world.rs` derived `SPAWN_CLEAR_M` at 4.0 from a sentence about a JS constant — *"the widest archetype the client draws is the tree cone at radius 1.7 m × 1.1 max scale ≈ 1.9 m, plus the 0.4 m capsule"* — and **nothing enforced it**. A canopy widened for taste in the renderer would have put fresh spawns back inside trees, the exact defect the beach-spawn row exists to have fixed, with every gate in `ci/gates.sh` green because none could see across the language boundary. The gate now reads all four numbers from the files that own them and closes the arithmetic; `PINE_MAX_R` at 1.7 is the ceiling it holds, and the tree draws at 1.38 so `ART.md`'s "tall, thin" has somewhere to go without anyone re-deriving a sim number. The other seven checks: slenderness floor 2.0, silhouette radii ≥ 30, rim heights ≥ 20 (a level disc stacks into horizontal lines — the same defect rotated 90°), downward area ≥ 15%, the wind cantilever rooted at the base ring and reaching 1 at the tip, two canopy greens, and byte-identical determinism across two builds. | ART §5/§6 · TERRAIN §4 · `web/src/props.js` · `ci/pine_shape.mjs` · sim-core `world.rs` |
| the prop bench, and what looking at the tree cost | **Three passes of tree work had shipped without anyone seeing a tree.** Wind, felling and the whorl geometry all landed on gates that were real but blind — silhouette counts, downward-area shares, determinism — and the first render of the whorl pine showed a **bare brown spike standing above the canopy on every tree in the forest**, because the whorls taper to a point and the trunk did not. It passed all seventeen structural checks. The lesson is not "add a check" (one was added, `PINE_TRUNK_H = 5.7`, and the gate now fails a trunk that reaches the crown); it is that a visual defect needs an eye, and this box has one: Chromium is pre-installed at `/opt/pw-browsers` and Playwright will drive it with `executablePath` and `--use-gl=swiftshader`. `web/bench.html` + `web/src/bench.js` render the shipped prop builders on a plain sky at four framings (`melee`, `silhouette`, `stand`, `canopyUp`) — no shard, no wasm, no WebTransport, ~4 seconds a shot. **It does NOT use `surfaceMaterial`**: that carries the clipmap patch, which needs N shadow-casting directional lights and will not link against one, so the bench builds a `MeshStandardMaterial` on the SURFACES table's own roughness. What it shows is the real silhouette, the real baked vertex colours and the real normals; what it cannot show is the procedural detail field or the shipped light rig. Judge shape and shading on it, never colour or exposure. **What it caught in one sitting:** the trunk spike; and that the first canopy normal blend (toward the canopy's own centre, which sounds more principled) points every whorl below the midpoint DOWNWARD and renders the bottom two thirds of the tree black — the fix is outward-from-the-axis tilted up (`PINE_NORMAL_BLEND = 0.7`, `PINE_NORMAL_UP` (0.85, retired with the browser client)), which is the same direction at every height because a needle fans out from the trunk and tilts toward the light. Nine segments to a whorl means nine enormous flat plates and no amount of silhouette work touches that; it is a shading defect, and blending the normals is free. **What the bench also settles, and it is the answer to "the trees still look like programmer art":** `Rust Images/choppingtree.jpg` is a canopy made ENTIRELY of alpha-tested cards — individual needle sprigs, transparent between them, no solid hull anywhere at any distance — and ours is opaque geometry whose every edge is a polygon boundary. That gap is not reachable by geometry. `ART.md` §5 asks for "needle-card silhouette break-up" by name and this client has no `alphaTest`, no `transparent` and no `map` anywhere in it. Foliage alpha is the next slice and it is the whole of the remaining gap. | ART §5/§6 · `web/bench.html` · `web/src/props.js` |
| the death screen + the choice · wire v16 | **A death picked your bed for you, and never told you what killed you.** Respawn-on-bag landed the anchor and stopped one step short of the flow `ALPHA.md` §1 actually specifies: *"death screen (who/what killed you — range and weapon, no map position), choose beach or a bag"*. Three nouns and a **choice**, and v2 had none of them — `World::respawn` ran inside the death, took the nearest ready bag unconditionally, and the player was already standing on it before the client heard a thing. So a body killed at its own doorstep by the raider still standing in it woke up in that raider's reach, naked, over and over until the bag's five minutes ran out. **The wait is the feature.** `World::die` now drops the backpack and leaves the body where it fell with `Player::dead` set; `World::wake` is a separate half that only `Command::Respawn` reaches. No timer releases it — a span nobody spoke would have been a knob invented into code, and the one thing the state exists for is that the player decides. A corpse keeps its id, its death count, its position and its facing; everything else is `Player::default()`, and every verb in `apply` resolves through a new `live_slot_of`, so a dead body cannot craft, build, feed, lock, drink or eat. It is still stepped by `movement` with a **zeroed** frame rather than skipped, which is what keeps the client's predictor in agreement about a corpse it can still see (the client zeroes the same three fields while the screen is up). **Wire v16, and every part of it fits a field an earlier version widened.** `ACT_RESPAWN = 11` is the twelfth action of sixteen (v12's 4 bits) carrying one bit — beach or your own bag — and **no bag id**, for `ACT_LOOT`'s reason and a stronger one: a forgeable id would let a client wake on somebody else's bag. `SUB_RESPAWN = 35` is the 36th event subtype of sixty-four (v13's 6 bits) carrying the same one bit *back*, because asking for a bag inside its cooldown gets a beach and a player who is not told that can only learn it by looking around. The one layout that moved is `Death`, which gained `cause` (`DEATH_BY_HAND` · `DEATH_BY_CLOCK` · `DEATH_BY_SALT`, two bits, the width being the range check), `item` (16) and `range_cm` (16) — and **still no position**, which is the rule ALPHA §1 states outright rather than an omission. Those three are read off the victim's own `Player` record at encode, the way a bag's position is read out of the backpack store, and the deferred respawn is what makes that safe: the corpse is still in its slot when the server walks the event ring. Fifty-six goldens regenerated in this commit (wall 6) plus two new ones; the death fixture pins `DEATH_BY_SALT` and not `DEATH_BY_HAND` precisely because the latter is code 0 and a fixture at the default cannot catch an encoder that dropped the field. **Counted, not timed.** `GOLDEN_FINAL_HASH` moved **structurally** — the five new `Player` fields are ten bytes per active body in the digest and nothing on the replay surface can die, so every one of those bytes is a live body's value. `test_alloc_zero` answers four screens every tick inside the counted window and asserts `screen_ticks > 0` and `corpse_acted == 0`; `probe_bags` presses both answers on every bot every tick, which makes `ci/gates.sh`'s existing "wakes > 0" strictly stronger than it was (a wake is now only reachable *through* the screen); `ci/client_smoke.mjs` hand-frames a Death naming its own player id, a Death naming a stranger, and a forged fourth cause, and reads the screen back out of the C ABI. **What no gate does is kill a body in a browser**, and the reason is content, not effort: melee needs a weapon neither smoke tab can gather, and the sea refuses a drink into a full meter, so salt suicide runs at the speed thirst drains. `browser_smoke` 17 asserts the half a browser can see — the overlay ships, it is closed on a live body, and both answers encode to the right byte through the real bridge. | open |

| the HUD files leaving the renderer tier (**ARMED by the operator 2026-08-04**, proposed by the ui lane the same day) | **Armed:** `ci/gates.sh`'s `renderer_touched` exempts `web/index.html`, `web/src/hud.js` and `web/src/input.js`; every other path under `web/` still schedules the renderer tier. **The standing rule armed with it:** a path joins that list ONLY in a commit that also extends `ui_smoke` to cover what it can break. Originally proposed as: `web/index.html`, `web/src/hud.js` and `web/src/input.js` stop scheduling it, so a one-line HUD edit pays `ui_smoke` instead of `browser_smoke` + `vantages` — 0.8 s against ~19 min, both wall-clock on this shared box and neither a reference-hardware claim. Built on this branch, then reverted: at tier `auto` — the tier the loop merges on — subtracting a path from that question subtracts a gate from the merge, and the tier mechanism is the operator's, not a lane branch's. **The evidence is landed rather than promised.** `ci/ui_smoke.mjs` now asserts, as a strict superset, every `index.html`/`hud.js` contract `browser_smoke` holds — the table is in that file's header, keyed to `browser_smoke` line numbers. The one that caught the first attempt: `#vitals` at inline `display:"block"` (`browser_smoke:1745`), a value the gate read and discarded. Eleven mutants of `hud.js`/`index.html` were run against it, including that one, and all eleven went red. **If armed:** one regex edit, and the standing rule that a path joins the list only in a commit that also extends `ui_smoke` to cover what it can break. | `ci/gates.sh` `renderer_touched` · `ci/ui_smoke.mjs` header table |
| the move verb · wire v17 | **A player could not move an item, so `ARCH_BOX` was unreachable, a hotbar could not be arranged, and `ALPHA.md` §1's "server validates every move (it already does)" was false — there was no move to validate.** Ranked gap 1 in *both* `findings/pass-20260804-173640-01-judge.md` and `-02`. `Command::Move { id, bag, from_kind, from_slot, to_kind, to_slot, count }` spans two containers today: `CONT_SELF = 0` (the player's `INV_SLOTS`, hotbar included, since the hotbar is slots `0..6` of the same array) and `CONT_BAG = 1` (a backpack by id, reach-checked at the moment the move resolves). `CONT_MAX` names the last live kind rather than restating it as a literal; the two spare values of the 2-bit field are reserved for the deployed box, which is why the field is a kind and not a bool. **The ordering trap is closed structurally, not by discipline.** `CLAUDE.md`'s trap list says this verb is the most bug-prone thing in the reference and that it fails *as the server disconnecting the client* — three Oxide fixes in 28 minutes on one 2019 day, the third a fix of the fix, all splice-point moves that landed as a dropped session because container state diverged and a request computed against a container the server disagreed about reads as forged. So `inventory.rs` splits the verb into `plan_move(cap, src, dst, count) -> Result<MovePlan, u32>` and `resolve(plan, src, dst) -> (ItemStack, ItemStack)`, **neither of which takes a `&mut` to anything**: they are handed `ItemStack` by value. A caller physically cannot half-apply a move, and the only two writes live in `World::move_item` past every `return`. A `MovePlan` value *is* the proof that every check passed. **Every ambiguity refuses; nothing is ever clamped.** A partial merge is the container form of the rounding the quantize-both-sides law forbids: the client drew 40, the sim did 25, and neither side can ever notice. `inv_add`'s take-what-fits is right for a pickup, where no particular number was asked for, and wrong for a verb the client has already drawn. Reasons `REFUSE_M_SLOT = 1` · `REFUSE_M_EMPTY = 2` · `REFUSE_M_COUNT = 3` · `REFUSE_M_NO_ROOM = 4` · `REFUSE_M_NO_CONTAINER = 5` · `REFUSE_M_REACH = 6` · `REFUSE_M_UNSTACKABLE = 7`, with `REFUSE_M_MAX` naming the last of them, three bits wide — and "it is out of reach" is deliberately not "it is gone", because they are different things to tell a player. A same-slot move refuses rather than no-ops: the caller reads copies and writes both back, so it would write the source and let the destination win, duplicating `count` items out of nothing. **Wire v17, every part inside a field an earlier version widened.** `ACT_MOVE = 12` is the thirteenth action of sixteen (v12's 4 bits), nine bytes, carrying a 32-bit container id *unconditionally* rather than conditionally on the kinds — a total decoder is worth five bytes on a lane that sends one per drag. `SUB_MOVED = 36` and `SUB_MOVE_REFUSED = 37` are the 37th and 38th event subtypes of sixty-four (v13's 6 bits), with `SUB_MAX` naming the highest live subtype, `CONT_KIND_BITS = 2`, `MOVE_SLOT_BITS = 5`, `REFUSE_M_BITS = 4` (three at v17 and widened with the oven's eighth refusal at v28 — restated here rather than left at 3, because this registry is checked against the constant that ships, not against the day it was written). No existing message moved a bit; 61 goldens regenerated in the same commit (wall 6). `EV_MOVED = 26` carries `(player, address, count << 16 \| item)` and `EV_MOVE_REFUSED = 27` carries `(player, reason, address)` — reason in `b` and address in `c`, matching every other refusal in the lane. The **address rides the refusal on purpose**: with it the client rolls back exactly the drag it predicted; without it the only safe response to any refusal is resyncing a container. `encode_event_move_refused` deliberately does *not* range-check the address, because the commonest refusal is `REFUSE_M_SLOT`, whose whole content is an address the sim just rejected — an encoder that refused to carry it would make the one message a desynced client most needs unsendable. **Slot contents are not in the ack**, and that is a decision: the server already diffs the whole inventory against its last-acked copy every tick and pushes changed slots as `EventMsg::Inv`, so applying the move client-side too would be two writers on one mirror. What the panel needs from the event is the thing the diff cannot say — whether the drag it drew was kept. **Gates added**: `crates/sim-core/tests/inventory_move.rs`, 14 tests, three of them properties over pseudo-random states rather than examples, because an example suite is exactly what the reference had — `refused_moves_never_mutate` (both containers bit-identical on every refusal, and every published reason must be reachable), `moves_conserve_every_item` (**the anti-dupe wall**: every item id's total across player and bags invariant on *every* move, checked per-iteration so a dupe and a matching loss cannot net to zero over the run), and `every_move_answers_and_none_panics` (**the anti-disconnect wall**: exhaustive over the address space one past every live bound, exactly one event each, never a panic). Plus `resolve_conserves_over_the_whole_small_space` over the pure pair. The conservation fuzz's sampler was **measured, not guessed**: drawing `count` blind refused on `REFUSE_M_COUNT` for 65% of ten thousand draws before reaching any arithmetic, so the count now comes off the source stack — the sampler moved to meet the coverage floor, not the floor to meet the sampler. `event_roles.rs` classifies both new codes by role in the same commit (ledger 16/25 → 18/27), which is the rule that item exists to enforce. **Deliberately absent**: the deployed box, which is `NOW.md`'s new top item and is purely additive from here — a third `CONT_*` kind and a store, with not one line of `plan_move` moving. | CLAUDE.md traps · ALPHA §1 · sim-core `inventory.rs` `world.rs` `backpack.rs` · `crates/protocol/src/{lib,event,goldens}.rs` · `findings/pass-20260804-173640-01-judge.md` `-02` · `NOW.md` items 1–2 |

Three lanes each run their own `./ci/gates.sh`, and `ui_smoke` is in the CODE tier that every lane runs on every pass, so the port it actually collided with was the other two copies of itself. Presented 2026-08-04 as a flaky wall: red, then green on an unchanged clean tree, dying at `EADDRINUSE` before a check ran. No fixed port can fix a gate colliding with itself, and a retry loop is the same race with a longer fuse. **Not fixed here, same class:** `browser_smoke` (8934, dev wire 4433/4434) and `vantages` (8971) are fixed too — rarer, because the renderer tier only runs when renderer paths change, but two lanes touching `web/` in one window is not unusual. | coast road v0 (TERRAIN §1 stage 7) | **The island had nowhere to go, and both judge reports said so independently** (`pass-…-01` gap 3 "the island has nowhere to go"; `pass-…-02` gap 2 "nothing in it that acts") — the fix each named first was one loot-bearing destination that manufactures traffic, and stage 7 is the one that needs no monument art. Built as a **pure function of (seed, x, z)**, not the memoized ring TERRAIN.md's stage 7–9 constraint block anticipated: the road is the set of points a fixed offset inland of the shoreline, so a sample is on it iff the shoreline crossing lies in a window around the point `ROAD_INLAND_M = 40.0` m seaward along its own outward radial. Three `height` taps, one for most of the island — no memo, no `limits.rs` cap, no signature threaded through eight `scatter` call sites, and **the goldens do not move** (`height` is read, never written; the golden's 256 scatter cells sit at the island center, ~600 m inside the ring). It also tracks the coastline's wobble exactly instead of approximating it with control points. Widths `ROAD_HALF_W = 2.0` (the ~4 m carriageway of TERRAIN §6) and `ROAD_SHOULDER_HALF_W = 5.0`; radial bracket `ROAD_R_MIN = 600.0`–`ROAD_R_MAX = 1000.0`, measured 630–920 m so the bracket is not doing the work; `ROAD_MAX_GRADE = 2.0` is the one-probe early-out's implied bound, deliberately above `CLIFF_SLOPE_RATIO` so declining to cross ground scatter already vetoes costs nothing. `ROAD_BARREL_PERMILLE = 250` is not invented — it is the rate the beach row of `alpha_default` already washes barrels up at, so shore and route agree. One measured correction on the way in: the window alone laid road across inlets (the ring dipped to 0.00 m), because a radial 40 m out of an inlet's far shore satisfies it — the land guard `LAND_MIN_H` (0.6 m, the threshold `scatter` had always used as an unnamed literal, now named and shared) fixed it and lifted the lowest road ground to 1.24 m. **Assumes height falls monotonically across the ~10 m shoulder window**; a shore that rises again inside it reads as off-road, which is a grade above the cliff ratio where nobody walks. Not built: the flattening half of stage 7 (it needs a mask inside `height`, which is the representation decision the constraint block defers) and the dirt material, which is client-side. Gated by `crates/sim-core/tests/road.rs`, 5 arithmetic tests over 4 seeds × 64 bearings — closure on every bearing, 0 slots on the carriageway re-derived from the slot list, ≥40 barrels and ≥20% of shoulder occupancy, <10% of the road over the cliff ratio (measured 5.9%), and the sea 39–42 m seaward against the stated 40. | sim-core `terrain.rs` · `tests/road.rs` · TERRAIN §1 stage 7 |
| barrel smash hits (`content/loot.toml` `hits`) | Swings to open a container. **Proposed default: barrel 3, crate 5** — a barrel is a roadside interruption and not a chore, and the crate is dearer because the monument that will hold it is not free to reach. It is a *content* field, not a code literal: re-pricing the walk between barrels is a balance pass (wall 7), and `validate` refuses zero because a container nothing can open never pays. Reaches the sim through `bake_loot`, is in the canonical content hash, and `tests/loot.rs` asserts the sim reads the declared number rather than a constant. | `CONTENT.md` §5 |
| barrel loot lands in the ground-container store | Not a new store: a smashed barrel stands up a `backpack.rs` record (`stand_up`), which is what a container on the ground already is — an address, a lifetime, and slots the move verb resolves as `CONT_BAG`. A second store would have bought a second wire message, a second sync walk and a second eviction policy for one difference nothing downstream reads (`owner` is state-hash and test material, never a gate). Consequence, stated rather than discovered: barrel loot shares `MAX_BACKPACKS` = 256 and its **evict-nearest-despawn** policy with death bags, so a barrel-heavy shard can evict an old death bag. Raise the cap or split the stores if that is wrong. | `NETCODE.md` §6.4 |
| haven pad v0 (TERRAIN §1 stage 8) | **The road led past barrels, not to anything** — the judge's ranked gap 2 (`findings/pass-20260804-205133-01-judge.md`): zero monument/landmark/POI anywhere in `crates/`, `content/` or `web/src`, and `ROAD_BARREL_PERMILLE` is literally the beach row's own rate, so "walking the loop is worth the same as standing where you spawned". Stage 8 is also the monument hook: every later POI is "carve pad + exclusion zone + scatter table". **The representation decision the stage 7–9 constraint block defers is settled here, and not the way it framed it.** The memo is trivially small — one site, `Haven { x, z, y, relief }`, no raster and no control points — so shape was never the constraint. The constraint is the **signature**: a carve is a write to `height`, and `terrain::height(seed, x, z)` is called from ~50 sites across four crates, `movement.rs`, `collide.rs`, `build.rs` and `deploy.rs` among them, all owned by the systems lane. It cannot be half-threaded either — a client mesh that sees the pad and a collision path that does not is a player standing in the air. So v0 **finds** a flat site instead of **making** one: the argmax scores flatness precisely so the eventual cut is small, and `haven` carries the relief it settled for so the gate can publish the number that decides whether the carve is optional. **It is: measured worst dense relief across all 16 gate seeds is 3.76 m over a 32 m pad (worst rim slope 0.21, against a cliff ratio of 1.19).** Placement: `HAVEN_CANDIDATES = 64` bearings off the 256-entry yaw LUT (no trig, wall 1), each marching seaward from `ROAD_R_MIN + ROAD_INLAND_M` to the **first** shoreline crossing in `HAVEN_MARCH_M = 4.0` steps then bisecting it `HAVEN_BISECT_ITERS = 12` times, and stepping `ROAD_INLAND_M` back inland — the road's own definition of its center line, inverted, so the site lands on the ring by construction and `road_band` confirms it rather than being trusted to agree. Score = footprint relief over a `HAVEN_PROBES = 8` rosette at `HAVEN_RADIUS_M = 16.0` + `HAVEN_HEIGHT_W = 0.25` × height above the land line, minimized, ascending bearings taking strict improvements so ties go to the lowest index. **One measured correction on the way in, and the gate caught it, not review:** bisecting the whole 400 m bracket has no single crossing to converge on where a radial meets water twice (an inlet, a channel behind an islet), and it put seed 1's pad 131 m off the best site — the coarse-march-first bracket is what fixed it. Same class as stage 7's monotonic-fall assumption. Bounded by `MAX_HAVEN_CANDIDATES = 64` in `limits.rs` (wall 4), resolved at `World::new` and never in a tick (wall 2), ~1,000 `height` taps once. `scatter` takes the `&Haven` rather than resolving it, because it is called 65,536 times per island. **The goldens do not move** (`test_terrain_golden` green unchanged: `height` is still read, never written, and the pad sits ≥600 m from the golden's center block). Gate `crates/sim-core/tests/haven.rs`, 6 arithmetic tests: determinism + 16 distinct sites over 16 seeds, on-road/on-land/in-bracket with both fallbacks asserted unreachable, sea at 40 m ±`4.0` (measured 2.50), flatness re-measured on a 48-tap footprint the selector never scored against `HAVEN_RELIEF_BAR_M = 5.0` (measured 3.76) and rim slope ≤ `0.45` (measured 0.21), the argmax re-derived by a 0.05 m linear march with no candidate allowed to beat the shipped score by more than `HAVEN_SCORE_SLACK_M = 0.5` (measured 0.29) and ≥48 of 64 bearings road-legal (measured 60), and the exclusion zone proved non-vacuous against a control haven parked off-island — `HAVEN_MIN_CLEARED_TOTAL = 12` slots cleared across 4 swept seeds (measured 15), 0 standing inside. **Not built: the carve and its blend radius** (needs the memo threaded through `height`, cross-lane — see NOW.md), the pad's own `loot.toml` table, and any greybox mesh: nothing renders the pad yet, so it is a clearing on the map and not yet a place. | sim-core `terrain.rs` · `limits.rs` · `tests/haven.rs` · TERRAIN §1 stage 8 |
| box v0: slot count, capacity, and who may open one (`limits.rs` `BOX_SLOTS` / `MAX_BOXES` / `MAX_BOX_SPILL_PER_TICK`) | **A deployed box holds things now** — the judges' "a raid takes nothing" half (ranked gap 2, `findings/pass-20260804-205133-02-judge.md`; gap 1 of `-01`). `ARCH_BOX` baked and placed since the deployable slice and had no contents array and no verb. Closed additively: a third `CONT_*` kind against a dense `BoxRec` list, **not one line of `plan_move` moved**. Three numbers needed defaults. **`BOX_SLOTS` = 12**, proposed against the reference's small box rather than picked, and deliberately under `INV_SLOTS`: a box is a place to put things down, not a second inventory. Both box rows in `content/deployables.toml` share it — a per-row `slots` field is the content-shaped answer and is the open half of this row, wanted once a client can see a box at all. **`MAX_BOXES` = 256**, overflow **refuse** (`REFUSE_D_FULL`), matching `MAX_HEARTHS`: nothing is lost by refusing a box that was never placed, and the refusal lands before the deploy record inserts so a full store can never leave a standing box with nowhere to keep what you put in it. **`MAX_BOX_SPILL_PER_TICK` = 16**, overflow **drop that box's spill** — headroom for a piece cascade taking several boxes with one wall, not for the sweep. **A box is open to anyone who can reach it, not owner-only**, and that is the load-bearing call here: a box a raider cannot empty is the complaint with an extra step, and it matches an unlocked door and a bag. Lock v0 (already §open, still unanswered) is what would change it, and it belongs on the `locked` bit a door already carries rather than a second rule. Addressed by packed `box_key(cx, cz, level)`, not an id — the client already draws `cx`/`cz`/`level` from the deploy sync, so naming a box cost no wire. | `limits.rs` · `deploy.rs` · `inventory.rs` · `crates/sim-core/tests/box_container.rs` |
| wire v18: a widened meaning is a wire change | `CONT_KIND_BITS` has been 2 since v17 with the third value reserved for the box, so **not one bit moved and not one fixture's bytes changed but the hello's** — verified by regenerating all 61. The version moved anyway. That is wall 6 working rather than an exception to it: the same nine bytes that were `Malformed` on a v17 server now mean "move it into the box at this address", so a v18 client against a v17 server would have its drags declined by the frame reader — the disconnect the move verb exists to never cause. `PROTO_VER` is the only thing that catches a mismatched build, and a reserved value being taken up is exactly what the reservation was for. | haven crates v0 (TERRAIN §1 stage 8, the scatter table) | **The pad existed and was worth nothing to a player** — the judge's ranked gap 2 (`findings/pass-20260804-205133-02-judge.md`): "a player who walks the loop to the one place the road goes arrives at *less* than the shoulder they walked: a circle where the trees stop." The hook is three parts — carve, exclusion zone, scatter table — and this builds the third, which is the one the carve does not block. `HAVEN_CRATES = 5` is not invented: it is the reference `SpawnGroup`'s default `maxPopulation` (`reference/SPAWN.md` §10), the only per-destination container count either document names. `HAVEN_CRATE_R_M = 10.0` is bounded on both sides by arithmetic — above `CELL_SIZE·√2 / (2·sin(180°/N))` so no two anchors share a scatter cell (a cell holds one slot, so a closer ring would DROP a crate with no panic, no golden move and no clippy wall — measured closest pair 11.716 m against an 11.314 m cell diagonal), and inside `HAVEN_RADIUS_M` with a walkable rim. Positions are **authored, not drawn**: `SPAWN.md` §6 records the reference hanging monument loot on hand-placed spawn points with no spacing rule, and §9.3 complains our scatter is white noise — a destination should read as arranged. `Occupant::CrateSlot = 9` **skips 8 deliberately**: the client's archetype table is indexed by this enum and its 8 is the felled-pine stump, so taking 8 would draw every pad crate as a stump with every gate green (CLAUDE.md's positional-payload trap, exactly). No wire change — no packet carries an occupant. **Placing something on the pad made the selector stricter, and the gate found both, not review.** A candidate's whole ring must clear `LAND_MIN_H` — seed 555555 centred a pad at 0.69 m on a shore shelf whose ring is under the land line at *every* radius tried, so shrinking cannot fix it and flatness does not see it (1.75 m of relief is unremarkable) — and no container may stand on the carriageway, which `tests/road.rs` requires clear and the pad sits on the road by construction. The second is answered by trying `HAVEN_PHASE_TRIES = 16` rotations of the ring and refusing the site if none is clear, rather than deriving the road's local direction and phasing away from it analytically: that closed form has 6.8° of margin against a coastline whose ring radius wobbles 630–920 m, and `road_band` at the anchor is exact. Both are `SPAWN.md` §5's placement-check chain reached from the other side — **refuse the position, never patch the object**. `Haven` gains `phase`, so `haven_crate(&Haven, k)` stays a pure function every reader agrees on. **The prize is gated at both ends, because it is two claims in two languages.** Geometry: `tests/haven.rs` grew to 8 tests — exactly `HAVEN_CRATES` crates islandwide re-derived by sweeping all 65,536 cells (the silent-drop wall), each inside the pad, on land, at its own terrain height, unscaled, yaw facing the centre, and the closest anchor pair clearing a cell diagonal; plus containers-per-m² on the pad over the same on the shoulder against `HAVEN_PRIZE_RATIO_MIN = 2.0` (measured worst 2.64), area counted from real shoulder cells rather than an annulus formula so the arithmetic cannot flatter the side it was written for. Content: `ci/haven_prize.mjs`, 47 checks, because no Rust gate reads `content/loot.toml` at all — no verb opens a container — so nothing in the workspace would notice a rebalance flattening the gradient back to the coast road's own defect. It asserts the ORDERING only (richer rolls, more expected items, ≥1 item reachable only from the pad: measured 2.31× and 5 items), never a balance target, which stays `CONTENT.md`'s. `loot.crate` was dead content until this pass gave it a spawn site; it is reachable content ahead of reachable loot, and the open verb is the systems lane's. One perf fix rides along because this change made it dearer: `client-core`'s `terrain_fill_slots` re-resolved the whole argmax once per streamed chunk (measured 5,453 height taps mean, `-02` judge), now memoized on the seed — its only input. The stale "~1,000 taps" claim in `terrain.rs` is corrected to a ~28,000 bound. **Not built: the carve, the dirt band on the carriageway, and denser bay-mouth slots.** | sim-core `terrain.rs` · `tests/haven.rs` · `ci/haven_prize.mjs` · `content/loot.toml` · `web/src/props.js` · `reference/SPAWN.md` §5 §6 §9 · TERRAIN §1 stage 8 |
| wire v19: the container view, and the first S→C message unicast by design | **Container contents had never crossed the wire — for a bag either** (`NOW.md` item 1; the ui lane's cross-lane request 1). Two messages, both inside fields earlier versions already widened, so **nothing existing moved a bit**: C→S `ACT_CONTAINER` is the fourteenth action code of sixteen, S→C `SUB_CONT_SYNC` the 39th event subtype of sixty-four. Verified rather than asserted — of the 61 v18 fixtures, **60 regenerate byte-identical** and only `v19_hello.bin` differs, because `PROTO_VER` is inside it; five new fixtures join them, one of which (`v19_action_move_box`) pins the box move v18's row shipped and no fixture ever exercised. **An open grants nothing.** It is a subscription, not a permission: the server re-resolves the handle and re-proves `in_reach` **every tick**, against the same quantized body position the move verb will judge on, so a forged open of a box across the map yields a close and not one slot, and a real open the player then walks away from does the same. That is CLAUDE.md's quantize-both-sides law applied to containers — the set a client is shown is exactly the set it can move in, so a refusal is computed on the values the panel was drawn from. **Unicast is the design, not an optimization**: a box's contents fanned to AOI is a raider reading a base's stock through its walls, plus a bandwidth bill for a panel nobody has open. **Not sim state**: no `Command` carries it, nothing enters the WAL, `World::state_hash` is identical either way, so a replay reproduces a round without reproducing who had a panel open. The action is **one field with three meanings** (the kind alone says open-bag, open-box, or close) rather than an `open` bit beside a kind, because two fields that must agree are two fields a forged frame can disagree. The contents batch is one message and not a drip: the widest container is `INV_SLOTS` and a slot is 37 bits, so a whole container is ~146 B inside `MAX_EVENT_MSG_BYTES`, and a cursor would buy a second walk to restart, a second reset flag to get wrong, and a window in which a panel draws half full. `ACT_MAX` gives the action lane the event lane's `SUB_MAX` discipline with a compile-time assert; two action codes remain. **Not built: any panel.** Nothing under `web/` calls the four new bridge exports — `ci/client_smoke.mjs` is their only reader, which is why they are pinned there the pass they land — and the ui lane's request 2 (`client_move_readout` carries no TO kind) is untouched and still open. | protocol `lib.rs` `event.rs` `goldens.rs` · server `core.rs` `client.rs` · client-core `core.rs` `bridge.rs` · `crates/server/tests/container_wire.rs` · `ci/client_smoke.mjs` · NOW item 1 |
| haven shelter v0 (TERRAIN §1 stage 8, the greybox) | **The pad stopped reading as authored, and the previous pass is why** — the judge's ranked gap 3 (`findings/pass-20260805-002720-01-judge.md`): a circle where the trees stop was self-evidently made while the forest was uniform speckle, but scatter clumping gave the island natural clearings by design (2.2–4.1% of forest windows empty, 35–52× the independent-draw rate), so "the authored clearing is now statistically indistinguishable from the scenery around it". Its concrete item was the greybox, gated as arithmetic. **One slot carries the whole building.** A scatter cell holds one occupant and the pad has no room for a second authored anchor — five 5.66 m disks is 503 m² of an 804 m² pad, and a sixth anywhere inside the ring is closer than the 11.31 m cell diagonal to two of them — so `Occupant::HavenShelter = 10` is one archetype's mesh, not a kit of wall-sized slots. **The road gate moved it off center, and that is the finding.** The pad's center is the road's own center line by construction, so the first draft put a building across the carriageway `tests/road.rs` requires clear; it went red on seed `0x4741544553` immediately. The correction is the one the reference already makes — a destination sits BESIDE the road it is reached by — so the structure stands `HAVEN_SHELTER_R_M = 6.5` off center on a bearing chosen by a bounded search (`HAVEN_SHELTER_TRIES`, one per gap in the container ring, at `HAVEN_SHELTER_YAW_STEP` past each anchor), refusing any bearing that is under the land line, on the carriageway, or in a cell a container has taken. The last is not sizeable away: shelter and container are ~6 m apart against an 11.31 m cell diagonal, so whether they collide is a property of where the pad's grid alignment fell — measured **reachable at 27 of 64 sub-cell alignments**, i.e. the guard is load-bearing, not ceremony. `SPAWN.md` §5 for the third time: refuse the position, never patch the object. `Haven` gains `shelter`, so `haven_shelter(&Haven)` stays a pure function every reader agrees on. **One positional-payload bug, caught by a gate rather than by review, and it is worth the line.** The first `haven_shelter` returned an OUTWARD bearing where `haven_crate` returns an INWARD facing — two functions, one `u8`, opposite meanings — and `phase + 25` in bearing space is `phase + 153 + 128` in facing space, so the doorway landed exactly on container 3 at every seed. Right arity, right field, wrong meaning; no type and no golden saw it. What caught it was `tests/haven.rs` asserting the ANGLE (a dot product between two shipped positions) rather than comparing the two bytes, which is the only kind of assert that could. Both functions now face in. `HAVEN_SHELTER_HALF_M = 3.5` is the sim's only claim about the mesh, and it is what the const block uses to prove the footprint clears the containers and stays inside the exclusion zone. **Gated at both ends, because the building is JS and the placement is Rust.** Rust: `tests/haven.rs` grew to 9 — exactly one shelter islandwide re-derived by sweeping all 65,536 cells (the silent-drop wall), at its authored position, unscaled, on its own terrain height, off the carriageway, `HAVEN_SHELTER_R_M` off center, every footprint corner inside the pad, clear of every container (measured tightest 1.05 m past the 4.95 m corner radius), in a gap in the ring, and the collision the search refuses proved reachable. JS: `ci/haven_shelter.mjs`, 40 checks over the exported box list — the mesh inside `HAVEN_SHELTER_HALF_M`, the peak (9.20 m) clearing a full-scale pine (7.26 m), a tower and not a block by bounding outline, 168 triangles, and the doorway proved passable by flood-filling the floor plan at 1.2 m **in both directions**: reachable through the door, and unreachable with the door filled in, because a reachability test on a structure with no walls passes trivially. **The goldens do not move** (`height` is still read, never written; the pad is ≥600 m from the golden's center block). **Not built: collision.** `collide.rs` knows only built pieces — no scatter occupant stops a player, so today you walk through these walls. That path is the systems lane's and it is a one-line request in `NOW.md`, not a reach into their files. Also not built: the carve, and any interior. | sim-core `terrain.rs` · `tests/haven.rs` · `tests/scatter.rs` · `web/src/props.js` · `ci/haven_shelter.mjs` · `ci/gates.sh` · TERRAIN §1 stage 8 |
| scatter clumping v0 (TERRAIN §1 stage 9, the grove field) | **Our forest was an orchard, and now there is a number for it.** `reference/SPAWN.md` §9.3 calls this "the highest-value item in this file": our `scatter` draws one hash per 8 m cell and decides that cell alone, so every slot is independent of its neighbours — white noise, no groves, no clearings — while `TERRAIN.md` §1 stage 6 asks forest for "wood, cover, low visibility", which independent draws cannot deliver. Measured before the fix, conditioning on the forest biome so biome structure could not be mistaken for clumping: the variance of the tree count in a 40 m window was **1.051 / 1.027 / 0.978** times the independent-draw null on seeds 0 / 1 / 7, and **3 windows in 10,000** were empty. The reference gets its clumping from a stateful sampler — `ClusterSizeMin..Max` objects out of one quadtree leaf, braked by a 20 m local density cap (§3.4) — and we cannot have that: `scatter` is a pure function of one cell and must stay one, or the client bridge resolving a cell per chunk has to resolve the island instead. So **the clumping moves from the sampler into the weight**: a cell still decides alone, but what it decides against is `biome_weight × clump(seed, x, z)`, a low-frequency field shared with its neighbours — exactly §9.3's own prescription, one extra fBm read, still O(1), still one hash draw per cell. `CLUMP_FREQ = 1.0 / 96.0` is derived from the thing it has to change rather than chosen: the measurement counts trees in a 40 m window, so one grove must be wide enough that a window sits mostly inside one grove or one clearing rather than averaging several — 96 m is twelve scatter cells and 2.4 windows, and two octaves down it is still 24 m so a grove has an edge instead of a contour. `CLUMP_OCTAVES = 3` is the top of §9.3's stated "2–3 octave" range; the third octave is what stops a clearing being an ellipse. `CLUMP_GAIN = 2.4` does for this field what `RELIEF_GAIN` does for height (fBm output clusters well inside [-1, 1]). `CLUMP_FLOOR = 0.15` is what a clearing keeps — not zero, because a bald clearing is as unlike the reference as an orchard and the roll must still be able to put a rock in one. The factor is then **squared**, which is `SPAWN.md` §9.4 taken at its word in the one place we now have a soft filter term to take it at: the reference accepts a decor candidate with probability `factor²`, so marginal ground thins quadratically and an edge reads as a gradient with a soft tail rather than a step. **`CLUMP_NORM = 2.70` is a measurement, not a preference** — the reciprocal of the field's island mean, so the field redistributes density without changing how much of it there is (`TERRAIN.md` §6's live-slot band is a budget and a texture change may not spend it). The squared factor means 0.3699 / 0.3695 / 0.3692 / 0.3734 on seeds 0 / 1 / 7 / 12345 over the 65,536-sample 8 m grid; 2.70 is the reciprocal of the middle and inside 1% of all four, and the residual is the field's own seed-to-seed wobble, which no single constant can remove and which the gate's tolerance carries instead. It scales **the whole biome row, not the tree entry** — a clearing is a clearing, not a clearing with the rocks left standing — which also leaves composition to the content pass and takes only density. It sits **below the road and the pad branches** in `scatter`: a shoulder barrel is drawn at the road's own `ROAD_BARREL_PERMILLE` and a pad crate is authored, and neither is weather. **Result, same units as the defect:** dispersion **2.90 / 3.11 / 3.05 / 3.34** (was 0.98–1.05) and empty forest windows **37–52×** the binomial null (was 0.5–1.2×), with live slots **9,825 / 10,033 / 9,337 / 9,770** against 9,901 / 10,269 / 9,503 before — inside §6's 8,000–12,000 band and within 2.3% of the counts it replaced. Gated by `crates/sim-core/tests/scatter.rs`, 5 arithmetic tests over 4 seeds, and **the suite carries its own null rather than a remembered number**: a per-cell independent draw at the measured density is binomial, so its variance is `n·p·(1−p)` and its empty share `(1−p)^n`, both closed forms — no control run, nothing to go stale. Two readings of the same field because one alone is gameable (dispersion says the counts are overspread, the empty share says the low tail actually reaches zero); a non-vacuity floor of 5,000 windows so a broken mask cannot pass by matching nothing; the normalizer re-derived by the gate with an sd floor so a `clump` returning the constant 1.0 fails; a saturation wall (the forest row peaks at 945 of 1,000 per-mille in a grove — past 1,000 the row's tail entries become unreachable and density falls with nothing reporting it); and the authored slots re-counted so a grove cannot thin the pad ring or the road. **Goldens moved and were regenerated in the same commit** (`GOLDEN_TERRAIN_HASH`, `GOLDEN_FINAL_HASH`) — this is a worldgen change, which by wall 7's own logic is a wipe-boundary change (`SPAWN.md` §9.2). **Not built:** the biome-edge half of §9.4 — `biome()` is still a hard classifier, so a forest/meadow boundary is still a step in composition even though density now ramps across it. | sim-core `terrain.rs` · `tests/scatter.rs` · `examples/terrain_stats.rs` · `reference/SPAWN.md` §9.3 §9.4 · TERRAIN §1 stage 9 |
| occupant volume v0 (TERRAIN §1 stage 6, cover) | **Four passes built a road, a pad, a container ring and a building, and every one of them is a hologram** — the judge's ranked gap 2 (`findings/pass-20260805-002720-02-judge.md`): `collide.rs` and `movement.rs` contain zero references to `Occupant`, so `TERRAIN.md` §1 stage 6 asks the forest for "wood, cover, low visibility" and **cover does not exist** — there is nothing on this island you can break line of sight on. Its concrete item names this lane's half: "one `terrain`-side occupant query the movement path calls, with a per-occupant radius in the archetype data". **The volume belongs to the occupant, not to the movement code** — whether a pine has a trunk you stop at is a property of the pine — so `OCCUPANT_R_M` / `OCCUPANT_TOP_M` (11 entries, indexed by `Occupant as usize`) and `slot_blocks` land in `terrain.rs`, and the movement path owns only which slots it asks about. That seam is deliberate: `scatter` costs a `height` fan, a `moisture`, a `clump` and a `road_band` per cell, so a movement step must never re-derive slots by calling it, and `slot_blocks` takes an already-resolved `&Slot` and no seed so that nothing invites it. **The numbers are read, not chosen.** Every radius is the maximum horizontal extent of the client's authored mesh (`web/src/props.js` `ARCHETYPES`), because the defect is walking through drawn geometry: erring outward blocks a little air at a dodecahedron's flats, erring inward is the bug. Tops come from the same rows, where `lift` is the mesh's half-height. **The tree is the one deliberate exception**: it blocks at the 0.26 m trunk for 5.7 m, not at the canopy, because you walk *under* a pine. **Three zeros, three different kinds** — the bush is a design call (a bush you cannot push through is a wall you cannot see over, and the reference treats it the same), index 8 is structural (the client's felled-pine stump is not a sim occupant; the enum skips the discriminant and this table keeps the hole so the two stay aligned by construction), and `HavenShelter` is **DEFERRED, pinned by a test so it cannot be read as tuned**: a doorway is the one shape a radius cannot express — a cylinder either seals the entrance or blocks nothing — so it needs a box list the sim owns and a gate keeping it equal to `props.js`'s fourteen boxes, which is its own slice. `OCCUPANT_PROBE_CELLS = 1` is derived, not chosen, and the const block proves it: every slot lies strictly inside its own cell (jitter is ±3 m on an 8 m cell; measured worst margin 0.122 m over 38,969 slots), so a 3×3 scan is complete while `max(r)·1.1 + CAPSULE_RADIUS_M` (2.050 m) stays under `CELL_SIZE`. **Two bugs the gates caught, both worth the line.** (1) The vertical test was written as a ceiling — `feet_y < slot.y + top` — which type-checks, reads fine, and makes a boulder on a clifftop block a body standing at sea level forty metres beneath it, because a body below the top is "not above" it. It is an INTERVAL OVERLAP against the body's own capsule height. (2) `yaw_dir` takes a **wire** yaw and indexes `LUT[yaw >> 8]`, so a new scan written as `for yaw in 0..256 { yaw_dir(yaw) }` sampled **one bearing 256 times**; the pad-ring test failed loudly (0/256 walkable, always the same crate) but the road test **passed without earning it**, which is the repo's own worst bug class caught inside one pass. Every pre-existing suite shifts correctly (`tests/road.rs`, `tests/haven.rs`); only the new one was wrong. **The road's walkability is measured as clear WIDTH, not point clearance, and that is a finding.** The first draft sampled the carriageway's inner edge and demanded it be clear; on seed 0 a pine at (1773.2, 1271.5) reached 0.69 m over that edge from the shoulder — legally, `tests/road.rs` owns the shoulder and is green — and a road lined with trees is a road. A circulation loop is walkable when a body FITS, so the gate is the longest clear run across the carriageway against the body's own diameter (2 × `CAPSULE_RADIUS_M` = 0.80 m). **Measured, all four seeds:** narrowest clear carriageway **3.88 / 3.12 / 2.62 / 3.00 m**; cover on **19.2–20.5% of land cells, ~18 m apart**; solid ground **0.43–0.47%** of the island. Those last two are separate instruments and conflating them was the third mistake: footprint answers "can I cross" (a trunk is 0.21 m² and 7,589 of them leave 99.6% standable) while density answers "is there cover", so the floor is on density and the ceiling on area. The crate ring is **82.4% walkable on every seed**, and the blocked 17.6% is asserted to be the pad's own containers and nothing else. **Nothing calls this yet** — `collide.rs`/`movement.rs` are the systems lane's files and wiring it is a one-line request in `NOW.md`, not a reach into them. **The goldens do not move**: no slot data changed, this reads `Slot` and writes nothing. | sim-core `terrain.rs` · `tests/solid.rs` (new, 8 tests) · `web/src/props.js` (read only) · TERRAIN §1 stage 6 |
| collapse v0 — a base comes down when you take its legs out | `MAX_COLLAPSE_PIECES = 64` pieces one cascade may drop in a tick; `SUPPORT_SWEEP_PER_TICK = 32` pieces the standing-support sweep re-checks a tick. The cap is derived, not picked: every removal spends one `EV_PIECE_REMOVED` slot of the 256-slot event ring, and that event is the only thing that tells a client the piece is gone — a cascade allowed to fill the ring leaves the rest of the collapse drawn on every screen for the session. A quarter of the ring keeps the tick's other events inside their own budget. Overflow policy **defer**, not shed: `build::support_sweep` drops one leftover piece and its own cascade per tick, so the cap costs latency and never correctness. | `limits.rs` · `build.rs` · `CLAUDE.md` wall 4 |
| build aim distance v0 (**client lane, 2026-08-06**) | `place::AIM_AHEAD_M = 3.5` m — how far ahead of the feet the native build ghost aims. Carried over from `web/src/main.js`'s `buildTarget`, which used the same 3.5 with no row here. Comfortably inside `build::BUILD_REACH_M` (5 m) with room for an anchor sitting up to half a cell further out than the cell centre, so an aimed cell is one the sim accepts on reach. Wanted from the operator: whether a ghost should sit further out (4.0–4.5, faster to lay a foundation line) or closer (2.5–3.0, easier to place against a wall you are standing at). | `crates/client/src/ui/place.rs` |

| shelter volume v0 (TERRAIN §1 stage 8, the greybox) | **The deferral in `occupant volume v0` is discharged: `HavenShelter` was the third zero and it is gone.** A doorway is the one shape a radius cannot express, so the sim now owns `SHELTER_BOXES` — fourteen `[cx, cy, cz, sx, sy, sz]` rows in the slot's local frame, ground at y = 0 and the door on +Z, a row-for-row mirror of `web/src/props.js`'s `HAVEN_SHELTER_PARTS`. **The numbers are read, not chosen**, exactly as the radii were: `SHELTER_PEAK_M` = 9.2 is the mesh's own peak and `SHELTER_CORNER_R_M` = 4.9498 is its bounding half-diagonal rounded OUTWARD (const-asserted by squared compare, since `sqrt` is not const). `slot_blocks` keeps its signature — the systems lane's call site is unchanged — and routes only this occupant to a narrow phase after the bounding circle accepts; the horizontal test clamps the point into each box rather than growing the box by `capsule_r`, which would round the corners the wrong way and stop a body that is diagonally past a jamb. **The plinth is FLOOR, not the fourteenth wall** (`SHELTER_FLOOR_IX`), and that exception is the load-bearing decision here: a 7 × 7 box spanning y ∈ [−1.4, 0.2] otherwise seals the building from outside, because a body at pad ground overlaps that interval and meets the floor's rim before it ever reaches the door. The exception is structural rather than a threshold — the const block asserts the plinth's top IS the lowest bottom of every other box (0.2 m, to 0.1 mm), which is what "the walls stand on it" means as a number — so it cannot drift onto a wall. **Its cost is stated:** nothing makes a body stand on the plinth either, so at 0.2 m the floor reads as a kerb a player sinks into; that is the ground-query half and it is the systems lane's `collide.rs`. **The cross-language gate is the point of the slice**, not a trimming: `ci/haven_shelter.mjs` now parses `SHELTER_BOXES` out of `terrain.rs` and compares all 14 × 6 fields against `props.js`, plus the floor index BY NAME ("plinth") and the broad phase against `props.js`'s own boxes — 40 checks became 156. Nothing else could catch this: rustc compiles, `test_terrain_golden` hashes slots not shapes, `browser_smoke` still renders a building, and the failure is a doorway one side has and the other walls off. Mutation-tested both ways (widen a jamb 0.2 m, move the peak 0.1 m) — names the exact field, exits 1. **Three defects found and fixed, all pre-existing.** (1) The const guard that claimed to stop the build when an occupant is added **fired on a table edit and never on the enum edit it named** — `len() == 11` and `HavenShelter as usize == len() - 1` both survive adding `Foo = 11`. Replaced by `occupant_volume`, an exhaustive `match` a new variant makes non-exhaustive; it also takes the last unchecked index off the sim path, so `slot_blocks` can no longer panic per body per tick. (2) That hole was **live, not hypothetical**: `examples/terrain_stats.rs` carried `[0u32; 10]` through the `HavenShelter = 10` commit and panicked on the first haven cell of every seed — found by running it, which no gate does. (3) `OCCUPANT_TOP_M[BarrelSlot]` was 0.95 against its own stated derivation of 0.5 + 0.475 = 0.975, erring INWARD, the direction the table's own rule calls the bug; now 0.975. **And one false sentence deleted** (judge check 9): the claim that "a trunk is 0.21 m² and 7,589 of them leave 99.6% standable" re-labelled the all-kinds blocker count as trunks — trunks alone are about a seventh of the measured area, because the sum is over r² and a 1.5 m rock is 50 trunks' worth. `tests/solid.rs` now **asserts that ratio** instead of asserting the prose. **Measured:** 14 tests in `tests/solid.rs` (was 8); the shelter blocks 46/256 of the crate ring on every seed (bounded < 85, it stands ON the ring at 6.5 m with a 4.95 m reach and always did), no container stands inside its walls on any seed, and the interior is reachable from outside only through the door — flood-filled, and re-filled with the door plugged to prove the walls are what enclose it. **The goldens do not move**: no slot data changed. **Nothing calls this yet** — `collide.rs` is the systems lane's and the request stays a line in `NOW.md`. | sim-core `terrain.rs` · `tests/solid.rs` (14 tests) · `examples/terrain_stats.rs` · `ci/haven_shelter.mjs` (156 checks) · `web/src/props.js` (read only) · TERRAIN §1 stage 8 |
| swing prompt precedence v0 (**ui lane, 2026-08-05**) | **Not a number — a precedence.** `web/src/main.js`'s `updatePrompt` draws `promptFor(aimPick(...)) || promptForSwing(swingAt())`: when E has a pick AND a swing has a target, E's text wins the one centre line. Both are true whenever you stand at a box inside 2 m of a tree, which is ordinary, so something must choose. Argued rather than spoken: E is the half a player cannot discover (the mouse button is already held down, and nothing on screen would ever suggest pressing a letter), and the reference's centre hint is a single row (`Rust Images/choppingtree.jpg`) — two stacked prompts under a crosshair is a menu, not a hint. Wanted from the operator: whether that is the intended order, or whether the NEARER target should win regardless of which key it belongs to (a tree you are touching outranking a box 4 m away), or whether both should draw on two rows. `ci/ui_smoke.mjs` §R pins the order that ships, so changing it is a one-line change with a gate on it. | `web/src/main.js` · `web/src/interact.js` · `ci/ui_smoke.mjs` §R |
| collapse budget v0 — the cap that composes | `MAX_REMOVALS_PER_TICK = 64` structural piece removals the whole tick may make, across every path that takes a piece out of the store: a raider's killing blow, the decay sweep, the support backstop, and every cascade any of them seeds. **`MAX_COLLAPSE_PIECES` bounds one cascade and a tick holds many** — the judge's ranked fix 1 on `20260805-020919-01`. `upkeep_sweep` does not return after a removal the way `support_sweep` does, so its 64 visits can each run their own `collapse_from`, and up to `MAX_PLAYERS` raiders can land a killing blow besides; the per-cascade cap's stated derivation ("a quarter of the ring, so one collapse cannot fill it") is true per cascade and was never true per tick. Measured on a mass-decay fixture with the budget removed: **103 pieces removed in one tick**, against the 64 the ring was sized for. The per-cascade cap stays — it is also what sizes `collapse_from`'s stack array — and this is the bound that composes. Sized off `MAX_EVENTS_PER_TICK` the same way: a removal spends one event slot, two when a deployable stood at the same address, so 64 removals is at most 128 of 256 and leaves half the ring for everything else the tick says. Overflow policy **defer**, and refused *before* the piece leaves the store so nothing is half-removed: the decay sweep rewinds its cursor onto the entry it did not serve, a cascade leaves the rest hanging for `support_sweep`, and a raid's killing blow stops one hp short rather than parking a 0-hp piece no sweep would ever clear. A tick-local in `World::tick`, reset with the ring it protects and deliberately not a `World` field — it is spent and forgotten inside one tick exactly as `events` is, and a store that lives across ticks is one `state_hash` has to answer for. | `limits.rs` · `world.rs` · `build.rs` · `deploy.rs` · `combat.rs` · `CLAUDE.md` wall 4 |
| box handle 0 is reserved (not a knob — a rule, stated) | `box_key(0, 0, 0)` packs to **0**, and 0 is what every layer already says when it means *no container open* (`CONT_SELF` zeroes the handle field, the client bridge answers 0 for "nothing open", a server-side close writes 0). One build cell therefore minted an address indistinguishable from the absence of one, and the client had to refuse a ground handle of 0 outright to avoid sending "no container known" as a real destination — the ui lane's cross-lane request 4. Closed the way `Backpacks` already closes it, **both halves**: the handle is never minted (`place_deploy` refuses a box at that one address with `REFUSE_D_SPOT`, as `next_id: 1` refuses it for bags) and the decode guards it (`box_index`, as `index_of_id` does). Either alone leaves a side unable to trust the other — a guard with no refusal is a box you can place and never open, a refusal with no guard still answers on a forged 0. **No packing moved**, so `BOX_KEY_LAYOUT`, `ui_smoke` §P and every wire golden are untouched; biasing `box_key` was the other option and was refused because its client-side mirror lives in `web/`, which this lane may not edit. Costs one build cell at the world's origin corner, which is water under every seed — the refusal is a rule rather than a terrain accident on purpose, because the grid origin is TERRAIN's to move. | `deploy.rs` · `tests/box_container.rs` · `build.rs` tests · `NOW.md` request 4 |

| waystations v0 (TERRAIN §1 stage 8, the second tier) — `WAYSTATIONS = 2`, `WAYSTATION_CRATES = 2`, `WAYSTATION_RADIUS_M = 15.0102` (**11.0 until 2026-08-16 — widened by the operator to let the carve be armed; see the Spoken row. A ring road with a single bead on it is a commute, and the two players who were supposed to meet on the way there have exactly one place to be. **The second tier cost no new search.** `haven()` already scores `HAVEN_CANDIDATES = 64` bearings and keeps the argmin; the other 63 passed the same land and road checks and lost on flatness by centimetres. The lesser tier is the best of those losers, recorded by the scan that was already running — no extra shoreline march, no extra bisect, no extra `height` fan — then filtered by a separation floor and its own ring-rotation check. `pick_minor` is greedy rather than an argmin over subsets because the floor is a CONSTRAINT and not a term: there is no trade to make between "flat" and "far", and a site that fails it is not a worse site, it is the same place twice. Determinism comes from the pad's own construction — ascending bearing order, strict improvement only, so ties go to the lowest index and the answer does not depend on evaluation order. **The count is derived, not chosen, and it is a compile error to widen it.** `WAYSTATIONS * WAYSTATION_CRATES < HAVEN_CRATES` — the whole lesser tier added up still pays less than the one destination — which at the two-crate floor (one anchor is a barrel with a story; `SPAWN.md` §6's point is that a destination reads as ARRANGED) admits two sites and refuses three. **The radius is derived from a bug the assert found.** The first draft used 10.0 m and the gradient pointed backwards: two crates in 314 m² is 0.00637 containers/m² against the pad's five in 804 m², 0.00622, so the site with FEWER containers was the better square metre and a player optimizing loot-per-walk would have skipped the haven. Every count was right, every site was on the ring, every container stood; nothing but writing `pad_density > site_density` could see it. The floor is arithmetic — density stays under the pad's iff `R > sqrt(WAYSTATION_CRATES * HAVEN_RADIUS_M^2 / HAVEN_CRATES) = 10.12 m` — and the const block now asserts the INEQUALITY rather than the number, so widening the crate count fails at the definition. `WAYSTATION_CRATE_R_M = 6.5` is bounded on both sides the way `HAVEN_CRATE_R_M` is: under `CELL_SIZE` so an anchor is never more than one scatter cell from its centre and `scatter`'s ±1 broad phase is complete by proof rather than by margin, and over `0.75 * CELL_SIZE` so the diametric pair is 13 m apart against an 11.31 m cell diagonal and cannot share a cell (measured closest anchors 13.00 m). `WAYSTATION_MIN_SEP_M = ROAD_R_MIN` is derived from the ring it sits on: three sites on the tightest ring the road can take are 120° apart, whose chord at 600 m is 1039 m, so the floor is satisfiable by construction on the worst ring while still forcing roughly-thirds spacing (measured closest pair over 16 seeds 614 m). **No signature moved, and that was the design constraint, not a nicety.** `scatter(seed, table, haven, cx, cz)` is called from `gather.rs`, `world.rs`, `bridge.rs` and their tests — the systems lane's files — and on 2026-08-04 adding one parameter to this exact function blocked this lane for two passes, because git merged three of the five call sites clean and they compiled green on both trunks. So the tier lives in a new `minor: [Waystation; WAYSTATIONS]` field on `Haven`, which only `terrain::haven` constructs; three literal constructions in test/fixture code took the field and no caller moved. **No client, wire or protocol change carries this either**: a waystation container is `Occupant::CrateSlot`, the archetype the pad ring already ships, so `props.js` draws it unchanged and no packet carries an occupant. **The pad does not move**, which is what keeps `test_terrain_golden` and all nine of `tests/haven.rs` answering what they answered before: the haven is resolved first and the lesser tier is chosen against it, and the one control-flow edit in `haven()` — reading `road_band` a few lines earlier so both tiers share one answer — is a pure function asked of more candidates. **Two island-wide asserts were partitioned, not relaxed.** `tests/haven.rs` asserted every `CrateSlot` on the island is on the pad and that there are exactly `HAVEN_CRATES` of them; both are now false and the weak repair (filter to the pad first) would have stopped counting the thing they exist for. Instead every crate must belong to EXACTLY ONE site, and the counts are asserted per tier — strictly more than the single count it replaces, since it now pins both tiers and the emptiness of everywhere else. `tests/scatter.rs`'s authored-slot count took the same treatment. **Gated as arithmetic** (`tests/waystation.rs`, 6 tests, 0.8 s): a full tier on every one of 16 seeds (a short tier is a FAILURE, not a shrug — a seed that quietly placed one site would look like a working island until someone counted); every site on the ring by `road_band` and by radius, on land, at its own terrain height; every pair clearing the floor; the island swept at all 65,536 cells for the container count, each anchor on the ring to 0.0001 m, facing IN by dot product (asserted as an ANGLE, because the shelter's yaw once shipped wrong with the right arity, the right field and the right type), unscaled, off the carriageway, no two in a cell; the exclusion zones clear AND a control arm with the tier switched off proving they displaced something; and the gradient measured in one unit across all three tiers, pad > waystation > shoulder. **Not built: the carve** (`height` has ~80 call sites in four crates, so it stays the cross-lane change `TERRAIN.md` §7 records), any greybox at a waystation, and any verb that makes a destination worth more than its crates — `-02`'s gap 2 asks for the recycler and that is a container verb, the systems lane's half. | sim-core `terrain.rs` · `tests/waystation.rs` (new, 6 tests) · `tests/haven.rs` · `tests/scatter.rs` · `occupy.rs` (fixture) · TERRAIN §1 stage 8 |

The pad, both waystations, all five pad crates, the shelter and all four waystation crates could each have taken different coordinates on wasm than on native with `test_terrain_golden` and `test_parity_wasm` both green. **That is a live exposure, not hygiene:** `client-core` resolves `terrain::haven` on the WASM build (`bridge.rs`, `core.rs`) and the server resolves it natively, so those two answers agreeing IS the client↔server contract for where the island's destinations are, and nothing was holding it. **Three additions, none of them worldgen.** `terrain.rs` is untouched — the pad, the waystations and the road all answer exactly what they answered before, and what moved is what the fingerprint can SEE. (1) `probe_sites`, a new `extern "C"` export hashing the authored geometry as VALUES plus the road ring, printed on its own line by `examples/probe.rs` and `ci/parity.mjs` so a divergence NAMES the sites instead of moving one opaque terrain digest. (3) `GOLDEN_TERRAIN_HASH` regenerated `0xD16BC36CBC4BD03C` → `0xEE0C0328604586AA` in the same commit. **Every number is derived.** `PROBE_WINDOW_CELLS = 16` is the block size the centre window already used, so a site window and it contribute the same 256 cells; the origin is clamped to the grid so a site near the island edge still contributes exactly 256 and the digest's byte count is not seed-dependent. `PROBE_ROAD_BEARINGS = 64` divides the 256-entry yaw LUT evenly. `PROBE_ROAD_RADII = 51` is the one that had to be measured: the first draft's 8 gave a 57 m radial step against a shoulder 10 m wide, so the sweep hit road on only **9–15 of 64 bearings** and the rest hashed a constant — coverage that looks exactly like coverage. 51 is an 8 m step, the first under `ROAD_SHOULDER_HALF_W * 2`, and reaches 64/64 on all three seeds for ~2 ms. **Coverage is asserted, not commented.** A hash cannot say what it covers — re-centre a window on empty sea and the golden regenerates perfectly clean over nothing — so `test_golden_covers_authored_sites` counts the authored occupants inside each window and the bearings the road sweep crosses, over exactly the cells `probe_window_origin`/`probe_road_point` hand the digest rather than a second copy of that arithmetic. | repair v0: what a mend costs, and the wall on what it may buy (`content/balance.toml` `repair_cost_pct`) | **A base could only ever be destroyed.** Both merge-gate judges ranked it first, twice, unmoved (`findings/pass-20260805-074623-01-judge.md` and `-02`): `combat::strike` reaches `damage_piece`, `upkeep_sweep` takes unpaid structures, and there was no repair verb anywhere in `crates/sim-core/src` — so every hit and every missed period was permanent, the correct play after any raid was **abandon**, and a hearth was a respawn point rather than a home. `build::repair` closes it, shaped as `upgrade`'s twin because the two verbs answer the same question about the same address: same order of checks, same refusals, same privilege rule (no *foreign* hearth claims the anchor — pieces carry no owner, and outside every claim anyone may mend anyone's wall, exactly the door `place` and `upgrade` already leave open), and the same hands-off treatment of the upkeep clock, because materials are not rent. What differs is the ending: an upgrade re-rows and carries damage across as a fraction, a repair leaves the row and buys the hp back. **`repair_cost_pct` = 100**, one number and it is the only one this slice needed: the price is the pro-rata share of the piece's own build cost for the hp being restored, rounded **up**, floored at 1 — 100 means you pay exactly what the damage destroyed. Proposed, not spoken. It is the conservative end of the range on purpose: the reference's hammer is cheaper, and cheaper is the direction only the operator may move it, because below 100 a defender begins to out-heal a raid on economics alone. Content validation refuses `0` (a free heal, and no raid could ever land) and anything over `100` (a rebuild with extra steps, and the verb is dead weight); a `0` reaching the sim means the table was never baked, and `repair` refuses there rather than healing free. Restoring to **full** rather than by an amount is also a wall, not a taste: no quantity crosses the wire, so nothing about *how much* can be forged, and there is no arithmetic between the store and the ceiling to get wrong — `pieces.set_hp(i, def.hp)` is the whole of it, gated by `a_repaired_piece_never_exceeds_its_baked_hp`. **Deployables are not repairable yet** and that is a stated cut, not an oversight: `Deploys` exposes no hp setter, and a deployable's cost is one item, which makes a fractional price of it degenerate. Open with this row. | `content/balance.toml` · `build.rs` · `validate.rs` |

Its volume is read off the mesh the client actually builds, `BoxGeometry(0.9, 0.55, 0.7)` — row 11 of `OCCUPANT_R_M` is 0.5701, the box's xz half-diagonal rounded out at the fourth decimal, `OCCUPANT_TOP_M` 0.55 — and `ci/occupant_volume.mjs` holds both to the vertex buffer. Nothing about which items or how many is pinned. **`ci/haven_prize.mjs` is restated in expected items PER SITE**, container count × what that container pays, against the shoulder each site stands on and therefore replaces (the exclusion zone vetoes scatter inside it, so the comparison is an opportunity cost): pad 165.3 items at 15.4× that baseline, a waystation 41.6 at 5.7×, and `tests/waystation.rs`'s aggregate rule restated in items — the whole lesser tier at 83.3 still under the one destination. | repair v0, the deployable half: what a door's mend costs (`DeployDef::costs`, joined from the recipe) — and repair wire **v21** | **The judge's ranked gap 3 and `NOW.md` §0a's stated cut, both naming the same two blockers: `Deploys` exposed no hp setter, and a deployable's cost is one crafted item, so a fractional price of it rounds to the whole thing.** The second is the only one that needed a decision. Placement charges one `door_wood`; pricing a repair that way makes mending a scratched door cost a whole door, which is not a price but a refusal with extra steps. So a deployable's repair is priced against **the recipe that made its item** — joined at bake time onto a new `DeployDef::costs`/`n_costs` (`MAX_DEPLOY_COSTS = MAX_RECIPE_INPUTS`, a structural cap like `MAX_PIECE_COSTS`, not a knob) and then divided by the *same* `repair_units` a piece uses at the *same* `repair_cost_pct`. **No new number is introduced** — a wood door is 200 wood by recipe, so a tenth of its 200 hp costs 20 wood, and the row above still owns the percent. `validate.rs` already enforces one recipe per output, so the join is 1:1; a deployable with no recipe bakes `n_costs = 0` and is refused as **unpriced** rather than mended free, which is an eleventh `REFUSE_B_*` (`DOMAINS` `min_members` 10 → 11, `live_max` 9 → 10, no width move). That refusal also closes the same hole on the piece path, where a zero-cost row would have fallen through both cost loops and healed for nothing. **The wire moved because the address is ambiguous and always was:** `place_deploy`'s `PLACE_DOORWAY` arm requires the doorway piece at the *identical* `(cx, cz, level, loc)`, so a door and its doorway share an address exactly, and `EV_STRUCT_HIT` has carried `STRUCT_DEPLOY_BIT` since damage first had to say which store it hit. `ACT_REPAIR` and `SUB_PIECE_REPAIRED` now carry the same leading bit, the event reusing `STRUCT_DEPLOY_BIT` in bit 24 of `b` — same bit, same position, same meaning — and on the wire it also *sizes* the row field (`DEPLOY_ROW_BITS` vs `PIECE_ROW_BITS`), exactly as `encode_event_struct_hit` does. `PROTO_VER` **20 → 21**, all 68 goldens rekeyed `v21_*` and regenerated, two fixtures added (70) so both values of the new bit are pinned. Reach and claim are taken at `anchor` for both stores, which puts a door and its doorway at one point as well as one address; that is half a cell from the cell centre `place_deploy` reaches an edge deployable at, and is the build lane's origin function winning inside a build-lane verb. **Also the judge's ranked fix 1, which was the pass's one failed check:** `build.rs` claimed setting `repair_pct` put the verb inside the parity/replay/alloc gates. It did not — it made it *possible*. `Command::Repair` now actually rides all three (`probe.rs`, `tests/replay.rs`, `tests/alloc_zero.rs`), both stores, and the comment says what the price does and does not buy. **Still not spoken.** | `content/bake.rs` · `sim-core/deploy.rs` · `sim-core/build.rs` · `protocol/src/{lib,event}.rs` |
| island boot check v0 — the short tier is a tripwire, not a live hole (systems lane, 2026-08-05) | `AUTHORED_SITES = WAYSTATIONS + 1 = 3`, derived rather than tunable; no new number. **Measured before building the refusal, and it is why the item's claim changed: seeds 0..20 000 scanned, `sites_complete` false on none, `sites_live == 3` on all 20 000** — as are all eight seeds this repo names (both tracked configs run 20260731; the suite seeds 0, 1, 42, 1024, 0xC0FFEE, 20260804, 0xDEADBEEF). So `pick_minor`'s short branch, which `terrain.rs` documents and `NOW.md` filed twice as a boot hazard, is **unreachable through `terrain::haven` on every seed measured**. `crates/server/src/boot.rs::check_seed` refuses it anyway from inside `spawn_shard` — before an identity loads or a port binds — because the branch becomes reachable the moment `WAYSTATION_MIN_SEP_M` (600 m, itself derived as `ROAD_R_MIN`), the ring radii, or the candidate search moves, which is exactly when nobody is looking at waystations. The refusal's own unit tests build a `Haven` with a dead `Waystation` by hand for the same reason: a gate that cannot exercise its failure path is decoration. The **counter** on the success path is the half with value today — `NOW.md` asked for "a counter, event or log line" and all three were missing even on a seed that fills. The alternative the request offered — relax `WAYSTATION_MIN_SEP_M` until the ring fills — was **not taken**: with no short seed to relax for, a new floor would be a number invented against no measurement (`CLAUDE.md` §loop discipline). | `crates/server/src/boot.rs` · `crates/server/tests/island_boot.rs` · `NOW.md` §0d |
| repair reach asymmetry, pinned not resolved (systems lane, 2026-08-05) | `build::repair` measures reach at `anchor(cx, cz, loc)`; `deploy::place_deploy` measures it at `cell_center(cx, cz)`. For a plane these are one identical point; for an edge (`LOC_EDGE_XLO`/`LOC_EDGE_ZLO`, spelled `_W`/`_N` when this row was written) they are `BUILD_CELL_M * 0.5` = **1.5 m apart**, out of a `BUILD_REACH_M` of 5 m. Documented and deliberate in `repair`'s doc comment, and until now **ungated** — a player who could place a door from a spot and not mend it from the same spot reddened nothing (`pass-20260805-074623-04-judge.md` ranked fix 2). `build::tests::repair_and_placement_measure_an_edge_from_points_half_a_cell_apart` now pins the **relation** rather than the behaviour: identical for a plane, exactly half a cell for an edge and no more, plus a witness position inside placement reach and outside repair reach. No knob and no default is claimed — whichever way a later pass resolves it (repair onto the centre, placement onto the anchor, or neither), the test goes red first and the new relation has to be written down. | `crates/sim-core/src/build.rs` · `deploy::cell_center` now `pub(crate)` |
