| waystation canopy v0 — where it stands (`WAYSTATION_CANOPY_OFF_M`), and how low its one wall is (`WAYSTATION_CANOPY_PARAPET_MAX_M`) | **The judge FAILED the first draft of this structure and named the mechanism (`findings/pass-20260805-074623-04-judge.md`, ranked fix 1): it stood at the site centre, and the centre of a waystation is the coast road's own centre line — every candidate `pick_minor` scores comes off the road ring. `tests/road.rs`, `tests/scatter.rs` and `tests/waystation.rs` all read the same two slots on the carriageway, at every seed probed.** `haven_shelter_bearing`'s doc had already written the rule down in one line — *"the pad's center IS the road's center line, so this is the condition that moved the structure off center at all"* — and the first draft argued past it. So the canopy now stands in a gap in the container pair, on a bearing `waystation_canopy_bearing` accepts, and **neither number is chosen**: the offset is `WAYSTATION_CRATE_R_M` itself (two containers on a 6.5 m ring leave two gaps of half the circle each, so the structure stands *on* that ring where the pad's five containers force its shelter *inside* theirs), and the parapet bar is `CAPSULE_HEIGHT_M * 2/3` = 1.133 m — the fraction this building kit already calls open, since the pad's doorway is 2.4 m of opening under a 3.6 m wall. The road test is the FOOTPRINT's, not the anchor's, and that is the one place this tier is stricter than the pad: the pad probes `road_band` at its shelter's centre and gets away with it because that structure sits well inside a 32 m pad, while this one stands 6.5 m off a centre that is the road, on a bearing the ring chose and the road did not — so a centre that reads `Off` can still be a 5.6 m eave lying across the carriageway at an angle. `road_band` measures the road's width radially from the island centre, so the two extremes across it are the anchor ± `WAYSTATION_CANOPY_R_M` along that radial: three taps, and they are the right three rather than a sample of a circle. Folded into `waystation_ring_phase` rather than run after it, because the gaps move with the rotation — 16 phases each offering 2 gaps, and a candidate that cannot house the whole arrangement is not a site (`reference/SPAWN.md` §5, refuse the position). **Costs:** worldgen moved, so `GOLDEN_TERRAIN_HASH` is regenerated in the same commit; some seeds now pick a different candidate off the ring. Open for the operator: whether a monument tier should carve its pad (TERRAIN.md §8) rather than find flat ground, which would make the road test a property of the carve instead of a search constraint. |
| scatter mix v0 — the scatter pass blends the four biome rows by the splat instead of picking one (`terrain::scatter_row`; no new constant) | `TERRAIN.md` §1 stage 9 shipped its clumping with one line open: "`biome()` is still a hard classifier, so a biome boundary is still a step in *composition* even though density now ramps across it". `clump` gates how MUCH stands somewhere; nothing gated WHAT. The ground material and the clutter population already ramp — both read `splat_from`'s four soft weights — so the props were the only one of the three populations still stepping, and a pine forest ended on the `moisture > 0.05` contour while the turf beneath it faded across `SPLAT_MOIST_BAND`. **No knob is invented here**: the blend reuses `splat_from`'s existing bands and the authored `ScatterTable` rows unchanged, and it is a blend rather than a mapping only because `splat_from`'s channels (sand · grass · forest-litter · rock) are `Biome`'s four identities (Beach · Meadow · Forest · Highland) in the same order. Measured: worst per-sample jump in the tree weight across a moisture sweep **4 per-mille against the classifier's 190** (the full Meadow→Forest span, which is what a classifier moves at one sample); **10.2–11.8% of land cells** sit in a band no single splat channel owns, which is the share of the island the change can reach; live slots moved by at most 35 of ~9,800 over the four gate seeds, because a convex blend redistributes what a partition assigned. Two properties hold by construction and are gated: **interior-preserving** (where the splat is one-hot the blended row equals the row that shipped, so the ~89% of land outside a band is bit-identical) and **convex** (every entry stays inside the range its four authored rows span, which is what keeps `test_no_biome_row_saturates` a valid bound on the blended row without knowing it exists). `GOLDEN_TERRAIN_HASH` moved in the same commit. **Open for the operator:** the blend includes `splat_from`'s cliff term, so steep-but-walkable ground (scatter vetoes above `CLIFF_SLOPE_RATIO`; the splat's cliff band reaches ~0.51 there) now draws toward the Highland row — scree and ore on slopes, matching the rock the ground is painted with. That is a composition change on slopes, not just at biome edges, and it was taken as the "one law, three populations" reading rather than as a second scatter-only variant of the splat. Say if the props on slopes should ignore the cliff mask instead. |
| jump verb v0 — the impulse, and why the wire version turned (systems lane, 2026-08-05) | `JUMP_SPEED = 7.0` m/s, applied in `movement::step` when a **grounded** body's frame carries `BTN_JUMP` (`input.rs`, bit 3). Apex is `JUMP_SPEED²/(2·GRAVITY)` = **1.22 m**, and it is derived from the two constants it sits between rather than chosen for feel: strictly above `STEP_UP` (0.6 m, which the capsule already climbs for free — under it the verb is an animation) and well under `build::LEVEL_H_M` (3.0 m — a hoppable wall would retire the whole build/raid loop that `repair`, `decay_of` and upkeep exist to serve). Both bounds are asserted against those constants in `tests/jump.rs`, so retuning the impulse past either is a red gate, not a playtest discovery. `grounded` is the only gate on the launch and it is false for the whole arc, so no double-jump; landing re-arms it, so a held button is a hop per touchdown — which buys no speed, because this capsule has no air-control penalty and horizontal speed is identical airborne and grounded. **`PROTO_VER` 21 ⇒ 22, all 70 goldens regenerated in the same commit, exactly one fixture's bytes changed (the hello, which carries the version).** No payload bit moved — `buttons` has been a full unmasked octet since v0 — but this is v18's "widened meaning is a wire change" case in its purest form, and sharper: `movement::step` is shared verbatim by the server and `client-core`'s predictor, so a v22 client against a v21 server would predict an arc the server never runs and be hard-snapped on every press. **Client half is NOT landed** — no key sets the bit, so jump is unreachable in play until the ui lane wires it; the server half is gated but unplayed. | sim-core `movement.rs` `input.rs` `bots.rs` · protocol `lib.rs` · `tests/jump.rs` |
| satchel fuse v0 — the raid verb's three numbers (`content/weapons.toml` `fuse_s`; `limits.rs` `MAX_LIVE_CHARGES`) | **Both merge-gate judges ranked it first and neither report was about the same pass** (`findings/pass-20260805-113001-02-judge.md` gap 1, `-03-judge.md` gap 1): the satchel was priced (`weapons.toml:70`), craftable (`recipes.toml:332`), and is what `balance.toml`'s anchor 1 divides wall hp by — and **`bake_combat` dropped every non-melee row**, so the number the whole economy is balanced around reached the sim as a zero. `build::repair`, `deploy::decay_of`, upkeep, four upgrade tiers and door locks all defended a threat no player could mount. `sim-core/charge.rs` closes it. **One new content number: `fuse_s = 10`** on the throwable row — 300 ticks at 30 Hz, proposed, not spoken. The fuse *is* the mechanic: it is what makes a raid a commitment rather than a click, and the defender gets exactly the seconds the raider does. 10 s is picked as the conservative end — shorter is the direction only the operator may move it, because a fuse under the time it takes to cross a base makes the raid unanswerable, and `validate.rs` refuses `0` (a charge that blows on placement is a different verb) and the bake refuses anything over a `u16` of ticks (the wire carries the fuse at that width, so the bake refuses rather than letting the encoder be the first thing that notices). **`MAX_LIVE_CHARGES = 64`**, sized off what a raid is rather than off a player count: a stone wall at 1750 hp takes four 500-structure charges, so 64 is sixteen simultaneous walls. Overflow **refuses** (`REFUSE_B_FULL`) and the charge stays in the pocket — evicting someone else's live charge would let one player disarm another's raid by planting into a full store. **No new refusal code and no new knob beyond those two**: a charge refuses for the five reasons `repair` already refuses for, so nothing crosses to `web/src/refusals.js` and no `REFUSE_B_BITS`/`DOMAINS` row moves. **What v0 deliberately does not do**, each its own verb and its own knob: no blast radius (a charge damages the one address it was planted on, so the anchor's arithmetic stays exactly `piece hp ÷ structure`), no damage to players, no dud chance, no defusing. **The one rule this verb breaks on purpose is `repair`'s claim check** — every other address-taking verb refuses on `deploys.foreign_claim`, and raiding a stranger's wall is the entire point, so its absence is commented at the call site against a later pass "fixing" it back. **Also closed: a live `canon.rs` hole, prospectively.** `fuse_s` reaches the sim through `ThrowDef` and walks the canonical hash, with `hash_moves_with_values` asserting it — the third field of that shape, after the `secondary` payout and the `[backpack]` ladder that `canon.rs:70` already records as paid for. | `content/weapons.toml` · `sim-core/charge.rs` · `limits.rs` · `content/{schema,validate,canon,bake}.rs` |
| raid wire v23 — the sixteenth action, and the lane is now full (systems lane, 2026-08-05) | `ACT_THROW` = 15 (address + the leading store bit — `encode_action_repair`'s layout to the bit, and deliberately not one shared encoder with a mode flag, which would put the two verbs one transposed argument apart at every call site), `EV_CHARGE_PLACED` = 29, `SUB_CHARGE_PLACED` = 40. `PROTO_VER` **22 → 23**, all 70 goldens rekeyed `v23_*` and regenerated, four fixtures added (74) — two per direction, so both values of the store bit are pinned in each, and the throw fixtures deliberately carry **different addresses from the repair fixtures** because the two encoders write identical field sequences and differ only in the subtype, so a shared address would let a decoder arm wired to the wrong verb still match. **The spare-code ledger now reads zero** (`the_action_lane_has_the_room_it_claims`, asserted, not commented): `ACT_REPAIR`'s own doc predicted a throw would spend the last code, and it has. A seventeenth C→S action widens `ACTION_SUB_BITS` 4 → 5, moves every action message by one bit, and regenerates every golden — the v12 turn again. That is the price of the next verb and the test is what makes its proposer read it first. **Why the version turns even though no existing message moved a bit:** a v22 client handed a `SUB_CHARGE_PLACED` decodes it as an unknown subtype, calls the whole event message malformed, and drops **every event that shared the datagram with it** — walls losing hp with nothing drawn to say why. The handshake refusal is what converts that into a clean wrong-version. `ci/client_smoke.mjs`'s literal `22` → `23` in the same commit, which is wall 6's "same commit" made mechanical on the client's side. **The event is broadcast and that is the point of it**, not the cheap choice: a burning fuse is the one fact in this game the *defender* needs more urgently than the actor, and `c` carries the fuse **remaining** rather than the tick it fires on because a client joining mid-fuse has no shared tick origin to subtract from. `APPLIED2_CHARGE` is the first flag to land in `client-core`'s second applied word — word 0 is full at bit 30, bit 31 being `client_on_stream`'s error sentinel. **Client half is NOT landed**: `client_action_throw` and the three readouts are exported and the event reaches the mirror, but no key plants a charge and nothing draws a countdown, so the raid is gated and unplayed until the ui lane wires it. | `protocol/src/{lib,event}.rs` · `server/src/core.rs` · `client-core/src/{bridge,core}.rs` · `ci/client_smoke.mjs` |
| clutter richness v0 — the second stratum (`CLUTTER_RICH_PER_TILE = 96`, `RICH_ACCEPT_MAX = 32`) | **Total coverage is also UNIFORM coverage, and that is the defect the coverage stratum cannot fix.** One element per land cell means sand carries exactly what a meadow does. `findings/pass-20260804-173640-01-visual.md` gap 1 asked for the other half in its own words — populate the near ground "so **density** follows the biome" — and `reference/SPAWN.md` §9.3 names it from the other side: the reference's scatter CLUSTERS, ours is per-cell independent, which is why ours reads as white noise. **What ships**: `terrain::clutter_rich_cell`, a SECOND element a cell may earn, at a rate the ground sets — the two growing splat channels (grass + forest litter) scaled by `clump`, the same grove/clearing field stage 9 scales its whole scatter row by. One cause drives both layers, so the ground thickens where the trees thicken and there is no third law to drift from. Measured: growing ground **0.088** extra elements per cell against bare ground's **0.005** (18×), index of dispersion **1.40** at 3.2 m rising to **8.51** at 12.8 m — an independent coin holds 1.0 at every scale, and the RISE is the part a constant rate cannot fake. **96 IS NOT A DESIGN NUMBER — it is what the frame budget left**, and the first draft asked for 256 and `ci/clutter_shape.mjs` §4 refused it: the 20%-of-1.5 M triangle share, the tuft at 12 tris and a 5×5 tile ring cap the whole-ring pool at 25 000, which is 1 000 a tile, minus 256 skirt and 625 coverage = 119 the ceiling. So the near ground is frame-budget-bound, not design-bound; raising it needs a cheaper tuft, a smaller ring or a bigger share, all three above a builder. **`RICH_ACCEPT_MAX = 32`, i.e. 32 in 256, is what keeps the budget a BACKSTOP**: at the first draft's 71% acceptance on wooded ground, `clutter_fill`'s row-major scan spent the 96 in the tile's first rows and left every row after them bare — a horizontal band edge every 16 m, a worse artifact than the uniformity being fixed. Truncation is the wrong instrument for a rate. **Proposed defaults, unspoken — the operator owns both numbers.** | sim-core `terrain.rs` (`clutter_rich_cell`, `clutter_richness_at`, `clutter_fill`) · `tests/clutter.rs` (3 new gates + the wall's stance) · `web/src/clutter.js` · `ci/clutter_shape.mjs` · TERRAIN §1 stage 10 |
| bay slots v0 — `BAY_SPAN_YAW = 2048`, `ROAD_BAY_BARREL_PERMILLE = 430`, `ROAD_OPEN_BARREL_PERMILLE = 170` | **The loop paid the same per-mille the whole way round, so no stretch of it was worth preferring** — TERRAIN §1 stage 7 asked for "junk piles at bay mouths, slightly denser slots **(knob)**" and it was the last placement line of stage 7 still open. `terrain::in_bay` reuses stage 7's own trick rather than inventing a second representation: never locate the coastline, only test against it. A sample's own shoreline sits at `d + ROAD_INLAND_M` along its outward radial (that IS the road's definition), so probe `height` at that radius on the two bearings `BAY_SPAN_YAW` either side — both land means the neighbours' shorelines are farther out, i.e. the coast curves around water here. Two taps, no march, no bisect, no memo, and paid only on `RoadBand::Shoulder`. `BAY_SPAN_YAW = 2048` is 1/32 turn ≈ 188 m of arc at the ring's ~960 m radius, about a quarter of `COAST_FREQ`'s 900 m lobe, so the probes sit on the headlands flanking a bay rather than inside it. **It is a redistribution, not a raise, and that is the load-bearing choice**: `tests/haven.rs`'s `HAVEN_PRIZE_RATIO_MIN` and `ci/haven_prize.mjs` both price the pad against the shoulder it replaces, and that floor's own doc says it sits high enough that doubling the shoulder rate trips it — so inflating the route to make bays interesting would have been paid for out of the destination's lead. The two rates are set against the measured sheltered share so the island-wide mean lands back on `ROAD_BARREL_PERMILLE = 250`: **measured with both rates pinned flat, the shoulder grew 239 barrels over 2,033 cells (117.6 per-mille); under the split it grows 236 (116.1)**, a 1.3% move, and the nominal weighted mean comes to 253 against 250. Measured over the four gate seeds: **25–39% of the ring sheltered in 2–5 contiguous arcs**, bays carrying **2.46–2.76×** the open coast. `tests/road.rs` gates coherence separately from density on purpose — a classifier answering in speckle would still pass a density ratio while being unlearnable, and per-cell parity substituted for `in_bay` gives 14–17 arcs against the cap of 8 (the first draft's `n/4 = 16` cap caught only one of four mutant seeds and was tightened). **Open for the operator:** whether "slightly denser" means the 2.53× nominal contrast shipped here — it is the one number in this row with no measurement behind it, only the read that a bay a player cannot tell apart from open shore is not a place. | `TERRAIN.md` §1 stage 7 |
| satchel blast v0 — `blast_m = 3` · linear falloff · `damage = 475` reaches bodies (systems lane; landed 2026-08-11) | **PROPOSED and now APPLIED — the follow-up slice this row used to defer to.** A detonation is an area: everything structural inside `blast_cm` of the planted anchor takes `structure` on a **linear falloff** (integer arithmetic over centimetres), and every *body* inside it takes `damage` the same way — **the planter included**, because standing at your own bomb is the oldest lesson the reference's raiders learn. The planted wall is the target at distance zero, so it still takes the full 125 and anchor 1's `piece hp ÷ structure` is undisturbed — `tests/blast.rs` pins exactly that. Two consequences of "area, not address" are deliberate: a charge whose wall broke early **still detonates** (a bomb is not defused by its excuse disappearing — the old spent-on-nothing arm is gone), and a kill is **`DEATH_BY_CHARGE`** (sixth cause, same v36 widening, same merge window) with the planter named — "you blew yourself up" is its own sentence. **The scan is bounded and proves its own bound**: a 3×3 column ring plus one deploy-store pass plus one player pass, complete because `blast_m` is capped at one build cell — `limits::BLAST_MAX_CM`, enforced by `validate` at boot, by the save decoder against forged files, and restated in a `charge.rs` const block so widening the radius and widening the ring are one commit. Targets are collected as addresses and re-resolved one at a time before damage, because `damage_piece` swap-removes and a held index would point at a stranger's wall. The blast's two numbers ride `ChargeRec` copy-at-plant (the store's standing rule), which moved `WORLD_SAVE_FORMAT` to 4 — **and the same bump deletes a latent refusal**: format 3's decoder compared `structure` (a damage amount) against the content table's row *count*, so any real save holding a live satchel would have refused to load; found by reading, gated by `a_fuse_survives_a_save_and_still_blasts`. At 475/3 m the lethal radius on a 100 hp body is ~2.37 m and a 2.9 m stand-off is a scratch — both pinned in `tests/blast.rs`. **Open for the operator:** the 3 m, the falloff's shape (linear is the simplest defensible curve, not a measurement), and dud/defuse, which remain unbuilt verbs. | `content/weapons.toml` · sim-core `charge.rs` `world.rs` `worldsave.rs` `limits.rs` · `content/validate.rs` · `tests/blast.rs` · client `ui/death.rs` |
| shard list url — where `scry-shardlist-v1` is served from (client `--servers`; scry `data/launcher/gates.manifest.json` `servers.url`) | **No default, and that is the decision.** Nothing is baked into the client and `servers.url` stays `null` on scry's side, so with neither set the menu offers the Direct row and says *"no shard list to fetch — pass --servers URL, or start the game from the scry launcher's Servers window"*, and the launcher's Servers window stays dark. Both are the honest state: **the document is not published yet**, and a url pointing at a 404 turns a dark panel that names what would light it into an error row that blames the network. `ci/shardlist.py` proposes the depot origin, beside the build it launches, so one publish step covers both — ⚠ **and the url it proposed until 2026-08-11 (`/depot/gates/servers.json`) was never served by anything**; scry answered with a route rather than an nginx alias, so it is `/api/launcher/servers/gates` now and the scp destination is unchanged — but proposing is all it does; it prints the `scp` and the manifest line and performs neither, the same refusal `ci/depot.py` makes about publishing a build. **What the operator is actually being asked for is two things, and they are separable**: the url, and whether the *first* published list should carry the live public shard at all — `game.moreright.xyz:61234` is a real server and listing it is the act that makes it findable by anyone who installs the launcher. **What is deliberately absent from every row**: `players` and `ping_ms`. The shard serves no status endpoint, so there is no number to read, and both readers draw `?` for an absent count — writing a `0` would make a busy shard read as empty. `stats.rs` already holds `joins`/`leaves` as atomics and its own header names "the status page" as the thing that would read them; that endpoint is the whole of what would light the counts, and it is not this slice. | `ci/shardlist.py` · `shards.toml.example` · `crates/client/src/shardlist.rs` |
| connect timeout v0 — `menu::CONNECT_TIMEOUT_S = 20` (client lane, 2026-08-06) | **PROPOSED, and it exists because of a measurement rather than a guess.** Clicking a shard that is not running left the intro screen on *"connecting to 127.0.0.1:4433…"* indefinitely — measured under Xvfb, still connecting 18 s after the click, with no way back and no error. **The cause is the transport, not the client**: QUIC over UDP gets no refusal from a closed port (there is no RST to deliver), so the attempt waits on quinn's own handshake timeout while the screen says nothing. The old code path could not have this bug because it was `exit(1)` before the window existed — the menu is what makes an unanswered connect a *state* somebody has to be able to leave. **Two exits, and only one of them is a number**: Esc cancels immediately and needs no knob, and that is the half worth trusting; the timeout is the backstop for a player who does not know Esc works. 20 s is the conservative end — comfortably past a slow handshake across an ocean, short enough that a dead shard reads as dead rather than as a hung client. **What it deliberately does not do is abort the attempt.** Dropping the receiver is the whole of it: nothing reads that channel outside `Screen::Connecting`, and the spawned task ends on its own send failure. Aborting a `JoinHandle` mid-handshake to save a socket for twenty seconds is a race for no gain. **Open for the operator:** the number, and whether a failed connect should offer a retry row rather than only a status line. Note this is a *product* timeout and not a gate — `CLAUDE.md`'s "a gate that waits on a clock is not a gate on this box" is about assertions, and nothing asserts on this value. | `crates/client/src/render/menu.rs` · `NOW.md` §0v |
| menu skin v0 (the native in-game menus' geometry and palette) | The first in-game menus the native client has: `Tab` opens inventory + crafting, a container panel appears beside it when the sim says one is open, and `B` holds the build wheel. Two kinds of number, and only the first is arguable. **Palette** — type and rules are `render::ui`'s, the menu chrome's, so the Esc menu and an inventory read as one product; the panels declare only what a thing drawn OVER a lit world needs and a full-screen menu does not: panel `0.082,0.082,0.090` at 0.97 alpha, scrim 0.72, cell / cell-full / cell-hover, one hot line `0.98,0.86,0.55`, a short-price red `0.86,0.36,0.30`. **Geometry is a budget, not a taste**: cell 44 px, browser and detail pane 276 px tall, recipe grid 218 px of that and it SCROLLS, 8 browser columns, 26 px per wheel line. Those were solved against **720p**, which is Bevy's default window and the smallest thing this has to fit: the first cut at 54 px measured ~830 px of column against 720, and a centred overflow clips at *both* ends — the title went off the top and two inventory rows off the bottom, and nothing in the code says so. The grid scrolls rather than being sized to today's 36 recipes, because a browser sized to the content is a browser that hides the 37th. **Open for the operator:** whether the panel should scale with the window rather than being fixed, which is the shape that stops this being a budget at all. | `crates/client/src/render/panels/mod.rs` |
| build wheel v0 (radial rings, and what a selection may do) | The wheel is **two rings**, not one: outer picks the shape (6), inner picks the material (3), centre is a dead readout — because `content/building.toml` is 6 × 3 and flattening it onto one ring is eighteen segments a thumb cannot hit. Radii 96 / 152 / 214 px. Segment 0 of each ring is centred on straight up and indices grow clockwise; the labels are placed from the same function the pointer is resolved by, so they cannot disagree. **The selection is latched on hover and committed by letting go**, which is the reference's feel and puts the price on screen before the commitment. **What it deliberately does not do is place anything.** There is no build ghost natively — nothing draws the cell being aimed at or colours it by whether the sim would accept it — and placing a piece whose destination the player cannot see spends materials on a guess. The latch is drawn on the HUD so the wheel's effect is visible rather than living in a field nothing reads. **Open for the operator:** the radii, and whether releasing over the dead centre should cancel rather than keep. | `crates/client/src/ui/build.rs` · `NOW.md` |
| settings v0 — `FOV_MIN_DEG = 60`, `FOV_MAX_DEG = 110`, `FOV_STEP_DEG = 5`, `SENS_MIN = 0.25`, `SENS_MAX = 3`, `SENS_STEP = 0.05` (client lane, 2026-08-06) | **PROPOSED — six bounds, and deliberately not six new defaults.** The screen's two numeric settings both START at a value that already ships: field of view at `rig::FOV_DEG = 75`, sensitivity at 1.0, which is the identity against `input::MOUSE_RAD_PER_PX`. So nothing here changes what the client draws on a fresh launch — these are the ends of the two ranges a player may walk to, and the size of one click. **The bounds are the whole knob.** 60-110 vertical degrees is the band either side of the shipped 75 that stays a first-person view rather than a fisheye or a telescope, and 5 is a step coarse enough that ten clicks crosses it. Sensitivity is a MULTIPLIER, 0.25x-3x, on the radians-per-pixel that is already registered as a client cosmetic — it scales the free-running view angle **before** `yaw_u16`/`pitch_u8` quantize it, so both sides still quantize the same way and the quantize-both-sides law is untouched. Rounded to the step on every change rather than only clamped, because 40 float adds of 0.05 do not sum to 2.0 and the value is printed to two decimals. **Three settings are not numbers and so are not here**: vsync (`AutoVsync`/`AutoNoVsync`), fullscreen (borderless), and invert-look, all booleans defaulting to the client's current behaviour. **Persistence landed (client lane, 2026-08-09), answering the row's second open question as a proposal.** Format: hand-rolled `key = value` TOML in `crate::config` — the `shard.toml` precedent (`server/src/config.rs`: "three keys don't earn a serde dependency", and the client links no TOML parser), with the OPPOSITE unknown-key policy for the opposite reason: `shard.toml` refuses an unknown key because an operator's typo silently running defaults is the failure there, while this file is game-written, so an unknown key is another build's knob and is **ignored-and-preserved** verbatim — an older build cannot strip what a newer one saved, which is what loses least and what makes reading a newer file safe. Path: the platform config dir resolved from the environment by hand (`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` or `~/.config`, `%APPDATA%`, `~/Library/Application Support` — each `/gates/settings.toml`; no resolvable base means persistence is off for the run, said in the footer; never a file beside the binary, which the launcher replaces wholesale). Version: `SETTINGS_VERSION = 1` stamped in every file, bumped ONLY when a key is renamed or changes meaning — adds and removals need no bump because missing keys default and unknown keys ride — and the writer stamps `max(file, ours)` so the stamp never walks backwards over keys it cannot read. Loaded once before the first frame and sanitized against these same bounds (a hand-edited value cannot reach what a hundred clicks cannot); saved on change rather than on exit, so a crash costs nothing; a missing or corrupt file is the defaults, silently; a `--capture` run neither loads nor saves, so the visual gate's frames cannot depend on the box's config. **Open for the operator:** the bounds, still. | `crates/client/src/render/settings.rs` · `crates/client/src/config.rs` · `MENUS.md` §3 |
| audio v0 — the whole cue table, four bounds, and the spatial scale (client lane, 2026-08-06) | **PROPOSED — every number in `crate::sound`, and NONE of them was measured against anything.** They are opening values in `CONTENT.md`'s sense: each ships as a default until somebody with ears moves it. Four groups. **(1) The bounds, which are the load-bearing ones**: `VOICE_CAP = 24` concurrent voices (overflow policy: refuse the new voice, never steal a sounding one — a stolen voice is an audible cut), `STARTS_PER_FRAME = 4` (the reference's `audio.framebudget 0.3` in the only unit a pure module has — work items, not milliseconds; **a millisecond probe is still owed**), `CUE_QUEUE_CAP = 32` (drop-newest and count, because a cue's value is that it happened, unlike a datagram's which is freshness), `MAX_AUDIBLE_M = 96`. **(2) The cue table** — 38 rows of bus/radius/gain/cooldown/priority/positional in `sound/mod.rs`, plus the falloff law `(1 − d/R)²`, chosen quadratic rather than inverse-square because it is *exactly zero at the radius* and a law that is merely small there clicks when a source crosses it. **(3) Footsteps**: `STRIDE_M = 0.85` (cadence is distance covered, not a timer, so sprint cadence is free), `STEP_MIN_SPEED = 0.4 m/s`, `STEP_FULL_SPEED = 5.5 m/s`, `STEP_MIN_GAIN = 0.45`. **(4) The Bevy boundary**: `SPATIAL_SCALE = 1/128` and `EAR_GAP_M = 0.22`. The scale is **not** a taste knob — rodio attenuates spatial audio by `min(1/d², 1)` in world units, which makes anything past ~5 m inaudible in a metre-scale world, and 1/128 puts every audible emitter inside the clamp so rodio only *pans* and `sound::falloff` is the client's one distance law. `tests/sound.rs` asserts the relation. Bevy's own ear-gap default is 4.0 m, a head the size of a car. **What is deliberately absent and is the reference's own ordering, not a shortcut**: no occlusion (it gated its first pass behind a convar, off, with a decade of audio behind it), no ducking, and no EQ carve — `reference/AUDIO.md` §4's 2–5 kHz rule is a stated design rule we have not paid. **"No music" came off that list on 2026-08-11** (see "music v0" below), which also put the first reverb in the tree — in the one place §8 says a tail is load-bearing rather than decorative, and nowhere else. **Open for the operator:** all of it, and first the question `ART.md` cannot answer today — there is no audio section in the art bible at all, so the bank has a gate but no bar. | `crates/client/src/sound/` · `reference/AUDIO.md` §9 |
| water v0 — the sea's mesh, its optics, its swell and its foam (client lane, 2026-08-08) | **PROPOSED — the whole of `render/water.rs`, and the two halves have very different standing.** *Derived, not chosen*: the wave speeds are `ω = sqrt(g·k)`, so a 52 m swell runs at its own physical period and the long waves outrun the short ones; `WATER_REFLECTANCE = 0.3535534` is `F0 = 2%` from water's IOR of 1.333 and **corrects a real error** — the plane this replaced shipped 0.55, i.e. `F0 = 4.8%`, water lit as a polished dielectric; `band_weight` retires a wave at its own Nyquist rather than at a distance; and `AlphaMode::Premultiplied` is what keeps the sky's Fresnel term alive in shallow water, which a first capture showed straight blending killing. *Chosen and open*: the wave set (four waves, 52/31/17/9 m at 0.42/0.26/0.13/0.06 m amplitude — a mild sea, peak slope 0.25), `EXTINCT = [0.62, 0.16, 0.22]` per metre and `SCATTER_ALBEDO = [0.018, 0.125, 0.100]` (coastal/green rather than open-ocean/blue, which is the reference's own stated retune to "a more Atlantic/Baltic sea colour"), `ALPHA_MAX = 0.94`, `SHOAL_FULL_M = 3.5`, the wash band (`FOAM_DEPTH_M = 2.6` outer, `FOAM_PEAK_M = 0.60` — **the wash stands OFF the waterline**, because a band peaking at zero depth puts the brightest thing on the sea along the exact polygon edge and *outlines* the seam it should hide; `FOAM_JITTER_M = 0.55` and `FOAM_NOISE_M = 9` displace its contour so both edges are lobes rather than iso-depth curves of a smooth heightfield; `FOAM_SURGE_FLOOR = 0.45` is how far it draws back between waves, and a moving edge cannot be a hard edge; `FOAM_SLOPE_FULL = 0.30`, `FOAM_SLOPE_FLOOR = 0.35`, `CREST_FOAM_MAX = 0.30`), the ripple map (`RIPPLE_TILE_M = 12`, 256² with mips, drift 0.028/0.011 tiles/s), and the mesh's own shape (`STEP_M = 2`, `CORE_M = 64`, `SKIRT_RATIO = 1.7`, `SEA_REACH_M = 2600`, `SNAP_M = 8`, `DEPTH_R_M = 220`) — 7,921 vertices, which is the per-frame budget. Plus the land side, which is **two bounds because neither survives alone**: `WET_BAND_M = 2.5` metres of rise and `WET_REACH_M = 7` metres of horizontal run, whichever ends the band first — height alone makes a 4% beach damp for sixty metres and run alone makes a cliff damp for fourteen metres of vertical rock, and the gate walks four bank steepnesses to check neither collapses to a line. Then `WET_VALUE = 0.55`, `WET_SATURATION = 0.35`, floored at `ALBEDO_LUMA_FLOOR = 0.05` because forest litter soaked would otherwise leave `ART.md`'s band. **Open for the operator:** the sea state — one wave set, no weather, so it is always this calm. | `crates/client/src/render/water.rs` · `crates/client/tests/water.rs` · `reference/WATER.md` §9 |
| water audio v0 — snapshots, the surf bed, the submerged duck (client lane, 2026-08-08) | **PROPOSED, and it lands the thing `reference/AUDIO.md` §9.1 named as owed**: the reference built groups, then *snapshots* (a whole mixer state), then *fades between them*, and only then occlusion — we had the groups and nothing else. Two snapshots, `Above` and `Submerged`, crossfaded over `SNAPSHOT_FADE_S = 0.30` (fast, because the cause is instantaneous; not zero, because five gains stepping at once is a click in five voices). Submerged ducks the game bus to **0.45** — not to zero, because being underwater must not be a stealth advantage handed out by the mixer — kills the wind bed, holds the surf at 0.22, and brings up a bed generated dark. **What it cannot do, stated:** real submerged audio is a steep low-pass and rodio gives us gain, rate and panning only; ducking plus a dark bed is a stand-in, the same kind as pitch jitter standing in for a recorded variation bank. The surf level is `SURF_R_M = 96` with `SURF_FLOOR = 0.18`, off a fixed 24-point `terrain::height` probe (three rings weighted by the client's own falloff law, so the level cannot flicker as a search finds different water). Three new cues: `Splash` (gain from vertical speed, `SPLASH_FULL_SPEED = 4` m/s, `SPLASH_MIN_GAIN = 0.35`, 220 ms cooldown because a body bobbing on the waterline crosses it every frame), `BedSurf` and `BedUnder`. **Open for the operator:** the same thing audio v0 is open on — nobody has heard any of it, and `ART.md` still has no audio section. | `crates/client/src/sound/` · `reference/WATER.md` §7 |
| music v0 — the gap, the theme, the tiers and the bumps (client lane, 2026-08-11) | **PROPOSED, and it is `reference/AUDIO.md` §8 built rather than invented** — `NOW.md` §0x item 2 called it the highest-value unbuilt thing and said the blocker was content, not engineering; the engineering is now in `sound/music.rs` and the content is still generated. Four groups. **(1) Theirs, taken verbatim** (`reference/BALANCE.md` §6 — a case is owed for differing, not for taking): `SONG_GAP_MIN_S = 240` and `SONG_GAP_MAX_S = 480`, their `music.songgapmin`/`songgapmax`, so music is minutes of silence punctuated by a song and never a soundtrack; and `MUSIC_DEFAULT = 0.2`, their `audio.musicvolume`, the one bus that does not open at full. **(2) The structure**, which is their design at our size: a theme of `SECTIONS = 3` sections × `TIERS = 3` intensity clips, a song of `SONG_SECTIONS_MIN = 4` to `SONG_SECTIONS_MAX = 7` sections, each `SECTION_S = 8` seconds of body plus `TAIL_S = 2.5` of reverb ring-out — the tail is the *mechanism*, not polish: it is what lets the director cut from any piece to any other without stopping playback and without fading. **(3) The intensity**, whose ORDER is published and whose magnitudes are not: `BUMP_SWING = 0.08` < `BUMP_HIT = 0.22` < `BUMP_HURT = 0.55` (a weapon in play < a bullet past your head < taking damage), read into a tier at `TENSE_AT = 0.30` / `COMBAT_AT = 0.70` **only at a section boundary** so the music never lurches, and falling by `SECTION_DECAY = 0.34` at the end of any section in which nothing happened. **(4) Ours, and each is a departure with a reason**: `FIRST_GAP_S = 30` because a player who joins for three minutes at their gap hears no music at all — including the operator, on the pass that decides whether it is any good; the menu mode, which is continuous (their `audio.menumusicvolume` is a separate convar, so a menu with its own behaviour is theirs — that it never pauses is ours); `MUSIC_FADE_S = 1.2` for the ONE case where a piece is faded rather than joined (leaving a world starts the menu's music on top of it); and the tier gains `M_CALM`/`M_TENSE`/`M_COMBAT` at 0.55/0.78/1.0, which exist because `synth::PEAK` normalizes every cue to one peak and would otherwise flatten the tiers into inaudibility. **(5) The generator** (`synth::score`): `ROOT_HZ = 110`, `BPM = 90` so a section is exactly twelve beats, a i–♭VI–♭VII turn chosen because it never resolves and so can be cut at any boundary, and a Schroeder reverb sized for the tail. **Open for the operator:** all of it, and the same question audio v0 already asks — `ART.md` has no audio section, so the score has gates and no bar. Nine generated pieces are placeholder by construction and `synth`'s licence posture is why they are generated rather than sourced. | `crates/client/src/sound/music.rs` · `synth::score` · `reference/AUDIO.md` §8 |
| ambience layers v0 — the bird over the bed (client lane, 2026-08-11) | **PROPOSED, and it is the half of `reference/AUDIO.md` §3 we did not have.** Their ambience is a bed *and* layers — "the timing of layers like birds and crickets is tuned separately from the beds themselves" — and ours was a bed alone. A layer is not a bed turned down: it is sparse, it is somewhere rather than everywhere, and its cadence is its own. `CALL_PERIOD_S = 11` seconds between calls in full cover, stretched by `OPEN_STRETCH = 12` at zero cover (a stretch, not a gate, for `SURF_FLOOR`'s reason: a level that reached "never" would switch the layer off as a player walked a treeline), jittered by `CALL_JITTER = 0.6` off a hash of the cycle number so it is not a metronome and needs no OS randomness, emitted `PERCH_H_M = 5` above a drawn prop the bed system already walked. **Deliberately NOT keyed to time of day**, which is what would buy crickets: there is no day/night cycle in the client, so dusk would be a cause we invented. **Not** the reference's localized-emitter *system* either — §9.3 says emitters are a later slice that arrives with a cull budget, and both of their published bugs in it (the cull ran too often; the emitter set leaked) are properties of having many. This is one voice with one clock and nothing to leak. **Open for the operator:** the period and the stretch, neither measured. | `crates/client/src/sound/birds.rs` · `reference/AUDIO.md` §3 |
| the world waits for the server to say where (`PLACE_FRAMES = 300`, client lane, 2026-08-06) | **The client was building a world the server had not named.** The welcome carries `player_id`, `seed` and `tick` — and no position; where the player stands arrives one snapshot later, when the first packet carrying our own entity lets `Predictor::adopt` take the authoritative spawn. Between those two the native client entered `Screen::Loading` and ran the whole `Stream` set anyway, and every streamer in it reads `Eye::pos`. An unplaced predictor reports `Body::default()`, whose position is the **world origin** — a real place on this island, not a sentinel — so nothing faulted and nothing logged: the rings built a good neighbourhood of somewhere nobody asked for. **Two confidences, stated apart.** Unconditionally, the first frames of every connect built origin chunks and threw them away when the eye jumped — small, since the rings fill one cell a frame and the first snapshot is normally a frame or three behind the welcome. The severe outcome is a **race**: it needs that snapshot to be later than the whole ring build (~25 frames), and then `Progress::done()` goes true at the origin and `InWorld` opens around a player the server has not placed. Packet loss on the first snapshot, a server hitch or a distant shard is all it takes — and a defect whose severity rides on an RTT is the worst shape for one to have, which is why the fix is a precondition and not a tuned wait. **The fix is a gate in front of the mean, not a term in it**: `Eye::placed` mirrors `Predictor::started`, `render::world_placed` stands the `Stream` set down until it is true, and `ui::load::Progress` reads 0.0 and is never `done()` while it is false — so the loading screen covers *both* waits and says which one it is in ("WAITING FOR THE SERVER TO PLACE YOU") rather than printing three honest-looking zeroes for work no ring has been asked for. `place_eye` itself is deliberately **not** gated: pumping is how the placing snapshot arrives, so a condition on it would deadlock. **The one invented number is `PLACE_FRAMES`**, the capture harness's bound on that wait — a frame count and not a timeout, for the reason every budget in `capture.rs` is one (the gate box is a CPU rasterizer, so elapsed milliseconds measure the box). Placement is one round trip after a completed connect; 300 frames is loose enough that only "never" reaches it, and reaching it exits nonzero with the reason rather than hanging, because a hung gate reports nothing at all. Gated in the **code** tier: `crates/client/tests/ui.rs` §E, three tests, no GPU and no `--features render`. **Measured end to end** on seed 20260731 under Xvfb + lavapipe: the shard placed us at `1001.6, 1.2, 1935.3` — **2,179 m from the origin on a 2,048 m island**, which is the whole diagonal, so the chunks the old path spent its first frames on were not merely elsewhere, they were off the opposite corner. World built at frame 27 (the wait cost ~2), all six vantages written, exit 0. | `RENDER.md` §1 · `crates/client/src/ui/load.rs` · `crates/client/tests/ui.rs` §E |
| client asset hot-reload (`--features hot`, client lane, 2026-08-06) | Off by default, its own feature rather than part of `render`. Edit a file under `assets/` and Bevy reloads it into the running client instead of asking for a rebuild and a reconnect. **It is `bevy_asset`'s file watcher and not `bevy_scene`** — the distinction is the whole point: the reload works on the shipped textures (and on WGSL when there is some) without a single component becoming `Reflect` or a single entity's spawn description leaving Rust, which is what adopting the scene format would have cost. Never on for a capture run: it spawns a filesystem watcher thread, and a visual gate whose inputs can change under it mid-run is a gate whose frames nobody can reproduce. `ci/gates.sh` builds `render`; a human types `--features hot`. | `crates/client/Cargo.toml` · `RENDER.md` §9 |
| ui type v0 — the face, the two weights, and the twelve sizes that are not a scale (client lane, 2026-08-07) | **PROPOSED, and the first half is measured rather than chosen.** Until this landed, **nothing in this client owned the typeface**: all 42 `TextFont` sites in the render path were `..default()`, so the server list, the loading bar, the Esc menu, settings, the HUD, inventory, crafting, the build wheel, chat, the map and the death screen were every one of them drawn in Bevy's embedded debug mono. That is not a style that was picked and lost an argument — it is the absence of a decision, and it is the loudest single difference between our frames and the reference's. **The face is Roboto Condensed**, and the source is the reference game's own public modding API rather than a guess off a screenshot: `Facepunch/Rust.Community`'s `CommunityEntity.UI.cs` defaults both its `Text` and `InputField` cases to `"RobotoCondensed-Bold.ttf"`, loads from `Assets/Content/UI/Fonts/`, and falls back to the same file when a lookup misses. Apache-2.0, © 2011 Google Inc.; `crates/client/fonts/` carries both faces and the licence text. **The weight hierarchy is theirs too, and it is the half that does the work:** bold is the DEFAULT and regular is the exception. Every label, heading, number, button, item name, chip and prompt is `ui::font_bold`; prose — a blurb, a hint, a status line, a chat message, an empty state — is `ui::font`. 31 label sites against 12 prose ones. A screen of bold condensed labels is most of what makes a reference frame read as chunky and ours read as a terminal. **Embedded with `include_bytes!`, not loaded from `assets/`**, for two reasons that are both failure modes rather than preferences: a `Handle<Font>` that has not resolved draws NOTHING — not a fallback glyph, not a box — which is strictly worse than the white-texture fallback this repo has already been bitten by twice, because a client with no text cannot report its own defect; and `OnEnter(Screen::Loading)` runs before `Startup`, so the first screen a connected start draws would render blank for the length of an asset load. `ui::build_fonts` is called beside `audio::build_bank` for exactly the same documented reason. It also overwrites Bevy's own default font handle with the bold face, so a site that slips past the gate is the wrong WEIGHT rather than a different typeface from every other word on screen. **What is deliberately NOT taken: the sizes.** There are twelve distinct ones across the six files that draw text (58, 30, 26, 20, 17, 16, 15, 14, 13, 12, 11, 10) and that is not a hierarchy, it is twelve independent decisions. Collapsing it to five steps is a real improvement and it is not a change that may be made blind: the sizes were budgeted against 720p (the "menu skin v0" row above — the first cut clipped a column at BOTH ends) and **nothing in this repo can photograph a panel**, because `render/panels/` is deliberately not registered on a `--capture` run. So the honest state is that the face moved and the scale did not. **Open for the operator:** the scale, and whether the panels get a screenshot mode (a viewer, not a gate — the visual-gate rule is retired) so that moving it stops being a guess. **Verified by picture for this slice, and the panel half is not repeatable:** six vantages under Xvfb + lavapipe with zero panics, and the inventory and the wheel photographed by driving a live client with `xdotool` and grabbing the root window — by hand, from outside the tree, the way the SIGTERM path was measured. Two defects came straight out of looking and are in `NOW.md` §0p: an item name overflows its 44 px cell, and the wheel's hint line is drawn through the hotbar. | `crates/client/src/render/ui.rs` · `crates/client/tests/ui.rs` §F · `crates/client/fonts/` |
| ui palette v1 — the panel colours are measured now, and the old ones were a different family (client lane, 2026-08-07) | **PROPOSED, and it replaces the palette half of "menu skin v0" above.** Every value is sampled off `Rust Images/crafting.png` — the reference's own inventory frame, checked in — by averaging a 7x7 patch at a named place. What the measurement found is not a near miss: ours was a **cool near-black** (`0.082,0.082,0.090`, blue the largest channel) selecting in **olive**, against a reference that is **warm** (R > G > B in every single sample), about **twice as light**, and selects in **blue**. **The values:** panel body `#2b2723`, rail/raised panel `#423e39`, detail pane `#47433c`, row `#5e5a54`, search box `#4d4943`, selection **`#3982ba`**, station badge `#9abc5c` (it was a mustard yellow, which is not a colour on that panel), and the three vitals `#8cb640` / `#4e97d0` / `#c36f36`. **The selection colour is the one that changes how the screen reads.** A lighter grey block on a panel built entirely from lighter and darker grey blocks says *hovered*; blue is the only hue on the reference's whole inventory and the eye finds it without reading. The olive it replaces was documented as measured off "the reference's own options screen" — a frame that is **not in `Rust Images/`** and cannot be re-checked, so it is not evidence in the sense `ART.md` means. If that screen really does select in olive, it is a per-screen exception to add back **with the frame committed beside it**. **One caveat, stated rather than hidden:** the reference panel is translucent over a blurred world, so each sample carries some of what was behind it. These are composited values — what a player actually sees — and still far closer than an invented number. **Also landed off the same frame: the vitals are bars, not text.** `HP 100/100` in a column is a debug readout wearing a HUD's position; the reference draws an icon square, a trough, a fill and the number inside it, and a bar is the only one of the two that answers the question asked mid-fight (*how much is left*, not *what integer is it*). `VITAL_BAR_W/H = 118/19`. The zero-max rule is unchanged and now expressed as `Display::None` on the row rather than an absent line. **Open for the operator:** all of it, and specifically whether the panels should be translucent over a *blurred* world the way the reference's are — we have no blur, which is why ours must sit at 0.97 alpha where theirs sits lower. | `crates/client/src/render/ui.rs` · `crates/client/src/render/panels/mod.rs` · `Rust Images/crafting.png` |
| ui icons v0 — the cells are pictures now, and the wheel is a ring (client lane, 2026-08-07) | **PROPOSED.** Operator, on the wheel: *"our circular selector to build look horrible worse than even avg programer art"*, and *"YES please use game-icons.net"*. **The diagnosis was structural, not colour.** `Rust Images/building.jpeg` is a **cream annulus cut into wedges** with a red line-art glyph in each and the world showing through the middle; ours was a flat dark disc with rounded label boxes floating on it. No palette fixes that, which is why the previous pass's repaint did not. **The annulus is a baked texture** (`render/panels/ring.rs`): Bevy UI has no arc and a wedge is not a rectangle, so ten images are rasterised at plugin-build time — one base pair, one highlight per shape segment, one per material segment. Baked once rather than on demand because the wheel rebuilds every time the pointer crosses a wedge, several times a second while sweeping, for a thing with ten states. Geometry comes from the same `Rings` that `ui::build::pick` resolves the pointer with, so what is drawn and what is picked cannot disagree. Colours located programmatically in the frame rather than sampled by eye: band `#f5e7dd`, chosen wedge `#d13f28`, inner/outer 0.66. **Icons are game-icons.net, CC BY 3.0** — 57 white-on-transparent PNGs baked by `ci/bake_icons.py` into `assets/icons/`, tinted at the draw (`ImageNode::color`) so one file serves chosen/ordinary/unaffordable. Keyed by the item's **display name**, not its content id, because `protocol::ItemCatalog` carries names and nothing else; 21 of the 48 differ, so the baker derives the keys from `content/items.toml` rather than having them typed twice. Attribution ships in `assets/icons/CREDITS.md` (CC BY is a notice licence) and `tests/ui.rs` §G fails on a missing credit, on stem/file drift, or on a content item with no picture. Nothing traced from the reference. **Known and left:** two items share a glyph where the set had no second candidate; the centre readout sits on a 0.45 disc because the reference blurs the world behind its centre and we have no blur (the first cut had the horizon running through the word "Foundation"); and the wheel is still **two** rings where the reference has one — which is the hammer/blueprint finding below, not a drawing bug. **Open for the operator:** whether to split the wheel the way the reference does — **Building Plan picks the shape, hammer picks the upgrade tier**. `content/items.toml` already ships both `item.building_plan` and `item.hammer`; the client opens one wheel regardless of what is held, and `sim-core/src/build.rs` gates neither place nor upgrade on a held item. That is a design + sim-lane change, not a repaint. | `crates/client/src/render/panels/ring.rs` · `crates/client/src/ui/icons.rs` · `ci/bake_icons.py` · `crates/client/tests/ui.rs` §G |
| the build wheel is one ring: a blueprint places, a hammer upgrades (client lane, 2026-08-07) | **SPOKEN.** Operator: *"you dont upgrade from holding blueprint … upgrade is the hammer you hold"*, then *"just remove the inner ring on the building screen"*. Our wheel had an outer ring of shapes and an inner ring of materials, which merged **two** of the reference's menus into one: its building plan opens a ring of shapes and places the bottom rung, and its **hammer** opens a different wheel (demolish / rotate / upgrade / repair / pick up) that walks a standing piece up the ladder. Picking a material at placement time is a verb the reference does not have. **Nothing was lost by cutting it, and that was checked before cutting.** `sim_core::build::upgrade` has existed since the build slice, `ACT_UPGRADE` is on the wire, and `render/verbs.rs` already sends it — **`U`** takes the piece you are looking at one rung up via `structure::next_material`. So stone and metal are reached the way the reference reaches them. `ui::build::PLACE_MATERIAL` pins placement to `MATERIALS[0]` (wood) and `Ui::material` is **deleted** rather than left as a field nothing writes. The ring band is now `0.66 × rim` — the ratio measured off `building.jpeg` — which also gives the centre readout a 282 px circle where it had 192 and was already tight. `Rings` lost its `split`, `pick` returns `Option<usize>`, `Hover` is gone, and the seven baked ring images are down from ten. Gates: `tests/ui.rs` §D, which now also asserts the band ratio, because the same `Rings` feeds the texture bake and the pointer resolve and the two must not drift. **Still owed, and it is the other half of the operator's sentence:** `U` should be a **hammer wheel**, not a bare keypress — the reference's second radial. `NOW.md` §0p2. | `crates/client/src/ui/build.rs` · `crates/client/src/render/panels/{ring,wheel}.rs` |
| spawn kit v0 — a fresh character is granted a kit, in content (server + content lanes, 2026-08-07) | **PROPOSED, and it is testing scaffolding an operator should empty for a public shard.** Operator: *"we might have to give players starter items for a bit for testing LOL"*. The need was real and not cosmetic: a fresh spawn had nothing, so the build wheel read `350 Wood (0)`, no piece could be placed, and nothing could be upgraded or repaired — the entire building interaction was unreachable by anyone testing it. **It is content, and that is forced by the walls rather than chosen.** Two other routes were checked first and both cross one. A new grant command is a **wire** change (wall 6: version bump plus regenerated goldens) for a message no player ever sends. A `shard.toml` flag changing what `Join` seats would make a **WAL replay diverge** from the run that wrote it (wall 5), because the flag is not in the header and the join command carries no kit. Content is safe by construction: the content hash is *already* pinned into that header, so a replay replays the kit it was played under. **Shape:** `[[spawn_kit]]` in `content/balance.toml`, `#[serde(default)]` so **absent is legal and means a naked beach spawn** — which is what a public shard wants, expressed by deleting a table rather than editing code. Baked to item indices, granted in `World::seat`'s *fresh* arm beside `survival::grant` and never on the restore arm, because re-granting per login is an item printer. Written into slots **in order, never merged**, so the first six entries are the belt and the plan and hammer are on 1 and 2 at spawn. Cap `MAX_SPAWN_KIT = INV_SLOTS` (wall 4). **The alpha kit:** building plan, hammer, stone hatchet, stone pickaxe, torch, 5 bandages, 900 wood, 500 stone, 100 metal fragments. Wood is deliberately **under** its 1000 stack ceiling — a grant sitting exactly at the ceiling is one balance edit from being refused, and `test_content` lowers every stack to prove the hash moves. **Gates:** `crates/content/tests/content.rs` — the kit bakes, the plan and hammer land on the belt, it carries something to build with, an absent table grants nothing, and four refusals bite (unknown item, zero count, past stack size, the same item twice). **Open for the operator:** whether a public shard ships any kit at all. | `content/balance.toml` · `crates/sim-core/src/inventory.rs` · `crates/content/src/{schema,validate,bake}.rs` |
| the mouse is held-item modal (client lane, 2026-08-07) | **SPOKEN, and researched at the operator's instruction** (*"lol look this up fr because im unsure"*). The reference's building interface is entirely held-item modal and ours was not: `B` opened the shape wheel whatever was in your hand, place was on **right** click and only fired **while the wheel was held open**, and upgrade/repair/pick-up were bare keys. **What the research settled, against what was assumed:** Facepunch's own wiki says *"right click when equipped for more options"* for **both** the building plan and the hammer — so the radial is **hold RIGHT** and the action is **LEFT**, which is the opposite of the model we were about to build. It also settles the open question: the **hammer's Damage Total is 0** and the building plan has no damage stats at all, so binding left click away from the swing costs a player nothing and the held-item gate fully resolves the conflict. And the reference's placement ghost is **blue** — *"lights up bright blue when it is ready to place"* — where ours was green. **The model:** plan → hold right for the shape wheel (**latches**), left click **places**, ghost visible for as long as the plan is out rather than only while the wheel is open. Hammer → left click **repairs** (`R` stays as a keyboard shortcut). Neither item swings; `BTN_PRIMARY` and the swing cue are both suppressed, or one press would place a foundation *and* send a gather at whatever is in front of you. **Identified by display name**, because `protocol::ItemCatalog` carries names and nothing else — no id, no tag, no flag on any table the client receives, and adding one is a wire change (wall 6) for a fact the client can derive. Same normalisation the icons key off, so a rename in `content/items.toml` breaks one thing in one place. `crates/client/src/ui/hold.rs` is pure and code-tier; gates are its five unit tests plus `tests/ui.rs` §H, which fails if the content stops naming an item that normalises to `building_plan` or `hammer` — the silent-revert failure this design has. **Still owed:** the hammer's own radial (demolish / rotate / upgrade / repair / pick up). Until it lands, holding right with a hammer opens **nothing** rather than opening the shapes, because opening the shape wheel would place with the wrong verb. `NOW.md` §0p2. | `crates/client/src/ui/hold.rs` · `render/{ghost,input,verbs}.rs` · `render/panels/mod.rs` |
| the hammer lights what it is aimed at (client lane, 2026-08-07) | **PROPOSED.** The reference's hammer acts on the block you are looking at and says which one first; ours resolved the same target — `verbs::Near` has since the build slice — and drew nothing, so every hammer verb was a keypress into the dark and you learned which piece you repaired by watching its hit points move. A single entity, moved rather than respawned, carrying **the piece's own mesh and transform** read off the entity `StructRing` already spawned. Not re-derived: `spawn_piece`'s addressing is subtle (edge pieces canonical to a cell's low-x or low-z boundary, a low-z wall carrying a quarter-turn a low-x one does not) and a highlight computing its own placement would be a second implementation of *which one is this* — the defect `panels/wheel.rs` refuses by construction. `StructRing::entity_at` is the lookup that makes that possible. Blue for the same reason the placement ghost is blue: it is the reference's colour for *this is the thing*, and the two are one statement about different verbs. Unlit and `swell = 1.012` — zero swell z-fights with the face it covers, a lit wash reads as a material and goes dark on shaded faces, which is the opposite of a highlight. **Open:** the wash is a little flat over an untextured piece; it wants re-looking at when pieces have real materials. | `crates/client/src/render/highlight.rs` |

| player persistence v0 — the record, the store, and the identity it is filed under | **A shard remembers you, and the identity question stopped being a blocker.** Before this, `Command::Join` built a fresh character every time — beach spawn, full hp, empty inventory, nothing loaded because there was nothing to load from — so every disconnect was a wipe of one and no admission scheme bought anything: you could authenticate perfectly and still lose everything on a dropped connection. The unlock is one signature change: `auth::validate_session` answers `Option<PlayerKey>` instead of `bool`, and a save is filed under that opaque key. Session token, signature, scry player id, dev flag — each is a different body of that one function, and none of them moves the wire, the store, the sim or a test. **Proposed defaults:** `MAX_SAVED_PLAYERS = 4096` distinct players per shard (a ~1 MB fixed-slot file and one ~1 MB allocation at boot; a full table evicts the least recently saved, counted); `PLAYER_KEY_MAX_BYTES = 48`, held at or above 42 by a static assert — a key holds a lowercase `0x`-prefixed Ethereum address, which is exactly 42 bytes, and a smaller cap would leave every verified player unnameable (the assert originally guarded the session token's cap; the row below replaced the token model, and the number it guards is now the address's); `SAVE_RING_CAP = 256` per hop of the sim→index→file path (drop-newest, counted — the sweep re-takes the player and a record is idempotent); `SAVE_BACKUP_COUNT = 2` numbered backups rotated at boot, matching the reference game's `server.saveBackupCount` **default and minimum** — and the minimum is the half worth copying, because their own recovery docs warn `.1` can share the corruption when it predates the newest backup by a few cycles (`reference/SAVES.md` §6); `SAVE_Y_LIMIT_Q = 100000` and `SAVE_VY_LIMIT_Q = 100000` as decode sanity bounds (±1000 m, ±1000 m/s — corruption, not gameplay); `save_file` unset by default, which is today's behaviour exactly and is what keeps every test hermetic. **Durability, stated rather than implied:** exact at a clean leave; otherwise up to `MAX_PLAYERS` ticks stale (3.3 s at 30 Hz) because the autosave sweep visits one connection slot per tick and skips a player whose state has not moved. A killed shard costs that. There is no graceful-shutdown flush and none is claimed. **What is NOT persisted, and it is the bigger half:** the world. Structures, deployables, box contents, hearths, backpacks and harvested slots all still die with the process, so "your base" is a true sentence about your *inventory and your position* and not yet about your walls — `NOW.md` §0x carries the world half. Two operator calls this row does not invent: whether a content change should be able to refuse a boot (today it does, because item rows are indices and restoring under moved content would hand players the wrong things — moving the file aside is how an operator says "wipe"), and what the real validator returns, which is the same open question the row below already carries. |
| world persistence v0 — the file, the cadence, and how a loaded world keeps wall 5 | **A restart stops deleting the island, and sleepers are why it had to.** Before this a restart cost the world's furniture and every player came back through their own record; after sleepers landed it deleted every *body* on the shard, and the raid somebody had landed on one was undone by the store record that outlived it. So this is the second half of one feature rather than a separate one. **Proposed defaults:** `world_file` unset (today's behaviour exactly, and what every test runs); `world_save_interval_ticks = 1800` — 60 s at 30 Hz, and chosen against what a *crash* costs rather than what a save costs, which is the inversion `reference/SAVES.md` §4 makes available: their knob's other end is a shard-wide freeze so they cannot go below ten minutes, ours has no freeze to trade against because the walk is off the sim thread and the write is off it again; `WORLD_RING_CAP = 2`, which is not a queue depth to tune but the definition of a double buffer, with **skip-the-save** as its stated overflow policy (a later save takes a *fresher* world, so a skip costs nothing a deeper queue would have saved); `WORLD_SAVE_FORMAT = 8` (1 → 2 lock v1 · → 3 the oven/lock merge · → 4 the satchel blasts two copied-at-plant numbers, 2026-08-11, which also deleted a decoder check that would have refused any save holding a live charge · → 5 the world-containers section, 2026-08-14, so a reboot stops re-rolling every crate on the island · → 6 the piece facing byte, hard/soft v0 · → 7 every item stack carries its condition, 2026-08-15/16, item durability v0 — the canonical-empty rule widens with it · → 8 a body carries what it is wearing, 2026-08-19, armor v0: `PlayerSave` grows `WEAR_SLOTS` stacks at the same stride, so a player record goes 308 → 320 and `WORLD_SAVE_MAX_BYTES` 612 872 → 614 072) (1 when the file landed; **two lanes then claimed 2 independently** — the oven's state joined the container record on one, the code-lock section, the hearth's crew tail and the two placement ticks landed on the other — and the merge of them is a third layout that is neither, so it takes the next free number. A version two different files can both claim is worse than no version) and `WORLD_FILE_FORMAT = 2` (**1 when the file landed; turned to 2 on 2026-08-10** when the header gained the island digest `H_WORLD` — the container's version moves when the header does, which is exactly the split the next clause names), two numbers because the sim's state layout and the file's container can move independently. **The design decision worth recording is not a number.** A player restore rides `Command::JoinAs` because it happens *during* a run and the WAL is the command stream. A world load cannot — the state is a quarter of a megabyte — and does not have to, because it happens **before tick 0**: the loaded world is the *origin* of a run, not a mutation inside one. Wall 5's sentence widens by one word and stays checkable — same build + same **origin** + same command stream → same hashes — and a WAL header, when there is one, pins the origin hash beside the seed and the content hash. `reference/SAVES.md` §8 records that the reference game is no help here: it has no replay, so a loaded world only has to be legal for them; ours has to be legal *and* repeatable from. **Two things the state hash cannot see**, and both have their own gates because of it: the collision index is derived and rebuilt from the pieces at load, and a door's shut bit rides a *deployable* while the surface it blocks is a *piece* — so a piece-only rebuild leaves every door on the shard open, hash-identical and wire-identical. Deleting `rebuild_doors` leaves every hash test green and both door tests red, which was verified by doing it. |
| sleepers v0 — the eviction order, and the question that is still open | **The body stays, so a disconnect stops being a hiding place** — `Command::Leave` now sleeps the body instead of clearing `active`, which is `reference/SAVES.md` §9.1 built (operator adopted the model 2026-08-07: *"i want what rust has pretty much so we cant go wrong"*). No new tunable numbers, which is why this row is short: the slice's constants are `MAX_PLAYERS`, the combat table and the survival table, all of which already ship. What it does introduce is **one policy that was chosen rather than spoken, and one question it did not answer.** **The policy — evict the longest-asleep.** Sleepers hold player slots and there are `MAX_PLAYERS` of them, so a shard every slot of which is asleep must still admit somebody or wall 4 fails in the direction that looks like the server being down. The order is the *oldest* `slept_at`, ties breaking on slot index; the two alternatives are worse in ways worth naming, because "lowest slot" reaps whoever joined first and then never anybody else, and "most recently asleep" reaps the player likeliest to still be coming back. Evicting is not deleting the player — the store's record is exactly the "how you come back when the world has not got you" path §9.2 describes — so the cost of an eviction is the interval since that record was last swept, which is a bound the autosave already carries. `World::evictions` counts it and the shard publishes it, because a policy nobody can measure is the mood `CLAUDE.md`'s wall list warns about. **The open question, unchanged: does a sleeper block movement.** `NOW.md` §0y item 1 asked it and this slice did not settle it — players have never collided with players here, so a sleeping one collides with nobody either and the honest report is that nothing changed rather than that a decision was made. It matters for the case the whole feature is about: a body asleep in a doorway is either a barricade or a ghost, and the reference game's answer is not in any source `SAVES.md` cites. |
| siwe identity v1 — `ADDRESS_BYTES = 20`, `SIGNATURE_BYTES = 65`, `NONCE_BYTES = 32`, `DOMAIN_MAX = 64`, `SIWE_MESSAGE_MAX = 512` | **Identity is an Ethereum address, proved by signature, and this row replaces "scry session auth v0" entirely** (operator, 2026-08-07: *"SIWE is what we are using now"*, *"rework this so its normal and not odd"*). The old row proposed an opaque session token relayed to scry for validation, with a 48-byte token cap. Both the row and the constant are gone, and the reason is not mainly the security one. **A session token rotates.** The shard filed a save under the token's own bytes, so a new session would have meant a new key and a new character — persistence would have silently done nothing for every real player, with every gate green, because no gate can see that a stub disagrees with itself about who somebody is. We had built persistence on an identity that did not exist and would not have been stable if it had. **The shape now is the ordinary one:** hello → the server sends a 32-byte nonce it chose → the client signs the EIP-4361 message built by `protocol::auth::siwe_message` → the server rebuilds the same text, `ecrecover`s the signer, and admits only if it equals the claimed address. **What that dissolved:** the old row left one operator decision unanswerable — what a shard does when it cannot reach the issuer — and there is now no issuer to reach, because `ecrecover` is arithmetic. It also removed the nonce table: the nonce lives on the handshake task's stack, so a signature captured on one connection is worthless on every other and there is nothing to size, expire or sweep. **The key is the lowercase `0x…` address, 42 bytes**, and that spelling is load-bearing forever — changing how it is derived orphans every save on the shard — so it is pinned twice: `protocol::auth` has a golden on the address derivation against the published vector for private key 1, and `server::auth` has one on the whole recover-and-compare path. `require_auth = false` stays the default and stays a knob: a public shard wants everyone identified, a dev shard must be joinable with nothing but the binary, and every test here depends on the second. `domain` is new and defaults to the bind host — it must be the host players dial, because that is the entire domain binding. **Cost:** `KIND_BITS` widened 3 → 4 (all eight codes were spent) and `MAX_STREAM_MSG_BYTES` 64 → 128, so `PROTO_VER` 27 moved every message on the wire by a bit and all 76 fixtures regenerated. |
| ticket door v0 — `entitle_origin` unset, `DEFAULT_SWEEP_SECS = 120`, `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_SECS = 6`, `CHECK_MAX_WALLETS = 100` | **PROPOSED.** Whether a joiner owns a copy (`crates/server/src/entitle.rs`): one `GET /api/ticket/<slug>/of/<wallet>` at join and one `POST …/check` per sweep, both a `balanceOf` at the far end. **Unset is the default and checks nothing**, which is the 2026-08-04 row made operational — official shards arm it, community and training shards do not, and the armed set is the perimeter. **The sweep interval is the knob that matters and is the reason this row exists**: it is exactly how long a sold copy keeps playing, so it is posted rather than buried. 120 s is one request every two minutes against a 100-player roster (one page — scry caps at 100 wallets), which is cheap enough that the number is chosen for the player rather than for the origin; the timeout is refused at boot unless it is shorter, or a slow origin stacks rounds until the shard polls itself in a loop. **Three answers, never two, and this is the whole design**: `Owns` admits, `Nope` (a definite on-chain zero) refuses and kicks, and `Unknown` — a timeout, an unreachable origin, an `entitled: null` — **admits on both paths** and bumps `entitle_unknown`. An outage that booted every paying player would be worse than the freeloader it caught, and the counter is what keeps that fail-open visible instead of silent. scry states the same rule in its own response body. **Refused at boot without `require_auth`**: a guest has no wallet, so an armed door over an open one checks nobody while the config file looks right. Gates: `crates/server/tests/entitlement.rs`, `entitle.rs` unit tests. | `crates/server/src/{entitle.rs,config.rs,net.rs,stats.rs}` · `shard.toml.example` · scry `docs/store/TICKET.md` §2b |
| shard status endpoint v0 — `status_addr`, `STATUS_REQUEST_CAP = 512`, `STATUS_IO_TIMEOUT_MS = 2000` | **PROPOSED.** The piece the shard-list row above names as "the whole of what would light the counts", built: `status_addr` in `shard.toml` binds a plain HTTP/1.1 responder on its own thread, and `GET /status.json` answers `{"players":N,"max_players":100,"tick":T}` — integers only (`stats.rs` L5), no strings, no timestamps. **Absent is the default and serves nothing**, for the same reason `servers.url` stays `null`: a shard advertises no numbers until its operator says where to listen, and a live shard redeployed with this code changes behaviour not at all. Set it, and a bind failure refuses the boot — a config that names an endpoint the shard silently does not serve would be the defect the parser refuses everywhere else. **`players` is a gauge, not `joins - leaves`**: the sim loop mirrors `ShardCore::connected` (bodies with a live connection, off `clients[].connected`) into `stats.players` each tick beside `sleepers`, because the counter pair legitimately drifts — a refused install rides the LEAVING sweep out and bumps `leaves` with no matching `join`. `max_players` is `sim_core::limits::MAX_PLAYERS` read from the constant, the same source `ci/shardlist.py` pins so a published cap cannot disagree with the enforced one. **Bounded (wall 4's spirit, though this thread is not the sim)**: a request is read to `STATUS_REQUEST_CAP` bytes and no further (the acted-on request line is 26 bytes; past the cap the connection closes unanswered), reads and writes time out at `STATUS_IO_TIMEOUT_MS`, one response per connection then close, and connections are answered serially on the one thread so a slow-loris costs the next poller a bounded wait and never a thread. Malformed input closes, never panics; a well-formed GET of a wrong path gets a 404 because an operator probing with curl deserves an answer that names the mistake. Daemon-style thread — shutdown neither signals nor joins it (nothing is owed to a disk, unlike `store.rs`), so the flush path cannot hang on it. **What stays an operator act**: opening the TCP port (on the public box the cloud firewall too — the 2026-08-03 row), and pointing anything at the URL. `ci/shardlist.py` still omits `players` and both readers still draw `?`; teaching the generator or the readers to fetch this document is its own slice and was deliberately not touched. Gate: `crates/server/tests/status.rs`. | `crates/server/src/{status.rs,config.rs,stats.rs}` · `crates/server/src/bin/shard.rs` · `shard.toml.example` · `ci/shardlist.py` (consumer, untouched) |
| cell label abbreviation — `CELL_LINE_CHARS = 7` (client lane, 2026-08-09) | A 44 px inventory/browser cell with no baked icon prints the item's name, and the operator's first look found the names bleeding (`Gunpowde`, `Workbenc` — `NOW.md` §0p2 item 3). The cell now clips (landed with the icons) and `ui::craft::cell_abbrev` cuts any word over this budget to it with a trailing `.`, so the cut reads as chosen rather than as a defect. The default is derived, not felt: `CELL_PX` 44 minus 3 px padding and 1 px border each side leaves 36 px, and 10 px bold Roboto Condensed averages ~5 px a glyph → 7 characters a line. Cosmetic; a bigger cell is refused by the 720p budget on `CELL_PX`'s own row. Gate: `tests/ui.rs` §I. | `crates/client/src/ui/craft.rs` · `render/panels/{inv,craft}.rs` |
| pickup tier at a lock — full grant, not any grant (systems lane, 2026-08-09) | **The guest code no longer lifts what the lock is bolted to.** `deploy::pick_up` gated on `Locks::passes` — the door-verb tier — so a GUEST-code holder could lift a locked door or box out of the world and pocket its lock: `ACCESS_OP_TAKE` wearing the pickup verb's clothes. The gate is now `Locks::passes_full` (locked ⇒ `GRANT_FULL` only). The basis is the reference's own scoping of the tier (`reference/DOORS.md` §2.2, Devblog 149: a guest may open and close and may **not** unlock, change the code, or take the lock off) and demolish v1's rule that the lock scopes the destructive verb. Bare and unlocked stay anyone's (§5's rule, unchanged — an unlocked shop front is a choice its owner made with a full-rights op). No wire change, no new knob: a tier, not a number. The same slice makes the save decoder's stated distrust of the mirror bits true for **every** archetype: the file's `locked` byte is consumed and dropped, `rebuild_doors` derives the pair for lockable archs, and a forged locked:true on a hearth or fire loads cleared (`tests/worldsave.rs` `forged_lock_bits_load_cleared_never_trusted`).
