Gates: two guest-pickup mutant-killers in `deploy.rs`, the tier test in `lock.rs`. | `reference/DOORS.md` §2.2/§5 · `sim-core/{lock.rs,deploy.rs,worldsave.rs}` |
| the place cue has a producer — broadcast rings, walks stay silent (client lane, 2026-08-09) | **No new number — a mechanism row, recorded because the join-flood guard is a design call.** `Place` was generated, in the table and playable since audio v0, and nothing asked for it (`NOW.md` §0x item 5). The producer is `render/audio.rs::place`, off a new own-fact ring in `ClientCore`: the `PiecePlaced`/`DeployPlaced` **broadcasts** ring it (a placement happening), the `PieceSync`/`DeploySync` **walks** never do (a restatement — a join streams every standing piece and a resync streams them again), so a fresh join into a base plays zero place cues by construction and no timer knob decides when the flood is over. The wire sends both for one placement (broadcast, then the walk's tail batch); the mirror insert is idempotent, so the duplicate cannot double-ring. Drained by `feed::drain` — still the client's one `pop_*` site (`tests/sound.rs` greps) — and fired positionally at `structures::base_transform`'s own anchor, so the sound comes from where the wall appears. Ring is `TOAST_RING` deep, drop-oldest, cosmetic (the knock ring's policy). A lost broadcast costs the cue, not the wall: the walk restates state silently, which is the correct half to lose. Diagonal pairs in phase — a trot, π between the pairs. Phase is **distance-integrated** (`phase += d / CYCLE × TAU`, the step odometer's rule: flight cadence for free, a hitch wraps instead of banking swings) and its origin is hashed from the roster slot (`sound::voice::hash01`, the snort's own convention — deterministic, no OS randomness, no chorus line). Origin is derivable on both sides (the shooter's snapshot position plus `ARROW_EYE_MM`, a constant), and an arrow is an arrow to look at. What had to cross was the ballistics, because `client-core` holds no content tables at all; carrying them is what makes the tracer run **the same integer integration the sim runs**, so the drawn arc is the same arithmetic rather than an approximation that drifts over a second of flight — quantize-both-sides, applied to a tracer. **It is a picture, not a projectile**, and that boundary is the whole of why this is safe: nothing downstream reads it, and the arrow that kills you is the server's whose `EV_HIT` arrives whether or not anything was drawn. A forged tracer changes only what its author sees — which is the opposite of the reference game, where the client owns the projectile and thirteen `antihack.projectile_*` convars try to catch it lying (`PROJECTILES.md` §2/§3, §9.1). Knob, a proposed default: `TRACERS = 16`, a **view** cap rather than the shard-wide `MAX_ARROWS` 128, refusing the newest tracer rather than stealing a live one — a streak that vanishes mid-flight reads as a bug where a missing one reads as nothing. Wall 6 paid in full: all 82 goldens renamed `v32_*`→`v33_*` and regenerated in the same commit, plus `v33_event_shot.bin`; `event_roles.rs` grew its 35th row and was proven red under a b/c swap before being called covered. **It was authored as v31/33/44 and landed as v33/35/47**: research v0 took `PROTO_VER` 32, `EV_RESEARCH`/`_REFUSED` 33–34 and `SUB_RESEARCH`/`_REFUSED`/`SUB_KNOWN` 44–46 while this branch was open, so both numbering axes collided head-on — the exact case CLAUDE.md's "`protocol` and `limits.rs` never land from two branches in one merge window" names, and a silent one, because two ends would have agreed on bytes meaning two different things. A longer list is **refused, never truncated**: a silently dropped round is a bow that stops firing when its first three run out, which reads as a bug in the quiver rather than in the table. **What this does NOT do**, stated rather than hidden: there is no damage column on a round, so every arrow out of one bow still hits equally hard — theirs scales the weapon's damage by the round's (a high-velocity arrow is −20 %) and that multiplier is a number nobody has spoken, so it waits here rather than being invented into content. And there is no switch verb, so list order is the whole of the ammo policy: a bow spends the first round its holder is carrying. | ranged v0 — the bow fires, the arrow flies, the trunk stops it (systems lane) | **The content had been armed since the content crate was written and the sim could not read it.** `content/weapons.toml` has carried a bow, a crossbow, their ammo ids and their `[weapon.ballistic]` blocks through validation, the balance bands and the content hash, and `bake_combat` threw every one of them away at `if w.kind != WeaponKind::Melee { continue; }`. So `combat.rs` had `strike` and `raid` and nothing else, the pad's loot table handed out `item.arrow_metal` for a weapon that could not be fired, and every fight on the island was a walk-up club fight — which also meant the occupant collision that landed the pass before had nothing to be cover *from*. **Design, and the one line that matters:** an arrow's position is millimetres and its velocity **millimetres per tick**, both `i32`, and the whole flight is integer addition. Metres-per-second becomes per-tick once, at bake, and never again — the quantize-both-sides law applied to a projectile, so there is no per-tick rounding to accumulate over a second of flight and no float for native and wasm to disagree about. The only floats are the launch direction and the collision queries, which take metres because `terrain` and `collide` do. **Ground, occupants and pieces are point samples** along the tick's segment; **bodies are solved**, an exact planar closest-approach against the same segment, and a body is hit only if its approach comes before the distance at which the world stopped the arrow. That comparison is the judge's ask ("a shot stopped by a trunk does not reach the body behind it") and it is only *exercised* when trunk and body fall inside one 1.33 m segment — the 4 m-behind case is decided by the arrow ceasing to exist, so `tests/shoot.rs` gates the pressed-against-cover case separately over a swept standoff, and mutating the comparison away reddens only that one. **`frame.pitch` is now read by the sim** for the first time (`gather.rs` had said "aim is planar in v0" since melee): the byte was already transmitted, interpolated and hashed, so this costs no wire — it costs a `pitch_lut.rs` generated by `ci/gen_pitch_lut.py` exactly as `yaw_lut.rs` is, and unlike the yaw table it is **not circular**, because 255 is the opposite pole from 0 and not its neighbour. Knobs, all proposed defaults: `MAX_ARROWS = 128` (refuse the shot — checked before the ammo leaves the quiver, and deliberately not drop-oldest, because stealing a live arrow to make room would make a hit depend on how many other people were shooting), `ARROW_STEP_MM = 170` (the smaller of two existing assumptions — `collide::blocked`'s documented 0.19 m endpoint shortcut, and two interior samples across a 0.468 m trunk), `MAX_ARROW_SUBSTEPS = 16` (with the step, also a **content wall**: `bake_bow` refuses a muzzle speed past 2.72 m/tick rather than shipping a projectile that tunnels), `MAX_ARROW_LIFE_TICKS = 120`, `ARROW_EYE_MM = 1600` (the eye, not the chest — a shot from the navel would clear cover the shooter cannot see over), `ARROW_R_M = 0.05`. **What v0 does not do**, stated rather than hidden: no arrow is drawn on any screen (a wire event plus a client tracer, neither built, and the wire half is this lane's); no structure damage, so an arrow stops on a wall rather than chipping it; no headshots and no falloff (the schema has no curve to read); firearm and throwable rows stay dropped at the bake, the revolver because hitscan wants M2's rewound raycast. And `collide::blocked` bakes `CAPSULE_RADIUS_M` into its own query, so an arrow is inflated to a player's width — it goes exactly where a body could go and no further, which is defensible for a doorway and wrong for an arrow slit. Open for the operator: whether `MAX_ARROWS` should scale with `MAX_PLAYERS` rather than sit flat, and whether a bow should be able to chip a wall at all. | ranged.rs, limits.rs, combat.rs `RangedDef`, bake.rs `bake_bow`, tests/shoot.rs |

The oven's own state (`arch`, `lit`, `burn`, `bank`, twelve cook counters) rides an index-aligned array beside the box records, which is `bag_ready`'s argument one store down: `DeployRec` is what the deploy-sync packet mirrors, so a burn timer may not ride on it. Both halves move together in `place_deploy` and in `remove_at`'s swap-remove, and `tests/oven.rs`'s `a_removal_moves_both_halves` is the gate for exactly that. **A use press is the switch**: `Command::Use` routes by address (a door lives on a doorway's edge, an oven on the plane, so it is a lookup and not a guess), lighting refuses with `REFUSE_D_FUEL` when nothing inside burns — a match lights what is laid, it does not lay it — and an oven that runs dry snuffs itself, announcing with **actor 0**, which is the one field separating it from a hand on the switch. **Only fuel and what it cooks go in** (`REFUSE_M_OVEN`), which is the reference's own 2025 rule and what stops a 100-wood campfire being a cheaper twelve-slot box than the 100-wood box whose whole job is to be one; that reason is the eighth, so `REFUSE_M_BITS` widened 3 → 4 and the wire moved with it. **The byproduct is banked, not rolled**: the reference gives ~75% of a charcoal per wood by chance, and a chance is an RNG draw inside `state_hash`, so `byproduct_pct` accumulates in hundredths and pays one unit per 100 — the reference's expectation, exactly, with nothing random in the hash. **The sweep is bounded without a cursor**: each oven steps on the tick where its index and the tick agree mod `OVEN_PERIOD_TICKS`, so no tick visits more than `ceil(MAX_BOXES / 15)` ovens and every fire still burns at one real rate; `TICK_HZ % OVEN_PERIOD_TICKS == 0` is asserted, because a step that does not divide the second makes every content `seconds` approximate. **Wire v28** (`SUB_OVEN = 41`, the 42nd subtype of sixty-four — no field widened but the refusal above), 78 goldens regenerated in the same commit (wall 6), and `event_roles.rs` classifies `EV_OVEN` by role in the same commit (ledger 24/29 → 25/30). **Content is `content/cooking.toml`**, new to `Content::FILES`: one `[fuel]` row (wood, 3 s a unit — the reference's 20 wood/minute — leaving charcoal at 75) and `[[cook]]` rows, hashed whole by `canon.rs` because the `[backpack]` defect is what happens when a value reaches the sim and not the digest. **It ships with no cook row, and that is a fact about the island rather than the oven**: cooking wants raw food, the alpha food set is berries/mushrooms/corn, only berries are payable by anything in the world, and none of the three is a thing you cook — the meat was cut with the animals that would drop it. It stops being invisible the moment a generated mesh is scaled into one: a furnace mesh built to a stated **1.44** width:height was going into a row of **0.73**, so the row would have squashed a correct mesh by 2×. **The basis is the physical object**, which anyone can check and which is what the reference game's own art is drawn from; it is corroborated where corroboration exists — Meshy's `auto_size` vision estimate returned **0.880 × 0.585 m** for a steel drum against a real 55-gallon drum's 0.88 × 0.58, landing within a centimetre on a standard object. **`BUILD_CELL_M` is 3.0 and 3 m is also the reference's foundation**, so these transfer 1:1 with no scale conversion — which is the fact that makes "match their proportions" a well-posed request rather than a vibe. **Moved**: bag `1.2→1.9` long (a bedroll must out-length the body that spawns on it), hearth `0.9³ → 1.2×1.0×0.6` (a perfect cube is the tell of a number nobody chose), box `1.0×0.7×1.0 → 1.2×0.65×0.7` (a chest is wide and shallow; `Rust Images/storageandtoolchest` shows the same shape language), furnace `1.1×1.5×1.1 → 1.3×0.95×0.85` (1.37 W:H, against the 1.44 a generator reached unprompted), workbench depth `0.9→0.7`. **Deliberately unmoved**: the fire pit's square footprint (a fire ring *is* radially symmetric — correct here and nowhere else), the door (0.12×2.1×0.9 was already a real door), the lock (never drawn), the recycler and research table. Also moved, same defect one file over: the road barrel was `Cylinder::new(0.45, 0.95)` — 0.9 m across by 0.95 tall, ratio **1.06**, near-spherical and ~44 % too fat — now 0.585 × 0.88, ratio 1.50. **Free of the walls**: `deploy_size` has two callers, the build ghost and its own test, and nothing in the sim reads the table, so a row is a render fact costing no wire byte, no golden and no replay. The ghost tests assert *relationally* (a door stands at half its height above the base) and stayed green. **Open for the operator**: `box_small` and `box_large` share `archetype = "box"` and therefore draw at one size, so a 12-slot and a 48-slot box are visually identical — splitting them costs an `ARCH_*` code and a `PROTO_VER` bump, which is why it was filed rather than taken. | `crates/client/src/render/structures.rs` `DEPLOY` · `crates/client/src/render/props.rs` barrel · `crates/client/tests/ghost.rs` |
| generated-asset recipe — the Meshy call, and the UV contract a skin depends on (2026-08-11) | **PROPOSED defaults, all measured the same day.** The pipeline is **`nano-banana-pro` reference image → `image-to-3d` → `retexture`**, ~54 credits an asset. Text-to-3D is *not* it: it skips the image stage, and the mesh it returned was the one the operator called rough. **The knobs that mattered, with the measurement rather than the claim.** `ai_model: "meshy-7"` + `ultra_mode: true` is the geometry step. `origin_at: "bottom"` is the **import fix** — `min.y = +0.0000` on all twelve assets generated so far, which retires the recentre step outright as *a parameter we were not passing* rather than pipeline work. **`auto_size` does NOT retire the rescale step, and an earlier draft of this row said it did.** It was believed on one measurement — a steel drum, returned as 0.880 × 0.585 m against a real 55-gallon drum's 0.88 × 0.58 — and a drum is a *standard object with a strong size prior*, which is exactly the case where a vision estimate cannot fail. Run across eleven starting-tier assets it is wrong more often than right: within 18 % on four (storage box, hammer, stone hatchet, workbench), and off by **0.34× on a hand rock** (returned a 0.58 m boulder), **0.55× on a campfire**, **2.5× on a bedroll** and **9× on a spear** (a 1.8 m pole returned at 0.20 m). The failure is legible rather than random — the estimator has no prior for how big *our* version of a generic object is, and long thin objects are its worst case. So **scale is normalised on import from our own table**, which costs one multiplier per asset and is the same table the `DEPLOY` row above already fixes; `auto_size` stays on only because the origin flag is coupled to it. Texture quality came from `retexture` with `ai_model: "meshy-7"` + `multiview_image_urls`: relative albedo contrast (sd/mean) **0.263 → 0.382**, the first generated texture to land inside the CC0 photoscan band (rock 0.347, metal 0.302, stone 0.561). **`remove_lighting` stays `true`, the default, and this is a settled question rather than a preference.** It was first credited with that jump; that attribution was wrong, because two variables moved at once. Held everything else fixed and flipped it alone: **0.382 vs 0.379, luma identical to four decimals, thumbnails indistinguishable.** So delighting costs nothing measurable here and is correct for a world with a moving sun — a free default, not a trade. **Two ordering traps.** `retexture` **discards** `auto_size` and `origin_at` (the barrel went from `0.585×0.88×0.585, min.y=0` back to `1.33×2.0×1.33, min.y=−1.0`), so scale is re-applied after texturing, never before. And `texture_richness` is **deprecated and does nothing** — it reads as a quality knob and is not one. **The UGC skin creator is what constrains this, and it constrains it hard.** A player-authored skin is a texture painted against an item's UV layout, so **the UV layout is a public contract: once one skin exists for an item, that item's UVs can never move again** — and Meshy generates a fresh arbitrary unwrap per call, so a regenerated asset silently invalidates every skin ever sold for it. `skins.toml` is empty until A3, which means the window to get this right is now and closes the day the first row lands. Three things follow: the **UV Unwrap API** (5 credits, ≤ 40 k faces) becomes a deliberate pinned step; `enable_original_uv: true` is mandatory on every subsequent retexture so texturing can never move a seam; and the unwrapped mesh, not the generated one, is what enters `assets/models/`. It was run and it **preserves `auto_size` and `origin_at`** (bbox and `min.y = 0` unchanged), so unwrap is safe to apply after sizing where retexture is not. **But the layout it produces must not be handed to players, and this is the finding that decides the skin creator's shape.** Rasterised, the barrel's unwrap is 67.9 % atlas coverage in ~20 *diagonal, arbitrarily-oriented* islands: the cylinder body is sliced into several skewed strips rather than the one rectangle a label wants, and the lids are fans. That is optimised for **bake coverage**, which is the right target for our own retexturing and the wrong one for a human — a player putting a logo on the front would have to find which diagonal strip is the front and paint across seams. So the skin template is an **authored** layout we own and freeze per item (for a barrel, trivially: one body rectangle + two caps), and Meshy's unwrap stays an internal texturing detail. The alternative, and probably the better product, is that the creator never exposes an atlas at all — the player composes from constrained inputs (base colour, wear amount, decal + placement) and we composite onto our own known layout, which also makes the frozen-UV contract ours to keep rather than a vendor's to break. **`smart-topology` (`meshy-t2`) was measured against `meshy-7` on the same input image and wins on every axis that was compared**: 4,164 tris against 4,051, relative albedo contrast **0.441 against 0.382** (both inside the photoscan band, t2 higher), **6.8 MB against 19.0 MB**, **15 credits against 55**, and it *preserved* `auto_size`/`origin_at` where the meshy-7 retexture destroyed them. Two caveats, both real: it **refuses `remove_lighting`** outright (`400`, "not supported for model_type: smart-topology"), which is nearly free given that flag measured as a no-op above but was measured on the *other* path so does not fully generalise; and its texture stage duplicated the reference image's single hazard placard into two, which is a real artifact and not a proportion. **Its "natively separated parts" claim did not manifest here** — 1 node, 1 mesh, 1 primitive, 1 material — but a barrel has no parts to separate, so that claim is untested rather than false and wants a multi-part object (a furnace with body, hood and chimney) to settle. **Size is now the binding constraint, not quality**: 19–21 MB per prop at `texture_resolution: "4k"`, against 6.5 MB for the entire current texture set. Levers in order of effect: resolution (4K → 1024² is ~16× the pixels for a 0.88 m prop nobody inspects at 20 cm), then format — Bevy 0.18 is built here with `features = ["jpeg", "wav"]` and **no `ktx2`/`basis-universal`**, so every texture decompresses to full RGBA in VRAM; and the emissive map measured black (luma 0.0001) while `emissiveFactor` is `[1,1,1]`, so it is pure waste plus a latent glow bug. **Both texture questions are now answered and only the skin one is open.** Textures ship **KTX2/UASTC at 1024** and the `ktx2` + `basis-universal` + `zstd_rust` features are on (2026-08-11, *"cant we also just reskin super easy if we need"* — the reversibility argument, and it is the right one: the 2K sources are out of tree and re-encoding is a script run, so this is a file swap and not a commitment). Four encodings were measured on one prop rather than argued: 2K JPEG 7.9 MB disk / ~50 MB VRAM / no mipmaps; 2K ETC1S 1.4 MB / 8.4 MB but **28.0 dB albedo, a drop you can see**; 2K UASTC ~lossless but disk *grows* to 11.8 MB; **1K UASTC 3.2 MB / 4.2 MB at 46.9 dB**, which is better than the JPEG on both axes and gains the mipmaps the JPEGs never had. Whole set 95 MB → 42 MB. **One trap paid in full**: the spec-correct way to reference a KTX2 texture from glTF is `KHR_texture_basisu`, Bevy 0.18 does not implement it (its own table marks it ❌, bevyengine#19104), and its validator rejects an unknown `extensionsRequired` entry outright — so the *correct* file fails to load on all twelve while a file that keeps `textures[].source` and merely sets `mimeType: "image/ktx2"` works, because Bevy dispatches ktx2 off the mime type. Ours are therefore deliberately outside core glTF and a third-party tool may refuse them; `assets/models/MANIFEST.md` carries the caveat at the file. **Still open for the operator**: whether the skin creator paints one UV per item or per separated part. | `assets/models/MANIFEST.md` · `content/skins.toml` · `crates/client/Cargo.toml` · `ART.md` §7 · `BUSINESS.md` |
| site footprints v0 — a site publishes masks, not a radius (`SiteFootprint { scatter_m, swept_m }`, `swept_m` = container ring + one clutter cell) (world lane, 2026-08-10) | **No new spoken number — both radii are DERIVED, and the row exists because a behaviour changed.** `reference/MONUMENTS.md` §3 is the research (operator briefing, same day): the reference game shipped monuments on visible circular plateaus for years because a monument's footprint was one radius doing every job. Ours was the same shape one level down — `HAVEN_RADIUS_M` / `WAYSTATION_RADIUS_M` answered exactly one question, "does the scatter grid stand anything here", and **`clutter_fill` had no `Haven` parameter at all**, so grass, twigs and scree grew straight across the haven pad and both waystations while the carriageway running through them was correctly swept to grit. The road got an override; the destination it leads to did not — measured on the pad floor over the suite's three seeds, 661 / 62 / 506 grass-and-litter elements and 80 / 11 / 24 understory elements stood on it, all now zero with ~870 grit elements in their place, so the coverage count is unchanged and only its identity moved. `scatter_m` is the number `in_haven` already used, asserted equal to it so the two readings cannot drift apart. `swept_m` is the container ring plus one clutter cell — *the ground the site's own arrangement stands on*, so a site that moves its ring drags its floor with it, the way `skirt_base_r` reaches off `occupant_volume`. **The mechanism is the decision, not the radii**: `site_sweep` is a scalar with a smoothstep profile between the two masks, and each element dithers against it with a hash byte it had already drawn, so the boundary is a thinning population rather than a ring on the ground — a hard circle would have re-created the exact defect §3 is about, and the gate refuses one (`clutter.rs` §S, proven red under a hard-circle mutant). Coverage becomes grit rather than nothing, because a hole would be a hole in `ART.md` rule 4's guarantee; the understory is refused outright. **Looked at, 2026-08-10** (lavapipe + Xvfb + `--capture`, camera parked at each site with the `dev_spawn` override — see `NOW.md` §0p3 for the recipe). It works, and the contrast follows the splat rather than being uniform: on the **waystation**, which stands in meadow, thick grass tufts thin to bare grit around the structure with a dithered edge and no ring — the clearest confirmation the profile is doing its job. On the **haven pad**, which stands on sandy coast where the splat was already drawing bare ground, the change is subtle: grit where the wilderness 20 m out has brown litter. That asymmetry is correct behaviour, not a shortfall — a swept floor should read against what surrounds it. **One thing to fix later, seen only by looking:** at 2 m the `Clutter::Pebble` instances read as scattered grey angular shards rather than grit, so swept ground looks littered rather than swept. That is the pebble archetype's mesh, not the sweep, and it is a separate art row. **Open for the operator:** whether a destination should also be **build-blocked** (`build.rs`/`deploy.rs` mention no site today, so a player may raise a base on the pad — a design call, not a defect, and the mask row is free once the answer exists), and whether the swept floor should be grit or a distinct made-ground kind the splat does not otherwise draw. | `crates/sim-core/src/terrain.rs` (`SiteFootprint`, `site_sweep`, `swept_here`) · `crates/sim-core/tests/clutter.rs` §S · `reference/MONUMENTS.md` §9.2 |

| greybox mirror v1 — the drawn structure is DERIVED from the sim's box table (`props::authored`, `archetype_mesh`, `archetype_lift`, `SINK_M`); the drawn/blocked equality gate `SLACK_R_M = 0.001`, `SLACK_TOP_M = 0.001` (client lane, 2026-08-10) | **No new spoken number, and one PROPOSED tightening at the end.** `TERRAIN.md` §7.1 recorded the drift and did not fix it because "which list is authoritative" was open. **It is the sim's**, and the reason is not preference: `ART.md` §6 and the whole tier-silhouette argument were written against those numbers, `OCCUPANT_R_M`/`OCCUPANT_TOP_M` are *defined* as the tables' own bounds, and the drawn list was the one that had lost rows. Measured before deciding: the canopy was drawn to 2.09 m against a blocked 4.1 m and the shelter to 5.6 m against 9.2 m, missing four corner posts and its tower-cap, with the two tables in **different units** (full size vs half extent). **The fix is derivation, not a second table plus a gate** — a gate over two hand-written lists only catches drift after someone writes the gate, and eight of those gates went with the browser client. Only colours stay client-side, because what a wall is made of is not a fact about the volume it occupies. `tests/greybox.rs` then checks what derivation cannot make true: the unit conversion, every row reaching the mesh by vertex count, the authored bounds equalling the published broad phase in both directions, and every OTHER archetype fitting the volume the sim blocks — closing `OCCUPANT_R_M`'s own admission that "nothing in the Rust workspace can see a triangle". Proven red under the historical drift itself. **LANDED 2026-08-10 on the operator's call** (see the Spoken row of that date); this row records what it was. The generated props blocked wider than they drew — `blob_mesh` pulls vertices inward from its nominal radius, so a boulder `OCCUPANT_R_M` calls 1.5 m reaches 1.1145 m, an 0.39 m invisible collision skirt, and the ore nodes carry 0.52 m of headroom. It is the canopy's defect in the survivable direction (stopped short of what you see, rather than walking through what looks solid), and closing it moves collision radii, which is a gameplay change and a replay-golden move. The gate shipped as a **ratchet** at the measured values and is now an **equality gate** at a one-millimetre rounding allowance, so the skirt cannot come back. **Looked at, 2026-08-10**: both structures render as their tables describe — the pad is a walled mass with the tower and cap above the roofline, the waystation an open canopy on four posts with both plates and its finial, and the tier-silhouette argument `terrain.rs` has always made ("opaque and vertical" against "transparent and horizontal") is now true in the frame instead of only in the doc, because the tower and the upper plate were the parts that were not drawn. **One finding that is new and is an art call**: the shelter's four corner posts top out at 5.4 m against a 4.2 m roof, so they stand 1.2 m proud and read as broken-off stubs rather than posts. That is in the sim's table and always was; nobody had seen it because the client was not drawing them. | `crates/client/src/render/props.rs` · `crates/client/tests/greybox.rs` · `TERRAIN.md` §7.1 · `reference/MONUMENTS.md` §9.3a |
| float contraction is gated on the axis this box can reach (world lane, 2026-08-10) | **A gate, not a knob, recorded because it closes a hole the parity gate could not see.** `sim-core/clippy.toml` bans `mul_add` by name with the comment "FMA is the parity killer", and `test_parity_wasm` diffs native against wasm32 — but **both sides of that diff are `--release`**, so an optimizer that fused an `a*b + c` at -O3 and left it alone at -O0 would move worldgen's digest with the build profile and neither side would notice. `ci/gates.sh` now also diffs the debug probe against the release one. Verified identical 2026-08-10. **Why this rather than a third CPU:** aarch64 and MSVC are the real latent targets and neither is runnable here (no qemu, no cross linker, and the depot is Linux-only anyway), but the failure that would bite there is contraction, and contraction is reachable on one machine. The remaining cross-target check is one `diff` on the day a Mac build exists. | `ci/gates.sh` · `crates/sim-core/clippy.toml` · `reference/MONUMENTS.md` §9.4 item 7 |
| the announce stack — `TOAST_LINES = 4` lines, drop-oldest-note then drop-oldest, one clock each (client lane, 2026-08-14) | **PROPOSED default.** The HUD's announce sink was one `String` and one timer until now, so `hud::feedback` wrote refusals, knocks, grants, hearth stock, charge warnings, kills, gathers, crafts and spills into the same slot in a fixed order and the player saw whichever spoke last — the merge-gate judge's ranked GAP 2 (pass 20260813-230343-10), and its ranked fix 1 is the proof it already misreported the shipped world: a tree pays a secondary, so one swing into a full pack says two spill lines and the player was told about the mushrooms and never about the wood. **Four** because four rows fit between the prompt and the readout at `TOAST_PITCH_PCT = 2.8` of screen height without reaching the crosshair; row 0 keeps the old single line's `TOAST_TOP_PCT = 58.0`, so the one-line case did not move and the readout slid to the bottom of the stack; each row is `TOAST_ROW_DIM = 0.16` dimmer than the one above it, because four equal lines are a paragraph and the eye needs a reason to start at the new one. A repeat of the newest line refreshes its clock and counts (`+1 × WOOD  ×4`) rather than spending a slot, so ten swings at a tree cannot flush a refusal off the stack; the suffix is rewritten from a stored base length, which is also why a repeat allocates nothing. **Overflow is drop-oldest-*note*, then drop-oldest** — corrected 2026-08-14, and the correction is the point. Drop-oldest alone made `feedback`'s write order load-bearing without saying so: refusals were written first, so the first line a five-fact frame ate was the sentence the player asked for by pressing a key, while four gathers they could read off their own pack stayed (the merge-gate judge's ranked fix 3, pass 20260813-230343-11). `Rank` puts that decision where the eviction can read it — a line is an `Alarm` when the fact dies with it (refusal, spill, charge going live) and a `Note` when looking somewhere recovers it (gather, craft, hearth stock, kill) — so the cap eats every note before an alarm, falls back to oldest-outright only on an all-alarm stack, and **never refuses a push**. Rank picks what leaves and nothing else: drawing order stays recency, so a frame under the cap is unchanged. The count has a reader now (`Toast::unseen`, drawn as `…+2 more` on the last live row, cleared when the stack empties) — appended rather than drawn over a sentence, because after the rank change the bottom row is exactly where a rescued alarm sits. Cosmetics, all of them, and none is on the wire or in the sim. **The four are COUPLED, and 2026-08-14 is when anything checked it** — they were literals inside `setup`, read at two spawn sites that computed one rule twice, and the four numbers above were each defensible alone. Named and derived now, with four gates in `hud.rs`: the stack clears the crosshair, the prompt and the hotbar (`TOAST_LINES`' own doc sentence, which was prose); the rows do not overlap; every row the cap holds draws at an alpha above zero; and the spawns read the geometry rather than recompute it. Two findings the naming produced, neither a new knob: the pitch is a percent of window height while the type is px, so the stack self-overlaps below a **600 px** window against the 720 the client opens at — a margin nobody had computed, and a `TOAST_PITCH_PCT` trimmed to 2.3 would spend all of it; and `TOAST_LINES` × `TOAST_ROW_DIM` means 8 rows would draw the deepest at alpha 0, a line the cap accepts, counts as delivered and cannot show. A repeat also raises a line's rank now and never lowers it — the fast path was the one write that returned without setting the field the eviction reads. | `crates/client/src/render/hud.rs` · `NOW.md` §0tq |
| the wire raid profile's addresses — `RAID_CHARGE_SLOT = 0`, `RAID_GOODS_SLOT = 2`, `RAID_OWNER_CODE = 4242`; one action per cadence tick (systems lane, 2026-08-14) | **PROPOSED defaults, and two of the three are addresses rather than balance.** `bin/bots` now feeds `bots::raid_step` over the wire, so it must name a hotbar slot for the charge, a container slot for the goods, and the code every owner bot sets. All three are `sim-core/tests/raid_storm.rs`'s, reused deliberately: the wire raid and the gated in-sim storm then exercise one profile instead of two nearly-identical ones, and a divergence between them would be a real finding rather than a fixture difference. The code matters least — `lock.rs`'s shock/lockout ladder only runs on a *wrong* guess and a draw from 10,000 is wrong either way. **The rate is not a proposal at all**: `core::wants_action` hands the sim at most one action per client per tick and `push_action` silently drops the rest, so the ceiling the server already enforces is the rate, and a load tool that exceeded it would be measuring a pressure no player can apply. The four content ROWS are not knobs and are not here — they are resolved by id (`piece_index("build.foundation_twig")` and friends), because `bake.rs` ranks ids and a typed row silently addresses its neighbour the next time an item lands. **Open for the operator:** whether a fleet should vary the code per plot, which would need the owner bot to derive one from its own id and is the shape a real crew would have. | `NOW.md` §0rs · `findings/pass-20260813-230343-14-judge.md` gap 1 · server `botclient.rs` `bin/bots.rs` · `server/tests/bot_smoke.rs` |
| capture and shard seed v0 — which island we photograph and which one we ship (client+world lane, 2026-08-14) | **CLOSED 2026-08-14 — the question was built on a broken measurement and there is nothing to answer.** It asked which island `shard-public.toml`, `shard.toml.example` and the `--capture` probe should run, after a 40-seed scan found the shipped 20260731 was the minimum of the set on all three axes. That scan swept one quadrant of each island (§Spoken, same date); over the whole world square the shipped seed is upper-third for relief and granite, and at the capture camera's own spawn it is far above median. **No seed moved, no shard was wiped**, and the second option offered here — narrowing worldgen's relief variance so no seed is a pancake — is moot: the spread was never measured correctly, and 46→106 m turned out to be 106→106. The third option, moving the splat bands, stays refused for the reason `relief.rs` gates. | `DECISIONS.md` §Spoken 2026-08-14 · `sim-core/tests/relief.rs` · `sim-core/examples/seed_scan.rs` |
| capture clock v0 — a `--capture` run pins its hour to noon, `rig::DayPin` / `CAPTURE_DAY_FRAC = DAY_PORTION * 0.5` (client lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED, and it is a derived number rather than a chosen one — a harness knob, nothing about a player's sky changes.** `DAY_PORTION * 0.5` is the sine arch's peak, which is the ONE fraction at which `sun_elevation` returns `RIG_SUN_ELEVATION` and `daylight` returns 1.0 exactly; pinning anywhere else would be a taste call, and pinning here is just "shoot at the register the rig is authored for". The defect it closes: a capture shard boots at tick 0 with no `world_file`, and the probe fires whenever the client finished building and settling, so **the sun's height was a function of how long `cargo` took**. Measured on this tree — tick 0 is **24.47°**, the ~2 200 ticks a build costs put it at **27.33°** (which is what the visual judge reported as 27°), and a slower box reaches **30.36°**: a **5.89°** swing, all of it at or below `ART.md` §1's 30–40° band and *monotonically rising*, so a slower box scored a brighter frame. No frame any visual judge has scored was taken at the authored register; the pin puts it at **34.95°**, mid-band. It is the windowed-pin rule one field further (§Spoken 2026-08-13: a capture run takes the defaults wholesale except where the box would otherwise decide the frame), and it needed no harness edit — `art/capture-native.sh` lives outside this repo and cannot be changed from inside it, so the pin had to key off `--capture` alone. **It pins the TICK, not the sun.** Two consumers read the clock off `Feed`: `rig::day_night` through `day_frac` and `render/audio.rs` through `world::is_night`. A pinned elevation would have roosted the birds at the box's hour while the light stood at noon — the seam `to_sun` was closed for on 2026-08-15, re-opened one level up. One tick in, everything derives from it through the sim's own map, so there is still one model of what a tick means. **Consequence for reading reports:** every `-visual.md` before 2026-08-15 was shot at 24–27°, so its tonal numbers are not comparable to later ones. **Open for the operator:** whether noon is the right hour to score at at all — a rubric that only ever sees the sun at its peak never scores a long shadow, and `ART.md` §1's band is a *band*. A second pinned vantage set at a lower sun is the alternative, and it doubles the frames. | client `render/rig.rs` (`DayPin`, `CAPTURE_DAY_FRAC`, `tick_at_frac`) · `render/mod.rs` · `render/audio.rs` · `crates/client/tests/daynight.rs` · `NOW.md` §0gi · `ART.md` §1 · `RENDER.md` §capture |
| ground splat material v0 — IDENTITY_ROUGH (retired 2026-08-16, see the row below; it was `[0.86, 0.93, 0.96, 0.88]`) · `BLEND_DEPTH = 0.2` · `HEIGHT_INFLUENCE = 0.15` · `NORMAL_Z_FLOOR = 0.2` (client lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED defaults. The one that was a taste call is gone.** The four ground identities each carry their own photograph now (`render/ground_splat.rs`, `assets/shaders/ground_splat.wgsl` — the first WGSL in the tree). `IDENTITY_ROUGH` replaces the single `perceptual_roughness: 0.92` all four used to share, which is the other half of "granite has stone's value and not stone's surface"; it is ordered by how much a wet specular lobe belongs on each surface and nothing measures it, so it is the row here that most wants a spoken number. The other three are bounds rather than tastes: `BLEND_DEPTH` and `HEIGHT_INFLUENCE` together cap the height blend's vote at ±0.15 so it can only arbitrate a band where two weights are already within 0.3 — and **that blend measured as a no-op** (+0.1% contrast over raw luma), because `splat_from` is near-binary (92.2% over 0.8), so both are insurance for a softer classifier rather than live settings. `NORMAL_Z_FLOOR` is a defect fix with a number in it: the sources are JPEG normal maps, their blue channel dips at block edges, and the 1e-4 floor it replaced turned that into gradients in the thousands. **Measured, at a pinned `dev_spawn` with the identity mix stated:** at 1500,600 (litter 611‰, rock 329‰) near-ground neighbour contrast goes **6.43 → 8.53, +32.8%**, every frame improved; at 1024,1024 (grass 909‰) it is neutral, which is correct — the old single map WAS grass's luminance field, so that spawn is a no-op by construction. ⚠ **It also costs 8.0% mean luma** at the mixed spawn, and that is not this material's to spend: brightness belongs to the coupled tonemap/sky/exposure/fog owner (`CLAUDE.md` traps). It is the expected consequence of granite finally having granite's relief — more surface variation is more self-shadow — but it is a debt against that owner, recorded here rather than absorbed silently. ⚠ **`IDENTITY_ROUGH` is retired (2026-08-16) and the way it was wrong is worth more than the number was.** It was authored by hand, ordered "by how much a wet specular lobe belongs on each surface", and this row called it the one that most wants a spoken number. The roughness maps it was a placeholder for are now sampled per texel, and measured off the shipped files the four surfaces are **sand 0.9631 · grass 0.9364 · litter 0.9197 · rock 0.6108** — which disagrees with the knob about the ORDER, not just the level. Dry beach sand is the *roughest* ground on the island and granite by far the smoothest; the knob had sand smoothest and granite near it. Both errors came from one premise: it priced *damp* sand, which is the wet term's job and not an identity's. See `ROUGH_MEAN` and `WET_ROUGH` below. |
| ground roughness v1 — the maps replace the scalars: `ROUGH_MEAN = [0.9631, 0.9364, 0.9197, 0.6108]` (a record, not a setting) · `WET_ROUGH = 0.75` (client lane, 2026-08-16) | **One PROPOSED number and one measurement.** `ROUGH_MEAN` chooses nothing — the shader samples `<role>_rough.jpg` per texel and this is what those four files measure (raw, no sRGB decode; `tests/ground_splat.rs` re-measures them and fails on drift, so a source swap cannot change the island's specular silently). It is written down because `ART.md` §3 has **no roughness row**, so this is the only record of what our ground is, and because granite moving 0.88 → 0.611 is the biggest single change the slice makes. `WET_ROUGH` is the actual knob: what a soaked surface keeps of its dry roughness, the same shape `WET_VALUE = 0.55` uses for value, closing the residual `terrain_mesh.rs` names in `WET_VALUE`'s own doc (*"a wet surface is also smoother … cannot vary per vertex without the shader `RENDER.md` §8 owns"*). 0.75 is not measured off anything and is deliberately shorter than the value keep: a visible tightening at the waterline, not a mirror. It also recovers by MECHANISM the intent `IDENTITY_ROUGH` was hand-authoring — "damp sand is the smoothest thing on the island" is now true where the tide reaches and false on a dry dune, which is the correct shape of that sentence. ⚠ **Measured effect: none detectable.** Six vantages at `dev_spawn = 1500,600`, seed 20260731, `--no-hud`: contrast 9.008 → 8.973 (−0.4%), mean luma −0.1%, near saturation −1.0% — **all of it inside the harness's own run-to-run spread**, which was measured for this and is ~0.3% on contrast (re-running an identical build moves it that much; `RENDER.md` §5). The cause is the row below, and it is why this landed anyway — see `render/ground_splat.rs`'s header. |
| ground specular v0 — `reflectance: 0.18` on the ground material puts F0 at **0.52%** where a dielectric is ~4% (client lane, found 2026-08-16) | **OPEN, and it is the coupled lighting owner's, not the ground material's.** Bevy maps reflectance to normal-incidence specular as `F0 = 0.16 × reflectance²`, so `terrain_mesh`'s 0.18 delivers **0.0052** against the 0.04 of reflectance 0.5. Roughness shapes the specular lobe and nothing else, which is the whole explanation for the roughness maps measuring as a no-op: they redistribute an eighth of the energy a real surface would put there. The constant is not in this file and never was — it is an undocumented number in `terrain_mesh::ground_material`. **Not changed by the lane that found it**, on purpose: it moves every ground pixel's specular at once on an island already carrying an 8.0% mean-luma debt from the albedo half, and `CLAUDE.md`'s trap reserves tonemap/sky/exposure/fog for one owner in one iteration. **The ordering matters and only runs one way:** raising reflectance over a shared roughness scalar makes the whole island uniformly shiny, which is the defect; raising it over the per-texel field that now exists is the fix. So this is the next thing that owner should look at, and it is unblocked. |
| minimap palette v0 — `ui/map.rs`'s ROCK (168, 152, 120) is darker than its SAND, and on the ground granite is now the BRIGHTER of the two (client lane, found 2026-08-16) | **OPEN — an operator call, because the fix departs from a measured reading and only a person looking at the map can price it.** `NOW.md` §0gp item 5 said the map palette is held against nothing and *"did not move with either edit"*; `crates/client/tests/map_palette.rs` is that connection now, and building it found what the drift actually is. Three of four identities track the ground's hue to within 2° and the map's value span (1.913×) is properly compressed under the ground's (2.239×) — the header's claims hold. Two value ORDERS disagree, and only one is innocent. **grass/litter** is convention: a map draws woodland darker than meadow, `mapraw.jpg` does it, and standing on it litter is brighter because its value absorbs granite's share of the held mean. Both are right about their own question. **sand/rock is the drift.** The 2026-08-15 re-place put granite on `ART.md` §3's own 147.0 luma, up from the ~109 it held when it was pinned to a ratio against litter — so on the ground granite passed beach sand, and `ROCK` did not follow because nothing connected them. A player reads the map, picks the brightest band as beach, walks there and stands on granite. **Not fixed by the lane that found it**: the map palette is read off `mapraw.jpg`, the one reference frame that is a map and nothing else, and departing from a measured reading needs a case — `BALANCE.md` §6 admits only a mechanism difference, and the candidate one (our granite is not their granite, and it owns 9.16% of our land) is real but its cost is whether the minimap still reads as terrain against the hillshade, which is a look and not an arithmetic. The gate pins both inversions by name, so a THIRD one is red and closing either is red. |
| armour protection v1 — theirs are burlap headwrap 15, burlap shirt 10, road sign body 20, every `move_penalty_pct` 0; ours stay 10 / 15 / 25 / 5 | **SOURCED AND BLOCKED, not proposed** (`reference/RIPLIST.md` §1h and §2 row 1j, page tier: the official wiki's **Protection** table on all three items). Two findings outlast the numbers. **(1) The reason nobody had taken them was not a reason.** `BALANCE.md` §4.1 listed the armour ladder under *real reasons to differ* — "their protection is per damage type, ours is a flat percentage, copying would mislead" — and that is false for every piece we own: their **Projectile and Melee cells are equal** on all three, and those are the only two damage classes `combat.rs` has. §4.1 is **retracted**, and that half lands here regardless, because it is a claim about their data. **(2) Our ladder is inverted** — the headwrap weaker than the shirt where theirs is the reverse — so this is an ordering error, not three magnitudes. **Why nothing moved**: the take was applied and run through the content crate. It validates, and `armor_extra_hits_max` holds with no re-speak because the set gets *weaker*; what fails is `band_breaks_refused`, which anchors on the shipped `reduction_pct = 25` to mutate it into a band break. That is one string in `crates/content/tests/content.rs`, `RIPLIST.md` §5 step 4 puts it in the same commit as the numbers, and the content lane that found it may not edit `crates/` — so the numbers were reverted and the research written down. **Best landed inside "equipment v0"** (spoken 2026-08-17), which replaces the scalar with a per-type vector and must touch the same fixture: §1h carries all five of their columns so that slice need not re-fetch. | `content/armor.toml` · `reference/RIPLIST.md` §1h · `reference/BALANCE.md` §3b/§4.1 · `crates/content/tests/content.rs` · §open "equipment v0" |

## Retired with the browser client (2026-08-06)

These knobs were defaults that shipped in `web/src`. That code is deleted, so
none of them ships anything now and none belongs in §open — a registry of
"defaults ship until spoken" cannot hold a default with no code under it. They
are kept here, not deleted, because each row is the *derivation* of a number
and several of those derivations survive their renderer: the sampling law, the
tint's luminance-neutrality argument, the wind cantilever, the lighting
register's measured bar. When the native client needs one of these, re-derive
it against Bevy and open a fresh §open row — do not copy the value across.
`ci/knob_registry.mjs` deliberately does not scan this section.

| knob | default | doc |
|---|---|---|
| materials v2 — the sampling law, and a bump that stops reading the mesh | **The world was not untextured; it was aliasing, and the ground was rendering its own triangulation.** The visual judge's pass 20260802-050932-01 returned FAIL on all ten criteria, with a blind reader naming "untextured surfaces and visible mesh" on 6/6 frames. Both halves turned out to be arithmetic, and neither was the absence of texture the words describe. **(a) The octave fades retired past Nyquist.** `FADE_MESO` was `[2.0, 7.0]` and `FADE_MICRO` `[0.3, 1.1]` metres per pixel, hand-derived per octave; against their own wavelengths those are **0.74 and 0.65 cycles per pixel**, past the 0.5 sampling limit, so each octave spent the last stretch of its life being sampled after it stopped being representable. An aliased height field has a per-pixel-random gradient and the bump divides gradients by the pixel footprint, so what reached the frame was a two-pixel speckle in the grass — measured on `04-ground-down.png` at autocorrelation 0.53 (lag 1) / 0.05 (lag 2), one hue at two luminances, 10.2 luma/px of neighbour contrast against 0.18 on the sand of the same frame. **Proposed default:** every octave retires on ONE law, the one the grain octave was already written against — `FADE_OCTAVE_CPP = [0.03, 0.09]` cycles per pixel, with the metres *derived* (`fade_m = cpp / scale`). Grain's array is now that same array. **(b) The bump's gradient was reconstructed on the triangle.** `dFdx(vGmPos)`/`dFdy(vGmPos)` span the facet's flat plane, so the gradient came back tangent to whichever facet the fragment sat on, and adjacent facets of a smooth-shaded heightfield are tilted against each other while the shading normal is not — a seam of (facet tilt) × (bump slope) at every edge. Measured as a ~5-level ramp on a ~58 px period with a hard reset at each edge. Rebuilding the basis on the geometric normal is the textbook correction and is **not** the fix (0.422° → 0.325°, `ci/bump_basis.mjs`). **Proposed default:** solve the gradient in world XZ from the 2×2 screen system and add the two heightfields — exactly continuous at 8.5e-7°, mesh-independent by construction. The same lines also mixed spaces (world derivatives crossed with three's view-space `normal`), which rotated the bump as the camera turned; the perturbation now happens in world via `vGmNorm` and returns through `mat3(viewMatrix)`. **(c) `BUMP_MAX_SLOPE = 0.55`** — proposed default, the cap on the reconstructed gradient (wall 4: a screen derivative over a screen footprint has no upper bound of its own). Derived as the sum of what the octaves that actually reach `gmH` ask for, on the identity that asks most (rock, `bump` 2.2), in the file's own `amp × bump / wavelength` convention — the one the grain block states its 0.07–0.29 range in: meso `0.55 × 2.2 / 9.5` = 0.127, micro `0.09 × 2.2 / 1.7` = 0.117, grain `0.008 × 2.2 / 0.060` = 0.294, summing to 0.538 → **0.55**. Three terms and not four: `gmH` is meso + micro + grain, and the macro octave drives the splat weights only, never the height. So every surface this material is *designed* to make passes the cap untouched, and the cap holds only what arithmetic cannot justify. **The first cut of this row said 1.0**, summing four octaves at the top of the documented 0.03–0.25 per-octave band, and it fell inside the same pass: 1.0 is a 45° perturbation, which is a licence and not a bound. With (b)'s reconstruction fixed the bump reached the frame at full strength for the first time — the retired space-mixed formula had been silently attenuating it, which is what the octave amplitudes were unknowingly calibrated against — and 45° against a key light 21° above the horizon turns most of the ground away from it. `browser_smoke`'s shadow probe named it immediately: 0.131% of the frame moved when the shadow map was toggled, against a 15% floor. **Gate:** the 0.55 shipped while this row still read 1.0 for one pass, with nine gates green over the disagreement and only the merge-gate judge's reading to catch it, so `ci/knob_registry.mjs` now pins every knob this section declares — a name and its value inside one backtick span, the form this clause and the two below it already use — against the constant that actually ships — `BUMP_MAX_SLOPE`, `FADE_OCTAVE_CPP` and `MAX_BACKPACKS` today, Rust and JS alike. **(d) Re-placing the meso octave (`SCALE_MESO` 1/9.5 → 1/4.0 m with `AMP_MESO_M` re-derived 0.55 → 0.23) was tried and BACKED OUT, and the reason to try it stands. With every octave now retiring on the law above, the coarsest one that survives mid distance sets the texture a player sees, and at 9.5 m it completes a third of a cycle inside a typical 8 m ground framing — a gradient across the whole frame, which is the "single flat hue" the acceptance names. What stopped it is a coupling, not the arithmetic: the splat break-up wobble is driven by gmMacro and gmMeso, so moving meso moves which identity owns a face, and the grain octave takes its scale, contrast and ridge as dot products against those same weights. `browser_smoke` 15c — the grain projection on the 46.6° face this spawn offers — went red on the changed mix (gain ×0.864 against its ×1.15 floor). Re-placing meso belongs with the albedo work on `NOW.md` item 1, with 15c's face re-measured alongside it rather than a constant changed underneath it. **Gates:** new `ci/bump_basis.mjs` (13 checks, headless, no GPU — evaluates all three formulations on both sides of one triangle edge of a synthetic heightfield quad, asserts the retired one still reproduces the seam, asserts the geometric-normal correction does *not* cure it, asserts the shipped solve is continuous to floating point and invariant to pixel footprint, and pins the JS mirror to the shipped shader source with comments stripped); `browser_smoke` 15a2 asserts the sampling law over the whole octave table (every faded octave retires ≤ Nyquist, the derived metres match the cpp, an unfaded octave may only go unfaded if it cannot alias before the faded ones are gone, and the bump cap exists). Grain's own fade ceiling **tightened** 1.0 → 0.5 cycles per pixel: aliasing starts at half a cycle, not one, and the two structural octaves demonstrated what the slack buys. |
| materials v3 — the tint: the ground gets a hue, and the gate that can see one | **The world was one colour per identity at forty brightnesses, and no gate in this repo could tell that from forty colours.** Read the channels materials v0–v2 shipped: `MOTTLE` multiplies albedo, `ROUGH_VAR` offsets roughness, the grain's swing multiplies albedo again. A multiply by a scalar cannot move a hue — `k·(r,g,b)` has exactly the chromaticity of `(r,g,b)` — so every square metre of grass in this world was one green, of sand one tan, and the visual judge's blind reader named it on five of six frames ("a single untextured green sheet", "hue-only", `findings/pass-20260802-050932-01-visual.md` ranked gap 1). materials v2 fixed the two arithmetic defects underneath that sentence; this is the sentence. **The octave:** one noise sample at a per-identity tile scale — sand 0.59 m, rock 0.71 m, grass 0.91 m, litter 1.00 m, all inside the report's own "~0.5–1 m repeat" and all between the micro octave (1.7 m) and the coarsest grain (12 cm), which is the hole the ladder actually had — driving a signed chromatic DEVIATION per identity, added rather than lerped to, so `color` stays the identity's exact mean and what this changes is the ground's variance. Proposed defaults, per identity `dev` in linear working space: sand [0.07, −0.011, −0.10] (warm damp gold ↔ cool bleached quartz), grass [0.12, −0.03, −0.06] (straw-olive ↔ lush blue-green), litter [0.09, −0.019, −0.081] (needle duff ↔ deep moss), rock [0.08, −0.016, −0.08] (buff feldspar ↔ blue-grey biotite). **`TINT_GAIN = 2.5`**, proposed default: `(g − 0.5) × 2` maps the field's NOMINAL [0,1] onto the deviation, and a value-noise field does not visit its nominal range — measured over 160,000 samples of this exact `gmNoise`, its deviation about the midpoint is 0.2262, so at a gain of 2 the mean |tint| is 0.379 and the authored pole is reached on 0.00% of the ground. At 2.5 the mean is 0.468 and 6.2% saturates, so the poles are real ground; 3.0 saturates 15.6% and starts flattening the tails. **`TINT_ROUGH = 0.06`**, proposed default, on the file's law that a modifier drives colour and roughness together: ±0.06 against the micro octave's ±0.09, the same band, neither dominating — and the one place this octave touches brightness, causally, through the light response. **`TINT_LUMA_NEUTRAL = 0.02`** — a design law, not a tolerance, and the pass earned it. Every deviation's Rec.709 luminance is zero to within 0.25% of its own length, so the swing is pure hue. Two earlier cuts swung value as well, and the value half cost the material a gate it already held: assertion 15 requires the field to lighten AND darken on every probed yaw, and at this spawn's yaw 0 the field already darkens 95% of what it touches (the bump against a 21° sun, plus the macro octave's ±0.16 multiply, itself a cast at any framing narrower than 48 m). `main` cleared the 0.2% floor there with 0.5%; a value-swinging tint took it to 0.14%. Neutrality is not a way around that, it is the division of labour: three scalar octaves and a per-identity grain already move VALUE at four scales, nothing moved HUE, and the defect the judge named is that sentence exactly. With it, the probe's own six-luma mask catches 0.000% of either tint frame and the ground still varies in both, because the material is the sum. **Deleted, twice, with the measurements kept:** the report also asked for "a low-frequency macro-variation octave to break tiling", and this pass built that term off macro (48 m) and then off meso (9.5 m). Both read as a colour CAST rather than a variation, because an octave wider than the frame is a constant inside it — macro: +0.10% of a yaw lightened against −19.80% darkened; meso: on a standing view the chromaticity centre moved 0.061 while the spread gained 0.009. The premise came with the words and was never checked: **there is no tiling here to break**, because this octave is value noise on world XZ and does not repeat. Distance is the splat's job (macro drives `gmWob`, so a far hillside changes IDENTITY, not one identity tinted) and the lighting owner's aerial perspective. **Cost, counted, not timed** (`NOW.md` item 3's instruction): noise sample sites 6 → **7 of 8**, program 81,820 → 83,901 chars of 96,000, depth fetches unchanged at 18 of 24. The budget was NOT widened to fit — the octave spends the headroom that was being held, one site is left, and an octave that wants triplanar taps now has nowhere to put them. **Gates:** `browser_smoke` 15d, and a second measurement track in `contrastProbe` to carry it — the luma track every probe in this file used is structurally blind to this octave, so the probe now also masks on |Δchromaticity| > 0.004 (one 8-bit code step on a mid-grey pixel) and reports the cloud's spread per state, its centre's movement, the signed split on the red-chromaticity axis, and the whole frame's mean luma. 15d asserts, structurally: `uTint` ships at 1, four distinct tile scales inside the ladder, the fade rises and retires at or before Nyquist (15a2's law), the gain is above 2, every deviation is off-parallel to its own colour (measured −0.036/0.152/−0.118/−0.047 against a 0.5 ceiling — a parallel one is a brightness multiply wearing a texture's name), three distinct hue axes, and luminance neutrality. And in pixels, at two views: chromatically moved 76.4% near / 26.2% level (floors 0.30/0.10), warm and cool both present (27.6/48.8 and 12.6/13.6, floor 0.05 each), chroma spread ×1.390 and ×1.385 (floor 1.2 — a scalar octave scores exactly 1.00), the centre's movement under half the cloud's own width (0.29 and 0.12; a cast scores ~0.95 by the same arithmetic), and mean luma within one step (77.43 → 77.17, 67.35 → 67.36). The centre ceiling is measured against the cloud's WIDTH and not against the width it GAINED, and that is deliberate: chromaticity is a nonlinear coordinate on tone-mapped output, so a strictly zero-mean swing still moves the measured centre by a Jensen term, and the gained form scored 1.03 on ground that is demonstrably not a cast — it was scoring the curve. **Both failure controls exercised and reverted**: compiling `gmTile` to a constant (a cast of this octave's own typical magnitude) fires three of 15d's measures at once — spread ratio ×0.840, cool side 0.00%, centre share 0.966 — and re-authoring all four deviations as a fixed fraction of their own colour (a brightness multiply wearing a texture's name) scores cos 1.0000 on every identity and fires the structural half. | `web/src/materials.js` · `web/src/scene.js` · `ci/browser_smoke.mjs` · `findings/pass-20260802-050932-01-visual.md` · `NOW.md` item 1 |
| the quad-constant gradient — the ground's dither, measured and bisected (instrument only; the fix is blocked) | **The ground renders a 2x2 mosaic, it is the bump's gradient solve, and inside that it is the grain octave alone.** The visual judge's pass 20260802-163821-01 returned FAIL on all ten criteria with a blind reader passing 0 of our 6 frames as real; its ranked gap 1 called the ground "either a flat colour wash or a per-pixel dither" and its ranked fix 1 put the cause on the splat break-up wobble using the meso octave unfaded. Measured on its own frames, the artefact is **quad-locked**: `05-held-level.png` x 1000-1270 y 560-700 carries 1.9 luma/px of neighbour contrast WITHIN each 2x2 quad against 21.4 ACROSS quad boundaries, and `04-ground-down.png` 0.35 against 4.91. The wobble reaches albedo per fragment and cannot produce a quad-locked signal at all, so that was the symptom's name and not its cause (`CLAUDE.md`'s own trap: a judge names the symptom, fix the cause). **The bisection**, by a new probe (`scene.aliasProbe`) driving live uniforms: with `uOct` and `uGrainAmp.x` zeroed — gmH identically zero, every albedo term untouched — the ratio falls **6.15 -> 1.01** at the down vantage and **3.12 -> 1.00** at the level one; zeroing **grain's bump alone** does the same (1.03 / 1.02). All of it comes through the bump's gradient solve, and grain's share of that is all of it. **Why.** `dFdx`/`dFdy` are differences across the rasterizer's 2x2 quad, so the reconstructed gradient is constant per quad by construction and can only change at a boundary. Over the 2 px between quad centres a wave of wavelength L advances `4*PI*cpp` radians of its own phase, so successive quads' gradients differ by `2*sin(2*PI*cpp)*sinc(cpp)` of the gradient's amplitude — 26% at 0.021 cycles per pixel, **106% at 0.09**, more than the whole of itself. Every octave is faded on `FADE_OCTAVE_CPP = [0.03, 0.09]`, a law written for whether a VALUE resolves, and a value that resolves is not a gradient that resolves. It bites grain and not meso or micro because of where each band falls: grain's runs 33 to 11 px per cycle, which for a 12 cm grass tuft is 1-4 m from the eye — most of the ground a player looks at — while meso's equivalent is 165 m out and micro's 30 m. **The fix, and why it is NOT in this commit.** A second, stricter retirement band for gmH (proposed `BUMP_QUAD_PHASE_MAX_DEG`, 15 degrees, with cycles per pixel derived as `phase / 4PI` and metres from those) was built and measured: it takes both vantages to **x1.01 with within-quad detail unchanged** (0.52 -> 0.53, 0.64 -> 0.65) — the mosaic goes and the material does not. It then reddens `browser_smoke` assertion 15, and correctly: at yaws 0 and 4.71 the surface probe finds **21% and 24% of the frame moved and not one pixel brighter**, because the only thing the field brightened there WAS the mosaic. Those two yaws are a macro-octave cast plus this artefact and nothing else, so assertion 15's two-sidedness has been passing on the defect. Three candidate unblocks were built and measured and none is sufficient: a steeper bump fade (same 15° end, 2/3 of the band at full amplitude) leaves yaw 0 at +0.00%; centring grain's albedo swing — which is genuinely one-sided, `gmGrain` runs [0, 2x fade] with mean `+fade x contrast`, so it can only brighten — makes it worse (+0.00%, and 41% moved), because removing a positive bias can only reduce the up count; and fading the macro octave in on the DUAL of the sampling law (cycles per FRAME, so an octave too coarse to vary inside the frame stops being a cast) recovers yaw 4.71 to +0.17% against a 0.2% floor and leaves yaw 0 at +0.01%, whether it is applied to the albedo multiply alone or to the splat wobble as well. The blocker is what `NOW.md` item 1 already names as its top remaining want, and it wants the coarse-octave slice, not a constant changed underneath it. **Also tried and NOT shipped, with the measurement kept:** the textbook cause of a noisy screen derivative is float32 cancellation — `vGmPos.xz` is a world coordinate in the high hundreds, so a varying interpolated at float32 reaches the fragment quantized to ~1.2e-4 m while the near-field pixel it is differenced over is ~2e-3 m wide, a 6% staircase on `dFdx`. A camera-relative varying for the Jacobian alone (exact by Sterbenz, ~500x the resolution for the same slot) moved the ratio **6.16 -> 6.15**. Not the cause here, and it costs a varying, so it was removed. **Left open, and it is arithmetic:** the file states per-octave bump slope as `amp x bump / wavelength`, and a sinusoid's peak slope is `2*PI` times that, so the materials v2 row above understates all three octaves by 6.3x (true peak slopes ~0.80 / ~0.74 / ~1.84 on rock against the 0.127 / 0.117 / 0.294 recorded) and `BUMP_MAX_SLOPE = 0.55` is not the sum it is documented as — it is a bound the octaves exceed, and it clips. The cap is unchanged here and so are the amplitudes; re-deriving them in the right convention changes how much relief the ground has and wants the visual judge on it. **What SHIPS here:** `scene.aliasProbe` (four states per view off existing uniforms — ship, `nobump`, `nograinbump`, `flat` — a ground mask from the field's own footprint, and a control render that must differ nowhere) and `browser_smoke` 15e, which asserts the mask is real, the control is clean, and **both** the `nobump` and `nograinbump` legs stay under **x1.35** — the ceiling the ship leg will be held to when the law lands, calibrated now against measured floors of x1.00 and x1.02 so the wall is ready the day it can be armed. The ship leg is printed as `KNOWN DEFECT, unwalled` with its own bisection verdict, because a ceiling set where the defect fits is worse than no ceiling. `ci/bump_basis.mjs` gains 8 checks that BUILD the reconstruction — real quad corners, the one-pixel difference each quad actually gets, swept over every grid alignment — pin it to `2*sin(2*PI*cpp)*sinc(cpp)` to 1e-4 at four footprints, and assert that at the albedo band the jump is >= 100% of amplitude (the defect, reproduced as arithmetic, on any box, with no GPU) while a 15° phase bound holds it <= 30%. |
| prop albedo v1 — the band a surface has to be delivered into, and the gate that has a unit in it | **The ratios were all green and the surface was invisible; this is the number with a unit.** Prop surfaces v0 gave seven classes a field and three assertions, and every one of the three is scale-free by construction: `contrastRatio` is `(baseline+added)/baseline`, `diffStructure` is step over magnitude of the difference image, `chromaRatio` is spread over spread. A field swinging **±0.8 of a level on a surface delivering luma 6** scores *exactly* what the same field swinging **±17 levels on a surface delivering 120** scores. v0 shipped through all three (structure 0.050 and 0.041 against a 0.02 floor) and the visual judge, measuring the merged frames, found "a solid" — best-fit-plane residual 1.23/255 over 7,800 px — and named the amplitude, not the absence, as the defect. Both readings were correct; the gate simply had no number with a unit in it. **The instrument**: `propProbe` now returns the delivered value the class occupies as a histogram over its own mask — p05/p50/p95 in 8-bit luma — beside `diffMean`, the field's own amplitude in those same levels. Percentiles and not a mean, because the question is a RANGE: the visual rubric's criterion 2 fails on "the same rock asset reads L=78 in one frame and L=10 in another, so stone has no recognisable value range to identify it by". **The law** (`web/src/materials.js`): `ALBEDO_LUMA_BAND = [0.05, 0.55]`, the linear luminance every authored dielectric albedo must sit in, both ends of every ramp. The floor is the bottom of the band real outdoor materials occupy — dry conifer bark measures 8–12% visible reflectance and conifer needles 6–15%, and nothing in a forest is charcoal; the ceiling is its top (fresh gypsum sand, white paint ~0.55; snow is out of scope until there is a snow biome). Two of the scatter table's nine authored bands were under the floor: **pine trunk 0.0265→0.0570 (×1.887) and pine skirt 0.0453→0.1344 (×1.106)**, shipping as published band ends trunk 0.050→0.108 and skirt 0.051→0.147 — the skirt's lo hex is one green level above the exact rescale because 8-bit quantization put the arithmetic's own answer at 0.0495, and the gate scores the HEX. Corrected by scaling each part in LINEAR, both ends of its ramp together, so hue and ramp shape are preserved exactly — a scalar multiply leaves chromaticity where it found it, `TINT_LUMA_NEUTRAL`'s own arithmetic used in the other direction. Everything else measured in band and did not move (crown 0.084→0.196, bush 0.089→0.200, rock node 0.290, boulder 0.169, ore 0.218 and 0.418, barrel 0.143). **The gate** (`browser_smoke` 15g): a structural half over all SEVEN archetypes, asserting every authored band end inside `ALBEDO_LUMA_BAND` with the luminance derived through the renderer's own sRGB conversion in `albedoLuma()` rather than restated beside the hex; and a pixel half over the two views the probe can reliably frame, `PROP_MIN_VALUE = 24` on the delivered median and `PROP_MIN_AMP = 2.2` on the field's amplitude. Shipped measurements at this gate's own dev spawn (1024,1024), value p05/p50/p95 and amplitude in 8-bit luma: **rock framed at 3.7 m, 29/59/123 amp 8.47; pine framed at 3.5 m, 12/48/74 amp 4.86** (before the rescale: 27/59/123 amp 8.43 and 11/46/74 amp 4.72). Neither floor has a `BROWSER_SMOKE_*` env override, unlike the five prop floors above them, and that is the direction this should travel — an environment variable that can lower a wall is a way to weaken a gate without editing a file anyone reviews, and it is also what stops the registry pinning it. Both floors are calibrated against the measured pair and not modelled — that is `PROP_MIN_STRUCTURE`'s own discipline and its recorded lesson, a model wrong by 8× — sitting at roughly half the worse of the two, an infinite distance above what the failure they guard reaches. **`p05` is deliberately NOT walled, and this is the half that is not mine.** The dark tail of a prop's mask is its shaded side, and the shaded side is the light rig's. Measured, and reproduced to within one level of 255 from the constants alone: a down-facing prop face receives only `FILL_GROUND × FILL_INTENSITY`, so it is delivered at `groundColor × 1.15 × albedo ÷ π × EXPOSURE`, which for the pine skirt's albedo is **RGB (2,6,1) — against the visual judge's measured (2,6,0)** on `03-canopy-up`. The albedo correction above moves that to (3,7,1). **Albedo cannot fix it and no amount of material work can**: at 3× the authored albedo the canopy underside still reads (5,17,3), because there is no light on it. The whole indirect budget is one `HemisphereLight` at 1.15, and the fix is the coupled sky/sun/exposure/fog edit `CLAUDE.md` reserves for one owner in one iteration — this row is the arithmetic that owner inherits, so the next pass on it starts from a solved cause instead of a symptom. **Also cleaned** (merge-gate judge's notes on the previous pass, all three stale prose against shipped code, no behaviour change): the `PROP_BUMP_FADE_CPP` comment still described the abandoned half-band the gate now goes red on; the boulder contrast ratio was quoted as x1.91 in `scene.js` and `browser_smoke.mjs` where every record of record says x1.62; and the prop probe's summary line reported the spawn-to-instance search distance while reading as the framing distance — it now publishes and prints both. **Not in this row**: the light rig (above), needle cards, a second species, the prop shader's unwritten program-size and noise-site budget, and rendered pixel coverage for the five classes the probe cannot reliably find near the pinned spawn. |
| ground base maps v0 (ART.md §7's CC0 working set, wired) | **The renderer had never had real detail to sample, and the number said so.** `ART.md` §3 measures the reference set's near-ground neighbour contrast at **6.3 luma per pixel** and ours at **0.26**, and eight passes of procedural octaves did not move the second number — a noise field cannot encode what a camera measured. Operator, 2026-08-03: *"if its CC0 im fine to pull in whatever helps us. then we can replace later"*. **What landed.** The four ground identities' albedo / normal / roughness from `assets/textures/` (Poly Haven, CC0, manifested), sampled in the splat shader at each identity's own declared tile — `BASE_SCALE` is read off `IDENTITIES[i].tint.scale` (0.59-1.00 m) rather than restated, so the two cannot drift. **Hybrid, not replacement** (§7): every existing layer stays as the variation layer — the splat blend and its wobble, the tint octave, the grain octave, the three structural octaves, wetness, snow, cliff — and the base lands under all of them, after the identity is decided and before anything modifies it. **The mean is preserved and that is the whole policy as arithmetic.** Each albedo layer's mean is measured at load in LINEAR light (`textures.js`, 256-entry sRGB LUT, every texel, not a subsample) and delivered through a per-channel gain `IDENTITIES[i].color / mean`. So the photograph contributes its VARIANCE and the palette keeps its MEAN — every assertion in the tree about an identity's authored colour survives a source swap, and §7's *"an off-band source is tinted, not tolerated"* is enforced by construction rather than by discipline. `MANIFEST.md` predicted this for `rock` (cliff_side, "hue ~25 deg and far more saturated than granite") and the gate now prints the prediction coming true: gain spans sand x1.38, grass x1.64, litter x3.82, **rock x5.72**. The file on disk is never edited; replacing one is still a file swap. Roughness gets the same treatment against `IDENTITIES[i].roughness`. **`BASE_NORMAL_MAX_SLOPE = 0.25`** — the most a photographed normal may tilt the shading normal, as a surface slope, bounding `|n.xy| / n.z` before it is added to gmH's gradient (wall 4: that tap diverges as a tangent normal approaches horizontal). `BUMP_MAX_SLOPE` then bounds the SUM, so the two are nested and not additive. Not a fresh judgement: 0.03-0.25 is the band `AMP_MESO_M`, `AMP_MICRO_M` and `AMP_GRAIN_M` were each chosen against, stated three times in `materials.js` as where a normal reads without turning into a relief map, and the base is now the finest and steepest thing in that ladder. **The base retires on `FADE_OCTAVE_CPP`, the same law as every octave**, and the argument is stronger here than for any of them: an octave retires because a reconstructed field cannot be filtered, but a texture's mip chain filters correctly at any footprint — what a texture has instead is a limit, its own mean, and the gain makes that mean exactly the authored colour the flat path already delivers. Past the fade the three fetches return an answer the shader has written down, so `mix(a, b, 0.0)` is `a` and the skip is exact rather than approximate. It is also the cost decision, stated as one: twelve fetches with anisotropic filtering is the most expensive thing in the ground program, and on this box it was the difference between a second browser tab reaching the world and not (240 s / never, against 61 s / green). **`BASE_ANISOTROPY_MAX = 4`**, and the shipped value is `min(this, device max)`. `getMaxAnisotropy()` is a capability and not a recommendation — every renderer this client has run on answers 16 — and taking it whole was the first cut. It is the wrong read twice: anisotropic filtering on a ground texture saturates by 4-8, past which the extra taps resolve detail the mip chain has already averaged, while the cost is linear in taps and this ground takes up to twelve filtered fetches per fragment. 16 x 12 is 192 texel samples on a fragment, which the reference VPS would not notice and a software rasterizer does: at 16, a second browser tab did not reach the world at all on this box. Proposed default, not spoken. **Counted, not timed** (`NOW.md` item 9's six wrong-signed cost runs stand): one `sampler2DArray` per map, so **3 texture units** against the WebGL2 guaranteed minimum of 16 — the terrain program already binds five shadow maps and twelve `sampler2D` would not have fit — and **<=12 fetches per fragment** as a ceiling, with an EXACT `gmW > 0.0` skip (a weight is exactly zero where the identity is absent, and `0.0 * finite` is `0.0`) taking a single-biome fragment to three. Payload 3.1 MB of §7's 12 MB. **Gated at 15h, and it is the first assertion in `browser_smoke` whose sharp number is an ABSOLUTE** — every other material gate there is a ratio, which is exactly what 15g was written to say cannot see amplitude. Measured on the shipped frame in `ART.md` §3's own units: **5.90 luma/px at the level vantage and 8.61 at the near-ground one, against 0.42-0.47 from the octaves alone at the same two framings** (floor 4.0, and §3's 6.3 target printed beside it on every run so the gap cannot quietly stop closing). Plus the structural half: layer order is identity order, the tile is the identity's own, anisotropy was asked for, every layer's texel sd is non-zero, and `uBase` ships at 1. **Two consequences elsewhere, both honest.** (1) `contrastProbe` and `projectionProbe` now hold `uBase` at 0 across ALL their legs. Their outputs are ratios, so anything common to both states sits in the denominator, and a photograph delivering ~10.7 luma/px puts grain's own +-0.2 albedo swing at x1.02 — not because grain stopped working but because the question stops being answerable. Held off, 15b scores **x8.65** against its unchanged x2.0 floor and 15d **x1.43/x1.44** against its unchanged x1.1; the absolute the base delivers is 15h's job, which is 15g's lesson applied to the ground before it had to be learned twice. The per-program half of that was caught by 15c's own flat control going red at 65.8% when only the shipped material's uniform was zeroed. (2) **15e's ship leg is a wall now**, at the unchanged x1.35: the quad-locked mosaic reads **x1.00** at both vantages against x3.12/x6.15.
