Nobody fixed it — a `textureGrad` fetch is evaluated per fragment, so its detail varies inside a quad as much as across one, and two orders of magnitude of not-quad-locked energy diluted it. The defect is still in the shader and the sampling law is still the actual fix; the wall only stops the dilution disappearing silently. |
| base chroma stretch bound (the artifact 15h could not see) | **The pass that landed the base maps cleared every amplitude gate by an order of magnitude and shipped per-pixel rainbow speckle across four of six captured frames.** Visual judge, 20260803-145507-01, ranked gap 1: *"a live render artifact in four of six frames and it is a sampling bug, not an art task"*, and its blind reader named it unprompted on four frames as *"rainbow per-pixel moire — no mipmapping/aniso, a shipped title would never expose this"*. **The mechanism is arithmetic, and it is not the one the report ranked first.** The gain that places a source's mean is `IDENTITIES[i].color / measured mean`, PER CHANNEL, and it multiplies the whole sample — so it scales the photograph's DEVIATION by the same uneven per-channel factors. Measured over the four shipped files: sand gain [3.43 4.09 4.73] span x1.38, grass [1.20 1.97 1.94] x1.64, litter [1.08 3.33 4.12] x3.82, **rock [2.35 5.24 13.45] x5.72**. `rock` is the sandstone `MANIFEST.md` marks as too warm for granite; its blue mean is 0.034 in linear light, near the source's own JPEG chroma floor, so the x13.45 that corrects its mean amplifies that noise thirteenfold. **The measurement that separates detail from noise is DIRECTION, not amplitude**, which is why 15h could clear its floor and the frame could still be broken: resolve the near ground's high-frequency residual along the local mean colour (a surface lighter here and darker there — what 15h counts) versus orthogonal to it (the hue changed between neighbouring pixels). Over the near-ground band of the thirteen `Rust Images/` frames that actually contain ground — the four UI screenshots and the top-down map render cannot define a statistic about ground, and two of them are the highest readings in the unrestricted set — the references run **0.077-0.193, median 0.120**; over the six judged frames ours ran **01 0.659 · 02 0.798 · 03 0.237 · 04 0.284 · 05 0.760 · 06 0.092** — every frame showing ground is over the reference maximum, and `06-hud.png`, the only frame with no near ground in it, is the only one inside the band. Our along-colour term was inside the reference range the whole time, so **the defect was never amplitude and neither extra blur nor more anisotropy addresses it** — both would have cost the detail 15h asserts. `BASE_ANISOTROPY_MAX` is therefore untouched at 4, against the report's second suspect. **`BASE_CHROMA_STRETCH_MAX = 1.0`** — the most a per-channel palette gain may stretch a source's own colour deviation. 1.0 is the identity: the chroma variation delivered at the scale the camera measured it. Above 1 the correction is amplifying colour noise rather than placing a mean; below 1 it is discarding measured colour the references demonstrably carry (their band's floor is 0.068, not 0). Applied per layer as `min(1, MAX / span)` off each source's OWN measured span — sand 0.72, grass 0.61, litter 0.26, rock 0.17 — so nothing here is per-identity taste and a better-matched source file raises its own keep with no edit to code or `content/`. This is `ART.md` §7's *"an off-band source is tinted, not tolerated"* applied one derivative up, and §7 now carries both halves. Proposed default, not spoken. **Mean preservation is now a property of the shape rather than of the tuning.** The tap delivers `color * mix(vec3(k), rel, keep)` where `rel` is the sample over its layer's measured mean (mean exactly 1 per channel) and `k` is that field through the layer's own luma weights (mean exactly 1 too) — both ends average to 1, so the delivered mean is `IDENTITIES[i].color` at ANY keep, and at keep = 1 it is algebraically the `sample * (color / mean)` the previous pass shipped. It is also non-negative by construction, which the subtractive form of the same correction is not, so no clamp and no hue shift in the darks from one. **Counted, not timed:** no new fetch, no new texture unit, three uniforms swapped for four. **The luma-only floor is printed too, because it is the next pass's number, not this one's:** with every keep at 0 the frame still reads **0.186 / 0.174**, above the reference median of 0.120 — so a majority of what is left after this bound belongs to terms that are not the photograph (the tint octave's deliberate off-colour deviation, which 15d asserts at x1.43, plus the sky dither and the fog), and tightening this knob further cannot reach the references on its own. Per `CLAUDE.md`'s coupled-lighting law that set has one owner and it is not this pass. **What it cost, measured:** 15h reads **5.90 / 8.61 luma/px** against 5.91 / 8.58 before it — the along-colour term is 0.0219 / 0.0327 against 0.0223 / 0.0331 unbounded, i.e. the detail is untouched, and only chroma moves (0.0069 / 0.0080 against 0.0097 / 0.0104). **Gated at 15i**, the second assertion in `browser_smoke` whose sharp number is taken off the reference images rather than off our own frames, and the first that is a CEILING: ship 0.317 (level) and 0.243 (down), moved from 0.434 and 0.313, against a **wall of 0.35** — which is deliberately NOT the reference target of 0.193 max / 0.120 median, for 15h's own stated reason applied to a ceiling instead of a floor: a bar set where the tree has never been is a gate that fails on merge day and gets widened on the next one, which is the same weakening taken slowly. **0.317 is still 1.6x over the reference maximum and this row says so**; the wall is two-sided (1.10x above the worse shipped reading, 1.24x below the 0.434 the replaced per-channel gain scores at the same vantage, so a regression goes red) and the target is printed beside it on every run. The first cut of this gate got the ceiling wrong in the flattering direction by measuring the references with a 2x2-box residual while the probe used a 4-neighbour-mean one — 0.336 instead of 0.193 — which would have walled our 0.317 in as a pass. A ceiling from a differently-computed statistic is not a ceiling, and §7 now says so. Three more halves so the gate cannot pass by matching nothing: the keep is asserted to still be DERIVED from its span (a keep that stopped tracking its source is a knob that has become a taste setting), the `stretch` leg is rendered live every run and must sit **x1.15** above ship (floor midway between the measured x1.37 / x1.29 and the x1.00 that deleting the base maps would score — a flat swatch has no chroma residual and would sail under any ceiling), and `uBase` plus all four keeps are re-read LIVE after the probe rather than from a snapshot taken before it. |
| materials v4 — the projection the base maps never got, and the fade law nobody propagated | **Every steep face in the world was rendering an artifact, and it had two causes with one shape: a horizontal answer to a question about a surface.** Operator, 2026-08-03, with three frames of cliffs and a snow dome: *"lets fix these odd visual errors please"*. **(1) The base maps ship on world XZ**, which is a projection from above: on a face of upness `u` it stretches the photograph by `1/u` along the fall line and leaves it alone across it. That is materials v1's own finding about the grain octave, applied to the octave that landed after it — at the finest tile in the ladder, carrying 1K photographic content rather than a noise field. At this seed's steepest faces (upness 0.357, a 69.1 deg slope, measured over the whole island) the stretch is **x2.8**, and it is unbounded as a face goes vertical. **(2) Every octave in the file retired on the HORIZONTAL footprint.** `TERRAIN.md` has stated the law since materials v1 — *"its fade reads the world footprint, not the horizontal one, because a pixel on a steep face barely moves in XZ while covering metres of surface"* — written about grain, applied to grain, and never propagated to the three structural octaves grain was derived from, nor to the tint, nor to the base maps. Four consumers of one law and one obeying it. On that same 69 deg face the XZ footprint is 1/2.8 of the real one, so every other octave stayed at full strength roughly three times past its own retirement; a reconstructed field cannot be filtered, which is this file's own argument for why it fades at all, so what reached the image past Nyquist was its alias. **(3) Snow REPLACED the albedo.** `gmWet` and `gmCliff` multiply, so the photograph survives them; `mix(albedo, SNOW_COLOR, 1)` is a constant, and above 80 m that is whole hillsides — a fifth of the island reverted to the octaves alone (0.42-0.47 luma/px against the base's 5.90-8.61) with no gate red, because 15h and ART.md rule 1 are both measured on ground the snow does not reach. **What landed.** The base is **biplanar**: the existing top tap plus one wall tap on the vertical plane containing the face's own FALL LINE. Not an axis plane and not triplanar, and both halves are load-bearing. The fall-line plane's stretch is `1/sin(tilt)` where the top plane's is `1/cos(tilt)` — exact complements, so between the two the worst case anywhere in the world is 45 deg at x1.41 where they average, and a third tap would buy nothing. It also turns the plane CHOICE into a rotation instead of a swap: the axis-aligned pair flips wherever `|n.x| = |n.z|`, which is a hard line of changed photograph down every diagonal cliff, and this frame rotates through that locus continuously. Its footprint is the world-position derivative PROJECTED onto the frame, `(dot(dFdx(p).xz, across), dFdx(p).y)`, and **the first cut of this row got that backwards and shipped it** — it differentiated the finished UV, `dFdx(gmWallUV)`, and claimed the frame's rotation was carried "for free". It is not free, it is an error of about eighty times. `gmAcross` is per-fragment, so the product rule gives `dot(dFdx(p.xz), across) + dot(p.xz, dFdx(across))`, and the second term is the frame turning rather than any footprint — multiplied by a WORLD coordinate, ~1568 at this spawn. A frame rotation of 1e-4 rad/px injects 0.16 m/px against a true footprint of ~0.002, so `textureGrad` selected a mip roughly seven levels too coarse in bands following the terrain's curvature. Quilez states the rule for the axis-aligned case — take the gradients of `p` before the projection is chosen and pass the components — and holding a rotating frame fixed is the same rule. Measured on the fix: the near-cliff vantage's neighbour contrast went **7.42 -> 14.58 luma/px** and the far cliff's **2.88 -> 4.35**, because half the photograph was being averaged away by the wrong mip; every vantage at or under 45 deg is bit-identical, since the wall tap does not run there. **`BASE_WALL_SHARPNESS = 8.0`** — the exponent the two plane weights are raised to before blending, and it is Quilez's own stated value rather than a tuning: a triplanar blend is `pow(abs(n), k)`, his biplanar variant folds the sharpening into a local-support remap, and where that remap does not apply he prescribes the other shape directly (*"only use high values of k... with k = 8, we get (1/sqrt(3))^8 = 1.2%, small enough that in practice it will be totally unnoticeable"*). The remap does not apply here and that is a property of the projection: its job is to kill the discontinuity where two candidate planes SWAP identity, and the fall-line frame rotates through that locus instead of swapping. **The first cut shipped no exponent at all**, which is the second half of the same defect: cos and sin are the two planes' foreshortenings, so a LINEAR blend at this seed's steepest face (69.5 deg) hands **32.3%** of the sample to the top plane while that plane is stretched **x2.86** — a third of the photograph arriving smeared, which is the streak. At k = 8 that share is 0.05%, and 60 deg goes 51.6% -> 2.2%, while 45 deg and below are untouched because the gate below still owns them. Proposed default, not spoken. **`BASE_WALL_ON = 0.70711`** — where the wall tap turns on, as `sin(tilt)`, and it is derived rather than chosen: `sin = cos` at exactly 45 deg, so below it the projection the ground already had is the less distorted of the two and above it the wall is. The rule is *"add the second tap only where the one you have stops being the better one"*, and the number falls out of the geometry. Independently it is where this file's own accepted residual lands — materials v1 measured world-XZ coarsening the grain x2.02 on a 46.6 deg face and shipped a triplanar leaving x1.40 behind; a 45 deg face is x1.41. Proposed default, not spoken, and written as a literal rather than `Math.SQRT1_2` so the registry can pin it. **The relief stays the top tap's alone**, so the wall costs two fetched maps and not three: `gmBaseSlope` is a world-XZ HEIGHTFIELD gradient by construction — the quantity `gmSurf` speaks, which is what lets the photograph's relief and the procedural octaves' add as one gradient — and a tangent normal sampled on a vertical plane describes a surface whose up is world +-X or +-Z. There is no honest reading of it as a height over XZ, so it is not taken. It sends the photographed relief to zero exactly as a face goes vertical, in step with the `gmNy` factor that already scaled it to nothing there. **`gmFw` is now the world footprint**, which deletes grain's private `gmFw3` rather than giving the other octaves a copy of it: grain was the only octave written against the sampling rate it is actually sampled at and the only one that never aliased, so the fix is to make the shared measurement correct. A side effect worth naming — no derivative is taken inside a branch anywhere in this shader any more, so the non-uniform-control-flow hazard grain's block carried, and documented at length, is simply gone. **Snow keeps the surface's luminance**: `gmBaseK` leaves the base tap as the photograph resolved through each layer's own luma weights, mean exactly 1 by the same argument the tap's colour end already rests on, so `SNOW_COLOR * gmBaseLum` has mean exactly `SNOW_COLOR`. Snow keeps its authored colour to the last bit and gains the relief's light and shade and none of its colour, which is what snow over rock looks like. At `uBase = 0` and past the base's own fade `gmBaseLum` is exactly 1, so the toggle stays an instrument. **Measured, at six pinned vantages** (a scratch harness on the browser gate's own shape: fresh page per shot, settle on observable state, never on elapsed ms): the level-ground control `04-ground-down` moves **0.0% of its pixels at a mean delta of 0.00** — bit-identical, which is the property the construction claims, since on flat ground the tilt is exactly 0, the wall weight exactly 0 and the top weight exactly 1. `05-level` reads 0.1% / 0.02 (a few sloped pixels at distance). On the cliffs the directional-anisotropy ratio of the near-ground residual (|dx| over |dy|, 1.0 = not combed) moves **0.896 -> 0.989** at the near cliff and **1.165 -> 0.978** at the far one, the latter also dropping its across-slope contrast 3.85 -> 2.91 as the octaves finally retire out there. On the snow dome the neighbour contrast **rises 2.76 -> 3.74 luma/px** (+36%): the photograph reaching a surface that had none. **The gated numbers are unmoved, measured before and after on one box at the pinned spawn**, which is what the bit-identical control predicts and the only honest way to say it here: `ci/gates.sh` cannot complete in the container this landed in — its two-tab join is a 60 s clock and the second tab does not make it, *identically on a clean tree*, reporting `inWorld=true` and timing out anyway, which is verbatim the instrument failure `CLAUDE.md`'s trap list records. So the probes it asserts on were called directly against one tab, same views, same deltas: **15h 5.98 / 9.24 luma/px before and after** (floor 4.0, ART.md §3's target 6.3), **15i 0.309 / 0.236 before and after** (ceiling 0.35, unbounded leg x0.418 / x0.304 both runs), **15e's ship leg 0.9970 -> 0.9965 and 1.00242 -> 1.00242**, noise sample sites 7 both. `bot_smoke` also fails on the clean tree here (`Address family not supported by protocol` creating the test client's endpoint) and is likewise environmental. **Counted, not timed** (`NOW.md` item 9's six wrong-signed cost runs stand): texture units unchanged at **3**; fetches per fragment **12 unchanged below 45 deg** — the wall block is skipped whole, and that is every fragment of level ground and most of the world — against a worst case of **20** at a four-way splat boundary on a face steeper than 45 deg. Both numbers are published (`baseTexFetches` and `baseTexFetchesLevel`), because reporting only the ceiling would say the projection costs 67% more everywhere and it costs that nowhere a player stands. **What this did NOT fix, with the measurement, so the next pass starts bounded.** The remaining comb on a near cliff is the BUMP, not the projection: `gmSurf` is clamped by `BUMP_MAX_SLOPE`, and instrumenting the clamp shows it saturating over **68.0% of the near-cliff frame and 28.4% of the snow dome, against 0.6% at the level near-ground vantage**. A gradient that is saturated over two-thirds of a surface is not a gradient, it is a direction field with a fixed magnitude, and a noise gradient's direction flips fast — which is the hard crosshatch. The suspect is amplitude on one identity (rock's `bump` is 2.2 against grass's 0.6, and `BUMP_MAX_SLOPE` was cut 1.0 -> 0.55 to protect the shadow floors without rock's multiplier being re-derived under it), so it is an amplitude question against the shadow gate's own floors and `NOW.md` warns what amplitude fixes cost the detail 15h asserts. It is `NOW.md`'s next visual item, not this one's. The base map's relief also still dies as a face approaches vertical, for the reason stated above; that is a property of the bump's whole formulation and wants `ci/bump_basis.mjs` re-derived under it. | `web/src/materials.js` · `TERRAIN.md` · `ART.md` §7 · `NOW.md` item 1 |
| wind + felling v0 (the client's first animated uniform, and the swing's missing half) | **Nothing in the frame had ever moved, and the most physical verb in the game was a `scale = 0`.** Two gaps, one slice, both client-only. (a) **Wind.** The terrain is a pure function of the seed and every prop is an instance matrix written once, so the only motion in the image was the camera — a forest that is bit-identical frame to frame reads as a diorama at any framing the visual judge takes. (b) **Felling.** `sim-core` has owned chopping for a long time (`gather.rs`: ten swings, per-tool yield rows, a harvested bit, a 20–45 min timer, two event codes on the wire) and the client's entire contribution was setting the instance scale to zero — the tenth swing landed and the tree was *not there*, which reads as a rendering bug rather than as the thing you just did. **Technique from SeedThree** (`CLAUDE.md` third-party credit), re-expressed for WebGL because its wind is TSL node-material source and none of it can be lifted — the same wall `shadows.js` hit with the shadow skill. Three of its calls are load-bearing and kept: ONE per-vertex weight `aWind` rising 0 at the trunk base to 1 at the tips (a tree is a cantilever, and the weight IS the cantilever — it is also what roots the base, the single most obvious tell); phase taken from the instance's own **world position** rather than a per-instance random, so what crosses the forest is a *gust* and not 4,096 trees each doing their own thing; and two octaves, because one sine is a metronome. Knobs, all client cosmetics: `WIND_DIR = [0.85, 0.53]` (SeedThree's own off-axis bearing — on an axis every trunk sways in the plane of the terrain grid and the eye finds the lattice), `WIND_STRENGTH = 0.3` m at the tip of a 5.2 m pine (~3.3° lean), `WIND_SPEED = 1.0`, `WIND_PHASE = [0.35, 0.27]` rad/m (a ~14 m gust wavelength), `WIND_CURVE = 1.7` (in `props.js`, baked into the attribute; above 1 the trunk is stiffer than linear and the canopy carries the motion, which is the shape a conifer has); felling `FELL_TICKS = 33` to flat, `FELL_SINK_TICKS = 60` to gone, `FELL_SINK_M = 2.4` m of instance scale. **Three things this slice is careful about, each a wall it would otherwise have broken.** The clock is the **sim tick**, never `performance.now()`: wind is the first animated thing in this client and therefore the first thing that could make two captures of one seed differ, and `NOW.md` item 12 wants frame goldens that diff to zero — taking the clock from the sim means a shot at tick N is that shot every time, and the cost (33 ms steps, under a millimetre at a pine's tip at these frequencies) is free here and would **not** be free for leaf flutter. The swaying pools get their **own depth material** (`makeWindDepthMaterial`): three hands every plain caster one shared `MeshDepthMaterial`, so a tree patched only in its surface material sways out from under a shadow bolted to the ground — SeedThree does not solve this half either, its wind being "engine-specific, not exported". And the patch is **one source with the variation in uniforms and one attribute**, so it adds no program to the surface family; the depth program is the one new link and it is prewarmed in `prewarmObjects` or `browser_smoke`'s count gate fails, correctly. **The fall direction is hashed from the cell**, not sent: two clients must agree on where a tree lands, and the cell hash is a fact both already have for zero bytes. Falling *away from the axe* is better, the sim knows it, and it costs a `PROTO_VER` bump under wall 6 — a real slice, not this one. Stumps stand for the whole respawn window (`TERRAIN.md` §2 has said "Trees fell to stumps the same way" since before there was a client to draw one), on a client-only archetype index 8 that `_addScatter` can never mint because sim-core scatters 1..7. | TERRAIN §2/§4 · NOW item 12 · `web/src/materials.js` · `web/src/terrain.js` |
| the pine is generated, not authored (ez-tree wired) | **The library was the answer from the first message and I argued against it for three passes.** "Take the technique, no code ships" is the three.js skill packs' own licence term and Carbon's GPL problem; `ez-tree` (MIT, © Daniel Greenheck, npm `@dgreenheck/ez-tree`) is neither — it is a dependency, and the operator precedent is already in this table: *"if its CC0 im fine to pull in whatever helps us. then we can replace later"*. Three passes of hand-rolled cones proved the thing `Rust Images/choppingtree.jpg` says in one glance: a conifer canopy is made of ALPHA CARDS, and an opaque hull whose every edge is a polygon boundary cannot get there from any amount of geometry. **What the client draws now:** a generated pine, 6,496 triangles — 2,336 of textured bark and 4,160 of alpha-tested needle cards — at 6.60 m and a 1.52 m canopy radius, from a fixed seed so every client grows the same forest and a chunk that streams out and back is bit-identical. **Every objection I raised was wrong or a parameter, and each is now measured rather than argued:** height is `branch.length[0]` (the preset's 50 gives a 55 m tree; the geometry is normalized to `PINE_H` on the way out); width is `branch.length[1]` + `leaves.size`, swept in the bench against `PINE_MAX_R` — 23.87/1.9 gives 2.22 m against a 1.7 m ceiling, 13.2/1.45 gives 1.52 with margin for seed variance; density is `leaves.count`, where 15 left the bottom half of every tree bare trunk and 26 fills it; and the first branch whorl moved from 27% of trunk height to 16%, because 27% on a 6.6 m tree is 1.8 m — above eye level, so a player walking a forest saw a colonnade of poles. **The one thing that genuinely could not be dialled:** the cheap LOD cannot be a stripped mesh. Cutting first-level branches from 82 toward 14 does not make a smaller tree, it makes a BALD one — measured at 1,136 and 568 triangles, both reading as dead sticks, worse than the cones they would replace. Fullness lives in branch count and branch count is most of the cost, so distance has to be billboard impostors, exactly as `TERRAIN.md` §4 has said all along. **Structure.** An archetype is now a GROUP of `InstancedMesh`es sharing one instance-matrix stream, not one mesh: bark is opaque and needles are alpha-tested, and an object gets ONE `customDepthMaterial`, so a merged two-material geometry would have to alpha-test the trunk against the leaf texture in the shadow pass. Every other archetype is a group of one and the rest of `terrain.js` — fell, stump, streaming, swap-remove — did not have to learn anything. Two new surface identities, `bark` and `needle`, both `field: null`: they are the only surfaces in the table carrying real texture maps, and running the procedural field over a texture is two fights over the same pixels (water already proved the shape). `makeLeafDepthMaterial` gives the needles their own alpha-tested depth material, or a 60%-transparent card casts a solid rectangle — the shadow half everyone forgets. **The costs, stated.** The bundle goes **3.6 MB → 7.4 MB**: `ez-tree` inlines every species' bark and leaf textures as base64 data URIs in its own build, so we ship birch, oak, willow and four leaf atlases to use pine. That is a real follow-up, not a rounding error. And `ci/pine_shape.mjs` needs `ci/dom_shim.mjs` — four lines of `document`/`Image`, imported first because ESM hoists — so it can keep scoring the SHIPPED builder rather than a copy. **`PINE_VARIANTS = 1`**: a generated conifer is not rotationally symmetric the way a cone is, so yaw alone buys variation the old pool could not have, but 400 copies of one silhouette will read as 400 copies eventually and the second variant is four draw calls against DESIGN §9's 300. | ART §5/§6 · TERRAIN §4 · `web/src/props.js` · `ci/pine_shape.mjs` |
| lighting v1 — the register, metered against a measured bar | **Merged 2026-08-04 from `loop/lighting-midday`, and it is the coupled set's owner in this tree.** Read the branch history before the numbers: this pass was authored against lighting v0 and never saw the daylight-register row above it, which re-metered the same coupled set eight hours later on a branch that never saw this one — two owners, one set, the exact failure `CLAUDE.md`'s coupled-lighting trap names. v1 ships, so the register row's knobs are demoted to mentions there and its "before" (a dark sky, no air, a vertex-ramp dome) describes v0 rather than the tree this landed on; both passes independently found that same before, and both independently declined to raise `SUN_ELEVATION` for the same measured reason. **The one thing the register knew that this row does not**: three uploads `fog.color` in the output colour space and mixes it downstream of the tone map, so sharing one `THREE.Color` between fog and dome (17a) pins the inputs and leaves the outputs a transfer apart. Open, carried in `NOW.md` under the register's item. **The image sat a stop and a half under `Rust Images/` for six consecutive passes of visual work, and every instrument in the tree was structurally unable to say so.** `shadowProbe` counts pixels a toggle darkened, `surfaceProbe` counts pixels a field moved, `propProbe` divides a field by what it was laid on: all differences, all blind to an offset. The visual judge measured ours at p10 41 / p90 70 against a reference of 95-172 and returned FAIL on criterion 8 four captures running. **So the bar is read off the reference frames now, in the same run, by the same code path that reads ours** (`ci/reference_bar.mjs`: six outdoor-daylight frames of `Rust Images/` decoded in the page, Rec.601 luma percentiles, median **p10 40 / p50 91 / p90 170**, sky 121 in 232 levels). **Landed: p10 38 / p50 128 / p90 184**, per vantage p90 179/190/180/129/184/193. **Three defects, all arithmetic.** (1) **The transfer squared the shadows.** Khronos PBR Neutral subtracts `x - 6.25x*x` for `x < 0.08` (three r178), which for a roughly neutral colour resolves to `out ~= 6.25c*c` — a QUADRATIC toe over exactly the range a shaded surface occupies, so a face arriving at linear 0.02 left at 0.0025 and displayed as 8/255. Reinhard was built and measured next and fixes the toe at the cost of the midtones (`c/(1+c)` compresses everywhere, slope `1/(1+c)^2`): it took the shadow probe to **14.24% against its 15% floor** with the light rig identical. What ships is **Neutral minus its offset** — exactly identity below `StartCompression`, then Neutral's own hyperbolic shoulder and 0.15 desaturation, both constants unchanged — installed through three's `CustomToneMapping` hook by one string replacement read off the installed chunk and required to appear exactly once (the clipmap `getShadow` patch's discipline). Single ownership unchanged: the renderer maps, no material sets its own. `EXPOSURE = 1.0`. (2) **The ambient was a rumour, and it is two knobs, not one.** A hemisphere lands `mix(ground, sky, 0.5 + 0.5*N.y)`, so up-facing ground in shadow is lit by the SKY half — which is what the shadow gate's floors are calibrated against — and down-facing prop faces by the EARTH half, which is the (2,6,0) canopy underside the prop albedo v1 row reproduced from these constants. The first cut raised `FILL_INTENSITY` to 1.5 and moved both: it bought the prop floor and cost the shadow probe half its darkened share (24.0% -> 11.5%, worst yaw 20.4% -> 4.6%), which is two floors lowered for a fix that needed one. Shipping: sky half held at v0's delivered level (`FILL_SKY` 0xbcd4ee at `FILL_INTENSITY = 0.95` gives 0.478/0.625/0.812 linear against v0's 0.455/0.627/0.873 — a hue change, not a level one), earth half **2.4x** (`FILL_GROUND` 0x6b5f4a -> 0xa89b7e). Nothing about ground shadow depth moved and no shadow floor was touched. (3) **The sky was not the top of the image.** An un-gained dome is at most as radiant as a white diffuse surface in full sun, and it measured sky 142 against a ground median of 138 — an image with no highlight anywhere in it, which is why p90 would not move however the exposure was set. The dome is a ShaderMaterial now (three bands, `SKY_HORIZON` 0xa9c6e0 / `SKY_HAZE` 0x8fb4d8 / `SKY_ZENITH` 0x4b7cb4, `SKY_HAZE_TOP = 0.22` of sin(elevation) then `SKY_CURVE = 1.15`) at `SKY_GAIN = 1.15`, and the same gained colour object is handed to `THREE.Fog` — **one value, two consumers**, so the horizon seam the judge measured stepping 31 levels is exact by construction rather than by two hexes kept equal by hand. Fog `FOG_NEAR = 50` (was 180: at 180 nothing inside a first-person framing was ever touched by it) to `FOG_FAR = 1000`. The dome also draws the **sun** — `SUN_DISC_RAD = 0.0105` (0.6 deg, twice life size, because the real 0.27 deg disc is 3 px at this FOV), `SUN_DISC_SOFT = 0.006`, `SUN_DISC_GAIN = 6`, aureole `SUN_GLOW_POWER = 220` at `SUN_GLOW_GAIN = 0.55`, the disc's own tint `SUN_SKY_COLOR = 0xfff6e6` (a whiter warm than `SUN_COLOR` — the disc is drawn, not lit, so it carries its own hex) — off the SAME `Vector3` the key light uses, asserted by object identity. And a **relative** hash dither, `SKY_DITHER = 0.05`: the shipped chain's slope falls ~12x across the dome's real range (toeless Neutral is identity below `StartCompression` then a hard shoulder — the round-2 judge's evaluation of the shipped GLSL), so a constant linear dither spans a quarter of a level at one end and four at the other; the relative dither narrows that to 0.44–2.14 levels (×4.9, peak ~2.1) — imperfect against a shoulder this hard, and honestly so. Clear colour is now **black**, not the sky's own hue — a clear that matches the dome hides the dome failing to draw, which is exactly what happened here for two runs (the sky measured a flat un-tone-mapped 194 and read as a plausible overcast; the ShaderMaterial had redefined two chunks three's own ShaderMaterial prefix already carries and never linked). **THE SUN DID NOT MOVE, and that is this row's finding rather than a thing it skipped.** `NOW.md` item 1 asks for a midday register. It was built at 0.785 rad and at 0.50 rad and measured, and the world is not ready: a normal perturbed by d changes `N.L` on flat ground by `cot(elevation)*d` relative, so the ground's entire bump relief scales with cot, and with the shipped field byte-identical `browser_smoke` 15 went **12.81% moved / mean dLuma ~19 / worst-yaw brightened +0.5%** at 0.36 rad, to **2.03% / 7-8 / +0.01%** at 0.50, to **0.47% / 7 / +0.00%** at 0.785. The last column is the wall: 15's two-sidedness is what separates a field from a wash, it runs on 2.5x of margin at this spawn, and the pass before this one built a bump fix, measured it and deliberately did not ship it rather than spend that margin. `SUN_ELEVATION = 0.36` stays, and it is now blocked with a number on the ground's structure moving from bump into albedo (`NOW.md` item 2's top want) — when 15 holds its margins with the bump removed, the sun can rise. The register cost nothing for it: p10/p50/p90 are set by the transfer and the exposure, not by where the sun is. `SUN_COLOR` 0xffe1b8 -> 0xfff4e2 is the one sun change (a warm white rather than an amber, which was a low-sun colour that survived by looking deliberate). **Gates.** New **16** (`scene.tonalProbe`, six vantages copied from `art/capture.mjs` and named as copied, since the harness is outside this repo): whole-frame luma percentiles at each, each needing `SKY_MIN_FRACTION = 0.02` of dome to be scored on one, plus an EXACT sky mask taken by re-rendering with the dome hidden and the clear set to a sentinel, so every sky statistic is the dome and not "the top of the frame". Walls `TONAL_MIN_P90 = 150`, `TONAL_MIN_P50 = 70`, `TONAL_MAX_P10 = 60` (a lift is not a light rig), `TONAL_MIN_RANGE = 90` — **and the gate asserts the reference bar itself clears them**, so a floor can never drift above the thing it was derived from. **17a**: fog and horizon linear triples equal to the bit, the sky's mean at least `SKY_MIN_OVER_GROUND = 25` luma over the NON-sky median of the same frame (comparing against the whole-frame median is degenerate on a vantage that is 73% sky), and the dome's sun vector identical by reference to the key's. **17b**: banding as `SKY_MIN_BREAK = 0.25` of adjacent dome pairs differing, with `SKY_MAX_RUN = 48` as a backstop. Counting DISTINCT levels was written first and is the wrong instrument — the count is bounded by how many levels the gradient spans, so a 16-level sky band scores 16 however well dithered; measured 16-44 against a reference of 232 and said nothing. **17c** (`scene.sunProbe`): camera aimed down the key's own direction, so the disc belongs at the principal point — `SUN_MIN_PEAK_OVER_SKY = 30`, `SUN_MAX_OFFSET_PX = 24`, `SUN_MAX_SATURATED = 0.02`, all on the dome mask (without it the "brightest pixel" was a world specular 227 px off). **10b**, `shadowProbe`'s fourth leg: the same frames with `castShadow` off every mesh that is not another player, so the world's own share of the shadow is attributed by construction — this is the mutation 2026-08-01 calibrated the area floors by hand against, taken every run, and unlike an area floor it survives the sun moving (measured 13.99% at 28.6 deg and 4.79% at 45 deg while the area floor was failing at both). Floors 0.015 aggregate / 0.02 best yaw / `SHADOW_MIN_WORLD_CASTERS = 8` suppressed, and the `isMesh` test in `_worldCasters` is load-bearing: `castShadow` is an `Object3D` property, so a bare traverse collects the clipmap's three DirectionalLights and switching THOSE off removes the shadow map entirely — it did, and the leg reported 100% of a frame. **`PROP_MIN_P05 = 16`**, the wall prop albedo v1 left unwritten because the light rig owned it: the darkest twentieth of a prop's pixels, i.e. the judge's "94.9% of the skirt under 10/255" as a number. And a **console-error wall** on tab A at the tonal gate, because a program that fails to link reports there and nowhere else and then silently draws nothing. **Measured, all green, none of the pre-existing floors touched:** shadow sweep **24.16%** (per yaw 24.8/22.4/21.5/28.0, floors 15/10) against 24.0% before this slice · world's share **16.96%** (10.3/22.4/21.5/13.7) of it, 33 casters · far shadows **0.72%** (was 0.78) · horizon **0.49%** at mean dLuma 41.2 (was 0.54) · surface field **11.20%** moved, two-sided +0.4/+3.2/+4.4/+0.8 (was 12.81%, floors 4 / 1 / 0.2) · sky break 71-75%, longest run 8-27 · sun peak 251 at 6.7 px, 78 over its sky, 0.017% at 250+ · props `rock 43/95/158 amp 7.50` and `pine 20/38/100 amp 3.51`, against **29/59/123 amp 8.47** and **12/48/74 amp 4.86** before — the delivered floor up **48% and 67%**, and the field's own amplitude DOWN 11% and 28%, which is the toe removal declining to exaggerate a dark surface any more and is disclosed rather than netted out. Budget 147 draw calls / 1,057,866 triangles peak against < 300 / < 1.5 M. No timing claim is made from this box. | web `src/scene.js` `src/main.js` · `ci/reference_bar.mjs` `ci/browser_smoke.mjs` |
| prop photograph v1 — the props get the sourced base the ground already had | **The visual judge named the cause in one line and it was literally true: "the terrain got a sourced photograph this pass and the props did not — this is a coverage gap, not a tuning one" (`findings/pass-20260804-153032-01-visual.md`, ranked gap 1).** Every drawn archetype reached the material with an authored colour and a hand-rolled noise field, while the granite photograph had been decoded and resident on the GPU since the ground took it. This binds the SAME `groundTextures().albedo` array into the prop path and selects a layer — no new asset, no new decode, no new load order. `PROP_PHOTO_GAIN = 1.0` (the photograph at its own contrast; `ART.md` §7 already forbids stretching deviation past ×1), `PROP_PHOTO_SCALE = 1.6` repeats/m (0.63 m tile, inside the 0.6–1 m band the ground samples over), `PROP_PHOTO_BLEND = 8.0` (triplanar peak, `ART.md` §7(2)'s `pow(w,8)`), `PROP_PHOTO_CLAMP = [0.4, 1.6]` (makes `ALBEDO_LUMA_BAND` checkable per pixel, not only in expectation — and SYMMETRIC about 1.0, because `ci/prop_photo.mjs` measured the asymmetric [0.6, 1.6] biasing the delivered mean to 1.05 at full spread, which is the authored granite value handed back to the source file after all). **Three properties are load-bearing.** It is delivered as a RATIO about the layer's own linear mean, so `E[multiplier] = 1` at any gain and the authored granite VALUE survives untouched — a photograph that replaced the mean would be the REPLACING modifier `ci/vantages.mjs` exists to catch. It moves LUMA only, because the same report measured our near-ground chroma per unit luma at 0.34–0.73 against a reference band of 0.077–0.193 and a photograph's chroma would push the worst number the wrong way. And the three projections are blended variance-preservingly (÷`sqrt(dot(w,w))`, Heitz & Neyret) rather than by a plain weighted mean, which at the three-way point delivers only 0.577 of the source's contrast — without that line a triplanar granite reads FLATTER than the noise it replaced. **What is NOT done**: bark for `wood`, needle cards for `foliage`, and the frequency split that would hand the field's detail octave to the photograph and leave it only the coarse patchiness a tiling map cannot supply (`NOW.md`). | `ART.md` §3 · §7 · `web/src/materials.js` · `ci/prop_photo.mjs` |
| map marker cap v0 (**ui lane, 2026-08-05**) | `MAP_MARKS_MAX = 64` (`web/src/map.js`) and `MAP_MARK_PX = 4` (`web/src/hud.js`). The map draws a marker for every deployed sleeping bag and hearth the client knows and every standing death backpack — the trip's other end, so a player can afford to leave a base they can find their way back to. The cap is a **wall** (`CLAUDE.md` §4, bounded everything) and not a budget a player meets: the two marked archetypes are the ones a base has one of, so a real map draws two or three plus a bag. Overflow policy is drop-newest, and the refused count is kept on the result rather than discarded (`CLAUDE.md`: no silent caps) — `ci/ui_smoke.mjs` §Z asserts both halves. `MAP_MARK_PX` is smaller than the player triangle's `MAP_MARKER_PX = 7` on purpose: the panel's first job is *where am I* and a marker the same size as the answer competes with it. Wanted from the operator: whether the marked set is right — boxes and doors are deliberately unmarked (a base is a dozen of each and marking them buries the anchors), and whether a player's own placed markers/waypoints belong here later. | `web/src/map.js` · `web/src/hud.js` · `ci/ui_smoke.mjs` §Z |
| interact aim radius v0 (**ui lane, 2026-08-05**) | `INTERACT_AIM_RADIUS_M = 1.0` (metres), `web/src/interact.js`. How far off the aim line a deployable may sit and still count as *aimed at* rather than merely *nearby* — the two ranks E's one resolver picks between, aimed always beating nearby. Derived from the deployable's own scale rather than chosen: a box or a hearth is roughly a metre across inside a 3 m build cell (`BUILD_CELL`), so a crosshair within a metre of a cell centre is a crosshair on the thing standing in it. It is **not** a reach — the reach is the sim's `build::BUILD_REACH_M`, mirrored as `INTERACT_REACH_M` and pinned to it by `ci/ui_smoke.mjs` §Q — and it is not a cone: nothing in reach is ever excluded for being off-aim, so being off-aim costs a candidate only against something that is on aim. Wanted from the operator: whether 1.0 m is the intended precision, or whether E should feel looser (1.5–2.0, easier in a cluttered base) or tighter (0.5, closer to a raycast). §Q asserts only that it is inside `(0, reach)`, because at or above the reach the two ranks collapse into the nearest-wins chain this replaced. | `web/src/interact.js` · `ci/ui_smoke.mjs` §Q |
| occupant volume gate v0 — the blocked cylinder is the drawn mesh (`ROUND_OUT_M = 0.001`, `TRUNK_BAND_M = 0.5`, `PINE_EZ_TRUNK_R_M = 0.3069`) | `OCCUPANT_R_M`/`OCCUPANT_TOP_M` say of themselves they are "read off the client's authored geometry (`web/src/props.js` `ARCHETYPES`)", and until `ci/occupant_volume.mjs` nothing checked that sentence for any occupant — the Rust const-asserts hold the tables equal to `occupant_volume()`'s match, but both live in one file, so they move together under one edit and prove only that the file agrees with itself. The gate measures the vertex buffer `ARCHETYPES` actually builds and **sandwiches** every row: lower bound, no vertex below the blocking top may lie outside the blocking radius (the doc's own rule — "erring inward is the bug"); upper bound, the radius may not exceed the mesh's widest horizontal extent and the top may not exceed `lift` + the mesh's bounding sphere (which is the doc's own derivation for why the nodes' tops are 1.5 and not 2.0). A lower bound alone is satisfied forever by widening, so both directions are gated. **`ROUND_OUT_M` is how far a row may round outward** off the measured extent — the doc permits it and `CrateSlot` uses it — and it is not tuned: the tables are written to four decimals, so a millimetre is 10x the finest row's own resolution and still 1000x inside the metre-wide widening it caught in mutation. Comparisons carry one f32 ulp (`2**-23` relative) because the table is `[f32; 11]` and the vertices come out of a `Float32Array`; at the crate's 0.68 that is 8.1e-8 against a real defect of 7.4e-5. **Two findings.** `CrateSlot` read 0.68 against a measured half-diagonal of 0.680074 — inward, the bug the doc names — and is 0.6801 now; `test_replay`'s golden does not move, because the five crates sit on the haven pad and 74 µm does not reach the bots' script. And the generated ez-tree pine's trunk is `PINE_EZ_TRUNK_R_M`, 18% wider than the cone's 0.26 that `OCCUPANT_R_M[Tree]` is read off — so the Tree pin is written against **whichever builder `ARCHETYPES[1]` selects**, and wiring `parts: pineParts` without moving the table goes red rather than shipping a trunk you walk through. Tree is exempt from the general rule (you walk under a canopy); the trunk band proves it is trunk-only rather than assuming it. 18 mutations, each caught by its own mechanism. |

Measured off `Rust Images/gameplayfoundbase.jpeg`: labels `10 20 30 40 NE 50 60 70 80 E` across roughly the middle third of a 2000 px frame, so ~90° over ~460 px, numbers every 10 with letters BETWEEN them rather than replacing them. 5 px/deg puts the window at 450 px on a 1280-wide canvas — the same fraction of frame width. Same class as `TOAST_MS`/`CHAT_CAP`: layout, not balance. | `web/src/hud.js` · `web/index.html` (the two px widths are asserted equal to these) |
| map screen v0 — the grid square (`MAP_GRID_M = 128`) | **The judge's ranked gap 3 (`findings/pass-20260805-074623-01-judge.md`) opens "There is nowhere to go" and leads with `MENUS.md:102`, Map MISSING against `mapraw.jpg` and `mapstylized.jpg`.** `web/src/map.js` + `#map` answer it. One number needed a default and no doc fixed it: how big a lettered grid square is. `mapstylized.jpg` runs ~26 columns across its map with `LETTER + NUMBER` labels in each square's top-left; 128 m gives 16 columns on our 2048 m island — the same square-to-world ratio at our smaller size, and it keeps the letters inside A–P so no square needs two. Rows count **1..16 north to south** and columns **A..P west to east**, which rests on the `+Z is North` row above and is asserted against it in `ci/ui_smoke.mjs` §U (all four corners). The palette (`MAP_SAND`/`MAP_GRASS`/`MAP_LITTER`/`MAP_ROCK`/`MAP_SEA_*`) is read off `mapraw.jpg` and is not a knob; the map invents no biome law of its own — the four weights come from `terrain::splat_from` through the bridge, the same law the 3D ground blends by, so the map cannot draw an island the player would not walk onto. `MAP_SHADE_FLOOR = 0.5757359312880714` is derived, not chosen: flat ground must come out at exactly the palette colour, so `floor + MAP_SHADE_GAIN * MAP_LIGHT[1] == 1`, and §U asserts that identity rather than trusting it. **Not spoken, so it ships as a default.** Two things the map cannot do yet and neither is a UI call: the haven pad is not exported client-side (`terrain::haven` has no bridge export), so the one authored destination carries no marker; and the world whitens the alpine channel above `materials.js`'s `SNOW_RANGE` while the map paints that channel as rock, because that constant is the looks lane's. |
| map screen v0 — the two cosmetic knobs (`MAP_SHADE_CLAMP = [0.45, 1.35]`, `MAP_MARKER_PX = 7`) | **The judge's ranked fix 3 (`findings/pass-20260805-074623-02-judge.md`): both shipped as literals with the map and neither reached this registry**, which is the `BUMP_MAX_SLOPE` shape the knob gate was built for. Same class as `compass strip geometry v0` above — legibility, not balance. `MAP_SHADE_CLAMP` bounds the hillshade multiplier either side of the flat-ground identity 1: the floor keeps a fully shaded north face showing its palette colour instead of going to mud, and the ceiling keeps a lit south face from washing to white, which is the failure that loses the biome read on exactly the slopes a player uses to navigate. It is a clamp on a cosmetic term and nothing derives from it; the derived number in this system is `MAP_SHADE_FLOOR`, one row up, and §U asserts that identity rather than this range. `MAP_MARKER_PX = 7` is the marker triangle's half-height in canvas pixels, and it is a ratio to the grid rather than a taste: a square is `MAP_DRAW_PX / GRID_COLS` = 32 px, so 7 names a square without covering it. **Neither is spoken, so both ship as defaults.** | `web/src/map.js` `MAP_SHADE_CLAMP` · `web/src/hud.js` `MAP_MARKER_PX` · `ci/knob_registry.mjs` now pins both |
| repair v0, the client half: which key, and which store an address names | **R sends `ACT_REPAIR`, and the store bit is a client decision the wire cannot check.** The sim half landed with wire v21 and `client_action_repair(deploy, cx, cz, level, loc)`; nothing could press it for two passes. Three calls were needed and none was spoken. **(1) The key is R, and it is mode-scoped.** R is already the build-level raise, so R repairs only out of build mode and the prompt is suppressed in build mode to match. Mnemonic beats a free letter (U upgrades, R repairs) and the conflict is confined to the one mode where a ghost is on screen — but it does mean a player holding the hammer must leave build mode to mend. Say if repair should take a free key (J/K/N/O/P/Q/V are unbound) and let R mean one thing everywhere. **(2) `REPAIR_STORE_PIECE = 0`, `REPAIR_STORE_DEPLOY = 1`** — not invented, read off the bridge's leading `deploy` argument; recorded because they are now mirrored in JS and a renumber would be silent. **(3) An exact address tie goes to the DEPLOYABLE.** `place_deploy` requires the doorway piece at the identical `(cx, cz, level, loc)`, so a door and its doorway are one address and one distance, and something has to break it. The door wins because it is the thing in front of the player and the breach point a raid uses; the doorway behind it is reachable by stepping aside, which is not true in reverse. Say if a damaged doorway under an intact door should win instead — **that reading needs the systems half below, not a different rule here.** **Blocked and measured, systems lane:** `EventMsg::StructHit` carries a `deploy` bit and `core.rs`'s `struct_hit` tuple drops it (`(cx, cz, level, loc, left, max)`), so the client cannot attribute damage to a store at a shared address. That is why the prompt names the verb and not the hp — a row reading `340/500` for the wrong one of two things is worse than one that reads `[R] REPAIR WOOD DOORWAY`. The number ships when the bit does. | `web/src/interact.js` · `web/src/main.js` · `ci/ui_smoke.mjs` §X · `crates/client-wasm/src/bridge.rs` |

The **species** row is the pig: `hp = 80` (three swings with the stone hatchet — priced against the tool a naked spawn has, not against the reference boar, which is a mid-game animal with a bite), `walk_pct = 50` (1.49 m/s, an amble), `flee_pct = 100` + `flee_seconds = 3` (5.5 m/s for three seconds past the last moment you were close — faster than a sprinting player, which is what makes the bow worth carrying), `roam_m = 60` / `spook_m = 12` (the leash and the fright radius; the leash must be the wider of the two or the animal spends its life being turned around, and `validate` refuses the inversion), `respawn_seconds = 300`, `drops = fat ×15 + cloth ×10`. The **structural** constants are `limits.rs` and are not balance: `MAX_MOBS = 64` (≈ 30/km² of land, and sized against the wire as much as the ground — at the 176 m AOI radius the expectation inside one client's interest set is 1.5 animals, nowhere near `MAX_SNAPSHOT_ENTITIES`), `MOB_THINK_TICKS = 15` (phase-offset by slot: ≈ 4 animals decide per tick), `MOB_WAKE_CM = 24_000` (240 m, deliberately outside `AOI_EXIT_CM` = 208 m so nothing freezes while a client can still see it), `MOB_ID_TAG = 0x8000_0000`, `MOB_LOOT_ROWS = 4`, and in `mob.rs` `HOME_TRIES = 24` / `HOME_MAX_SLOPE = 1.0`. Server-side, `PRIORITY_W_MOB = 25` against a player's 100 — **the shed order, stated where the shed happens**: a snapshot that cannot carry everything drops the animal before the player, because a player's position is what prediction reconciles and combat is fought on. One id-space change rides with it: the player-id allocator masks its claim generation to 23 bits (`PLAYER_ID_GEN_MASK`) so bit 31 is never set. **Open for the operator**: the roster size (a density call, and the one number here a playtest actually answers); whether a second species should be a content row or wait for the aggression path in `reference/ANIMALS.md` §9.5; and whether an animal should ever damage a player, which is the only item on that list that changes the wire. | mob.rs, limits.rs, content/mobs.toml, server/core.rs, tests/mob.rs, tests/mob_wire.rs, tests/mob_mesh.rs |

| ranged v0 — the bow fires, the arrow flies, the trunk stops it (systems lane) | **The content had been armed since the content crate was written and the sim could not read it.** `content/weapons.toml` has carried a bow, a crossbow, their ammo ids and their `[weapon.ballistic]` blocks through validation, the balance bands and the content hash, and `bake_combat` threw every one of them away at `if w.kind != WeaponKind::Melee { continue; }`. So `combat.rs` had `strike` and `raid` and nothing else, the pad's loot table handed out `item.arrow_metal` for a weapon that could not be fired, and every fight on the island was a walk-up club fight — which also meant the occupant collision that landed the pass before had nothing to be cover *from*. **Design, and the one line that matters:** an arrow's position is millimetres and its velocity **millimetres per tick**, both `i32`, and the whole flight is integer addition. Metres-per-second becomes per-tick once, at bake, and never again — the quantize-both-sides law applied to a projectile, so there is no per-tick rounding to accumulate over a second of flight and no float for native and wasm to disagree about. The only floats are the launch direction and the collision queries, which take metres because `terrain` and `collide` do. **Ground, occupants and pieces are point samples** along the tick's segment; **bodies are solved**, an exact planar closest-approach against the same segment, and a body is hit only if its approach comes before the distance at which the world stopped the arrow. That comparison is the judge's ask ("a shot stopped by a trunk does not reach the body behind it") and it is only *exercised* when trunk and body fall inside one 1.33 m segment — the 4 m-behind case is decided by the arrow ceasing to exist, so `tests/shoot.rs` gates the pressed-against-cover case separately over a swept standoff, and mutating the comparison away reddens only that one. **`frame.pitch` is now read by the sim** for the first time (`gather.rs` had said "aim is planar in v0" since melee): the byte was already transmitted, interpolated and hashed, so this costs no wire — it costs a `pitch_lut.rs` generated by `ci/gen_pitch_lut.py` exactly as `yaw_lut.rs` is, and unlike the yaw table it is **not circular**, because 255 is the opposite pole from 0 and not its neighbour. Knobs, all proposed defaults: `MAX_ARROWS = 128` (refuse the shot — checked before the ammo leaves the quiver, and deliberately not drop-oldest, because stealing a live arrow to make room would make a hit depend on how many other people were shooting), `ARROW_STEP_MM = 170` (the smaller of two existing assumptions — `collide::blocked`'s documented 0.19 m endpoint shortcut, and two interior samples across a 0.468 m trunk), `MAX_ARROW_SUBSTEPS = 16` (with the step, also a **content wall**: `bake_bow` refuses a muzzle speed past 2.72 m/tick rather than shipping a projectile that tunnels), `MAX_ARROW_LIFE_TICKS = 120`, `ARROW_EYE_MM = 1600` (the eye, not the chest — a shot from the navel would clear cover the shooter cannot see over), `ARROW_R_M = 0.05`. **What v0 does not do**, stated rather than hidden: no arrow is drawn on any screen (a wire event plus a client tracer, neither built, and the wire half is this lane's); no structure damage, so an arrow stops on a wall rather than chipping it; no headshots and no falloff (the schema has no curve to read); firearm and throwable rows stay dropped at the bake, the revolver because hitscan wants M2's rewound raycast. And `collide::blocked` bakes `CAPSULE_RADIUS_M` into its own query, so an arrow is inflated to a player's width — it goes exactly where a body could go and no further, which is defensible for a doorway and wrong for an arrow slit. Open for the operator: whether `MAX_ARROWS` should scale with `MAX_PLAYERS` rather than sit flat, and whether a bow should be able to chip a wall at all. | ranged.rs, limits.rs, combat.rs `RangedDef`, bake.rs `bake_bow`, tests/shoot.rs |
| oven v0 — the campfire burns, and it is the furnace too (`OVEN_PERIOD_TICKS = 15`, `MAX_COOK_ROWS = 32`, `REFUSE_D_FUEL = 13`, `REFUSE_M_OVEN = 8`, `EV_OVEN = 30`, `SUB_OVEN = 41`, wire v28) | **A campfire baked, placed, and did nothing at all** — `ARCH_FIRE` and `ARCH_FURNACE` were two codes in `deploy.rs` with no verb behind either, so the T0 deployable a player crafts first was furniture. `crates/sim-core/oven.rs` answers it, and the shape is the reference's own: every cooking hook in `reference/rust-systems.txt` hangs off one `BaseOven` (`OnOvenToggle SVSwitch`, `OnOvenStart`, `OnOvenCook Cook(Single)`, `OnFuelConsume ConsumeFuel(Item, ItemModBurnable)`), so a fire and a furnace are one class and the archetype is a **content** discriminator on a cook row, never a second code path. **The contents are a box.** An oven's slots live in `deploy.rs`'s existing `BoxStore` (`deploy::holds_items`), so `CONT_BOX` addresses a campfire, the open/move/sync verbs work unchanged, and **no wire field was invented for a second kind of container** — the server's container drip needed not one line. The oven's own state (`arch`, `lit`, `burn`, `bank`, twelve cook counters) rides an index-aligned array beside the box records, which is `bag_ready`'s argument one store down: `DeployRec` is what the deploy-sync packet mirrors, so a burn timer may not ride on it. Both halves move together in `place_deploy` and in `remove_at`'s swap-remove, and `tests/oven.rs`'s `a_removal_moves_both_halves` is the gate for exactly that. **A use press is the switch**: `Command::Use` routes by address (a door lives on a doorway's edge, an oven on the plane, so it is a lookup and not a guess), lighting refuses with `REFUSE_D_FUEL` when nothing inside burns — a match lights what is laid, it does not lay it — and an oven that runs dry snuffs itself, announcing with **actor 0**, which is the one field separating it from a hand on the switch. **Only fuel and what it cooks go in** (`REFUSE_M_OVEN`), which is the reference's own 2025 rule and what stops a 100-wood campfire being a cheaper twelve-slot box than the 100-wood box whose whole job is to be one; that reason is the eighth, so `REFUSE_M_BITS` widened 3 → 4 and the wire moved with it. **The byproduct is banked, not rolled**: the reference gives ~75% of a charcoal per wood by chance, and a chance is an RNG draw inside `state_hash`, so `byproduct_pct` accumulates in hundredths and pays one unit per 100 — the reference's expectation, exactly, with nothing random in the hash. **The sweep is bounded without a cursor**: each oven steps on the tick where its index and the tick agree mod `OVEN_PERIOD_TICKS`, so no tick visits more than `ceil(MAX_BOXES / 15)` ovens and every fire still burns at one real rate; `TICK_HZ % OVEN_PERIOD_TICKS == 0` is asserted, because a step that does not divide the second makes every content `seconds` approximate. **Wire v28** (`SUB_OVEN = 41`, the 42nd subtype of sixty-four — no field widened but the refusal above), 78 goldens regenerated in the same commit (wall 6), and `event_roles.rs` classifies `EV_OVEN` by role in the same commit (ledger 24/29 → 25/30). **Content is `content/cooking.toml`**, new to `Content::FILES`: one `[fuel]` row (wood, 3 s a unit — the reference's 20 wood/minute — leaving charcoal at 75) and `[[cook]]` rows, hashed whole by `canon.rs` because the `[backpack]` defect is what happens when a value reaches the sim and not the digest. **It ships with no cook row, and that is a fact about the island rather than the oven**: cooking wants raw food, the alpha food set is berries/mushrooms/corn, only berries are payable by anything in the world, and none of the three is a thing you cook — the meat was cut with the animals that would drop it. So the shipped fire's job today is fuel → charcoal, which is real, reference-exact and gives the powder chain a T0 source; a food row is a one-line content edit the day a raw food is reachable, and `NOW.md` carries it. **Client**: `Verb::Fire` on the E-menu resolver, `E` opens the panel where the wood goes, `C` lights or snuffs, and the lit set lives in `ClientCore` keyed by address rather than on the mirrored deploy record — a resync would otherwise overwrite every flame with the server's unlit copy. **Deliberately absent**: the burnt state (a burnt row is a cook row whose input is a cooked item, and the food to demonstrate it does not exist), spoilage, warmth/comfort (the sim has no temperature), and the smelting migration — the furnace's ore rows stay station-gated crafts in `recipes.toml`, and moving them here re-prices the powder chain, which is a content decision and not a code one. | `reference/rust-systems.txt` BaseOven/Fuel · CLAUDE.md traps · sim-core `oven.rs` `deploy.rs` `inventory.rs` `worldsave.rs` · `content/cooking.toml` · `crates/protocol/src/{lib,event,goldens}.rs` · `crates/sim-core/tests/oven.rs` |
| recycler v0 — the salvage converter, and the economy's arming point (`ARCH_RECYCLER = 8`, `CookRow::count`, `ARCH_BITS` 3 → 4, wire v31; content: `item.recycler` at 100 metal frags + 200 wood + 2 gears, hp 300, `placement = any`; yields gears → 15 metal frags, rope → 18 cloth, tarp → 60 cloth, all at 5 s — theirs since 2026-08-18, and 8/5/8 when this row was written) | **Components were dead weight and a destination had no verb.** The coast road's barrels pay gears, rope and tarp; two recipes consume gears and nothing consumes the other two, so the loot that makes the road worth walking was mostly inventory clutter — `NOW.md` §4b named the recycler as the one `DESIGN.md` §2 fixture not blocked on an operator act. **It is the oven again, and that is the whole design.** `oven.rs`'s own header argues that an archetype is a *content* discriminator on a cook row and never a second code path; a recycler is that argument's third case, so it reuses `OvenState` (its per-slot timer is the cook counter), `BoxStore` (via `holds_items`, so `CONT_BOX` addresses it and a raid spills it by the existing route), the use-press switch, and `oven::sweep`. **Two deltas, both small and both gated.** *No fuel*: the burn half of a step runs only for `OvenState::burns()`, so a recycler lights on an empty press where a fire refuses with `REFUSE_D_FUEL`, never snuffs itself, and `accepts` refuses it wood — an archetype that cannot burn must not become a wood chest by inheriting the fuel clause. *Multi-output*: `CookRow` grew a `count`, and **every row over one `(arch, input)` fires together off the slot's single clock**, which is what lets one gear pay metal AND coin. `validate::structural` holds such a set to one `seconds` and refuses two rows paying the same output; the sweep applies the whole set to a scratch copy of the container's twelve stacks and commits only if all of it fits, because with two outputs the half-applied case is reachable rather than theoretical — proven red under a per-row mutant (`a_conversion_that_cannot_pay_it_all_pays_none_of_it`). **Wire v31, and this is the one that cost a width.** `ARCH_RECYCLER = 8` is the ninth archetype against a three-bit field that held exactly eight, so `ARCH_BITS` widened to 4, both range checks moved to `ARCH_RECYCLER`, the domain gate's row went `min_members` 8 → 9 / `live_max` 7 → 8, and all 82 goldens regenerated in the same commit (wall 6). `every_domain_fits_its_wire_field` is what makes that a checklist rather than a memory. **The recycler pays coin out of a content table, not a function** — which is what let the faucet be turned on the same day by editing `content/cooking.toml` and nothing else. It shipped inert for a few hours on a misreading of `ALPHA.md` A1 (corrected by the operator that day: OBOL is scrap, and what stages is the claim rail); the correction cost three uncommented rows, because `oven.rs` cannot tell the difference between paying metal and paying a coin. **The yields were ours and that was a gap, not a choice** — `reference/RIPLIST.md` had no recycler row, so they were derived from our own anchor (`component_minutes` 4 against `farm_per_min` 30 would be ~120 frags a gear at parity — refused, because it would make the road strictly better than the ore node and invert the gradient nodes exist to hold), landing at ~15 seconds of mining. **Closed 2026-08-18** (§1f): theirs pays 15, an eighth of parity, so the gradient that derivation was defending survives the take without needing to be defended. **Deliberately absent**: no haven fixture (every deployable still comes from a player placing one, so the recycler is craftable and placeable and the destination gap `NOW.md` §4b names is only half closed), no service fee, no banked OBOL, no sink. | sim-core `oven.rs` `deploy.rs` `gather.rs` `probe.rs` · `content/src/{schema,validate,bake,canon}.rs` · `content/{items,deployables,recipes,cooking}.toml` · `crates/protocol/src/{lib,event,goldens}.rs` · `crates/sim-core/tests/oven.rs` (5 new) · client `ui/interact.rs` `ui/icons.rs` `render/structures.rs` · `ci/bake_icons.py` |
| research v0 — the sink, and the ladder's only gate (`ARCH_RESEARCH = 9`, `Player::known: u64`, `RecipeDef::blueprint`, `ACT_RESEARCH = 17`, `SUB_RESEARCH`/`_REFUSED`/`SUB_KNOWN` = 44–46, wire v32; content: `item.research_table` at 200 wood + 75 frags, hp 400 on a foundation; prices 20 / 40 / 75 obol over six rows) | **Asked for directly** (operator, 2026-08-10: *"yea we need a research table"*), and it is what makes the coin a currency instead of a trophy: the recycler landed a faucet the same day with nothing to spend it on. **You find the thing, you take it to a table with a fistful of coin, and from then on you can make it** — scrap's job in the reference, and the whole verb. **A station, not a container**: proximity-checked like a workbench (`craft.rs`'s own `STATION_RADIUS_M`, re-exported rather than restated) and the sample comes out of an inventory slot the request names, because a container would cost a box record, a wire handle and a second place for a stack to be lost in while reading identically to a player who is holding the thing either way. **The unlock is one bit per player**: `Player::known` is a `u64` mask over recipe indices, so the craft-path question is one shift and one AND — no map, no allocation, nothing to iterate (walls 1 and 2) — and `KNOWN_MASK_BITS` asserts `MAX_RECIPES <= 64` so a 65th recipe fails at compile rather than becoming quietly unlockable. It rides `PlayerSave` (`SCALARS_BYTES` 52 → 60, `SAVE_FORMAT` with it), because a blueprint you paid a hoard for and lost by closing the game would make the sink a punishment. **The blueprint outranks the station in `craft::enqueue`, and that ordering is the design call in the slice**: a recipe can fail both checks, and reporting the station first would send a player who needs a research table back to the workbench they are already standing at. `tests/research.rs` asserts it in both directions on one recipe that is gated by both. **Validation runs both ways**: a `blueprint` recipe with no research row is uncraftable forever, a research row for an item nothing outputs is a coin sink that unlocks nothing, and an item output by two recipes would make *which* one a blueprint teaches a matter of file order — all three refused at boot. **Wire v32 cost no width**: the action lane had fifteen spare codes from `ACT_DEMOLISH`'s v30 bump and this spent the first; the archetype field had room because the recycler widened it one version earlier. The one layout move is a single bit per recipe row, so the craft panel can draw **LOCKED** rather than offering a press the server will refuse. **What is gated behind it is small on purpose**: the powder era only — medkit, metal arrows, gunpowder, roadsign armour, revolver, satchel — all of them reachable as loot before they are craftable, so research is a reason to walk to the haven and never a tax on the bootstrap. Gating the hatchet you need to gather the wood to build the bench to research the hatchet is the circle a tech tree gets wrong once. **Deliberately absent**: no tech TREE (a row unlocks one recipe and depends on nothing — a `requires` column over these same bits is the next slice, and it is content plus a reachability check, not a sim change), no blueprint item to trade, no partial refund, and no wipe schedule for `DESIGN.md` §8's promise that blueprints outlive one, which is unbuilt because no wipe is. | `ALPHA.md` §2 · `DESIGN.md` §3.1 · sim-core `research.rs` (new) `craft.rs` `deploy.rs` `persist.rs` `world.rs` `limits.rs` · `content/research.toml` (new) · `crates/protocol/src/{lib,event,goldens}.rs` · `crates/sim-core/tests/research.rs` (new, 7) `tests/event_roles.rs` (34/34) · client `ui/{interact,craft,refusals,icons}.rs` `render/{verbs,feed,hud,structures,panels/craft}.rs` |
| OBOL faucet v0 — the recycler's coin yields (gears → 12 obol, on the row that already converts it; rope and tarp pay cloth and no coin) | **Theirs since 2026-08-18, and the row that used to sit here was the last of the recycler's invented numbers** (gears → 2, rope → 1, tarp → 1). It said so itself: the counts were *"anchored on our own prices rather than the reference's, because `reference/RIPLIST.md` still has no recycler row"*. It has one now — §1f, page tier, the official wiki's own **Recycle** table, on a source scoring 4/4 on the stack sizes we already hold. Their gears return 15 metal fragments and 12 scrap; their rope and tarp return cloth and no scrap at all, so those two coin rows are **deleted** rather than zeroed (a cook row paying nothing is inert and `validate::structural` refuses one). **The ratio clause this row states — move these with the research prices or it breaks — held, and holding is what it looks like when nothing moves.** Our barrel paid 0.848 obol and now pays 2.727; theirs pays ~2.42 scrap. Priced in barrels over the four researchable items we both have (their page-read Research Table column — HV arrow 15, revolver 30, satchel 60, gunpowder 120), **our research economy cost 2.66× theirs before this take and 0.83× after it.** Their *ordering* is still untaken and is a named outstanding row (§1f): an 8× spread against our 3.75×, gunpowder their top rung and our middle one. **No price in `research.toml` or `recipes.toml` moved; the *reason* they carried did** — both cited a "~10-scrap barrel" that was never measured and is wrong. No band moved either: `balance::check` reads no cook row, so every anchor is bit-identical and only the content hash changed. **Still open for the operator**: whether the faucet's era (these yields are the current game's) should be reconciled with the bench ladder's spoken pre-Oct-2025 one, and `loot.toml` — untaken, and the thing that actually decides what a barrel pays. | twig v0 — the placement grade, and the shape cost grammar (`MAT_TWIG = 0`, `DECAY_MATERIALS = 4`, wire v34) | **`reference/BUILDING.md` §7b, built 2026-08-10** (operator: *"we need to work on building more"*, with §7b's catalogue and cost table attached). Two halves of one idea. **(a) Placement and grade are separate acts.** Every piece now enters the world as **twig** — `build::place` refuses any other rung with `REFUSE_B_TIER` — and `build::upgrade` is what commits it, paying the grade's whole cost on top of the twig already spent. The client had already pinned this on 2026-08-07 (`ui::build::PLACE_MATERIAL`, operator: *"upgrade is the hammer you hold"*); what was missing was the sim agreeing, so a forged row could skip the skeleton. Twig is 10 hp — theirs outright — and is the one material `upkeep_sweep` **never charges and therefore never protects**: a scaffold pays no rent and gets no shelter, at 100 %/period (their 1 h, and our period is 1 h), so a draft you do not commit is gone in an hour under a full hearth. That is what makes it a draft rather than a cheap permanent base. **(b) Shapes are priced apart**, on their four ratios: foundation/wall/stairs 1.0, doorway 0.7, floor/roof 0.5. Ours priced every shape at its material's wall, and since hp is per material, every shape bought identical hp per resource — there was no reason to prefer one but fit. Their spread is 4× and it is the engine under every base shape that game is known for. **The whole cost column is theirs, absolutes included** — grade base twig 50 / wood 200 / stone 300 / metal 200, with the shape ratios off it, so our 24 cells are their 24 cells for the six shapes we have. It landed as a *ratio* against our own wood grade first (88 / 62 / 44) on the argument that our column was farm-minute anchored and theirs was not; the operator asked why our ratio was ever off, and the archaeology answered it. **Our cost column had never been taken and was not on the queue**: the 2026-08-08 balance pass took the hp ladder and the satchel out of `building.toml` and left `cost` untouched, `RIPLIST.md` never opened a row, so 350/350/200 stood as first written in the M1 build slice, derived from `farm_per_min` and compared against nothing. The **node take of 2026-08-10** is what made it visible — once a tree paid *their* 810 wood, a wall priced at *ours* cost **1.75× theirs in trees**, the unit a player feels, with the numerator theirs and the denominator ours. That is §4.1's false-familiarity trap one level out, and there was no second currency to convert between: only an anchor nobody had checked. **`wood_wall_minutes` re-spoken [5, 9] → [3, 5]** (value 4.0), which is `BALANCE.md` §7's rule working — a band written around our own invented number is the stale half — and `CONTENT.md` §4 anchor 3 moves with it. Measured at each step (starter farm-min · daily upkeep · stone raid ratio · wood wall farm-min), all four from `balance::check`: **85.6 · 8.56 · 1.38 · 7.0** before anything → **77.9 · 7.79 · 1.52 · 7.0** on the shape re-price → **93.2 · 7.79 · 1.27 · 7.0** once the twig skeleton is priced into the bill (`balance.rs` adds the shape's twig rung per piece, read off the table so a re-price moves with it) → **77.9 · 6.92 · 1.52 · 4.0** on taking their absolutes. Every band holds; only `wood_wall_minutes` was re-spoken, and only on the last step. Daily upkeep does not move on the twig step because the anchor charges the **graded half of the bill only** — the sweep charges no scaffold, so an upkeep anchor that did would price a wall nobody pays for. The raid ratio does move there, and should: a raid is priced against what the builder paid, and the builder now pays for the skeleton too. **Wire v34**: `MATERIAL_BITS` did not move — 2 bits held four rungs already — but wood/stone/metal became 1/2/3, which is a widened *meaning* under an unchanged layout, v18's case. Caught by `wire_domains::every_domain_fits_its_wire_field`, not by a reviewer. Three of 83 fixtures moved bytes (`action_upgrade`, `event_piece_defs`, and `hello`, which carries the version); the other 80 are byte-identical under a new name, which is what a widened meaning looks like. The 2-bit field is now **saturated**, so the decoder can no longer refuse a bad material and `test_upgrade_material_domain_is_enforced` says so in place of the forge test it used to run. |

| research ladder v0 — the tech tree's edges, and the one edge nobody spoke: `Research.requires` (item ids) · `ResearchRow::requires` (a `Player::known` mask) · `REFUSE_R_LOCKED = 5` (systems lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED, and the row exists for what is deliberately NOT in it.** The mechanism is built and gated; what is open is how many edges the tree should carry. Shipped: exactly one, `item.satchel_charge` requires `item.gunpowder` — and that is not a design choice, it is a fact of `recipes.toml` (a satchel is 240 gunpowder and gunpowder is itself blueprint-gated), which `validate::structural` now refuses to let the tree contradict. **The obvious second edge, revolver behind gunpowder, is not authored here on purpose**: the revolver's own inputs are metal/wood/gears, so nothing in the data implies it — it is a judgement about pacing, and inventing one is the thing this file exists to stop. Two more are downstream of a call nobody has made: `NOW.md` §0tt has the bench ladder with both eras sourced (`RIPLIST.md` §2 row 7) and says the era is a knob to speak, and until workbench 2/3 exist there is nothing for a tier edge to hang on. **What a reader should take from this row**: the ladder is one edge deep because one edge is what the content honestly implies, not because the mechanism is partial. `content/research.toml` and `CONTENT.md` §1 carry the floor rule; gates are `crates/content/tests/content.rs` (5 tests) and `crates/sim-core/tests/research.rs` (2). |
| item condition v0 — the denominator nobody can source: `CONDITION_MAX` (per tool, or one constant) · `[gatherable.condition_loss]` per (node, tool) · whether the rock wears at all (systems lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED — and one of these is not a taste call, it is a gap in the record.** `reference/DURABILITY.md` §5: the maximum condition of any reference item is on none of the reachable sources, so their `0.3` per swing is **a rate with no denominator** and hits-per-life is unknown. Taking 0.3 without a max is taking half a ratio, which `BALANCE.md` §6.3 does not permit — so `CONDITION_MAX` is **ours to choose out loud**, not theirs to take. Two more with it: their repair price is **DISPUTED** (primary tables 20% of craft, every guide 50% — §3, recorded both, never averaged), so nothing repair-priced may land until one is checked; and §4's contradiction — the rock's page shows a 0.3 condition-loss column while declaring `Repairability: False` with no maximum — leaves "does the rock wear" a **gameplay** call, since it decides whether a naked spawn can ever be stuck. v0 needs only the first (`NOW.md` §0dur, §9.4: tools, gather, no bench). |
| sun azimuth sweep v0 — the sun crosses the sky: the sweep's arc across the day, against `RIG_SUN_AZIMUTH = 2.35` fixed (client lane, 2026-08-15) | **PROPOSED, under a spoken call** (2026-08-15, *"the sun donest move"*). The elevation curve already exists and is gated; only the bearing is a constant, so the change is small and its blast radius is not. Two things it moves that are calibrated on the current rig, both on purpose: `ART.md` §1's shadow-length band (measured off the reference set at one bearing) and `DayPin`'s capture register — **every frame any visual judge has scored was lit by the fixed azimuth**, so the first capture after this is not comparable to the archive and should say so. Single owner by `CLAUDE.md`'s coupled-set trap (tonemap · sky · exposure · fog). Sits beside the existing `day/night v0` row, which is where the 45-minute cycle and the 70% split live; the reference runs ~45 min day + ~15 min night = 60 min total (measured 2026-08-15), so our night is close and our day is ~30% short — a second, separable knob. |
